# Requirements Document

**HAMR-DAHN: an IronOxide back end for HAMR**

Status: Draft 0.2 (requirements refined and merged; approved by the user on 2026-11-12, including the decisions UD-01 to UD-34; no design.md or tasks.md yet). Spec: `hamr-ironoxide-backend`. Facts checked on 2026-11-09 against IronOxide revision 423a366 and Sireum kekinian revision e459583, and the repository state behind UD-01 on 2026-11-12; the study behind them is `docs/hamr_study.md`. Mapping choices that design.md must record as design decisions are marked "DD candidate".

## Introduction

HAMR (part of Sireum) generates code from AADL and SysML v2 models for the JVM, Linux, seL4 (CAmkES and Microkit) and ROS 2. IronOxide ("rsk") is a Ravenscar-style concurrency profile for Rust with a Verus-verified SRP kernel on the nRF52840, a generator for its `rsk::app!` declarations, a source lint, a binary link checker, a configuration checker, and a response-time analyzer. This project builds the bridge between them:

- **A. Inputs and the property set**: which models the back end takes, the `IronOxide` property set, and diagnostics.
- **B. Mapping and generation**: AADL threads, processes, shared data, ports, devices and flows mapped to IronOxide Tasks, Partitions, Resources, Release_Signals, Endpoints, peripherals and chains; refusal of everything outside the profile; the generated Application.
- **C. Contracts**: GUMBO component contracts translated into Creusot proof obligations on the generated task bodies, with a stated correspondence between the two meanings.
- **D. Timing**: AADL timing properties and end-to-end flow latencies carried into IronOxide's Analyzer, and its verdicts reported against the model.
- **E. Translation validation**: a checker whose core is written in Verus that compares the generated Task_Model with a semantic model of the AADL input, instead of verifying HAMR's Scala code.
- **F. Testing**: property-based tests over generated models, differential tests against HAMR's JVM simulation, and proof mutations.
- **G. Case study**: a ground robot whose Raspberry Pi 5 side uses an existing HAMR back end and whose nRF52840 side runs on IronOxide, generated from one model, with the MCU side run on IronOxide's HIL_Rig.
- **H. Verification plan, registers and build**: the evidence for every claim, in the style of IronOxide's own Verification_Plan.

The result must pass IronOxide's own tools unchanged. IronOxide is an external dependency: where this project would need an IronOxide change, the change is written as a proposal in `docs/ironoxide_proposals.md` (IOP-nn) and the requirements below work without it.

### Fixed inputs (decided by the user; not open in this spec)

- Target runtime: IronOxide at the revision of PAR-01, used read-only from the sibling directory `../IronOxide` (UD-02: generated Applications are built against a workspace-local extraction of `IronOxide/` at PAR-01, never against `../IronOxide` in place, which stays unchanged). Its Profile (version 0.6, Profile_Variant Ravenscar), App_Declaration grammar, Task_Model schema (`rsk-task-model/1`), tools and limits are as `docs/hamr_study.md` sections 11 and 12 record.
- Code generator: HAMR in Sireum kekinian at the revision of PAR-02, cloned into `external/` and built in the workspace.
- Modeling languages: AADL (instantiated by OSATE or FMIDE into HAMR's AIR) and SysML v2 through HAMR's own front end (UD-05: the case study uses AADL through FMIDE installed inside the workspace, whose installation awaits the user's approval, because HAMR's SysML path drops `Actual_Connection_Binding`).
- Verification approach: translation validation of the generated configuration, with the checker's core in Verus, in preference to verifying or testing the generator.
- Case study hardware: Raspberry Pi 5 (Linux) and nRF52840-DK, connected by a serial link; IronOxide's HIL_Rig of three nRF52840-DK boards and an 8-channel logic analyzer.
- Phases P1 to P5 as in the table below, plus a later Phase P6 for the physical robot; each Phase ends in a checkpoint that runs every test and proof and asks the user questions.

### Phases

| Phase | Scope |
|---|---|
| P1 | Study (done: `docs/hamr_study.md`); minimal back end: one periodic thread per process, no ports; the property set; diagnostics; the Semantic_Model export; generated Applications that pass IronOxide's tools and run under QEMU; the Model_Generator and the property-based tests that P1 criteria cite (UD-25) |
| P2 | Ports, shared data, Release_Signals, Endpoints, data-port sampling, validation functions, devices and interrupts; GUMBO contracts to Creusot; property-based tests over generated models (extended to these constructs; the Model_Generator starts in P1, UD-25) |
| P3 | Timing: the WCET flow, flows to chains, the Verdict_Report; differential tests against HAMR's JVM simulation on QEMU |
| P4 | Translation validation (TV_Checker in Verus), the mechanized contract correspondence, the mutation suites; differential tests on the HIL_Rig |
| P5 | Robot case study: the two-processor model, the Deployment_Splitter, the Serial_Bridge, both sides generated, the MCU side on the HIL_Rig |
| P6 (later) | Robot integration on the physical robot (specified here, not run in P5) |

Each requirement heading names the earliest Phase in which it must hold.

### Conventions

- `Rn.m` is acceptance criterion m of Requirement n of this document. `IronOxide Rn.m`, `IronOxide DD-nn`, `IronOxide ASM-nn` point into IronOxide's `.kiro/specs/rsk-ravenscar-profile/` documents; `PR-nn` is a restriction of IronOxide's Profile (`IronOxide/docs/profile.md`).
- `MAP-nn` is a row of Table M-1 (mappings and refusals), `DEV-nn` a row of Table M-2 (deviations from HAMR's AADL semantics), `NG-nn` a non-goal, `ASM-nn` an assumption, `UD-nn` a decision that the criteria apply while it awaits the user's approval (section "Decisions applied pending the user's approval"), `ORQ-nn` an open research question, `PAR-nn` a parameter, `IOP-nn` an IronOxide proposal, `F-n` a finding of `docs/hamr_study.md`, and `[RN-nn]` a research note with its source.
- A refused case of a Table M-1 row carries a label (a), (b), … in the row's "Refused" cell and is cited as `MAP-nn (x)` (R4.5).
- Rows of the per-requirement tables have identifiers of their own: `CK-nn` is a row of Table 22-1 (GUMBO clause kinds), `EX-nn` of Table 22-2 (GUMBO expression forms), `CT-nn` of Table 23-1 (trusted steps of the contract path), `SM-nn` of Table 28-1 (contents of a Semantic_Model), `CR-nn` of Table 32-1 (the comparison relation of the differential tests), `PM-nn` of Table 33-1 (proof artifacts and their planted defects), `TH-nn` of Table 36-1 (MCU-side threads of the Robot_Model), `CN-nn` of Table 36-2 (connections of the Robot_Model), and `RM-nn` of Table 37-1 (rig measurements). The rows of Table 42-1 (pins of external repositories and tools) are numbered 1, 2, … and cited as "Table 42-1 row n".
- Evidence_Item names follow IronOxide's style: `test-…` (example tests), `pbt-…` (property-based tests), `proof-…` (Verus or Creusot), `check-…` (static checks and IronOxide tool runs), `mut-…` (mutation runs), `diff-…` (differential tests), `hil-…` (HIL_Rig runs), `analysis-…` (Analyzer runs), `review-…` (recorded reviews), `doc-…` (documents). `<model>` stands for each model of the corpus concerned. Each acceptance criterion ends with the Evidence_Items that will show it.
- Every diagnostic by which the Backend refuses a model element names the AADL element (instance path and source position, R3.1; R3.7 where the AIR_Model records no position), the MAP row, with the label of the refused case where the row labels it, and exactly one rule of one of three rule classes (R3.1, R4.5):
  1. an IronOxide rule: a PR-nn, an IronOxide Rn.m, an IronOxide DD-nn, or a named IronOxide limit with the file and constant that define it;
  2. the phrase "Backend restriction" followed by the item on which the restriction rests: an IronOxide rule, a criterion, an NG-nn, an ORQ-nn or an IOP-nn of this document;
  3. the phrase "Phase restriction (R16.8)".

  A refusal of the input file itself (R1.6) names the file and the condition that holds instead, because the Backend has read no model element. A diagnostic of an IronOxide tool is reported unchanged with the AADL element it traces to (R3.4), or marked as untraced where it traces to none (R3.8); a warning of MAP-25 names the element, the property and MAP-25 (R3.5).
- Priority: a larger value is more urgent, as in IronOxide.
- Time values in AADL are read as integers in picoseconds (HAMR's internal unit) and written to IronOxide with explicit units; IronOxide's own conversion to ticks and cycles then applies (IronOxide R21.2). Wherever the Backend reads a time value (R6), the picosecond reading, the rounding and the exactness mark of R28.3 apply, and a value marked `fractional` is refused under MAP-04 (b).
- For per-Task state and Port_Cell state, "since instant 0" reads "since instant 0 or the last restart of the Task's Partition" (UD-12).
- Content from external sources was rephrased for compliance with licensing restrictions.

## Glossary

- **AADL**: The SAE Architecture Analysis and Design Language, as HAMR supports it [RN-01].
- **AIR**: HAMR's intermediate representation of an instantiated AADL or SysML v2 system (`hamr/air`), serialized as JSON; includes GUMBO annexes as `GclSubclause` values.
- **AIR_Model**: One AIR instance model of a root system.
- **Front_End**: The HAMR component that produces the AIR_Model: OSATE or FMIDE for AADL text, or HAMR's SysML v2 front end (`sireum hamr sysml codegen`).
- **HAMR**: The HAMR code generator of Sireum at PAR-02, with its existing back ends (JVM, Linux, seL4, Microkit, ROS 2).
- **Backend**: The HAMR IronOxide back end that this project builds: it reads an AIR_Model and emits an IronOxide Application, or refuses the model with diagnostics.
- **Generation report**: The document that the Backend writes into the output directory with each generated Application. It carries the provenance of R1.5 and the entries that criteria require: each applied default (R2.7), the generated items of each Table M-2 row (R4.3), the table version and, for each App_Declaration item, its AADL element and MAP row (R4.7), each time value that IronOxide's conversion changes (R6.8), each thread's Priority assignment (R7.6), each developer-owned file and developer region (R18.5), and each GUMBO clause and GUMBO function with its outcome (R22.8). R18.4 requires it to be byte-identical across runs; design.md states its format.
- **IronOxide**: The rsk runtime and tool set at PAR-01: Profile, Kernel, Generator, Profile_Lint, Link_Checker, Config_Checker, Analyzer, Protocol_Checker, WCET_Harness, HIL_Rig.
- **IronOxide_Generator**: IronOxide's Generator (`rsk-macros`, `rsk-gen`), which consumes an App_Declaration and emits Generated_Config, the Task_Model and the linker script.
- **Profile_Lint**, **Link_Checker**, **Config_Checker**, **Analyzer**, **Protocol_Checker**, **WCET_Harness**, **Kernel**, **HIL_Rig**: The IronOxide components of those names, as IronOxide's glossary defines them.
- **App_Prover**: Creusot with Why3 and its solvers at IronOxide's pinned versions, the application prover IronOxide designates (IronOxide DD-13).
- **Application**, **App_Declaration**, **Partition**, **Task**, **Job**, **Release_Point**, **Periodic_Task**, **Sporadic_Task**, **Release_Source**, **Release_Signal**, **Resource**, **Critical_Section**, **Endpoint**, **Protocol**, **Chain**, **Budget**, **MIT**, **MIT_Violation**, **Criticality_Level**, **Health_Monitor**, **Task_Model**, **WCET_Record**, **WCRT**, **Kernel_Timing_Parameters**, **Flight_Build**, **Build_Manifest**: As IronOxide's glossary defines them (IronOxide calls a chain "chain" in the App_Declaration). A plain Endpoint is one declared with a payload type and no Protocol.
- **IronOxide_Target**: An IronOxide Target name: `nrf52840` or `qemu_mps2_an386`.
- **IronOxide_Processor**: The one processor of a model whose `IronOxide::Target` property names an IronOxide_Target.
- **Peer_Processor**: A processor of the case-study model other than the IronOxide_Processor, here the Raspberry Pi 5.
- **IronOxide_Property_Set**: The property set `IronOxide` of Table 2-1, as an AADL property set file and as a SysML v2 library package.
- **Dispatch trigger**: The in event or event data port whose arrival dispatches a Sporadic thread, given by `Thread_Properties::Dispatch_Trigger` or, where that property is absent, by the thread's in event and event data ports.
- **Upward connection**: A connection whose destination Partition has a higher Criticality_Level than its source Partition (A is the highest).
- **Port_Cell**: A Resource that the Backend generates to carry one same-Partition port connection that R26.2 does not map to an Endpoint: an event count (R10.2), a bounded FIFO (R10.3), or a latest value with a freshness flag (R10.4). Its declared accessors are exactly the connection's source and destination Tasks (R10.7), and a restart of its Partition re-initializes it as at instant 0 (UD-12).
- **Port_API**: The generated interface through which a thread's developer code reads frozen inputs and writes outputs: AADL's Get Value and Put Value run-time services [RN-01].
- **Port_Glue**: The generated code of each Task's Job that performs Receive Input before and Send Output after the developer's compute code, and the generated validation functions.
- **Developer-owned file**: A file that the Backend writes only when no file exists at its path (R18.5).
- **Developer region**: The lines strictly between a begin-marker comment line and the next end-marker comment line that name the same region identifier, in the form of HAMR's marker merge with `invertMarkers` set (R18.5, UD-14). Developer code lives only in developer regions and developer-owned files (R18.8).
- **Wire_Type**: The generated `Plain` Rust type (IronOxide R42.1) that carries an AADL data type across Partitions or over the serial link.
- **Validation_Function**: A receiver-side function that IronOxide requires for every role receiving from a lower Criticality_Level (IronOxide R42.4).
- **GUMBO**: HAMR's contract language for AADL components (integration, initialize, compute, state, functions, invariants, handlers, cases) [RN-02].
- **GUMBOX**: HAMR's executable translation of GUMBO clauses (Slang on the JVM, Rust in the Microkit back end), used by tests and monitors.
- **Contract_Translator**: The Backend component that turns GUMBO clauses into Creusot contracts, Validation_Functions, executable predicates and Connection_Obligations.
- **Executable twin**: The Rust function that the Contract_Translator generates for a translated GUMBO clause or GUMBO function (R22.2, R22.9, R23.8). Given the values that the clause reads, it returns without panicking either an indication that the clause is undefined on that input or the clause's truth value; the App_Prover proves that it agrees with the clause's Pearlite translation (R23.8). The contract differential test (R23.5) and the Trace_Body test of R32.1 evaluate it.
- **Connection_Obligation**: A proof obligation that the integration guarantee clauses of a connection's source port imply the integration assume clauses of its destination port.
- **Semantic_Model**: A JSON document (`hamr-rsk-semantic-model/1`) that records the parts of the AIR_Model relevant to the mapping, written by the Semantic_Model_Extractor.
- **Semantic_Model_Extractor**: The tool that writes the Semantic_Model from the AIR_Model, implemented independently of the Backend's mapping code.
- **Mapping_Relation**: The relation between a Semantic_Model and a Task_Model that Table M-1 and IronOxide's conversion rules define, stated as Verus specification functions.
- **TV_Checker**: The translation-validation checker that accepts a Semantic_Model and Task_Model pair only if the Mapping_Relation holds; its core is written and proven in Verus.
- **Verdict_Reporter**: The Backend component that maps the Analyzer's results back to model elements; its output is the **Verdict_Report**.
- **Timing evidence**, **development evidence**: The two labels that R25.5 gives an Analyzer run. A run is timing evidence only when the IronOxide_Target is `nrf52840`, no input has the method `placeholder`, every measured WCET_Record and Kernel_Timing_Parameter states at least IronOxide PAR-02 (10,000) executions and a margin of PAR-11, and every measured Task-entry and Critical_Section-site record meets the branch-coverage threshold of IronOxide's Verification_Plan; every other run is development evidence. Development evidence is a label, not an outcome (R39.2).
- **Model_Generator**: The property-based test generator of SysML v2 and AIR models (Requirement 31; from Phase P1, UD-25).
- **Model_Generator model**: A model that the Model_Generator generates, with a record of the Table M-1 rows, mapping alternatives and refused cases that it exercises and the instance paths of the elements concerned (R31.1).
- **Trace_Body**: A generated deterministic thread body, in Slang for HAMR's JVM platform and in Rust for IronOxide, used by differential tests.
- **Trace_Comparator**: The tool that compares port-level traces of HAMR's JVM simulation with those of the IronOxide Application.
- **JVM_Schedule**: The slot sequence of one hyperperiod that the JVM run of a differential test uses with ART's static scheduler: one slot per Release instant of each Periodic thread and, for each Sporadic thread, one slot after each slot of the source of its Dispatch trigger, ordered as IronOxide would start the Jobs if every Job took no time (R32.2).
- **Job record**: The record that a Trace_Body writes for each Job: the thread's instance path and dispatch index, the frozen input of each input port, what was put on each output port, and the GUMBO state variables at the start and at the return of the compute code; on IronOxide also the Port_API counts and the Kernel time base read first and last (R32.2).
- **Observable trace**: The initialization records and, for each thread, its Job records in dispatch-index order; for an IronOxide run also the Kernel event log at the end of the run with its overflow count (R32.2). The Trace_Comparator compares observable traces (R32.3).
- **Deployment_Splitter**: The tool that splits a two-processor AIR_Model into one sub-model per processor (R34.6) and inserts, on the Serial_Bridge threads that the model declares, the ports, connections and marker properties of the bridge paths (R34.1, R34.8, UD-15).
- **IronOxide sub-model**, **peer sub-model**: The AIR_Models that the Deployment_Splitter writes for the IronOxide_Processor and for the Peer_Processor (R34.1, R34.6).
- **Serial_Bridge**: The components on both processors that carry cross-processor connections over the serial link: on each side, the Serial_Bridge threads that the model declares (UD-15), with the code that the Backend generates for them (R35.2, R35.7).
- **Serial_Bridge thread**: A thread that has the property `IronOxide::Serial_Bridge_Role` (Table 2-1; enumeration `Receive`, `Send`, `Receive_And_Send`; no default). The receiving Serial_Bridge thread of a processor has `Receive` or `Receive_And_Send`, and the sending one has `Send` or `Receive_And_Send` (R34.8); on the IronOxide_Processor, `Receive_And_Send` is refused (R35.8 (c), UD-33). A thread without the property is not a Serial_Bridge thread.
- **Frame_Codec**: The generated printer and parser of Serial_Bridge frames.
- **MCU_Side**, **Pi_Side**: The generated software of the nRF52840 and of the Raspberry Pi 5 in the case study.
- **Pi_Stand_In**: The host program or board that plays the Pi_Side's part on the HIL_Rig.
- **HIL build**: A build of an Application in the release profile with IronOxide's HIL features at PAR-01 (`nrf52840,debug-port,quiet,trace-ram,trace-pins`, `xtask/src/hil.rs` `FEATURES`; IronOxide's lean HIL build `FEATURES_LEAN` has `record-only` in place of `trace-pins`). At PAR-01 the Kernel keeps the response-time, execution and event records that the HIL_Rig reads only in a build with `trace-ram`, and IronOxide measures its own Applications only on HIL builds, never on the Flight_Build (R25.1, R37.1, UD-11).
- **Event mirror**: The Kernel's copy, in a HIL build (`trace-ram`), of every Health_Monitor event of a run into a ring of the last 512 events in `.rsk.noinit` (`EVENT_MIRROR`, `crates/rsk-hil/src/events.rs` at PAR-01), with the number of events written. The HIL_Rig reads it after the run (R37.2, R37.4, R37.7).
- **Trace freeze**: The sequence number, recorded with the event mirror, of the first event outside a run's expected set, at which the Kernel's trace ring stopped so that it holds the entries leading to that event (`crates/rsk-hil/src/events.rs`; R37.2, R37.4, R37.7).
- **Robot_Model**: The case-study model of Requirement 36.
- **Robot_Integration_Plan**: The Phase P6 document of Requirement 38.
- **Powered test**: Any interval during which the robot's motor supply is connected to a motor driver (R38.2).
- **Hardware emergency stop**: The switch and circuit that remove motor power from every motor driver and that neither the nRF52840, the Raspberry Pi 5, nor any software of this project controls or can override; a switch that acts by radio removes motor power when its radio link is lost (R38.5 (b)).
- **Session**: The interval from the function check of the hardware emergency stop to the disconnection of the motor supply, with one operator; no powered test of a session runs after a failed check (R38.5 (c)).
- **Build_System**: This workspace's scripted build and verification pipeline (`cargo xtask`), which produces Evidence_Items.
- **Verification job**: One run of `cargo xtask verify` of this workspace, the single entry point through which the Build_System produces every Evidence_Item (R39.2).
- **Evidence_Item**: A versioned artifact (proof log, test report, analysis report, measurement data, review record) traced to acceptance criteria.
- **Evidence store**: The directory of Evidence_Item records inside this workspace, holding one record per run of each item (R39.2, R42.3).
- **Verification_Plan**: This project's verification plan (`docs/verification_plan.md`).
- **Gap_Register**, **Trust_Base_Register**, **Proof_Assumption_Register**, **Proof_Mutation_Register**, **Translation_Mutation_Register**, **Traceability_Matrix**: This project's registers in `docs/`, in the formats of IronOxide's files of the same role: `docs/gap_register.toml`, `docs/trust_base_register.toml` and `docs/proof_assumptions.toml` (R40.1 to R40.3); the Proof_Mutation_Register, a TOML file in the format of IronOxide's `docs/proof_mutations.toml` that holds the planted defects of the proof artifacts (R33.1, R33.6); the Translation_Mutation_Register, `docs/translation_mutations.toml` in the form of IronOxide's `docs/config_mutations.toml`, holding the mutants of the refusals, the TV_Checker and the Deployment_Splitter (R30.4, R34.7); and `docs/traceability_matrix.md`, which `cargo xtask trace` generates (R40.4).

## Non-Goals

- **NG-01** Modifying IronOxide. Needed changes are proposals (IOP-nn) in `docs/ironoxide_proposals.md`. The workspace-local extraction of UD-02 is a copy; the files that the Build_System changes in it (the workspace membership and `rsk-entry`'s binding) are listed in the Trust_Base_Register (R18.9).
- **NG-02** Verifying or testing HAMR's own Scala and Slang code as the main evidence; translation validation of each output is used instead (Requirement 29).
- **NG-03** AADL features outside the profile: modes, dynamic reconfiguration, subprogram calls across components, shared data across processes, and the others that Table M-1 refuses.
- **NG-04** Real-time guarantees for the Pi_Side. Linux timing is not analyzed; the MCU_Side treats the Pi_Side as an untrusted peer that may be late, silent or wrong.
- **NG-05** More than one IronOxide_Processor per model, and multi-core targets (IronOxide NG-01).
- **NG-06** Certification approval or applicant plans. Like IronOxide, the project keeps a DO-178C path visible in its Gap_Register without claiming readiness.
- **NG-07** Changes to the GUMBO language or to HAMR's Front_End; the project adds a property set and back-end code only.
- **NG-08** New HAMR back ends for platforms other than IronOxide.
- **NG-09** HAMR's runtime monitors, attestation and system-testing variants for the IronOxide side; IronOxide's Health_Monitor and HIL evidence play that role.

## Assumptions

Each assumption is flagged for confirmation in design or on hardware; ASM-09 is instead a choice of this project, confirmed by the user's approval of UD-04. "Affects" lists the dependent requirements and table rows.

| ID | Assumption | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| ASM-01 | The Front_End (OSATE or FMIDE for AADL, HAMR's SysML v2 parser for SysML) instantiates the model faithfully into AIR, including positions, properties and GUMBO annexes. Trusted, not checked. For constructs that AIR at PAR-02 records only in part or not at all (modes beyond their names, subprogram calls, annexes other than GUMBO), the Backend relies on the Front_End for what AIR does not record (R16.9; ORQ-34 to ORQ-36). Which elements carry positions is ORQ-24. | R1, R16.9, R23 (Table 23-1), R28, R29.6, R31.8, R40.2 |
| ASM-02 | AADL `Thread_Properties::Priority` values in the models this project accepts use "larger is more urgent", as IronOxide does (ORQ-04). | R4 (MAP-06), R7 |
| ASM-03 | The AADL defaults used in Table M-1 hold: Deadline inherits Period; Queue_Size is 1; Dequeue_Protocol is OneItem; Overflow_Handling_Protocol is DropOldest; connection Timing is Sampled; the Period of a Sporadic thread is its minimum separation of dispatches; `Memory_Properties::Access_Right` is `read_write` (R9.2). Taken from HAMR's SysML AADL library comments where present (for `Access_Right`, the comment in `Memory_Properties.sysml`) and otherwise from the AADL standard, which the project does not hold. The Semantic_Model records an absent property as absent and applies none of these defaults (R28.1). | R4 (Table M-1, DEV-06), R6, R7.2, R9.2, R10, R11, R27.4, R28.1, R31.5 |
| ASM-04 | HAMR's informal semantics [RN-01] is the reference semantics of the Semantic_Model: inputs frozen at dispatch, outputs released at completion, entry points Initialize and Compute only, Periodic and Sporadic dispatch only. | R4 (Table M-2), R19, R23, R28, R29, R32 |
| ASM-05 | IronOxide's own evidence at PAR-01 holds as its Verification_Plan states it (Kernel proofs, Config_Checker, Analyzer proofs, HIL results), including its stated trust base and residuals. This project relies on it and does not re-derive it. | R18, R23 (Table 23-1), R25, R27, R29.7, R30, R36.9, R37, R39.5, R40.2 |
| ASM-06 | The serial link corrupts, loses or reorders bytes only in ways that the frame CRC and sequence numbers detect, apart from the CRC's residual undetected-error probability, which the Verification_Plan states (the CRC itself is ORQ-59). | R24 (Table 24-1), R35, R38.2 |
| ASM-07 | The nRF52840-DK's on-board J-Link virtual COM port, or a board-to-board UART wired on the HIL_Rig's spare pins, carries the Pi_Stand_In traffic at PAR-12. To be confirmed on the rig (ORQ-20, ORQ-63). | R35.10, R37 |
| ASM-08 | HAMR's JVM simulation executes a model's dispatches in a deterministic order for a given schedule; observed with ART's static scheduler (`art.scheduling.static`) in the isolette run (`docs/hamr_study.md` row 7). R32.2 runs that scheduler with a generated JVM_Schedule. | R32 |
| ASM-09 | This project's chosen meaning of GUMBO arithmetic over the bounded integer types S8 to S64 and U8 to U64 (UD-04): a mathematical result outside the type's range is undefined, so it makes a `requires` false and is a proof obligation in an `ensures` (R22.3, Table 22-2). This is not Slang's meaning. At PAR-02 Slang wraps these types modulo 2^n: its parser marks a full-width `@bits` type as wrapped (`SlangParser.scala`, `isWrapped`), its runtime wraps (`Z.scala`), and Logika encodes them as bit-vectors with no range check (`Smt2.scala`). HAMR's truncating Verus translation therefore agrees with Slang, and only HAMR's Rust GUMBOX executable, which panics on overflow, differs (finding F-1; ORQ-05). The difference is DEV-11 and is listed under R23.7; under this meaning a proof says nothing for inputs on which an assume clause overflows. | R13.4, R22.3, R23.3, R23.7, DEV-11 |
| ASM-10 | Each robot actuator can be stopped by driving its PWM output to zero duty, with the motor driver's own enable and brake behaviour documented in the Robot_Integration_Plan. | R36, R37.6, R38 |

The user approved every decision of this table on 2026-11-12; the Verification_Plan's change log records the approval as entry 1 (R41.2).

The criteria named in each row already apply the decision. Each decision awaits the user's approval; a decision that the user rejects is changed, together with the criteria it names, through the change log of Requirement 41 (R41.2). "Alternative" is the option that was not applied.

| ID | Decision applied | Alternative | Criteria |
|---|---|---|---|
| UD-01 | IronOxide pin (revised). Checked on 2026-11-12 with read-only git: the repository kiro-experimentation is shared with other work (branch `jsinfer-rewrite`, HEAD 4d288ef); `IronOxide/` at HEAD differs from PAR-01 by one added tracked file, `IronOxide/docs/tutorial.md` (commit f5f776d), and has one untracked directory, `IronOxide/docs/paper/`. R42.1 checks IronOxide on the extraction of UD-02, whose tree must equal the `IronOxide/` tree of commit PAR-01 (apart from the changes of R18.9 that the Trust_Base_Register lists). R42.2 fails a command only on a change that the Build_System itself makes under `IronOxide/` during that command, found by comparing a snapshot taken at the start with one taken at the end. `check-pins` lists every difference between the working tree and PAR-01 as a warning (R42.9). | Fail on any difference between the working tree and PAR-01, which blocks every run while other work commits under `IronOxide/`; or move PAR-01. | R42.1, R42.2, R42.9, Table 42-1 row 1 |
| UD-02 | Generated Applications are built against a workspace-local extraction of `IronOxide/` at PAR-01 (`git archive`). In the copy, the Build_System changes only the workspace membership and the binding of `rsk-entry`, and the Trust_Base_Register lists each change. IOP-07 proposes the IronOxide change that would make these edits unnecessary. | Wait for IOP-07. | R18.9, R42.2 to R42.4, R40.2, Table 42-1 row 1, ORQ-37 |

The robot's stop bound PAR-15 is shown by the bound of R36.9 and the measurement of R37.6, not by a Chain. | Declare flows through a property of the IronOxide_Property_Set. | R26, R36.9, R37.6, R37.8, ORQ-48 |

This project keeps the stricter meaning: an out-of-range result is undefined, false in a `requires` and a proof obligation in an `ensures`. The difference is recorded as DEV-11, and ASM-09 is restated as this project's choice. | Adopt wrapping, which gives an exact correspondence with Slang and changes Table 22-2 row EX-07, R13.4 and R22.3. | ASM-09, R22.3, R23.3, R23.7, DEV-11, ORQ-05 |

No trusted item is admitted at levels A and B. | Substitute evidence at levels A and B in the style of IronOxide R46.5. | R21.3, R21.5, R21.7 |
| UD-20 | The Robot_Model has 5 Tasks in 3 Partitions: safety (level A, supervisor and encoders; owns P0, GPIOTE and PWM0), motor (level B) and Serial_Bridge (level C; owns UARTE0). The velocity-command assume clause accepts every value, so that the speed limiter of R36.6 is reachable. | Other peripheral ownerships; an assume clause that bounds the commanded speed. | R36, ORQ-11 |

| UD-22 | Deadline-monotonic ranks compare relative deadlines in picoseconds, before IronOxide converts them to ticks. Two deadlines less than one tick apart therefore get different Priorities although the Task_Model records equal deadlines. | Rank the converted tick values. | R7.2 |
| UD-23 | R31.5's metamorphic timing property is restricted to per-Task verdicts, on models without Chains and without MAP-16 connections, with `Thread_Properties::Priority` given on every thread. Without the restriction the property is false: Chain bounds add the receivers' periods, C_sample depends on the periods, and deadline-monotonic ranks can change. | Drop the property. | R31.5 |

| UD-25 | The Model_Generator and the property-based tests start in Phase P1, because P1 criteria cite `pbt-` items. | P1 criteria get their `pbt-` evidence in P2. | R31 heading, R31.1 |

| UD-27 | For IronOxide tool acceptance, the Analyzer may exit with PASS or FAIL; the timing verdict is judged by R25.7. | Require PASS in R18.2. | R18.2, R31.2 |

PR-16 accepts such loops; without them, no array-typed port can cross Partitions. | Unroll such loops in generated code. | R19.4, R14.2, R13.1, R22.9 |
| UD-29 | R2.9 refuses `IronOxide::Validation_Function` on any in port that is not the destination of an Upward connection (MAP-18 (b)). Requirement 13 is unchanged. | Declare `validate` on the receiving role at every level, which changes Requirement 13 and R29.2. | R2.9, Table 2-1, MAP-18 (b), R29.2 |

Every other entry still fails the run. A restart of the motor control Partition re-initializes the shared Endpoint (IronOxide R42.3) and can fill it, which is an expected effect of the injected fault and not a fault of the safety Partition. | Keep the strict bullet, under which these runs fail whenever the Kernel records such an entry. | R37.4, R37.7 |

On the Peer_Processor, `Receive_And_Send` stays allowed. | None recorded. | R35.8, MAP-27 (l), Table 2-1, R34.8 |
