## Requirements

### Part A — Inputs, Property Set, and Diagnostics

### Requirement 1: Model inputs and the IronOxide processor (Phase P1)

**User Story:** As a system modeler, I want the Backend to take the same AADL or SysML v2 model that HAMR's other back ends take, so that one model drives every platform.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Backend SHALL accept as input one AIR_Model JSON file that HAMR's AIR JSON reader at PAR-02 reads without error, whether the Front_End produced it from AADL text through OSATE or FMIDE or from SysML v2 text through HAMR's SysML v2 front end, where "accept" means that the Backend proceeds to R1.2 to R1.4 and Requirements 2 to 20 without the input refusal of R1.6. *Evidence: `test-input-aadl-air` (on the committed AIR JSON files of the INSPECTA models until OSATE or FMIDE is installed, ORQ-10, ORQ-22), `test-input-sysml-air`.*
2. THE Backend SHALL generate the Application, or refuse the model, from the following elements of the AIR_Model and no others, so that a change to any other element leaves every file that the Backend writes identical apart from source positions and the provenance of R1.5:
   - the IronOxide_Processor, with its properties, modes and annex subclauses;
   - the `IronOxide::Target` value of every processor (R1.3), and the `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Processor_Binding` value, or its absence, of every process and every thread (R1.7, R1.8);
   - every virtual processor bound to the IronOxide_Processor, and every process bound to such a virtual processor (MAP-08);
   - each process whose `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Processor_Binding` names the IronOxide_Processor and every component inside it at any depth, with the features, connections, flow specifications (MAP-31), modes, properties and annex subclauses of each: the GUMBO annexes, and the subclauses of other annex languages, which R16.7 refuses when they are not empty (UD-16);
   - the connection instances that have an end inside such a process, with their properties and the declared connections that they reference, and, for each of them, the component at its other end with its category and its `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Processor_Binding` value or its absence (R1.4, R16.6);
   - the devices of MAP-20 at the other end of those connection instances, with their features, properties, modes and annex subclauses;
   - the modes that the AIR_Model records for every component that contains one of the components above, the root system instance included, and every connection or connection instance of the mode-transition kind that lies inside or ends at one of these components (R16.1);
   - each end-to-end flow that has at least one thread inside those processes, with its elements and its `Communication_Properties::Latency` value: the flows that R26.1 maps, whose elements are threads of those processes, optionally preceded by a device of MAP-20 that releases the first thread and optionally followed by a device of MAP-20, and the flows that R26.4 and R26.7 report (MAP-21);
   - every bus that has `IronOxide::Serial_Link`, with that value (R35.1, R35.8);
   - the data component classifiers and GUMBO libraries that these elements reference.

   *Evidence: `test-processor-selection`, `pbt-processor-scope` (copies of a model that differ only in elements outside this list, including copies that the Backend refuses), `proof-tv-core`.*
3. IF the AIR_Model has no processor whose `IronOxide::Target` value is an IronOxide_Target (a value outside the enumeration of Table 2-1 is refused under R2.4 and does not count), or has two or more such processors, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model with a diagnostic of R3.1 that names MAP-27 (a) and either the root system instance (no such processor) or every such processor (two or more). *Evidence: `test-refusal-diagnostics`.*
4. IF a connection instance joins a feature of a thread inside a process of R1.2 to a component that is not inside such a process and is not a device of MAP-20, and the connection instance is one of the following cases, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model with one diagnostic of R3.1 naming the connection instance, the component at its other end, that component's `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Processor_Binding` value or its absence, and the MAP-27 label of the case:
   - (b) a port connection instance whose other end is a thread bound, directly or through a virtual processor, to another processor (a cross-processor port connection, R34.1);
   - (c) a data access connection instance, or (d) a subprogram access connection instance, whose other end lies inside a process bound to another processor;
   - (e) any other connection instance whose other end is not a Serial_Bridge thread that the model declares by `IronOxide::Serial_Bridge_Role` (R34.8, UD-15).

   *Evidence: `test-refusal-diagnostics` (one model per case), `mut-refusals`.*
5. THE Backend SHALL write into every file it writes (each generated file, the generation report, and each diagnostics document of Requirement 3) the SHA-256 digest of the AIR_Model file's bytes as 64 lowercase hexadecimal digits, the name and version of the Front_End that produced the AIR_Model as stated in the Backend's invocation, and the 40-digit commit hashes of PAR-01 and of both revisions of PAR-02. *Evidence: `test-output-provenance`.*
6. IF the input file is not valid JSON, is not read without error by HAMR's AIR JSON reader at PAR-02, does not contain exactly one root system instance, comes without the Front_End name and version of R1.5 in the invocation, or, where the invocation states that the input is a sub-model of R1.9, comes without the items that R1.9 takes from the invocation, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the input, writing no generated artefact and reporting a diagnostic of severity error that names the file, which of these five conditions holds, and, for a JSON syntax error, the line and column of the first error. Because the Backend reports this input refusal before it reads any model, the diagnostic names no AADL element and no MAP row, and R3.1 does not apply to it. *Evidence: `test-input-rejected`.*
7. IF no process's `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Processor_Binding` names the IronOxide_Processor, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model with a diagnostic of R3.1 naming the IronOxide_Processor, IronOxide R21.3 (an Application declares at least one Partition, PR-28) and MAP-27 (f). *Evidence: `test-refusal-diagnostics`.*
8. IF a process has no `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Processor_Binding`, or the binding of a process or thread names the IronOxide_Processor together with another processor, or a thread's own binding names a processor other than the one its process is bound to, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model with a diagnostic of R3.1 naming the process or thread, its binding value or its absence, and MAP-27 (g). *Evidence: `test-refusal-diagnostics`, `mut-refusals`.*
9. WHERE the Backend's invocation states that the input AIR_Model is an IronOxide sub-model that the Deployment_Splitter wrote (R34.6; Phase P5 onward), THE Backend SHALL also write into every file of R1.5 the SHA-256 digest of the original two-processor AIR_Model file, as 64 lowercase hexadecimal digits, and the name and version of the Deployment_Splitter, both as the invocation states them, the Front_End of R1.5 being the one that produced the original AIR_Model. *Evidence: `test-output-provenance` (an IronOxide sub-model of the Robot_Model and of each two-processor corpus model).*

### Requirement 2: The IronOxide property set (Phase P1)

**User Story:** As a system modeler, I want one documented property set for the values IronOxide needs and AADL does not define, so that criticality levels, fault responses and the other values come from the model rather than from hand edits of generated code.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Backend SHALL provide the IronOxide_Property_Set as an AADL property set file and as a SysML v2 library package that each declare exactly the properties of Table 2-1, each with the name, type and component or feature categories of its Table 2-1 row, with no default value in the declaration (so that the Backend, not the Front_End, applies every default), and with the row's Default and Feeds cells in a comment on the declaration. *Evidence: `doc-property-set`, `check-property-set-table` (both forms compared with Table 2-1), `test-property-set-parses` (the SysML package through HAMR's SysML v2 front end; the AADL file through FMIDE, reported as not run, never as passed, where FMIDE is not installed, ORQ-10).*
2. THE Backend SHALL write each Partition's `code`, `ram` and `stack` sizes from the `Memory_Properties` values of its process: `code` = `Memory_Properties::Code_Size`; `ram` = `Memory_Properties::Data_Size` + `Memory_Properties::Stack_Size`, because IronOxide places the Partition's stack at the bottom of its RAM region (IronOxide DD-07) (UD-06); and `stack` = `Memory_Properties::Stack_Size`; each converted to a whole number of bytes and written in the largest of IronOxide's size units `B`, `KiB` (1,024 bytes) and `MiB` (1,048,576 bytes) that represents it exactly. Where each size comes from for a process (`Stack_Size` does not apply to processes in HAMR's SysML library) and the factors of AADL's size units are ORQ-23. *Evidence: `test-map-07` (including a process whose Data_Size + Stack_Size is a whole number of KiB while neither size is).*
3. IF a property that Table 2-1 marks required for an element, or a size of criterion 2, is absent from that element, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model, without substituting a default value, with the diagnostic of R3.1 naming the element, the property, and the rule and MAP row that the property's Feeds cell names (IronOxide R15.7 and MAP-07 (b) for a size). *Evidence: `test-refusal-diagnostics`, `mut-refusals`.*
4. IF a property of Table 2-1 has a value outside the type, range or set that its Type cell states, or a size of criterion 2 is zero or not a whole number of bytes, or the stack size is not a multiple of 8 bytes or exceeds the RAM size, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model with the diagnostic of R3.1 naming the element, the property, the value, the rule of the property's Feeds cell that the value violates, and that cell's MAP row (IronOxide R15.7 and MAP-07 for a size: label (b) for a zero size, label (f) for a size that is not a whole number of bytes, label (d) for the stack size). *Evidence: `test-refusal-diagnostics`, `test-property-bounds` (each bound of each range of Table 2-1 and the value one past it).*
5. WHEN the Backend generates an Application in which an element lacks a property for which Table 2-1 gives a default, THE Backend SHALL use that default, and no other value, as the property's value for that element. *Evidence: `test-defaults-reported`.*
6. WHEN the Backend generates from the AADL form and the SysML v2 form of the same model, with the same components, connections, instance paths and property values, THE Backend SHALL write the same set of files, each byte-identical to its counterpart once source positions and the provenance fields of R1.5 are removed. *Evidence: `test-property-set-equivalence` over every model of the corpus that has both forms (reported as not run where FMIDE is not installed, ORQ-10).*
7. WHEN the Backend applies a default of Table 2-1 (criterion 5), THE Backend SHALL list it in the generation report as one entry naming the element's instance path, the property and the applied value. *Evidence: `test-defaults-reported`.*
8. IF `IronOxide::MIT_Policy` is set on a thread whose `Dispatch_Protocol` is Periodic, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model with the diagnostic of R3.1 naming the thread, the property, PR-11 and MAP-01 (b). *Evidence: `test-refusal-diagnostics`.*
9. IF `IronOxide::Validation_Function` is set on an in port that is not the destination of an Upward connection between threads of two processes bound to the IronOxide_Processor (MAP-15, MAP-16, MAP-18), THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model with the diagnostic of R3.1 naming the port, the property, IronOxide R42.4 and MAP-18 (b) (UD-29). The property is thereby refused on the destination port of a same-level or downward cross-Partition connection, for which Requirement 13 declares no `validate` entry. *Evidence: `test-refusal-diagnostics` (the property on the destination port of an Upward connection is accepted; on the destination port of a same-level, a downward and a same-Partition connection, and on an unconnected in port, it is refused), `mut-refusals`.*

**Table 2-1 — The IronOxide_Property_Set**

| Property | Type | Applies to | Default | Feeds |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Target` | enumeration (`nrf52840`, `qemu_mps2_an386`) | processor | required on exactly one processor, which it makes the IronOxide_Processor (R1.3 refuses zero or several) | header `target` (IronOxide R21.1); MAP-27 |
| `Operating_Duration` | Time: a whole number of ns that converts, rounding down (IronOxide R21.2), to at least 1 and fewer than 2^62 ticks of the IronOxide_Target (at least 63 ns on `nrf52840`, 16 MHz time base; at least 40 ns on `qemu_mps2_an386`, 25 MHz), and to no fewer ticks than the period or MIT of any Task or the offset of any Periodic_Task, each converted as IronOxide R21.2 converts it | processor | required on the IronOxide_Processor | header `operating_duration`, in the largest unit that represents the value exactly (IronOxide R9.9, R22.6, R9.5); MAP-27 |
| `Safe_State` | string: a Rust path without generic arguments (identifiers, `crate`, `self` or `super`, separated by `::`) into the system's safe-state crate (DD candidate: which crate), naming the system safe-state function of type `fn() -> !` (IronOxide `decl.rs`); the function's existence and type are checked when the generated Application compiles | processor | required on the IronOxide_Processor | header `safe_state` (IronOxide R14.4, R21.1); MAP-27 |
| `Log_Capacity` | integer 1 to 2^30 − 1 (1,073,741,823) | processor | 32 (IronOxide's default) | header `log_capacity` (IronOxide R14.6; `validate.rs` `MAX_LOG_CAPACITY`); MAP-27 |
| `FP_Flush_To_Zero` | Boolean | processor | false (IronOxide's default) | header `fp_flush_to_zero` (IronOxide R11.8); MAP-27 |
| `Criticality_Level` | enumeration (A, B, C, D, E), A the highest | process | required on every process bound to the IronOxide_Processor | Partition `level` (IronOxide R15.1); MAP-09 |
| `Fault_Response` | enumeration (END_JOB, RESTART_PARTITION, STOP_PARTITION, SAFE_STATE) | process | required on every process bound to the IronOxide_Processor | Partition `fault` (IronOxide R14.5, R15.7); MAP-09 |
| `Overrun_Response` | enumeration (END_JOB, RESTART_PARTITION, STOP_PARTITION, SAFE_STATE, RECORD_AND_CONTINUE); RECORD_AND_CONTINUE only on a process whose Criticality_Level is the highest among the Application's Partitions | process | required on every process bound to the IronOxide_Processor | Partition `overrun` (IronOxide R18.5, R18.6); MAP-09 |
| `Deadline_Miss_Response` | enumeration (RESTART_PARTITION, STOP_PARTITION, SAFE_STATE, RECORD_ONLY); applied to deadline misses and release overlaps of the Partition's Tasks (IronOxide R9.6, R9.7) | process | required on every process bound to the IronOxide_Processor | Partition `deadline_miss` (IronOxide R15.1, R15.7); MAP-09 |
| `MIT_Violation_Threshold` | integer 0 to 4,294,967,295 (2^32 − 1, the range of IronOxide's `mit_threshold`, `decl.rs`); the Partition's Fault_Response applies when its MIT_Violation count exceeds the value (IronOxide R10.4) | process | required on every process bound to the IronOxide_Processor | Partition `mit_threshold` (IronOxide R10.4); MAP-09 |
| `Peripherals` | list of distinct strings, each the name of a peripheral in the IronOxide_Target's table (IronOxide `target.rs`) that the Kernel does not own, that no other process lists, and that no device connected to another process names (MAP-20) | process | empty list | Partition `peripherals` (IronOxide R15.1, R21.5, PR-28, PR-29); MAP-07, MAP-20 |
| `Peripheral` | string, the name of a peripheral in the IronOxide_Target's table (IronOxide `target.rs`) that the Kernel does not own | device | required for a device connected to the IronOxide_Processor's threads | Partition `peripherals` of the owning Partition (IronOxide R21.5, PR-28, PR-29); MAP-20 |
| `Uses_FPU` | Boolean | thread | true where a port of the thread, a GUMBO state variable of the thread, or a data subcomponent the thread accesses (MAP-10) has a floating-point type (`Base_Types::Float`, `Base_Types::Float_32`, `Base_Types::Float_64`, or a data type that extends one of them; how the AIR_Model records that a data type extends one is ORQ-78) or a record or array type with such a component at any depth; false otherwise | Task flag `fpu`, written exactly when the value is true (PR-31); MAP-28 |
| `MIT_Policy` | enumeration (defer, discard) | thread, Sporadic threads only (R2.8) | defer | Task `mit_policy` (IronOxide R10.2); MAP-02 |
| `Validation_Function` | string: a Rust path without generic arguments into the destination Partition's crate, naming the receiver-side validation function for the port's Wire_Type | in port, only the destination of an Upward connection (R2.9, UD-29) | no default: where absent, MAP-18 supplies the function | role `validate` of the plain Endpoint (IronOxide R42.4); MAP-18 |
| `Serial_Link` | record: baud rate (integer, bit/s, equal to the rate of PAR-12, R35.1), frame payload bound (integer, bytes, 1 to PAR-13), device path on the Peer_Processor (non-empty string) | bus | required on a bus that binds a cross-processor connection | Backend restriction (R35.1, R35.8 (a); no IronOxide rule applies); MAP-27, Requirement 35 |
| `Serial_Bridge_Role` | enumeration (`Receive`, `Send`, `Receive_And_Send`) | thread | no default: a thread without it is not a Serial_Bridge thread | marks the Serial_Bridge threads that the model declares and on which the Deployment_Splitter inserts ports (R34.8, UD-15): on each processor, exactly one thread with `Receive` or `Receive_And_Send` and exactly one with `Send` or `Receive_And_Send`, both in one process (R34.4 (f)); on the IronOxide_Processor, `Receive_And_Send` is refused (R35.8 (c), UD-33); Backend restriction (R34.8); MAP-27 (i), (l) |

Partition sizes are not properties of the IronOxide_Property_Set, and R2.1 does not declare them. R2.2 takes them from `Memory_Properties`: `code` = Code_Size, `ram` = Data_Size + Stack_Size (IronOxide's RAM region holds the stack, IronOxide DD-07) and `stack` = Stack_Size (UD-06). Where each size comes from for a process is ORQ-23.

### Requirement 3: Diagnostics (Phase P1)

**User Story:** As a system modeler, I want every refusal to point at my model element and at the IronOxide rule it breaks, so that I can fix the model without reading generated code.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. IF the Backend refuses a model element, THEN THE Backend SHALL report, for each reason for which it refuses the element, one diagnostic of severity error that names the element's instance path (the segments of the element's name in the AIR_Model, from the root system instance to the element) and source position (the file URI, begin line and begin column, 1-based, that the AIR_Model records for the element), the MAP row of Table M-1 with the label of the refused case where the row labels it (for a refusal of R2.3 or R2.4, the MAP row of the property's Feeds cell in Table 2-1), and exactly one of the following three rule classes:
   - (1) an IronOxide rule: a PR-nn, an IronOxide Rn.m, an IronOxide DD-nn, or a named IronOxide limit with the file and constant that define it, as the Rule cell of that MAP row gives it under the label, or as the property's Feeds cell gives it for a refusal of R2.3 or R2.4;
   - (2) "Backend restriction" followed by the item on which the restriction rests (an IronOxide rule, a criterion, an NG-nn, an ORQ-nn or an IOP-nn of this document), where that Rule cell or Feeds cell marks the case Backend restriction;
   - (3) "Phase restriction (R16.8)", for a refusal of R16.8.

   Where several elements cause one refusal together (a MAP-26 limit, fan-in, two processes with the same sanitized name, or several IronOxide_Processors), the diagnostic SHALL name the instance path and source position of every contributing element. An input refusal of R1.6 is not a refusal of a model element: the Backend reports it before it reads any model, so it names no AADL element and no MAP row, and this criterion does not apply to it. *Evidence: `test-refusal-diagnostics` (at least one refusal of each rule class), `pbt-refusals-named`.*
2. THE Backend SHALL evaluate in one run every refusal condition that Table M-1 and the criteria of this document state over the whole model, except that, for an element that R16.8 refuses as a construct of a Phase later than the Phase for which the Backend is built, the Backend reports the Phase refusal of R16.8 in place of the conditions of that construct's Table M-1 row and of the criteria of the requirement that Table 16-1 names for the row; THE Backend SHALL report the resulting diagnostics ordered by file URI (compared by Unicode code point), then begin line, then begin column, with diagnostics whose element has no recorded position (R3.7) after all others, and ties ordered by instance path, then by MAP row number, then by label; a diagnostic that names several elements is ordered by the first of them under this order; after an input refusal of R1.6 or a refusal of R1.3 the Backend SHALL evaluate no further condition, because no model, or no IronOxide_Processor's side of the model, is defined. *Evidence: `test-refusal-diagnostics`, `test-phase-refusals` (a model with constructs of a later Phase and with refusals of the current Phase, all reported in one run).*
3. WHEN a Backend run ends, THE Backend SHALL write every diagnostic of the run, each with its severity (error or warning), a message, and the items that R3.1, R3.4 or R3.5 require, both as text and as one JSON document that states the schema identifier `hamr-rsk-diagnostics/1` and conforms to the schema that this repository publishes under that identifier, the two forms listing the same diagnostics in the same order, and the JSON document being written with an empty list of diagnostics for a run that reports none. *Evidence: `test-diagnostics-schema`.*
4. WHEN an IronOxide tool (the IronOxide_Generator, the Profile_Lint, the Link_Checker, the Config_Checker or the Analyzer) reports a diagnostic on the generated Application (its generated files and the developer code in it), THE Backend SHALL report the IronOxide diagnostic unchanged (the tool, its severity, its rule where the tool states one, its item or location, and its message) together with the instance path and source position of the AADL element from which the diagnosed item was generated: for an IronOxide_Generator diagnostic, the element of its item (a partition, task, resource, signal, peripheral, endpoint, protocol or chain, or a field of one); for a Profile_Lint finding, the element whose generation produced the file and line, or, for a line in a developer-owned file or region (R18.5), the thread whose Task the file or region belongs to, or the process where the file belongs to no single Task, marked as developer code; for a Config_Checker discrepancy, the element of its Task_Model item; for an Analyzer input rejection, the element of the Task or Chain it names (Analyzer verdicts are reported by Requirement 27); and for a Link_Checker violation, the element of the function its message names (ORQ-25). *Evidence: `test-ironoxide-diagnostic-backmap`.*
5. WHEN the AIR_Model lists a property that MAP-25 names on the IronOxide_Processor or on an element of R1.2, or a flow specification (an AIR `Flow` of kind Source, Sink or Path) of a component of R1.2 (MAP-31), THE Backend SHALL report one diagnostic of severity warning for each such element and property and one for each such flow specification, naming the element's instance path and source position, the property or the flow specification, and MAP-25 or MAP-31, and SHALL not refuse the model on account of that property or flow specification. *Evidence: `test-map-25`, `test-map-31` (a copy of the committed AADL AIR of the INSPECTA isolette model with an IronOxide_Processor added, in which each flow specification of a component of R1.2 gets one warning).*
6. IF the Backend refuses at least one model element, THEN THE Backend SHALL end the run with a non-zero exit status, writing no generated artefact other than the diagnostics of R3.3 and leaving every file already present in the output directory, including the developer-owned files and regions of R18.5, byte-identical. *Evidence: `test-refusal-diagnostics`, `test-refusal-no-output`.*
7. IF the AIR_Model records no source position for an element that a diagnostic names, or records a position without a file URI, THEN THE Backend SHALL state in that diagnostic that the AIR_Model gives no position (or no file) for the element, and SHALL not substitute another position, such as line 1, column 1 or the position of an enclosing element (ORQ-24). *Evidence: `test-diagnostic-no-position`.*
8. IF the Backend cannot trace an IronOxide tool diagnostic to an AADL element under R3.4 (for example a diagnostic on Kernel code or on an IronOxide crate, or a Link_Checker message that names no generated or developer function), THEN THE Backend SHALL report the IronOxide diagnostic unchanged with a statement that no AADL element was found, and SHALL not omit it, so that the Backend reports exactly as many IronOxide diagnostics as the tool emitted. *Evidence: `test-ironoxide-diagnostic-backmap`.*

### Requirement 4: Mapping and deviation tables (Phase P1)

**User Story:** As a certification engineer, I want each AADL construct's mapping, refusal and deviation stated once, so that I can trace every generated item and every refusal to a decision.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN the Backend generates an Application, THE Backend SHALL generate each model element on the IronOxide_Processor's side of the model (the elements of R1.2), and each of its property values, into exactly the IronOxide items and values that the "IronOxide mapping" cells of the Table M-1 rows describing that element or value state, mapping each port connection by exactly one of MAP-11 to MAP-16, except that MAP-21 replaces MAP-11 to MAP-14 for a connection on a flow that MAP-21 maps. *Evidence: `test-map-01` to `test-map-31` (at least one test per row, covering every alternative that the row's mapping cell states), `proof-tv-core`.*
2. IF a model element on the IronOxide_Processor's side of the model is of a kind that no row of Table M-1 other than MAP-24 (j) maps, refuses, ignores or reports as not carried, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the element with a diagnostic of R3.1 naming MAP-24 (j), where a kind is a component category, a feature category together with its direction, a connection category, a flow specification, an end-to-end flow, a mode, a mode transition, or an annex subclause of one annex language; an annex subclause in a language other than GUMBO that R16.7 defines as empty is of no kind under this criterion, and the Backend reports no diagnostic for it (UD-16); likewise, a flow specification of a component of R1.2 gets no diagnostic of this criterion, because MAP-31 reports it as not carried, with one warning (R3.5). This criterion covers element kinds only; whether properties that no row of Table M-1 reads but that change port or dispatch semantics are refused or ignored is ORQ-75. *Evidence: `pbt-refusals-named` (Model_Generator models containing at least one element of each kind that Table M-1 does not map), `test-annex-subclauses` (copies of the committed AIR files of R1.1 with an IronOxide_Processor added, whose empty EMV2 subclauses get no diagnostic), `test-map-31` (a copy of the committed AIR of the INSPECTA isolette with an IronOxide_Processor added, whose flow specifications get one MAP-31 warning each and no MAP-24 (j) diagnostic).*
3. WHEN the Backend generates an Application, THE Backend SHALL list in the generation report, for each row of Table M-2, every generated item that the row's "Applies to" cell selects, each with the instance path of the AADL element it was generated from and the DEV identifier, and SHALL list under that row no item that the cell does not select. *Evidence: `test-deviations-reported` (at least one model per DEV row, each checked against the exact set of items that the row selects).*
4. WHEN Table M-1 or Table M-2 changes, THE Verification_Plan SHALL record in its change log one entry for each changed row, stating the new table version, the row identifier, the change (row added, mapping changed, refusal added or removed, rule changed, or row retired), its rationale, and the affected requirements. *Evidence: `check-mapping-version` (each table version from 1 onward has a change-log entry for every row that differs from the previous version).*
5. IF a model element matches a labelled refusal of a row of Table M-1, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model with one diagnostic for each matched refusal that identifies the element as R3.1 requires and names the MAP row with the label and the rule that the row's "Rule" cell gives under the same label, in the rule class of R3.1 to which that entry belongs. *Evidence: `mut-refusals` (at least one mutant per labelled refusal of every row), `test-refusal-diagnostics`.*
6. WHERE the build includes the TV_Checker (Phase P4 onward), IF the table version that the TV_Checker's Mapping_Relation cites differs from the current version of Tables M-1 and M-2, THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail the build and report both versions. *Evidence: `check-mapping-version`.*
7. WHEN the Backend generates an Application, THE Backend SHALL list in the generation report the version of Tables M-1 and M-2 and, for each item of the generated App_Declaration (Partition, Task, Resource, per-Task state, Release_Signal, Endpoint, role, Chain and peripheral assignment), the instance path of the AADL element and the MAP row from which the item was generated. *Evidence: `test-generation-trace` (every App_Declaration item of every corpus model listed exactly once, with a row whose mapping cell produces that kind of item).*

**Table M-1 — Mappings and refusals** (version 2, shared with Table M-2; every change to either table increments the version by 1, and no version number is reused). The Verification_Plan's change log records each row that version 2 changes from version 1, as R4.4 states. "IronOxide mapping" uses the App_Declaration grammar of `docs/hamr_study.md` section 12; a parenthesized `Rn.m` in a mapping cell names the criterion that implements the mapping. "Refused" labels each refused case (a), (b), …; the "Rule" cell gives under the same label the rule that the case's diagnostic names (R3.1, R4.5). Each Rule entry belongs to one rule class of R3.1: an IronOxide rule (class 1), or, where the entry says "Backend restriction", that phrase followed by the item on which the restriction rests (class 2). "Backend restriction" marks a refusal that IronOxide at PAR-01 does not make itself. The Phase refusals of R16.8 (class 3, "Phase restriction (R16.8)") are not listed in this table. R2.3 and R2.4 refuse a Table 2-1 property that is absent where Table 2-1 requires it, or that has a value outside its Type cell, under the MAP row and rule of the property's Feeds cell; MAP-07 (c) and MAP-09 (a) label these cases for process properties. Time values are read from the AIR_Model as R28.3 states: an integer number of picoseconds, rounded to the nearest picosecond and marked `exact`, `noise` or `fractional`; MAP-04 (b) refuses a `fractional` value. A comparison of time values in a "Refused" cell refuses the element if it holds for the values so read or for the values after IronOxide's conversion for the IronOxide_Target (IronOxide R21.2, `crates/rsk-gen/src/convert.rs`: Budgets rounded up to cycles; periods, MITs and deadlines rounded down to ticks; offsets rounded to the nearest tick, a value halfway between two ticks rounding up).

| Row | AADL construct | IronOxide mapping | Refused | Rule | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| MAP-01 | Thread with `Dispatch_Protocol => Periodic` | Periodic_Task: `period` from `Timing_Properties::Period`; `offset` from `Timing_Properties::Dispatch_Offset`, 0 when absent (R5.1, R6.1) | (a) no Period; (b) `IronOxide::MIT_Policy` present (R2.8); (c) `Thread_Properties::Dispatch_Trigger` present | (a) PR-27; (b) PR-11; (c) PR-11, IronOxide R22.4 | IronOxide's absolute periodic Releases (PR-10) give AADL's periodic dispatch; a Periodic_Task has no Release_Source and no MIT policy (`syntax.rs` rejects `mit_policy` on a periodic task under PR-11) |
| MAP-02 | Thread with `Dispatch_Protocol => Sporadic` | Sporadic_Task: `mit` = Period (AADL's minimum separation of dispatches, ASM-03); Release_Source from the thread's single Dispatch trigger: a Release_Signal (MAP-11, MAP-13), an Endpoint role (MAP-15) or a peripheral interrupt (MAP-20); `mit_policy` from `IronOxide::MIT_Policy`, `defer` when absent (R5.2, R5.8) | (a) no Period; (b) no Dispatch trigger, or more than one, or a `Thread_Properties::Dispatch_Trigger` that names a feature other than an in event or in event data port of the thread (R5.3); (c) `Dispatch_Offset` present; (d) a Dispatch trigger with no incoming connection (R5.6) | (a) PR-27; (b) PR-11, IronOxide R22.4; (c) PR-27 (an offset belongs to a Periodic_Task only); (d) PR-11, IronOxide R22.4 | IronOxide gives each Task exactly one Release_Source (PR-11) |
| MAP-03 | Thread with `Dispatch_Protocol` Aperiodic, Timed, Hybrid, Background or a project-specified value (`AADL_Project::Supported_Dispatch_Protocols`), or with no `Dispatch_Protocol` | none | (a) Aperiodic, Background, a project-specified value, or absent; (b) Timed; (c) Hybrid | (a) PR-27 (no period or MIT); (b) PR-11 (a timeout is an alternative Release); (c) PR-11 (two Release_Sources) | response-time analysis needs a period or MIT, and IronOxide has no timeouts |
| MAP-04 | `Timing_Properties::Period`, `Deadline` and `Dispatch_Offset` values, and every other time value the Backend writes | each written in the largest of ns, us, ms and s that represents the value read by R28.3 exactly (R6.2); `deadline` = Deadline, or the Period when Deadline is absent (ASM-03) | (a) Deadline > Period (the period or MIT); (b) a value that is not a whole number of ns, or that R28.3 marks `fractional`; (c) a period, MIT or relative deadline that the conversion makes 0 ticks; (d) a period, MIT or offset beyond the IronOxide_Processor's `IronOxide::Operating_Duration` | (a) IronOxide R22.1; (b) IronOxide R21.2 (no IronOxide time unit is finer than ns); (c) IronOxide R22.1; (d) IronOxide R22.6 | constrained deadlines (PR-27); no rounding other than IronOxide's own (R21.2); (c) and (d) are IronOxide_Generator rejections that R17.4 requires the Backend to catch first |
| MAP-05 | `Timing_Properties::Compute_Execution_Time` (a range) | `budget` = the range's upper bound, covering the whole Job including the Port_Glue (R6.6); measured WCETs are checked against it (Requirement 25, IronOxide R28.4) | (a) absent (no default, unlike the 50 ms of HAMR's Microkit back end); (b) upper bound > relative deadline; (c) an upper bound that converts to 0 cycles or to more than 2^32 − 1 cycles (about 67.1 s at 64 MHz) | (a) PR-27; (b) IronOxide R22.1; (c) IronOxide R22.1 | the Kernel enforces the Budget at run time (IronOxide R18; DEV-08) |
| MAP-06 | `Thread_Properties::Priority` | where every thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor has it: the distinct values, in increasing order, onto Priorities 1 to k, larger being more urgent (ASM-02, R7.1); where no such thread has it: deadline-monotonic Priorities 1 to k, a shorter relative deadline receiving a higher Priority, relative deadlines compared in picoseconds before IronOxide's conversion to ticks (R7.2, UD-22); equal values or equal relative deadlines receive equal Priorities, and equal-priority Tasks start in instance-path order (R7.5, DEV-07) | (a) present on some threads bound to the IronOxide_Processor only; (b) more than PAR-03 distinct Priorities | (a) PR-03 (Backend restriction: one assignment rule for all Priorities); (b) PR-33 | fixed priorities with 7 Task levels (IronOxide DD-02) |
| MAP-07 | Process bound to the IronOxide_Processor | one Partition with its own Partition crate (IronOxide DD-11); `code` = `Memory_Properties::Code_Size`, `ram` = `Data_Size` + `Stack_Size` (IronOxide places the stack at the bottom of the RAM region, DD-07; UD-06) and `stack` = `Stack_Size` (R2.2; where these sizes come from is ORQ-23); the threads of its thread groups become Tasks of this Partition (R8.2) | (a) no thread; (b) a size of R2.2 absent or zero; (c) an IronOxide_Property_Set property that Table 2-1 marks required for a process absent; (d) a `Stack_Size` that is not a multiple of 8 bytes, or that exceeds the RAM size the Backend declares (IronOxide places the stack inside the RAM region); (e) two items of one kind of R8.6 that would receive the same name, or a per-Task state item that would receive the name of a Resource or Release_Signal of its Partition, or an Endpoint that would receive the name of a Resource, Release_Signal or per-Task state item of a Task bound to one of its roles (R8.7); (f) a size of R2.2 that is not a whole number of bytes (R2.4, R8.8) | (a) IronOxide R21.3 (Backend restriction: IronOxide requires at least one Task per Application, not per Partition); (b) IronOxide R15.7; (c) IronOxide R15.7; (d) IronOxide R15.7; (e) PR-28; IronOxide DD-11 for crate names; IronOxide R21.3 for the other kinds of collision (a per-Task state item named as a Resource or Release_Signal of its Partition; an Endpoint named as a Resource, Release_Signal or per-Task state item of a Task bound to it), as `crates/rsk-gen/src/validate.rs` reports them at PAR-01; (f) IronOxide R15.7 | the Partition is IronOxide's isolation unit; (e) is a guard, because the sanitization rule of R8.6 gives no two items one name, so `test-name-collision` shows it instead of an `air` mutant (R8.7, R30.2) |
| MAP-08 | Virtual processor bound to the IronOxide_Processor, or a process on the IronOxide_Processor bound to a virtual processor | none | (a) always | (a) PR-28 | IronOxide has one isolation level, the Partition; HAMR uses virtual processors for Linux guests |
| MAP-09 | `IronOxide::Criticality_Level`, `Fault_Response`, `Overrun_Response`, `Deadline_Miss_Response` and `MIT_Violation_Threshold` of a process | Partition `level`, `fault`, `overrun`, `deadline_miss` and `mit_threshold` (R8.1) | (a) a value outside the set or range that Table 2-1 states; (b) `Overrun_Response => RECORD_AND_CONTINUE` on a process whose Criticality_Level is lower than the highest Criticality_Level of the processes on the IronOxide_Processor | (a) IronOxide R14.5, R15.7; (b) IronOxide R18.5 | temporal isolation by criticality (IronOxide R18); IronOxide's Generator reports (b) under R18.6, citing R18.5 (`validate.rs`) |
| MAP-10 | Data subcomponent of a process on the IronOxide_Processor, accessed by threads of that process through data-access connections (whether the AIR_Model carries these is ORQ-03) | Resource of that Partition whose accessors are exactly those threads' Tasks; Ceiling computed by the IronOxide_Generator (PR-06); `Access_Right => read_only` as a shared reference inside the lock closure (R9.1, R9.2); initial value from `Data_Model::Initial_Value`, or from a developer `const fn` where the AIR_Model records none (R9.6, ORQ-29) | (a) accessed by threads of more than one process; (b) `Concurrency_Control_Protocol` other than Priority_Ceiling, on the data component or on a data access feature through which a thread accesses it (R9.4); (c) accessed by no thread; (d) a `Data_Model::Initial_Value` from which no constant of the Resource's type derives (R9.8); (e) a data classifier that is, or contains, an unbounded type (R9.9) | (a) PR-30; (b) IronOxide Requirement 8; (c) IronOxide R22.5; (d) PR-34; (e) PR-15 | SRP locking; a Resource stays inside one Partition |
| MAP-11 | Event port connection between two threads of one Partition whose destination port is the destination thread's Dispatch trigger | Release_Signal declared in that Partition, bound to the destination Task, raised by the source Task's Port_Glue at Job completion (R10.1) | (a) a source thread whose period or MIT is shorter than the destination thread's MIT | (a) IronOxide R10.2, R10.3 (Backend restriction: IronOxide accepts such a source and counts the excess raises) | every raise less than one MIT after the previous accepted Release, or while a Release is pending or deferred, is an MIT_Violation counted toward the Partition's `mit_threshold` (IronOxide R10.4, DEV-04); (a) does not exclude MIT_Violations caused by the source's completion jitter (ORQ-26) |
| MAP-12 | Event port connection between two threads of one Partition whose destination thread is Periodic | Port_Cell holding an event count that the source's Port_Glue increments at Job completion, saturating at the destination port's `Communication_Properties::Queue_Size` (1 when absent, ASM-03), and that the destination's Port_Glue reads and clears at Job start (R10.2, UD-30) | — | — | no Release; the count is AADL's event-port value: for a count, AADL's queue bound of Queue_Size gives the same value under any overflow policy (DEV-04) |
| MAP-13 | Event data port connection between two threads of one Partition | Port_Cell holding a bounded FIFO of capacity `Communication_Properties::Queue_Size` (1 when absent) with the destination port's `Overflow_Handling_Protocol` (DropOldest when absent, ASM-03), plus MAP-11's Release_Signal where the destination port is a Dispatch trigger (R10.3) | (a) MAP-11's case (a), where the destination port is a Dispatch trigger | (a) IronOxide R10.2 (Backend restriction, as in MAP-11) | Endpoints are kept for cross-Partition traffic (PAR-05) |
| MAP-14 | Data port connection between two threads of one Partition | Port_Cell holding the latest value and a freshness flag, written by the source's Port_Glue at Job completion and copied by the destination's Port_Glue at Job start (R10.4) | — | — | exact latest-value semantics under the ceiling protocol |
| MAP-15 | Event or event data port connection between threads of two Partitions | one plain Endpoint per connection, its sending role bound to the source Task and its receiving role to the destination Task (R11.1); capacity = the destination port's `Queue_Size` (1 when absent); receiving role `ops` = 1 for OneItem, `Dequeued_Items` for MultipleItems, the capacity for AllItems (R11.2); an event connection carries a 4-byte token (R11.3); where the destination port is a Dispatch trigger, the destination Task is released by its role (`endpoint = NAME`) with its `IronOxide::MIT_Policy` (R11.4, DEV-10) | (a) fan-in, refused under MAP-24 (d); (b) more than PAR-05 Endpoints in the Application; (c) a capacity or `ops` value outside 1 to 255, or a total Endpoint storage beyond PAR-06; (d) `Queue_Size` outside 1 to 255, or `Dequeue_Protocol => MultipleItems` with `Dequeued_Items` absent or outside 1 to 255 (R11.12) | (a) see MAP-24 (d); (b) PR-14; (c) PR-14; (d) PR-14 | Endpoints are the only cross-Partition channel (PR-30, PR-32); a plain Endpoint binds exactly two roles (IronOxide R38.1, `validate.rs`); no rate refusal applies to a Dispatch trigger here (DEV-10, ORQ-26) |
| MAP-16 | Data port connection between threads of two Partitions | plain Endpoint of capacity C_sample (R12.2) whose receiving role has `ops` = C_sample; the destination's Port_Glue drains the channel at Job start and keeps in per-Task state, as the port's frozen value, the newest message of the drain that both the decoder and the Validation_Function accept (R12.1, DEV-02, UD-13); IOP-01 would replace this | (a) C_sample > 255; (b) a total Endpoint storage beyond PAR-06; (c) a Sporadic destination thread (R12.3, UD-07) | (a) PR-14; (b) PR-14; (c) Backend restriction (ORQ-30) | latest-value semantics without an IronOxide change; a Sporadic_Task has no maximum interval between Releases, so no finite C_sample bounds the sends between two drains |
| MAP-17 | `Overflow_Handling_Protocol` of the destination port of a cross-Partition event or event data connection (DropOldest when absent, ASM-03) | DropNewest and Error: IronOxide's full-channel outcome, the send returning `Full` and the Kernel recording the event against the sending Partition (IronOxide R40.5, R11.5, DEV-05); DropOldest: accepted only where the connection meets the acceptance condition of R11.6, which excludes overflow | (a) DropOldest where the connection does not meet the acceptance condition of R11.6 | (a) IronOxide R40.4, R39.4 | an IronOxide sender cannot remove the receiver's oldest buffered message: a send never waits (IronOxide R40.4), a full channel refuses the newest message (IronOxide R40.5), and the buffer loses no message (IronOxide R39.4) |
| MAP-18 | Upward connection | the receiving role's `validate` = a generated Validation_Function: Wire_Type decoding (R14.2) and the conjunction of the destination port's GUMBO integration assume clauses, or the developer function named by `IronOxide::Validation_Function` after decoding (R13.1, R13.2); for a plain Endpoint whose source Partition has the higher Criticality_Level, a reject-all Validation_Function on the sending role, because IronOxide checks both channels of a plain Endpoint (R13.6, `validate.rs`) | (a) an Upward connection whose destination port has neither a GUMBO integration assume clause nor `IronOxide::Validation_Function`; (b) `IronOxide::Validation_Function` on a port that is not the destination of an Upward connection (R2.9, UD-29) | (a) IronOxide R42.4; (b) IronOxide R42.4 | data must not flow up in criticality unchecked |
| MAP-19 | Data types of ports | application Rust type and, for each type used across Partitions, over the serial link, or on an Endpoint that R26.2 declares for a same-Partition flow hop, a Wire_Type (`Plain`, IronOxide R42.1) with an encoder and a rejecting decoder (R14.1, R14.2) | (a) an integer type with no bit width and no `HAMR::Default_Bit_Width`; (b) an unbounded array or sequence, or a string without `HAMR::Max_String_Size`; (c) a Wire_Type larger than 65,532 bytes; (d) a type kind that Table 14-1 does not list (R14.8) | (a) PR-14; (b) PR-14; (c) PR-14; (d) IronOxide R42.1 | bounded storage (PR-14); every bit pattern of a Wire_Type across Partitions is a valid value (IronOxide R42.1) |
| MAP-20 | Device with `IronOxide::Peripheral` whose ports connect to threads of one process | the peripheral assigned to that process's Partition (R15.1); a device out event port connected to a Sporadic thread's Dispatch trigger releases that Task by the peripheral's interrupt (`binds = PERIPHERAL`) with `mit_policy = defer` (R15.2, DEV-09) | (a) a device data or event data port; (b) a device `Dispatch_Protocol`; (c) device connections to threads of more than one process; (d) a peripheral absent from the IronOxide_Target's peripheral table; (e) a Kernel-owned peripheral; (f) a peripheral assigned to more than one Partition; (g) an EasyDMA-capable peripheral without the Target's DMA register description; (h) `IronOxide::MIT_Policy => discard` on an interrupt-released thread; (i) an interrupt binding of a peripheral without an interrupt line (R15.3 (v)); (j) one interrupt releasing two or more Tasks (R15.7); (k) a device event connection other than from a device out event port to a Sporadic thread's Dispatch trigger (R15.8) | (a), (b) Backend restriction resting on PR-29 (only the owning Partition's Task code accesses a peripheral; IronOxide gives a peripheral no ports or dispatch of its own); (c) PR-28, IronOxide R21.6; (d) IronOxide R21.5; (e) PR-29; (f) PR-28; (g) IronOxide DD-08; (h) IronOxide DD-06; (i) IronOxide R21.5; (j) PR-12, IronOxide R22.3; (k) Backend restriction (PR-29, IronOxide R19.5) | peripheral ownership (PR-29) and interrupt MIT enforcement (IronOxide DD-06, R19.1); IronOxide's Generator names (d) to (g) and (i) with the same rules (`validate.rs`); IronOxide DD-06 states (h), but `validate.rs` at PAR-01 does not check it, so the Backend's check is its only enforcement (R15.6, IOP-06) |

**Table M-2 — Deviations from HAMR's AADL semantics** (ASM-04; version shared with Table M-1). Each row states the AADL or HAMR behaviour, the IronOxide behaviour and its observable consequence, the generated items it applies to (which R4.3 lists in the generation report), the rationale, and the requirement that bounds the difference.

| Row | AADL or HAMR behaviour | IronOxide behaviour and its consequence | Applies to | Rationale | Bounded by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DEV-01 | Inputs are frozen at the dispatch instant | Inputs are frozen when the Job starts (Receive Input in the Port_Glue), which follows the Release by the Job's start delay (preemption, blocking and J_rel); a value written in that interval is read by this Job instead of the next one. Receive Input freezes the ports one after another, each port in a separate Critical_Section or Endpoint operation, so a preempting writer can change a port's value after another port of the same Job was frozen (ORQ-07) | every Task with at least one input port | IronOxide has no Kernel operation at the Release instant on behalf of the Application | R19.2; the start delay is part of the WCRT (IronOxide R29); ORQ-07 |
| DEV-02 | A data port reads the newest value; an unread value is overwritten | Across Partitions, and on a same-Partition flow hop that R26.2 maps to an Endpoint, the frozen value is the newest message of the drain at Job start that both the decoder and the Validation_Function accept (UD-13); when the timing assumption of R12.2 fails, the newest send is refused and counted instead of overwriting, so the receiver can read an older value | every cross-Partition data connection (MAP-16), and every same-Partition data connection that R26.2 maps to an Endpoint | no overwriting Endpoint exists at PAR-01 (IOP-01) | R12 |
| DEV-03 | The Initialize entry point runs developer code | Initial values are constants from developer `const fn` functions, which hold at instant 0 and again after each restart of the Partition (UD-12; ORQ-40); no event or event data output is put during initialization, and a thread with `Programming_Properties::Initialize_Entrypoint_Source_Text` is refused (MAP-29 (b)) | every thread with output data ports, GUMBO state variables or an Initialize entry point (MAP-29) | no Application code runs during IronOxide's Init (PR-34) | R20; ORQ-16 |
| DEV-04 | An event port queues events up to Queue_Size with the port's overflow policy | Inside a Partition, the Release_Signal holds at most one pending Release; a raise less than one MIT after the previous accepted Release, or while a Release is pending or deferred, is an MIT_Violation that is recorded and counted toward the Partition's `mit_threshold`, and exceeding the threshold applies the Partition's fault response (IronOxide R10.4). An event connection to a Periodic thread (MAP-12) is not a deviation: its Port_Cell's event count saturates at the destination port's Queue_Size (1 when absent, ASM-03), and for a count AADL's queue bound gives that same value under any overflow policy (UD-30) | every connection mapped by MAP-11, and every connection mapped by MAP-13 whose destination port is a Dispatch trigger | IronOxide R10.2, R10.3 | R10.5 refuses sources faster than the MIT (MAP-11 (a)); violations caused by completion jitter are ORQ-26 |
| DEV-05 | Event data overflow follows `Overflow_Handling_Protocol` | Across Partitions, and on a same-Partition flow hop that R26.2 maps to an Endpoint, a full channel refuses the newest message, the send returns `Full`, and the Kernel records the event against the sending Partition; DropOldest is accepted only where overflow is excluded (MAP-17) | every cross-Partition event or event data connection (MAP-15), and every same-Partition event or event data connection that R26.2 maps to an Endpoint | IronOxide R40.5 | R11.5, R11.6 |
| DEV-06 | A Sporadic thread is dispatched by arriving events, no two dispatches closer than its Period (ASM-03) | Under `mit_policy = discard`, an event less than one MIT after the previous accepted Release is dropped as a Release instead of dispatched later; for an Endpoint Release_Source the message stays in the buffer (IronOxide R10.8) | every Sporadic_Task with `IronOxide::MIT_Policy => discard` | IronOxide R10.2 | `defer` is the default (Table 2-1) and the only policy for interrupts (MAP-20 (h)) |
| DEV-07 | Equal-priority threads have no defined order in HAMR's back ends | Equal-priority Tasks start in Task declaration order, which the Backend sets to instance-path order | every set of two or more Tasks with equal Priority | IronOxide DD-03 | R7.5 |
| DEV-08 | `Compute_Execution_Time` is a model property | Its upper bound is a Budget that the Kernel enforces on the whole Job, Port_Glue included; an Overrun applies the Partition's Overrun_Response (IronOxide R18.3). An Overrun during Send Output can leave some of the Job's outputs sent and others not, because each output port is a separate Critical_Section or Endpoint operation (ORQ-07) | every Task | IronOxide R18 | R6.6, R25.2; ORQ-07 |
| DEV-09 | A device's out event port delivers each event to the connected port, which queues it as DEV-04 states | While the Kernel holds the interrupt line masked (for one MIT from each event and until the released Job ends), further events stay latched in the peripheral and arrive as one event when the line is re-enabled, so several device events give one Release; the Task's code reads and clears the peripheral | every Task released by an interrupt (MAP-20) | IronOxide DD-06 (design.md "Interrupt intake and MIT enforcement"), R19.4 ("coalesced and deferred") | R15.2 (`defer` only); IronOxide R19.1 bounds the intake cost per source |
| DEV-10 | A Dispatch trigger port queues arriving events as DEV-04 states | Across Partitions, and on a same-Partition flow hop that R26.2 maps to an Endpoint, an arrival less than one MIT after the previous accepted Release, or while a Release is pending or deferred, is an MIT_Violation attributed to the sending Partition and counted toward that Partition's `mit_threshold`; the message stays in the buffer for a later Job | every cross-Partition connection whose destination port is a Dispatch trigger (MAP-15), and every same-Partition connection to a Dispatch trigger that R26.2 maps to an Endpoint | IronOxide R10.2, R10.3, R10.7, R10.8 | R11.2, R11.5; no rate refusal applies, unlike MAP-11 (a) (ORQ-26) |
| DEV-11 | Slang wraps arithmetic on the full-width bounded integer types S8 to S64 and U8 to U64, which are the types of GUMBO expressions over `Base_Types::Integer_8` to `Unsigned_64` (`SlangParser.scala` `isWrapped`; Logika encodes these types as bit-vectors) | The Contract_Translator treats an arithmetic result outside its type's range as undefined (Table 22-2 EX-07, R22.3): a `requires` or Validation_Function clause that contains it is false, and an `ensures`, invariant predicate or Connection_Obligation that contains it gets the obligation that the result lies in range. Consequences: a message on which a destination port's assume clause overflows is rejected instead of judged by its wrapped value; a guarantee that holds only by wrapping is not proven; and the proofs say nothing about inputs on which an assume clause overflows | every generated `requires`, `ensures`, invariant predicate, Validation_Function condition and Connection_Obligation translated from a GUMBO clause or GUMBO function that contains arithmetic of Table 22-2 row EX-07 on a bounded integer type | this project's choice (UD-04, ASM-09): no proven contract relies on wrap-around; adopting wrapping instead would change Table 22-2 EX-07, R13.4 and R22.3 | R22.3, R23.3, R23.7; ORQ-05 |

### Part B — Mapping and Generation

### Requirement 5: Threads to Tasks (Phase P1 periodic; P2 sporadic)

**User Story:** As a system modeler, I want each AADL thread to become one IronOxide Task with the release behaviour its dispatch protocol states, so that the model's threads and the analyzed Tasks are the same set.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Backend SHALL map each thread of a process bound to the IronOxide_Processor (R1.2), including each thread inside a thread group of that process (R8.2), to exactly one Task of that process's Partition, a Periodic_Task for `Thread_Properties::Dispatch_Protocol => Periodic` (MAP-01) and a Sporadic_Task for `Thread_Properties::Dispatch_Protocol => Sporadic` (MAP-02), and SHALL generate no other Task. *Evidence: `test-map-01`, `test-map-02`, `pbt-thread-task-bijection`, `proof-tv-core`.*
2. WHEN a thread is Sporadic, THE Backend SHALL give its Task an MIT equal to the thread's `Timing_Properties::Period` and exactly one Release_Source, chosen by the source of the single incoming connection (R11.7) of the thread's Dispatch trigger: for a source thread in the same Partition, the Release_Signal of R10.1 (`signal = NAME`; MAP-11, MAP-13); for a source thread in another Partition, the destination's role of that connection's Endpoint (`endpoint = NAME`; R11.4, MAP-15); for a device's out event port, the interrupt of the device's `IronOxide::Peripheral` (`binds = PERIPHERAL`; R15.2, MAP-20). *Evidence: `test-map-02`, `test-map-11`, `test-map-15`, `test-map-20`, `proof-tv-core`.*
3. IF a Sporadic thread has no Dispatch trigger or more than one, or its `Thread_Properties::Dispatch_Trigger` names a feature that is not an in event port or in event data port of that thread, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the thread naming PR-11 and MAP-02, with a diagnostic that lists every port the Backend counted as a Dispatch trigger. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `test-refusal-diagnostics`.*
4. IF a thread's `Thread_Properties::Dispatch_Protocol` is Aperiodic, Timed, Hybrid or Background, is absent, or has any other value than Periodic or Sporadic, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the thread naming MAP-03 and the rule that MAP-03 gives: PR-27 for Aperiodic, Background, an absent value or any other value, and PR-11 for Timed or Hybrid. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`.*
5. IF a thread of a process bound to the IronOxide_Processor declares an entry point other than Initialize and Compute (the AADL Finalize, Recover, Activate and Deactivate entry points among them), in the form that the AIR_Model records for its Front_End (ORQ-27), THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the thread naming PR-02 and MAP-24. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`.*
6. IF the Dispatch trigger of a Sporadic thread has no incoming connection, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the thread naming PR-11, IronOxide R22.4 and MAP-02 (d), since its Task would have no Release_Source. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `pbt-backend-checks-match-generator`.*
7. IF a Sporadic thread has `Timing_Properties::Dispatch_Offset`, whatever its value (0 included), THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the thread naming PR-27 and MAP-02. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`.*
8. WHEN a thread is Sporadic, THE Backend SHALL set its Task's `mit_policy` to the thread's `IronOxide::MIT_Policy`, or to `defer` where the property is absent (Table 2-1, DEV-06). *Evidence: `test-map-02`, `proof-tv-core`.*

### Requirement 6: Thread timing properties (Phase P1)

**User Story:** As a timing analyst, I want the model's timing properties carried into the Task_Model without silent changes, so that the analysis is of the system I modeled.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Backend SHALL set, for each thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor, the `period` of its Periodic_Task or the `mit` of its Sporadic_Task to `Timing_Properties::Period`; the `offset` of its Periodic_Task to `Timing_Properties::Dispatch_Offset`, or to 0 ns when that property is absent; its `deadline` to `Timing_Properties::Deadline`, or to the Period when that property is absent (ASM-03); and its `budget` to the upper bound of `Timing_Properties::Compute_Execution_Time`, the range's lower bound not being carried, each value read as criterion 10 states (MAP-01, MAP-02, MAP-04, MAP-05). *Evidence: `test-map-04`, `test-map-05`, `proof-tv-core`.*
2. THE Backend SHALL write each time value into the App_Declaration as an integer n followed by a unit u, where u is the largest of `s`, `ms`, `us` and `ns` of which the value in picoseconds (criterion 10) is a whole multiple and n is that value divided by u (for example 1,500,000,000 ps as `1500 us` and 2,000,000,000,000 ps as `2 s`), so that the only rounding between the model and the Task_Model is the IronOxide_Generator's (IronOxide R21.2). *Evidence: `pbt-time-exact`, `proof-tv-core`.*
3. IF a time value that the Backend would write into the App_Declaration has the exactness mark `fractional` (criterion 10, R28.3), or is not a whole number of nanoseconds (its value in picoseconds is not a multiple of 1,000), THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the element that carries the property, naming the property, the value string and unit that the AIR_Model records, its value in picoseconds and its exactness mark, IronOxide R21.2 and MAP-04 (b). *Evidence: `mut-refusals` (including a value marked `fractional` whose rounded picosecond value is a multiple of 1,000, such as `1000.4` ps).*
4. IF a thread lacks `Timing_Properties::Period` or `Timing_Properties::Compute_Execution_Time`, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the thread with one diagnostic per absent property, naming PR-27 and, for an absent Period, MAP-02 if the thread is Sporadic and MAP-01 otherwise, or, for an absent `Compute_Execution_Time`, MAP-05, and SHALL apply no default period, MIT or execution time (in particular not the 50 ms default of HAMR's Microkit back end). *Evidence: `mut-refusals`.*
5. IF a thread's Deadline exceeds its Period, or the upper bound of its `Compute_Execution_Time` exceeds its Deadline (the Period when `Timing_Properties::Deadline` is absent), compared as the values in picoseconds of criterion 10, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the thread with one diagnostic per violated relation, naming IronOxide R22.1 and MAP-04 for the Deadline exceeding the Period, or MAP-05 for the upper bound exceeding the Deadline. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`.*
6. THE Backend SHALL write the upper bound of a thread's `Compute_Execution_Time` as its Task's `budget` with no allowance added or subtracted for the Port_Glue, so that the Budget that the Kernel enforces with the Partition's Overrun_Response (IronOxide R18) bounds the whole Job, Port_Glue included (DEV-08). *Evidence: `test-map-05`, `proof-tv-core`.*
7. IF a time value of criterion 1, converted as IronOxide R21.2 specifies at the CPU clock and time-base rates that IronOxide `crates/rsk-gen/src/target.rs` gives for any IronOxide_Target for which R18.2 requires IronOxide's tools to accept the Application (64 MHz and 16 MHz for `nrf52840`; 25 MHz and 25 MHz for `qemu_mps2_an386`), would make the IronOxide_Generator reject the Task under IronOxide R22.1 (a period, MIT or deadline below 1 tick; a Budget below 1 cycle or above 2^32 − 1 cycles; a period, MIT, deadline or Budget not representable in 64 bits after conversion; a deadline in ticks above the period or MIT in ticks; a Budget in cycles above the deadline converted from ticks to cycles rounded down) or under IronOxide R22.6 (an offset not representable in the Kernel time base; a period, MIT or offset in ticks above the `IronOxide::Operating_Duration` in ticks), THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the thread naming the IronOxide_Target, the IronOxide criterion, the declared and converted values with their units, and MAP-01 for an offset, MAP-04 for a period, MIT or deadline, or MAP-05 for a Budget. *Evidence: `pbt-time-limits-agree` (over PAR-08 generated models per run and for each IronOxide_Target of R18.2, the Backend refuses a thread under this criterion exactly when the IronOxide_Generator, run on the App_Declaration that the Backend writes with the refusals of this criterion suspended (the test-only option of R17.1), rejects its Task under IronOxide R22.1 or R22.6), `mut-refusals`.*
8. WHEN the conversion of IronOxide R21.2 at the rates of an IronOxide_Target of criterion 7 changes a time value of criterion 1, THE Backend SHALL list the change in the generation report as one entry giving the IronOxide_Target, the thread's instance path, the AADL property, the value in picoseconds, the value as written with its unit, and the converted value in ticks or cycles. *Evidence: `test-conversions-reported` (for every model of the corpus and each IronOxide_Target of R18.2, the report's entries for that IronOxide_Target correspond one to one to the `conversions` entries of the Task_Model that the IronOxide_Generator writes for it).*
9. WHEN the Verdict_Reporter writes a Verdict_Report for an Application that has WCET_Records measured by IronOxide's WCET_Harness, THE Verdict_Report SHALL state for each thread the Port_Glue's WCET in CPU cycles, including the margin of PAR-11 and obtained as design.md records under ORQ-28, and that WCET as a percentage of the Task's Budget in CPU cycles as the Task_Model records it, rounded up to one decimal place (DEV-08). *Evidence: `analysis-glue-share-<model>`.*
10. THE Backend SHALL read each time value that it takes from the AIR_Model (a value with a time unit, alone or as a bound of a range) as the integer number of picoseconds and the exactness mark (`exact`, `noise` or `fractional`) that R28.3 states, by its own implementation of that rule (R28.2), and SHALL use that integer as the value in picoseconds wherever this document compares, converts, ranks or writes a time value of the model, so that a value marked `noise` (OSATE's floating-point scaling) is used as its rounded integer and a value marked `fractional` that the Backend would write is refused by criterion 3 (MAP-04 (b)). *Evidence: `test-time-reading` (the cases of `test-semantic-model-times`, read by the Backend, giving the same integers and exactness marks), `check-tv-<model>`.*

### Requirement 7: Priorities (Phase P1)

**User Story:** As a timing analyst, I want Priorities taken from the model or derived deadline-monotonically by one documented rule, so that the analysis and the model agree on which thread is more urgent.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN the Backend generates an Application from a model in which every thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor has `Thread_Properties::Priority`, THE Backend SHALL give each such thread's Task the Priority i, where i is the position, counting from 1, of the thread's `Thread_Properties::Priority` value in the increasing list of the k distinct values on all those threads across all Partitions, so that the smallest value receives Priority 1, the largest receives Priority k, equal values receive equal Priorities, and a larger value receives a larger Priority (ASM-02, ORQ-04). *Evidence: `test-map-06`, `pbt-priority-rank`, `proof-tv-core`.*
2. WHEN the Backend generates an Application from a model in which no thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor has `Thread_Properties::Priority`, THE Backend SHALL give each such thread's Task the Priority i, where i is the position, counting from 1, of the thread's relative deadline in the decreasing list of the k distinct relative deadlines of all those threads across all Partitions, so that the longest relative deadline receives Priority 1, the shortest receives Priority k, and equal relative deadlines receive equal Priorities; the relative deadline is the thread's `Timing_Properties::Deadline` in picoseconds, or its `Timing_Properties::Period` in picoseconds where Deadline is absent (R6.1, ASM-03), each as R6.10 reads it, compared before IronOxide's conversion to ticks (IronOxide R21.2), so that two relative deadlines that convert to the same number of ticks but differ in picoseconds receive different Priorities (UD-22). *Evidence: `test-map-06`, `pbt-priority-rank`, `proof-tv-core`.*
3. IF at least one but not every thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor has `Thread_Properties::Priority` (threads bound to any other processor are not counted), THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model with one diagnostic for each thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor that lacks the property, each naming that thread's instance path and source position, "Backend restriction" followed by PR-03 (one assignment rule for all Priorities), and MAP-06 (a). *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `test-map-06`.*
4. IF the assignment of criterion 1 or 2 yields a number k of distinct Priorities greater than PAR-03, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model with a diagnostic that names the IronOxide_Processor's instance path and source position, PR-33, MAP-06, the limit `MAX_PRIORITY` of IronOxide `crates/rsk-gen/src/validate.rs`, and the value of k, whether or not the model is also refused for exceeding PAR-04 Tasks (MAP-26). *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `test-map-06`.*
5. THE Backend SHALL write the Partitions into the App_Declaration in ascending order of process instance path and the Tasks of each Partition in ascending order of thread instance path, comparing instance paths segment by segment from the root, each segment by the Unicode code points of the identifier as the AIR_Model records it, with a path that is a proper prefix of another ordered first, so that the Task declaration order, which IronOxide uses as the start order of eligible equal-priority Tasks (IronOxide DD-03, IronOxide R7.3), is ascending thread instance path order (DEV-07). *Evidence: `test-declaration-order`, `pbt-order-invariance`.*
6. WHEN the Backend generates an Application, THE Backend SHALL list in the generation report, for each thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor, its instance path, the criterion that assigned its Priority (criterion 1 or criterion 2), the value that criterion ranked (the `Thread_Properties::Priority` value, or the relative deadline in picoseconds), and the assigned Priority. *Evidence: `test-priority-report`.*

### Requirement 8: Processes to Partitions (Phase P1)

**User Story:** As a system integrator, I want each AADL process to become one IronOxide Partition with the criticality level and fault responses I declare, so that spatial and temporal isolation follow the model's process structure.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Backend SHALL map the processes bound to the IronOxide_Processor (R1.2) one-to-one onto the Partitions of the Application, each Partition with its own Partition crate that no other Partition uses (IronOxide DD-11), and with `level`, `fault`, `overrun`, `deadline_miss` and `mit_threshold` equal to the process's `IronOxide::Criticality_Level`, `IronOxide::Fault_Response`, `IronOxide::Overrun_Response`, `IronOxide::Deadline_Miss_Response` and `IronOxide::MIT_Violation_Threshold` respectively, by the same enumerator name or the same integer (MAP-07, MAP-09). *Evidence: `test-map-07`, `test-map-09`, `proof-tv-core`.*
2. THE Backend SHALL map each thread that a process bound to the IronOxide_Processor contains inside a thread group, at any nesting depth of thread groups, to a Task of that process's Partition. *Evidence: `test-map-07`.*
3. IF a virtual processor is a subcomponent of the IronOxide_Processor or is bound to it by `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Processor_Binding`, or a process bound to the IronOxide_Processor also names a virtual processor in its `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Processor_Binding`, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model naming the instance path of each such virtual processor and process, PR-28 and MAP-08. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`.*
4. IF a process bound to the IronOxide_Processor has `IronOxide::Overrun_Response => RECORD_AND_CONTINUE` and an `IronOxide::Criticality_Level` lower than the highest `IronOxide::Criticality_Level` among the processes bound to the IronOxide_Processor (A highest, E lowest), THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the process naming IronOxide R18.5 and MAP-09. *Evidence: `test-map-09`, `mut-refusals`.*
5. IF a process bound to the IronOxide_Processor contains no thread, neither as a direct subcomponent nor inside a thread group at any nesting depth, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the process naming "Backend restriction" followed by IronOxide R21.3 (IronOxide requires at least one Task per Application, not per Partition), and MAP-07 (a). *Evidence: `mut-refusals`.*
6. THE Backend SHALL derive the name of each of the following items of the generated Application from the AADL element that the item is generated from, by the sanitization rule that design.md states:
   - each Partition and each Partition crate, from its process's instance path (criterion 1);
   - each Task, from its thread's instance path (R5.1);
   - each Resource, from the instance path of its data subcomponent (R9.1) or, for a Port_Cell, of its connection instance (R10.7);
   - each Release_Signal, from its connection instance's path (R10.1);
   - each Endpoint, from its connection instance's path (R11.1, R12.6, R26.2);
   - each Chain, from its end-to-end flow's instance path (R26.1);
   - each per-Task state item and each developer `const fn`, from the instance path of its thread or data subcomponent and, where the item belongs to a port or a GUMBO state variable, that port's or variable's name (R9.6, R12.1, R20.1, R20.2).

   The rule gives distinct names to any two distinct items of one kind of this list in the Application, gives each per-Task state item a name that differs from the name of every Resource and Release_Signal of its Partition, gives each Endpoint a name that differs from the name of every Resource, Release_Signal and per-Task state item of each Task bound to one of its roles (both IronOxide R21.3, as `crates/rsk-gen/src/validate.rs` checks them), and yields only names that the IronOxide_Generator's App_Declaration parser (`crates/rsk-gen/src/syntax.rs`) accepts as identifiers. *Evidence: `pbt-names-injective` (over PAR-08 generated models: distinct names within each kind of item, per-Task state names distinct from their Partition's Resource and Release_Signal names, and acceptance by IronOxide's parser).*
7. IF, through a defect of the sanitization rule (criterion 6 excludes both cases, so this criterion is a guard), two items of one kind of criterion 6 would receive the same name, or a per-Task state item would receive the name of a Resource or Release_Signal of its Partition, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model naming the instance paths of both elements, the rule that IronOxide's `validate.rs` reports for the collision (PR-28 for two items of one kind, IronOxide DD-11 for two Partition crates, IronOxide R21.3 for a per-Task state item), and MAP-07 (e). R30.2 requires no `air` mutant for MAP-07 (e); this criterion's test shows it instead. *Evidence: `test-name-collision` (run with a test-only substitute for the sanitization rule that maps two chosen elements to one name, because the rule of criterion 6 maps no two elements of a model to one name).*
8. IF a process bound to the IronOxide_Processor has a `Memory_Properties::Code_Size`, `Memory_Properties::Data_Size` or `Memory_Properties::Stack_Size` that is not a whole number of bytes, a code, RAM or stack size (R2.2) that is zero, or a stack size that is not a multiple of 8 bytes or exceeds the RAM size that R2.2 writes, where the RAM size is the process's `Data_Size` plus its `Stack_Size` because IronOxide's RAM region holds the Partition's stack at its bottom (IronOxide DD-07) (UD-06; where the stack size comes from is ORQ-23), THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the process naming the `Memory_Properties` property concerned, IronOxide R15.7 (`crates/rsk-gen/src/validate.rs`) and MAP-07, with the label (b) for a zero size, (f) for a size that is not a whole number of bytes, and (d) for a stack size that is not a multiple of 8 bytes or exceeds the RAM size. *Evidence: `test-partition-sizes`, `mut-refusals`.*
9. IF no process is bound to the IronOxide_Processor, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model with the one diagnostic that R1.7 states, naming the IronOxide_Processor's instance path, IronOxide R21.3, PR-28 and MAP-27 (f). *Evidence: `test-refusal-diagnostics`, `mut-refusals`.*

### Requirement 9: Shared data to Resources (Phase P2)

**User Story:** As an application developer, I want AADL shared data inside a process to become an IronOxide Resource with a computed Ceiling, so that threads of one Partition can share state under the ceiling protocol.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Backend SHALL map each data subcomponent contained in a process bound to the IronOxide_Processor, directly or in one of the process's thread groups (MAP-07), that one or more threads of that process access, to one Resource of that process's Partition whose name is derived from the subcomponent's instance path by the sanitization of R8.6, whose type is the application type that the Backend generates for the subcomponent's data classifier (MAP-19), and whose accessors are exactly the Tasks of those threads, so that the Ceiling that the IronOxide_Generator records for the Resource in the Task_Model equals the maximum Priority (Requirement 7) of those Tasks (PR-06, IronOxide R23.1, MAP-10). In this requirement, a thread accesses a data component when the AIR_Model contains a data-access connection instance between a data access feature of the thread and that component (ORQ-03). *Evidence: `test-map-10`, `proof-tv-core`.*
2. WHERE every data access feature through which a thread accesses a Resource's data subcomponent has `Memory_Properties::Access_Right => read_only`, THE Backend SHALL give that thread's Port_API, for that Resource, only a lock operation whose closure receives a shared reference (`&T`) to the Resource's value, so that developer code that writes the value through it is rejected at compile time; the thread's Task remains an accessor under criterion 1, so its Priority counts in the Ceiling. *Evidence: `test-map-10`, `test-read-only-view` (a compile-fail case that writes through the shared reference), `check-generated-<model>`.*
3. IF threads of two or more processes access one data component, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the data component with a diagnostic that names PR-30, MAP-10 and the instance path of each accessing thread. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `test-refusal-diagnostics`.*
4. IF a data component that threads on the IronOxide_Processor access, or a data access feature through which a thread accesses it, has `Thread_Properties::Concurrency_Control_Protocol` with a value other than Priority_Ceiling, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the data component naming IronOxide Requirement 8 and MAP-10; this criterion does not refuse a data component or data access feature without the property. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`.*
5. IF a data subcomponent contained in a process bound to the IronOxide_Processor, directly or in one of the process's thread groups, is accessed by no thread, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse it naming IronOxide R22.5 and MAP-10. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`.*
6. THE Backend SHALL declare as each Resource's initial value the constant of the Resource's type that the rule recorded in design.md derives from the `Data_Model::Initial_Value` that the AIR_Model records for the data subcomponent, and, where the AIR_Model records none, a call of a developer `const fn` without parameters that returns the Resource's type and that the generated skeleton of the Partition crate declares under a name derived from the subcomponent's instance path by the sanitization of R8.6 (PR-34, ORQ-29; whether IronOxide loads a non-zero initial value at reset and on RESTART_PARTITION is ORQ-40). *Evidence: `test-map-10`, `check-generated-<model>`, `proof-app-creusot-<model>`.*
7.
