WHERE the data classifier of a data subcomponent mapped by criterion 1 has one or more GUMBO invariant clauses, THE Backend SHALL declare the Resource's `invariant` as the conjunction of those clauses as the Contract_Translator translates them (R22.2), which is the Resource invariant that the App_Prover proves after the Resource's initialization and at the exit of every Critical_Section on the Resource in Partitions of Criticality_Level A or B (IronOxide R46.4), and at the exit of every Critical_Section on the Resource in Partitions of Criticality_Level C, D or E, apart from the trusted developer functions that R21.7 allows (R21.7). *Evidence: `test-contracts-translation`, `test-map-10`, `proof-app-creusot-<model>`.*
8. IF the AIR_Model records, for a data subcomponent that criterion 1 maps, a `Data_Model::Initial_Value` from which the rule of criterion 6 derives no constant of the Resource's type (for example a value outside the type's range, a number of list elements that differs from the type's structure, or text that the rule does not parse), THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the data subcomponent naming PR-34 and MAP-10 (d) (the format of `Data_Model::Initial_Value` is ORQ-29). *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `test-refusal-diagnostics`.*
9. IF the data classifier of a data subcomponent that criterion 1 would map is, or contains as a field or element type, an unbounded integer type without `HAMR::Default_Bit_Width` or an unbounded array, sequence or string type (the types that MAP-19 refuses for ports), THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the data subcomponent naming PR-15 and MAP-10 (e). *Evidence: `mut-refusals`.*

### Requirement 10: Port connections inside a Partition (Phase P2)

**User Story:** As an application developer, I want connections between threads of one process carried by Resources and Release_Signals, so that IronOxide's eight Endpoints are kept for traffic between Partitions.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN an event port connection links two threads of one Partition, the destination port is the destination thread's Dispatch trigger, and R26.2 does not map the connection to an Endpoint, THE Backend SHALL map the connection to one Release_Signal declared in that Partition, bound to the destination Task (`signal = NAME`) with the destination thread's `IronOxide::MIT_Policy`, listed in the source Task's `raises`, and raised by the source Task's Port_Glue exactly once in each Job in which the developer's compute code called Put Value on the source port, however many times it called it (ORQ-39), after the compute code returns and before the Job returns to its Release_Point (MAP-11, R19.3). *Evidence: `test-map-11`, `proof-tv-core`, `proof-app-glue`.*
2. WHEN an event port connection links two threads of one Partition, the destination thread is Periodic, and R26.2 does not map the connection to an Endpoint, THE Backend SHALL map the connection to a Port_Cell holding an event count whose initial value is 0, which the source's Port_Glue increments by 1, saturating at the destination port's `Communication_Properties::Queue_Size` (1 when absent, ASM-03) instead of wrapping (UD-30; for a count, AADL's queue bound gives the same value under any overflow policy, DEV-04), in each Job in which the developer's compute code called Put Value on the source port, however many times it called it (ORQ-39), after the compute code returns, and which the destination's Port_Glue, at Job start and within one Critical_Section, copies into the input port's frozen count and sets to 0 (MAP-12). *Evidence: `test-map-12` (including a source that puts more events between two destination Jobs than `Queue_Size`, for which the frozen count equals `Queue_Size`), `proof-app-glue`.*
3. WHEN an event data port connection links two threads of one Partition and R26.2 does not map the connection to an Endpoint, THE Backend SHALL map the connection to a Port_Cell holding a FIFO, initially empty, whose capacity is the destination port's `Communication_Properties::Queue_Size` (1 when absent, ASM-03), into which the source's Port_Glue enqueues the value put in each Job in which the developer's compute code called Put Value on the source port, one value per Job (ORQ-39), after the compute code returns, and from which the destination's Port_Glue removes at Job start, oldest first, one item for `Dequeue_Protocol => OneItem` (the default, ASM-03), up to `Dequeued_Items` items for MultipleItems and every item for AllItems, the counts that R11.2 uses across Partitions; and, where the destination port is the Dispatch trigger, a Release_Signal as in criterion 1 that the source's Port_Glue raises only after the enqueue, including any overflow handling of criterion 9, has completed (MAP-13). *Evidence: `test-map-13`, `proof-app-glue`.*
4. WHEN a data port connection links two threads of one Partition and R26.2 does not map the connection to an Endpoint, THE Backend SHALL map the connection to a Port_Cell holding the latest value, initially the source thread's initial output value (R20.2), and a freshness flag, initially not fresh, where the source's Port_Glue overwrites the value with the value put and sets the flag to fresh in each Job in which the developer's compute code called Put Value on the source port, after the compute code returns, and the destination's Port_Glue, at Job start and within one Critical_Section, copies the value into the port's frozen value, takes the flag's state as the freshness that the Port_API reports for that Job, and resets the flag to not fresh (MAP-14; freshness as R12.4 and R13.5 use it). *Evidence: `test-map-14`, `proof-app-glue`.*
5. IF the source thread of a connection that criterion 1 maps, or that criterion 3 maps with a Release_Signal, has a `Timing_Properties::Period` (its period, or its MIT for a Sporadic source, R5.2) shorter than the destination thread's MIT, the two compared as the picosecond values of R6.10 before any conversion, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the connection naming "Backend restriction" followed by IronOxide R10.2 and IronOxide R10.3 (IronOxide accepts such a source and counts the excess raises), and MAP-11 (a), and also MAP-13 (a) for an event data port connection (DEV-04; MIT_Violations caused by the source's completion jitter are not excluded by this check, ORQ-26). *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `test-map-11` (a source Period equal to the destination's MIT is accepted).*
6. THE Port_Glue SHALL enter at most one Critical_Section on each Port_Cell in each Job of each Task that accesses that Port_Cell, through IronOxide's lock API (IronOxide R8.6), so that each entry is one Critical_Section site of the Task_Model with a WCET_Record of its own (IronOxide R25.3, R28.2). *Evidence: `check-generated-<model>`, `proof-app-glue`.*
7. THE Backend SHALL declare each Port_Cell as a Resource of the connection's Partition that carries that one connection only and whose declared accessors are exactly the source Task and the destination Task, so that the IronOxide_Generator computes its Ceiling as the higher of their two Priorities (IronOxide R23.1, PR-06). *Evidence: `test-map-12`, `test-map-13`, `test-map-14`, `proof-tv-core`.*
8. IF an event port connection links two threads of one Partition, the destination thread is Sporadic, and the destination port is not its Dispatch trigger, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the connection naming MAP-24 and the rule that design.md's diagnostic table gives for this kind (R16.3), because no row of Table M-1 maps it (R4.2). *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `pbt-refusals-named`.*
9. IF the source's Port_Glue enqueues a value into a FIFO Port_Cell of criterion 3 that already holds `Queue_Size` items, THEN THE Port_Glue SHALL apply the destination port's `Overflow_Handling_Protocol` (DropOldest when absent, ASM-03): for DropOldest, remove the oldest item and then enqueue the new value; for DropNewest and Error, leave the FIFO unchanged, discard the new value and count the discard in the source's Port_API, as R11.5 does for a refused send across Partitions (DD candidate for Error). *Evidence: `test-map-13`, `proof-app-glue`.*

### Requirement 11: Port connections across Partitions (Phase P2)

**User Story:** As a system integrator, I want connections between processes carried by IronOxide Endpoints with the queue size and overflow behaviour the model declares, so that cross-Partition communication is isolated and bounded.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN a port connection links a thread of one Partition to a thread of a different Partition, THE Backend SHALL map the connection to exactly one plain Endpoint with two roles, whose sending role is bound to the source thread's Task, whose receiving role is bound to the destination thread's Task, and, for an event data or data connection, whose payload type is the Wire_Type of the connection's data type (R14.1) with `payload` equal to that Wire_Type's size in bytes rounded up to the next multiple of 4 and at least 4 (MAP-15, MAP-16). *Evidence: `test-map-15`, `proof-tv-core`.*
2. WHEN the Backend maps an event data connection to an Endpoint under criterion 1 or R26.2, THE Backend SHALL declare the Endpoint's `capacity` as the destination port's `Communication_Properties::Queue_Size` (1 when absent, ASM-03) and the receiving role's `ops` as n_recv, where n_recv is 1 for `Thread_Properties::Dequeue_Protocol => OneItem` (the default, ASM-03), the port's `Thread_Properties::Dequeued_Items` for MultipleItems, and the `capacity` for AllItems, and THE destination Task's Port_Glue SHALL, at Job start, perform receives on that Endpoint until a receive finds the channel empty or n_recv receives have been performed, and SHALL present the accepted messages, oldest first, as the port's frozen queue (for a Chain hop with n_recv below `capacity`, ORQ-50). *Evidence: `test-map-15`, `proof-tv-core`, `proof-app-glue`.*
3. WHEN the Backend maps an event connection to an Endpoint under criterion 1 or R26.2, THE Backend SHALL give the Endpoint a payload of one 4-byte token and the `capacity`, receiving-role `ops` and receive drain of criterion 2, and THE destination Task's Port_API SHALL present the number of tokens accepted by that drain as the port's frozen event count. *Evidence: `test-map-15`, `proof-app-glue`.*
4. WHEN the destination port of a connection mapped under criterion 1 or R26.2 is the destination thread's Dispatch trigger, THE Backend SHALL declare the destination Task as a Sporadic_Task released by its receiving role of that Endpoint (`endpoint = NAME`), with `mit_policy` equal to the thread's `IronOxide::MIT_Policy` (`defer` when absent, Table 2-1) (DEV-10; no rate check of this document excludes MIT_Violations on this Release_Source, ORQ-26). *Evidence: `test-map-15`, `proof-tv-core`.*
5. WHERE the destination port's `Overflow_Handling_Protocol` is DropNewest or Error, IF a send by the source Task's Port_Glue on the connection's Endpoint returns `Err(SendError::Full)`, THEN THE Port_Glue SHALL treat the result as the port's overflow by dropping that message without retrying it in the same Job, incrementing by 1 the saturating refused-send counter that the source thread's Port_API keeps for that output port, and performing the remaining Send Output operations of the Job, the Kernel having recorded the event against the sending Partition (IronOxide R40.5, DEV-05). *Evidence: `test-map-17`, `proof-app-glue`.*
6. IF the destination port's `Overflow_Handling_Protocol` is DropOldest (the default, ASM-03) and the connection does not meet the condition below, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the connection naming IronOxide R40.4, IronOxide R39.4 and MAP-17 (a). The condition shows that every send of the source finds fewer than `capacity` messages in the channel in every execution in which no Health_Monitor response suppresses a Release of the destination Task and both Tasks complete every Job by its absolute deadline (the hypothesis of R12.2). A connection whose destination thread is Sporadic does not meet it, because a Sporadic_Task has no maximum interval between Releases (R12.3; ORQ-06, ORQ-26). For a Periodic destination thread, with T_S, D_S, O_S, T_R, D_R and O_R as R12.2 defines them in Kernel time-base ticks and n_recv as criterion 2 defines it, the condition is:
   - where n_recv ≥ `capacity`, so that each drain empties the channel: `capacity` ≥ the C_sample that the formula of R12.2 gives for the connection;
   - where n_recv < `capacity` (OneItem, or MultipleItems with `Dequeued_Items` below `Queue_Size`): `capacity` ≥ B, where B is the largest value, over all instants s ≤ t from instant 0, of N_S(s, t) − n_recv × N_R(s, t). N_S(s, t) is ⌊(t − L) / T_S⌋ + 1 with L = max(s − D_S, O_S), or 0 where t < L; it bounds the number of the source's Jobs released in [s − D_S, t], which are the Jobs whose sends can fall in [s, t]. N_R(s, t) is the number of integers j ≥ 0 with s < O_R + j × T_R and O_R + j × T_R + D_R ≤ t; it counts the destination's Jobs whose drains start after s and before t. B is unbounded where T_R > n_recv × T_S, and the connection is then refused.

   The method that computes B is a DD candidate, and its tightness is ORQ-06. *Evidence: `mut-refusals` (including a OneItem connection with `capacity` = B − 1 and one with T_R > n_recv × T_S), `proof-tv-sampling-capacity` (the Verus lemma of R12.2, extended to show that, under the hypothesis above, the channel holds at most `capacity` − 1 messages before each send wherever the condition holds).*
7. IF an in port of a thread on the IronOxide_Processor is the destination of more than one port connection, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the port naming PR-12, IronOxide R38.1 and MAP-24, and listing every connection that ends at the port. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`.*
8. WHEN an out port has more than one outgoing connection that criterion 1 or R26.2 maps to an Endpoint, THE Backend SHALL map each such connection to its own Endpoint, and THE source Task's Port_Glue SHALL, in each Job in which the compute code put a value on the port, send that same value once on each of those Endpoints during Send Output (R19.3). *Evidence: `test-map-15`, `proof-app-glue`.*
9. THE Backend SHALL declare the `ops` of each role of an Endpoint mapped under criterion 1 or R26.2 as the largest number of send and receive operations that one Job of the role's Task performs on that Endpoint: 1 for the sending role, n_recv of criterion 2 for the receiving role of an event or event data connection, and C_sample for the receiving role of a data connection (R12.1), so that the Analyzer charges them (IronOxide R40.2). *Evidence: `proof-tv-core`, `check-generated-<model>`, `proof-app-glue`.*
10. WHERE the destination port's `Overflow_Handling_Protocol` is DropOldest, IF a send by the source Task's Port_Glue on the connection's Endpoint returns `Err(SendError::Full)`, THEN THE Port_Glue SHALL handle the refused send as criterion 5 states, and THE Verdict_Report SHALL list every such refused send observed under QEMU or on the HIL_Rig as a violation of the timing assumption under which criterion 6 accepted the connection. *Evidence: `test-qemu-<model>`, `hil-dropoldest-no-full`, `proof-app-glue`.*
11. IF a send or receive by a Port_Glue on an Endpoint mapped under criterion 1 or R26.2 returns `PeerChanged` (the other role's Partition was stopped or restarted since this role's previous operation, IronOxide R42.3), THEN THE Port_Glue SHALL increment by 1 the saturating peer-change counter that its Task's Port_API keeps for that port, drop a message whose send returned `PeerChanged` without retrying it in that Job, and end a receive drain at the receive that returned `PeerChanged`, keeping the messages accepted before it as the port's input for that Job (R12.1 for a data port). *Evidence: `test-peer-restart`, `proof-app-glue`.*
12. IF the destination port of an event or event data connection to which criterion 1 or R26.2 applies has `Communication_Properties::Queue_Size` outside 1 to 255, or has `Thread_Properties::Dequeue_Protocol => MultipleItems` with `Thread_Properties::Dequeued_Items` absent or outside 1 to 255, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the connection naming PR-14 and MAP-15 (d). *Evidence: `mut-refusals`.*

### Requirement 12: Data-port sampling semantics across Partitions (Phase P2)

**User Story:** As an application developer, I want a data port between processes to read the newest value, as AADL defines, so that control loops sample their inputs instead of consuming queues.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN a Job of the destination Task of a cross-Partition data port connection starts, THE Port_Glue SHALL, within Receive Input (R19.2) and before the developer's compute code runs, receive on the connection's Endpoint (the drain) until a receive returns the emptiness indication (IronOxide R40.4), a receive returns the peer-state error (criterion 7), or C_sample receives have each consumed a message, where a message is consumed when the receive delivers it or the Validation_Function rejects it (IronOxide R42.4), so that the drain performs at most C_sample receives, and SHALL store in per-Task state, as the port's frozen value for that Job and reported as fresh, the newest message of the drain that the receive delivered, because the receiving role's Validation_Function accepted it where the role has one (Requirement 13), and that the Wire_Type decoder (R14.2) accepts (MAP-16, DEV-02, UD-13). In this requirement, a cross-Partition data port connection is a data port connection that R11.1 maps to an Endpoint, or a data port connection between threads of one Partition that R26.2 maps to an Endpoint. *Evidence: `test-map-16`, `proof-app-glue`.*
2. THE Backend SHALL set C_sample, for each cross-Partition data port connection whose destination thread is Periodic, to 1 + ⌊(D_R + max(T_R + D_S, O_R − O_S)) / T_S⌋ messages, where T_S is the source Task's period (Periodic_Task) or MIT (Sporadic_Task), D_S its relative deadline, O_S its offset (0 for a Sporadic_Task), and T_R, D_R and O_R the destination Task's period, relative deadline and offset, each in Kernel time-base ticks as IronOxide R21.2 converts it and the Task_Model records it, so that no send of the source Task finds the channel full in any execution in which no Health_Monitor response suppresses a Release of the destination Task (IronOxide R9.1) and both Tasks complete every Job by its absolute deadline (IronOxide R9.6) (DD candidate; tightness: ORQ-06). *Evidence: `proof-tv-sampling-capacity` (a Verus lemma: over every sequence of due, start, completion, send and receive instants that IronOxide R9.1, R9.2, R9.6, R9.7, R10.1, R10.2 and R10.6, R11.8, R19.2, R19.3 and criterion 1 permit, under that hypothesis, the channel holds at most C_sample − 1 messages before each send), `proof-tv-core`, `pbt-sampling-no-full`.*
3. IF a cross-Partition data port connection meets one of the following cases, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model with one diagnostic for each connection and case:
   - (a) C_sample exceeds 255 (the Endpoint capacity limit of PAR-06 and the `ops` limit of MAP-26): the diagnostic names PR-14 and MAP-16 (a) and states the connection's C_sample;
   - (b) the sum over the Application's Endpoints of roles² × capacity × payload, with each cross-Partition data port connection's Endpoint as criterion 6 declares it and the connections of case (c) not counted, exceeds 8,192 bytes (PAR-06): the diagnostic names PR-14 and MAP-16 (b) and states the connection's C_sample, IronOxide's `MAX_ENDPOINT_STORAGE`, the connection's Endpoint storage and the total, in bytes;
   - (c) the destination thread is Sporadic (a Sporadic_Task has a minimum but no maximum interval between Releases, so no finite capacity bounds the sends between two drains, and criterion 2 defines no C_sample): the diagnostic names "Backend restriction" followed by ORQ-30, and MAP-16 (c) (UD-07).

   *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `test-map-16`.*
4. IF the drain of criterion 1 delivers no message that the Wire_Type decoder accepts, THEN THE Port_API SHALL present to that Job's compute code the value frozen by the latest earlier Job of the destination Task, since instant 0 or the last restart of the Task's Partition (IronOxide R9.1; R20.6; UD-12), whose drain delivered one or, where no such Job exists, the initial value that Requirement 20 gives the source thread's output port, and SHALL report the value as not fresh. *Evidence: `proof-app-glue`, `test-map-16`.*
5. IF a send on the Endpoint of a cross-Partition data port connection returns `Full` (IronOxide R40.5), THEN THE Port_Glue of the source Task SHALL count the refused send in the source's Port_API for that port and continue the Job without sending the message again, the Kernel having recorded the event against the sending Partition. *Evidence: `proof-app-glue`, `test-map-16`, `hil-sampling-no-full`, `test-qemu-<model>`.*
6. WHEN the Backend maps a cross-Partition data port connection (criterion 1: a data port connection that links threads of two Partitions, R11.1, or that R26.2 maps to an Endpoint), THE Backend SHALL declare its plain Endpoint with capacity C_sample (criterion 2), a payload equal to the size of the port's Wire_Type rounded up to a multiple of 4 bytes and at least 4 bytes (PAR-06), `ops` = C_sample on the receiving role and `ops` = 1 on the sending role, so that the Analyzer charges C_sample receive operations to each Job of the destination Task and one send to each Job of the source Task (IronOxide R40.2, R11.9, MAP-16). *Evidence: `test-map-16`, `proof-tv-core`, `check-generated-<model>`.*
7. IF a send or a receive on the Endpoint of a cross-Partition data port connection returns the peer-state error of IronOxide R42.3 (`PeerChanged`: the other role's Partition was stopped or restarted and IronOxide emptied both channels of the Endpoint), THEN THE Port_Glue SHALL count the error in its Task's Port_API for that port and perform no further operation on that Endpoint in that Job, so that the drain ends or the send is not repeated, the frozen value then following criterion 1 or criterion 4. *Evidence: `proof-app-glue`, `test-qemu-<model>`.*
8. WHEN a run of a generated Application under QEMU or on the HIL_Rig ends, THE Verdict_Report SHALL list, for each cross-Partition data port connection, the number of its sends that returned `Full` during the run, and SHALL report each non-zero number as a violation of the hypothesis of criterion 2, naming the connection's instance path, its source and destination Tasks, and its C_sample. *Evidence: `hil-sampling-no-full`, `test-qemu-<model>`.*

### Requirement 13: Validation functions for upward connections (Phase P2)

**User Story:** As a safety engineer, I want every message that flows up in criticality checked against the receiver's stated assumptions, so that higher-criticality code relies on no property of lower-criticality code.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN the Backend maps an Upward connection whose destination port has no `IronOxide::Validation_Function`, THE Backend SHALL declare on the receiving role of the connection's plain Endpoint (`validate = PATH`, IronOxide R42.4) a Validation_Function over the connection's Wire_Type that accepts a message exactly when the message decodes, by the decoder of R14.2, to a valid value of the port's data type (including the data type's GUMBO invariants, R22.2) and that value satisfies the conjunction of the destination port's GUMBO integration assume clauses under the semantics of R23.3 (MAP-18), and whose only loops are the decoder's loops over fixed-size arrays (R14.2) and the loops of the executable twins of R22.9 that evaluate those clauses and the data type's invariants, each with a count that is a compile-time constant and a Loop_Bound_Annotation (`#[rsk::bounds]`) that the App_Prover proves (UD-28, R19.4). *Evidence: `test-map-18`, `proof-app-validators-<model>`.*
2. WHEN the Backend maps an Upward connection whose destination port has `IronOxide::Validation_Function`, THE Backend SHALL declare on the receiving role of the connection's plain Endpoint a Validation_Function that accepts a message exactly when the message decodes, by the decoder of R14.2, to a valid value of the port's data type and the developer function that the property names, called with that value only after the decoding check succeeds, returns true. *Evidence: `test-map-18`, `proof-app-validators-<model>`.*
3. IF the destination port of an Upward connection has neither a GUMBO integration assume clause nor `IronOxide::Validation_Function`, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the connection with a diagnostic (R3.1) that names the connection, its destination port, IronOxide R42.4 and MAP-18, the decoding check of R14.2 and the data type's invariants alone not counting as either (whether GUMBO integration clauses may be attached to event ports is ORQ-46). *Evidence: `mut-refusals`.*
4. THE Contract_Translator SHALL generate each Validation_Function of criteria 1, 2 and 6 so that the App_Prover proves it, including any developer function it calls, total and free of panics, arithmetic overflow and out-of-bounds access, for every receiving Partition whatever its Criticality_Level, and so that each Validation_Function of criterion 1 returns false for every message on whose decoded value a GUMBO operation of the destination port's integration assume clauses is undefined under the semantics of R23.3 (R22.3, ASM-09). *Evidence: `proof-app-validators-<model>`, `mut-proof-validators`.*
5. WHEN a Validation_Function rejects a message that Receive Input takes from the Endpoint of an event or event data port, THE Port_API SHALL leave that message out of the port's frozen queue or event count for the Job, so that the frozen input holds exactly the accepted messages in arrival order and the port is presented as absent when Receive Input accepts none; the rejected receive counts as one of the receives that the role's `ops` bounds (R11.2, R11.9) and is not replaced by a further receive, the Kernel having recorded the rejection as a fault of the sending Partition and applied that Partition's `IronOxide::Fault_Response` (IronOxide R42.4), the rejection being counted by criterion 9 and not by R14.6; where the port is a Sporadic_Task's Dispatch trigger, a Job whose Receive Input accepts no message on it still runs the developer's compute code once, with that port presented as absent (UD-21, R19.6; the trigger's handler clauses are guarded by `HasEvent`, CK-14). *Evidence: `proof-app-glue`, `hil-validation-reject`.*
6. WHEN the Backend maps a connection between two Partitions to a plain Endpoint and the source Partition has a higher Criticality_Level than the destination Partition, THE Backend SHALL declare on the source Task's role, which IronOxide R42.4 treats as receiving from a lower Criticality_Level on the reverse channel that the AADL connection never uses, a Validation_Function that returns false for every value of the Wire_Type. *Evidence: `test-map-18`, `check-generated-<model>`, `proof-app-validators-<model>`.*
7. WHEN the Backend maps an Upward connection whose destination port has `IronOxide::Validation_Function` and at least one GUMBO integration assume clause, THE Contract_Translator SHALL emit a Creusot obligation on the developer function that a true result implies the conjunction of those clauses, under the semantics of R23.3, on the function's argument, for the App_Prover to prove (R21.2). *Evidence: `proof-app-validators-<model>`, `mut-proof-validators`.*
8. WHEN a Validation_Function rejects one or more of the messages that the drain of R12.1 takes on a data port in a Job, THE Port_API SHALL present for that Job, reported as fresh, the newest message of that drain that both the Validation_Function and the Wire_Type decoder accepted (R12.1), even where a newer message of the same drain was rejected, or, where the drain took no such message, the value that R12.4 presents (the frozen value of the latest earlier Job whose drain delivered one, or the initial value where no message has been accepted since instant 0 or the last restart of the Task's Partition), reported as not fresh; every message the drain takes, accepted or rejected, counts toward its bound of C_sample (UD-13, UD-12). *Evidence: `proof-app-glue`, `hil-validation-reject`.*
9. WHEN a Validation_Function rejects a message, THE Port_API SHALL increase the destination port's rejection count by exactly 1, the count being held in per-Task state (PR-25) with initial value 0, readable by the developer's compute code, covering messages that fail the decoding check as well as those that fail the integration assume clauses or the developer function (the Port_Glue receives `RecvError::Rejected` for both), and saturating at 2^32 − 1 instead of wrapping; the decoder count of R14.6 counts only messages that the Port_Glue's own decoder rejects, so that no message is counted by both. *Evidence: `proof-app-glue`, `hil-validation-reject`.*

### Requirement 14: Data types and wire formats (Phase P2)

**User Story:** As an application developer, I want AADL data types carried between Partitions in a form IronOxide accepts and decoded back exactly, so that my code works with the model's types.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Backend SHALL generate, for each data type of a data port or event data port of a thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor, the application Rust type that Table 14-1 gives, and, for each such type that a connection carries between two Partitions (MAP-15, MAP-16), on an Endpoint that R26.2 declares for a connection between threads of one Partition (MAP-21), or over the serial link (MAP-27), the Wire_Type that Table 14-1 gives, which meets IronOxide's `Plain` bound (IronOxide R42.1): Booleans as `u8`, enumerations as `u32` discriminants, fixed-size arrays as arrays, bounded strings as byte arrays with a `u32` length, records as `#[repr(C)]` structs of Wire_Types, and each record and string Wire_Type padded to a multiple of 4 bytes with no implicit padding (every padding byte, the trailing ones included, lies in an explicit byte-array field) (MAP-19). A `HAMR::Default_Bit_Width` or `HAMR::Max_String_Size` value is applicable to a port when it is set on the port's thread or on a component that encloses the thread, the nearest setting taking precedence (DD candidate; ORQ-31). *Evidence: `test-map-19`, `check-generated-<model>`.*
2. THE Backend SHALL generate, for each Wire_Type, an encoder from the application type to the Wire_Type and a decoder from the Wire_Type to the application type, both total and free of panics, whose only loops are loops over the elements of a fixed-size array of the Wire_Type or the application type (an array of Table 14-1, a string's byte array or a padding field), each with a Loop_Bound_Annotation (`#[rsk::bounds]`) of the array's length, a compile-time constant, that the App_Prover proves (UD-28, R19.4), and such that the encoder writes 0 into every padding byte and into every string byte at position L or later (L the string's length), and the decoder rejects, by returning no value, exactly the bit patterns that Table 14-1 lists as rejected: a Boolean byte other than 0 or 1, an enumeration discriminant of k or more (k the number of enumerators), a string length L greater than the bound n, a non-zero string byte at position L or later, a non-zero padding byte, and an array or record that contains a rejected element or field. *Evidence: `pbt-wire-round-trip`, `proof-wire-round-trip`, `test-wire-decoder-reject`.*
3. FOR ALL values v of an application type, decoding the encoding of v SHALL give v, and FOR ALL Wire_Type values w that the decoder accepts, encoding the decoded value SHALL give w (round trip), where two Wire_Type values are equal when all their bytes are equal, and two application values are equal when all their fields are equal, floating-point values being compared by bit pattern (whether pinned Creusot can state and prove bit-pattern equality is ORQ-43). *Evidence: `pbt-wire-round-trip`, `proof-wire-round-trip` (Creusot), `mut-proof-codecs`.*
4. IF the data type of a data port or event data port of a thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor, or a type that it contains as a record field or array element, is `Base_Types::Integer` without an applicable `HAMR::Default_Bit_Width` of 8, 16, 32 or 64; an unbounded array or sequence (an array whose `HAMR::Array_Size_Kind` is Unbounded, or that has no `Data_Model::Dimension` or a dimension of 0, which HAMR's type resolver classifies as Unbounded); or `Base_Types::String` without an applicable `HAMR::Max_String_Size` of at least 1 (how widths and bounds are obtained is ORQ-31), THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the port naming PR-14 and MAP-19. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`.*
5. IF the Wire_Type of a port's data type, padding fields included, is larger than 65,532 bytes (the largest Endpoint payload of PAR-06), THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the port naming PR-14 and MAP-19. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`.*
6. THE Port_API SHALL give developer code, for each input data port and event data port, the number of messages that the port's decoder in the Port_Glue rejected (R14.7), as a `u64` that is 0 when the Task's per-Task state is initialized, grows by 1 per rejected message and saturates at 2^64 − 1, as HAMR's Microkit Rust API does with `get_<port>_num_invalid` (a `u64`; `hamr/codegen/shared/.../microkit/plugins/rust/apis/CRustApiUtil.scala`); the number stays 0 for a port whose connection carries no Wire_Type, and a message that a Validation_Function rejects, including one that fails the Validation_Function's own decoding check, is counted by the rejection count of R13.9 instead and not by this number. *Evidence: `proof-app-glue`, `test-map-19`, `test-wire-decoder-reject`.*
7. IF the decoder in the Port_Glue rejects a message received on an input port, THEN THE Port_Glue SHALL discard the message without passing any part of it to developer code: for an event data port the Port_API presents the other messages taken in that Job, and the input as absent when none remains; for a data port the frozen value is the newest message of that Job's drain that both the receiving role's Validation_Function, where the role has one, and the decoder accepted or, where the drain took no such message, the value that R12.4 presents, marked not fresh (R12.1, R12.4, R13.8, UD-13). *Evidence: `proof-app-glue`, `test-wire-decoder-reject`.*
8. IF the data type of a data port or event data port of a thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor, or a type that it contains as a record field or array element, is of a kind that Table 14-1 does not list and criterion 4 does not refuse, among them `Base_Types::Character`, `Base_Types::Float`, an array whose `HAMR::Array_Size_Kind` is Bounded, an array with positive `Data_Model::Dimension` values and no `HAMR::Array_Size_Kind` (which HAMR's type resolver classifies as Bounded), an array with more than one dimension or without exactly one `Data_Model::Base_Type`, and a type that HAMR's type resolver classifies as neither a base, enumeration, array nor record type, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the port naming IronOxide R42.1 and MAP-19 (d) (ORQ-31). *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `pbt-refusals-named`.*
9. THE Backend SHALL declare the `payload` of each Endpoint that carries a Wire_Type as the Wire_Type's size rounded up to a multiple of 4 bytes, and at least 4 bytes (PAR-06). *Evidence: `check-generated-<model>`, `test-map-19`.*

**Table 14-1 — Data types, application types and Wire_Types.** "AADL type" is the kind that HAMR's type resolver gives the data component (`hamr/codegen/shared/src/main/scala/org/sireum/hamr/codegen/common/types/TypeResolver.scala`). Padding fields are explicit byte arrays (R14.1); the encoder writes 0 into them (R14.2).

| AADL type | Application Rust type | Wire_Type | Rejected by the decoder |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Base_Types::Boolean` | `bool` | `u8`: 0 for false, 1 for true | a byte other than 0 and 1 |
| `Base_Types::Integer_8`, `Integer_16`, `Integer_32`, `Integer_64`, `Unsigned_8`, `Unsigned_16`, `Unsigned_32`, `Unsigned_64` | `i8`, `i16`, `i32`, `i64`, `u8`, `u16`, `u32`, `u64` respectively | the application type | none |
| `Base_Types::Integer` with an applicable `HAMR::Default_Bit_Width` w of 8, 16, 32 or 64 | `i8`, `i16`, `i32` or `i64` for w | the application type | none |
| `Base_Types::Float_32`, `Base_Types::Float_64` | `f32`, `f64` | the application type | none (every bit pattern, NaNs included, is a value) |
| Enumeration with k enumerators (`Data_Model::Enumerators`) | a fieldless enum with one variant per enumerator, in declaration order | `u32`: the enumerator's 0-based position in declaration order | a value of k or more |
| Fixed-size array: `HAMR::Array_Size_Kind => Fixed`, exactly one `Data_Model::Dimension` value N ≥ 1, exactly one `Data_Model::Base_Type`, whose application type is A and Wire_Type is W | `[A; N]` | `[W; N]` | a value with an element that W's decoder rejects |
| `Base_Types::String` with an applicable `HAMR::Max_String_Size` n ≥ 1 | a bounded string type whose values are the byte sequences of 0 to n bytes (character encoding: DD candidate) | `#[repr(C)]` struct of a `u32` length L, an n-byte array, and padding fields up to a multiple of 4 bytes | L > n; a non-zero byte at position L or later; a non-zero padding byte |
| Record with data subcomponents f1 … fm | a struct with fields f1 … fm in declaration order | `#[repr(C)]` struct of the fields' Wire_Types in declaration order and padding fields, its size a multiple of 4 bytes | a value with a field that the field's decoder rejects; a non-zero padding byte |

### Requirement 15: Devices, peripherals and interrupts (Phase P2)

**User Story:** As a system integrator, I want AADL devices and process properties to state which Partition owns which peripheral and which interrupt releases which thread, so that IronOxide's ownership and MIT enforcement follow the model.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Backend SHALL declare in the `peripherals` list of the Partition of each process bound to the IronOxide_Processor exactly the union of the peripherals that the process's `IronOxide::Peripherals` names (an empty list where the property is absent, Table 2-1) and the `IronOxide::Peripheral` values of the devices of R1.2 whose port connection instances all end at threads of that process, each peripheral once, also where the process and one or more devices name it (the IronOxide_Generator rejects a peripheral listed twice in one Partition under PR-28), and no other peripheral (MAP-20). *Evidence: `test-map-20` (a peripheral named by the process only, by a device only, by both, and by two devices of the process), `proof-tv-core`, `pbt-backend-checks-match-generator`.*
2. WHEN the Backend generates an Application in which a device's out event port is connected to the Dispatch trigger of a Sporadic thread, THE Backend SHALL declare that thread's Task with exactly one Release_Source, the interrupt of the peripheral that the device's `IronOxide::Peripheral` names (`binds = PERIPHERAL`, PERIPHERAL being the property's value), which criterion 1 assigns to the Task's own Partition (IronOxide R21.6), with the MIT of R5.2 and with `mit_policy = defer` (IronOxide DD-06: the Kernel's interrupt intake masks the line for one MIT after each event, and the events latched in the peripheral meanwhile arrive later as one event, DEV-09). *Evidence: `test-map-20`, `proof-tv-core`, `pbt-backend-checks-match-generator`.*
3. IF a peripheral that criterion 1 would assign, or whose interrupt criterion 2 would bind, is (i) absent from the peripheral table of the IronOxide_Target that the IronOxide_Processor's `IronOxide::Target` names (`crates/rsk-gen/src/target.rs` at PAR-01), (ii) Kernel-owned in that table (at PAR-01: CLOCK, TIMER0, WDT and PPI on `nrf52840`; CMSDK_TIMER0 and CMSDK_TIMER1 on `qemu_mps2_an386`), (iii) assigned by criterion 1 to more than one Partition, (iv) EasyDMA-capable without a DMA register description in that table, or (v) bound by criterion 2 while it has no interrupt line in that table (at PAR-01: P0 and PPI on `nrf52840`; CMSDK_GPIO0 on `qemu_mps2_an386`), THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model with exactly one diagnostic of R3.1 per peripheral and case, counting any diagnostic that R2.4 reports for the same property value, that names IronOxide R21.5 for (i), PR-29 for (ii), PR-28 for (iii), IronOxide DD-08 for (iv) or IronOxide R21.5 for (v), names MAP-20 with the label (d) for (i), (e) for (ii), (f) for (iii), (g) for (iv) or (i) for (v), and names every process whose `IronOxide::Peripherals` lists the peripheral, every device whose `IronOxide::Peripheral` names it and, for (v), the thread of criterion 2. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `test-refusal-diagnostics`, `pbt-backend-checks-match-generator`; `test-map-20` for (iv) with a test peripheral table, since every EasyDMA-capable entry of the PAR-01 tables has a DMA register description.*
4. IF a device of R1.2 has a data port or event data port in either direction, has a `Thread_Properties::Dispatch_Protocol` value, or has port connection instances to threads of more than one process, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the device, whether or not its invocation sets HAMR's `--devices-as-thread` option (with which HAMR's Arsit back ends generate a thread for each device, `common/util/CommonCli.scala`, `arsit/StubGenerator.scala`; ORQ-01), with one diagnostic of R3.1 per case that holds, because the Backend maps no device to a Task (R5.1). Each diagnostic names MAP-20 with the case's label and the rule that MAP-20's Rule cell gives under that label:
   - (a) for a data or event data port, naming the port, and (b) for a `Dispatch_Protocol`, naming the value, each with the rule "Backend restriction" followed by PR-29;
   - (c) for connections to threads of more than one process, naming every such process and connection, with PR-28 and IronOxide R21.6.

   HAMR's SysML v2 Front_End sets `devicesAsThreads = T` in its base options (`hamr/sysml/frontend/jvm/.../FrontEnd.scala`); whether its AIR_Model then lists a `Dispatch_Protocol` on a device whose model sets none is ORQ-33. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `test-refusal-diagnostics`, `test-devices-as-thread` (the same AIR_Model with and without the option gives byte-identical outputs and diagnostics).*
5. IF criterion 1 assigns more than PAR-07 (5) peripherals to one Partition, counting one MPU window per assigned peripheral that the IronOxide_Target's peripheral table lists, as `crates/rsk-gen/src/layout.rs` does at PAR-01 (one 4 KiB window per owned peripheral, none grouped) and as R17.6 counts, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the process with one diagnostic of R3.1 that names IronOxide R20.4 (under which the IronOxide_Generator reports the case), IronOxide DD-07, the limit `MAX_WINDOWS` of `layout.rs` and MAP-26 (e), lists the assigned peripherals, and names every process and device from which criterion 1 assigns them. R17.6 states the same refusal, and this one diagnostic meets both criteria. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `test-map-26` (5 peripherals accepted, 6 refused), `pbt-backend-checks-match-generator`.*
6. IF a Sporadic thread whose Task criterion 2 releases by an interrupt has `IronOxide::MIT_Policy => discard`, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the thread with a diagnostic of R3.1 naming the thread, the property, IronOxide DD-06 and MAP-20 (h), because IronOxide's interrupt sources have the coalesced and deferred behaviour of IronOxide R19.4 (DEV-09); the IronOxide_Generator at PAR-01 does not reject this case (`validate.rs` reads no policy of an interrupt-released Task), so this check of the Backend is its only enforcement (IOP-06). *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `test-refusal-diagnostics`, `test-map-20` (the same model with `defer` is accepted).*
7. IF out event ports of devices whose `IronOxide::Peripheral` values name the same peripheral are connected to the Dispatch triggers of two or more Sporadic threads, including several out event ports of one device and one out event port with several connections, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model with one diagnostic of R3.1 that names PR-12, IronOxide R22.3 and MAP-20 (j) and every such device, port connection and thread, because one interrupt releases exactly one Task (`validate.rs`; ORQ-11). *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `test-refusal-diagnostics`, `pbt-backend-checks-match-generator`.*
8. IF a port connection instance of R1.2 joins an event port of a device to an event port of a thread and is not a connection from the device's out event port to the Dispatch trigger of a Sporadic thread (for example a connection to an in event port of a Periodic thread, to an in event port of a Sporadic thread other than its Dispatch trigger, or from a thread's out event port to an in event port of the device), THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the connection with a diagnostic of R3.1 naming the connection, the device, the thread, MAP-20 (k), and the rule "Backend restriction" followed by PR-29 and IronOxide R19.5, because IronOxide gives a peripheral no ports and keeps disabled every interrupt line that releases no Task (IronOxide R19.5), so no generated item would carry the connection. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `pbt-refusals-named`.*
9. WHEN a Job of a Task released under criterion 2 starts, THE Port_Glue SHALL make Get Value on the thread's Dispatch trigger port return a frozen event count of exactly 1, because the Kernel accepts one Release for the one or more device events latched in the peripheral and records no count of them (DEV-09; DD candidate); how the developer's compute code reads and clears the peripheral's events is ORQ-32. *Evidence: `proof-app-glue`, `test-port-api`.*

### Requirement 16: Constructs outside the profile (Phase P1)

**User Story:** As a system modeler, I want features that IronOxide's static profile cannot honour refused by name, so that no generated system silently differs from its model.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. IF the AIR_Model records at least one mode for a component of R1.2 (the IronOxide_Processor, a process bound to it, a component at any depth inside such a process, or a device of MAP-20) or for a component that contains one of these (the root system instance included), or records a connection or connection instance of the mode-transition kind that lies inside or ends at one of these components, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse each such component and each such connection with one diagnostic of R3.1 naming PR-01, PR-23 and MAP-22 (a). This refusal also covers mode transitions and mode-dependent subcomponents, connections and property values, through the modes they depend on, because the AIR_Model at PAR-02 records a mode by its name only and records neither transitions nor `in modes` clauses (`hamr/air` `AadlAST.scala`; ORQ-34). *Evidence: `test-map-22`, `mut-refusals` (one AIR mutant per component kind listed, each adding one mode, and one mutant adding a mode-transition connection), `test-refusal-diagnostics`.*
2. IF a component of R1.2 (criterion 1) or a data component classifier that R1.2 includes has a subprogram or subprogram group subcomponent, or a subprogram access or subprogram group access feature (provided or required), or IF the AIR_Model records, inside or at a component of R1.2, an access connection between subprogram or subprogram group access features, a parameter connection, or a subprogram call (at PAR-02 the AIR_Model records calls only inside BLESS subclauses; ORQ-35), THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse each such subcomponent, feature, connection and call with one diagnostic of R3.1 naming PR-32 and MAP-23 (a). *Evidence: `test-map-23`, `mut-refusals` (one AIR mutant per construct listed), `test-refusal-diagnostics`.*
3. IF a process, thread group or thread of R1.2, or a device of MAP-20, has any of the following features, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse each such feature with one diagnostic of R3.1 for each label it matches, naming MAP-24 with that label and the entry that design.md's diagnostic table gives for that label (an IronOxide rule, or the mark Backend restriction with the item on which the restriction rests):
   - (a) a feature end (a port or an abstract feature) whose direction is `in out`;
   - (b) a feature group, including a feature end of the feature-group category, which is how OSATE records an empty feature group (comment in HAMR's `SymbolResolver.scala`);
   - (c) an abstract feature.

   *Evidence: `test-map-24`, `mut-refusals` (one AIR mutant per label on each of the four component kinds), `test-refusal-diagnostics` (each diagnostic names the table entry for its label).*
4. IF a port connection instance of R1.2 (a connection instance with an end inside a process of R1.2), or a declared connection that the AIR_Model lists as part of that connection instance, has `Communication_Properties::Timing` with a value other than Sampled (Immediate, Delayed, or any other value, compared without regard to letter case), THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the connection instance with one diagnostic of R3.1 naming the value, MAP-24 (f) and the entry of design.md's diagnostic table for MAP-24 (f) (criterion 3). The Port_Glue implements neither an immediate nor a delayed communication instant, and it freezes every input at Job start (DEV-01). *Evidence: `test-map-24`, `mut-refusals` (Immediate and Delayed, each set once on the connection instance and once on a declared connection only), `test-refusal-diagnostics`.*
5. IF a port of a component of R1.2 has `Communication_Properties::Queue_Processing_Protocol` with a value other than FIFO (MAP-24 (g)), or a thread or thread group of R1.2 has `Timing_Properties::Dispatch_Jitter` with any value, 0 included (MAP-24 (i)), THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the port, thread or thread group with one diagnostic of R3.1 naming MAP-24 with that label and the entry of design.md's diagnostic table for that label (criterion 3). Whether other properties that no Table M-1 row reads but that change port or dispatch semantics (`Thread_Properties::Urgency`, `Communication_Properties::Input_Time`, `Communication_Properties::Output_Time`) are refused or ignored is ORQ-75. *Evidence: `test-map-24`, `mut-refusals` (one AIR mutant per label), `test-refusal-diagnostics`.*
6. IF a port connection instance joins a feature of a thread of R1.2 to a component that is neither a thread nor a device of MAP-20, for example a process, thread group, system or abstract component at which the connection instance ends, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the connection instance with one diagnostic of R3.1 naming the connection instance, the component at its other end and that component's category, MAP-24 (e), and the entry of design.md's diagnostic table for MAP-24 (e) (criterion 3). A Serial_Bridge thread is a thread that the model declares and marks with `IronOxide::Serial_Bridge_Role` (R34.8); the Deployment_Splitter adds only ports, connections and markers, so this criterion needs no exclusion for it (UD-15). Where that component lies outside every process of R1.2, R1.4 adds a second diagnostic naming MAP-27 (e). *Evidence: `test-map-24`, `mut-refusals`, `test-refusal-diagnostics`.*
7. IF a component of R1.2 (criterion 1) has a non-empty annex subclause in a language other than GUMBO, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the subclause with one diagnostic of R3.1 naming the annex language, MAP-24 (j), and the entry of design.md's diagnostic table for that language (criterion 3). A subclause is empty when every list that the AIR_Model records in it is empty, every optional part is absent or empty in the same sense, and every text it records is white space only. This is the form of the EMV2 subclause that OSATE's AIR export attaches to every component whose AADL text has no EMV2 annex, as in the committed AIR files of the INSPECTA models (R1.1). The Backend SHALL report no diagnostic for an empty subclause (UD-16). *Evidence: `test-annex-subclauses` (no annex diagnostic on the AIR files of R1.1; one AIR mutant with a non-empty subclause for each annex language that AIR represents at PAR-02: EMV2, BLESS, SMF and other annexes), `mut-refusals`, `test-refusal-diagnostics`.*
8. WHERE the Backend is built for Phase Pn (P1 to P5), IF an element of R1.2 is a construct that Table 16-1 assigns to a Phase later than Pn, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the element with one diagnostic of severity error. The diagnostic SHALL name the element's instance path and source position as R3.1 requires, the Table M-1 row, the Phase that Table 16-1 assigns to that row, and Pn, and it SHALL name as its rule class, in place of an IronOxide rule, the phrase "Phase restriction (R16.8)" (R3.1). *Evidence: `test-phase-refusals` (at each Phase checkpoint from P1 to P4: for each Table 16-1 row assigned to a later Phase, one model with that construct, which is refused; for each row assigned to that Phase or an earlier one, a model with that construct, which gets no Phase diagnostic), `test-refusal-diagnostics`.*
9. THE Trust_Base_Register SHALL hold one entry for each construct that criteria 1, 2 and 7 refuse but that the Backend can see only as far as the Front_End records it in the AIR_Model. Each entry SHALL state the construct, what the AIR_Model at PAR-02 records of it, that the Backend relies on the Front_End for the rest (ASM-01), and the open question that covers it. The entries cover:
   - modes, of which the AIR_Model records names only (ORQ-34);
   - subprogram calls, for which the AIR_Model has no element outside BLESS subclauses, so a call to a subprogram classifier that has no subprogram subcomponent, access feature or parameter connection is not visible to the Backend (ORQ-35);
   - annex subclauses that the Front_End leaves out of the AIR_Model. For example, the committed AIR of HAMR's `CodegenTest_CASE/attestation-gate` test model has no entry for the AGREE subclause of its thread type `CASE_AttestationGate_thr` (ORQ-36).

   *Evidence: `check-registers`, `review-air-coverage`.*
10. IF a thread of R1.2 (a thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor) has an in data port that is the destination of no port connection instance of the AIR_Model, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the thread with one diagnostic of R3.1 per such port, naming the thread, the port, MAP-24 (k) and the rule "Backend restriction (R19.1)", because no criterion defines the value that Get Value presents for a data port that no connection writes (UD-31). An in event or in event data port with no incoming connection is not refused: R19.1 presents it as absent, with an event count of 0, in every Job. *Evidence: `mut-refusals` (one AIR mutant that removes the only connection instance into a thread's in data port), `test-refusal-diagnostics`, `test-map-24`.*

**Table 16-1 — First Phase of each Table M-1 mapping** (criterion 8). The Phase is the one that the heading of the implementing requirement names for the construct. Every row of Table M-1 that is not listed here holds from Phase P1.

| Table M-1 row | Construct | First Phase | Implementing requirement |
|---|---|---|---|
| MAP-02 | thread with `Dispatch_Protocol => Sporadic` | P2 | Requirement 5 (R5.2) |
| MAP-10 | data subcomponent of a process, accessed through data-access connections | P2 | Requirement 9 |
| MAP-11 to MAP-14 | port connection between two threads of one Partition | P2 | Requirement 10 |
| MAP-15, MAP-17 | event or event data port connection between threads of two Partitions, and its destination port's `Overflow_Handling_Protocol` | P2 | Requirement 11 |
| MAP-16 | data port connection between threads of two Partitions | P2 | Requirement 12 |
| MAP-18 | Upward connection | P2 | Requirement 13 |
| MAP-19 | data type of a port | P2 | Requirement 14 |
| MAP-20 | device with `IronOxide::Peripheral` | P2 | Requirement 15 |
| MAP-29 | Initialize entry point; GUMBO initialize clause | P2 | Requirement 20 |
| MAP-30 | GUMBO subclause | P2 | Requirements 21 to 24 |
| MAP-21 | end-to-end flow with `Latency` | P3 | Requirement 26 |
| MAP-27 | cross-processor port connection (through the Deployment_Splitter and the Serial_Bridge) | P5 | Requirements 34 and 35 |

### Requirement 17: IronOxide limits (Phase P1)

**User Story:** As a system modeler, I want IronOxide's capacity limits checked against my model, so that I learn which model elements exceed a limit before any Rust is compiled.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. IF a model exceeds a limit that criterion 2, 3, 5, 6 or 7 states, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model without invoking the IronOxide_Generator for it, with the diagnostics that the criterion states, each of which also names MAP-26 with the limit's label ((b) for criterion 2, (c) for criterion 3, (d) or (f) for criterion 5, (e) for criterion 6, (g) for criterion 7) and the instance path and source position of every AADL element that the criterion lists as contributing (R3.1). The Backend computes each count, size, sum and region end of those criteria as an exact integer from the values that it would write into the App_Declaration. It does not reduce any value modulo 2^32, as IronOxide's App_Declaration parser does for `capacity` and `payload` at PAR-01 (`crates/rsk-gen/src/syntax.rs`; IOP-05 proposes that the parser reject such values). Where another criterion refuses an element whose value a quantity needs, the Backend computes the quantity from the remaining values, which can only lower it. MAP-26 (a) is refused by R7.4, and the `capacity`, `ops` and `payload` of each Endpoint are also checked by R11.12, R12.3 and R14.5. *Evidence: `test-map-26` (for each IronOxide_Target and each label of MAP-26, one model at the limit, which is accepted, and one just beyond it, which is refused with exactly the diagnostics that this requirement states, or for (a) the diagnostics R7.4 states), `pbt-limits` (over PAR-08 Model_Generator models per run, drawn at and around each limit of criteria 2, 3 and 5 to 7, the Backend refuses a model under one of these criteria exactly when the IronOxide_Generator reports the rule that the criterion names (DD-01, PR-14 or R20.4) on the corresponding item, run on the App_Declaration that the Backend writes with these refusals suspended, a test-only option that design.md defines; for criteria 6 and 7 the comparison covers only App_Declarations that `validate.rs` accepts, because the IronOxide_Generator computes the MPU layout only after validation succeeds, `crates/rsk-gen/src/lib.rs`).*
2. IF the number of threads in the processes bound to the IronOxide_Processor exceeds PAR-04 (7), counting threads at any nesting depth of thread groups (R8.2), the Serial_Bridge threads that the model declares in those processes (marked with `IronOxide::Serial_Bridge_Role`, R34.8; UD-15) included, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model with one diagnostic that names IronOxide DD-01, PAR-04, IronOxide `crates/rsk-gen/src/target.rs` `dispatch_irqs` (7 dispatch vectors, one per Task, on `nrf52840` and on `qemu_mps2_an386`), IOP-02, the number of threads and the limit 7, and lists every such thread as a contributing element. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `test-map-26`, `pbt-limits`.*
3. IF the number of port connections that the Backend would map to an Endpoint exceeds PAR-05 (8; IronOxide `crates/rsk-gen/src/validate.rs` `MAX_ENDPOINTS`), counting one Endpoint per connection (R11.1), each connection of an out port with several outgoing connections separately (R11.8), and each same-Partition connection on a flow of MAP-21 (R26.2), THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model with one diagnostic that names PR-14, `MAX_ENDPOINTS`, the number of such connections and the limit 8, and lists every such connection as a contributing element with the MAP row (MAP-15, MAP-16 or MAP-21) under which it needs an Endpoint (fewer Endpoints by multiplexing: ORQ-08). *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `test-map-26`, `pbt-limits`.*
4. IF, for a model that the Backend did not refuse, any of the following holds:
   - the IronOxide_Generator, run by the Backend or the Build_System on the App_Declaration that the Backend wrote, reports at least one diagnostic from its App_Declaration parser (`crates/rsk-gen/src/syntax.rs`), its validation (`validate.rs`) or its derivation (`derive.rs`, including the MPU layout of `layout.rs`);
   - compiling the generated Application fails with an error located in code that the IronOxide_Generator emitted (the system crate and Partition crate manifests of IronOxide R24, which at PAR-01 assert that each plain Endpoint's payload type fits its declared `payload`, IronOxide R38.1, and bound that type by `rsk::Plain`, IronOxide R42.1);
   - the Task_Model that the IronOxide_Generator writes records an Endpoint capacity or payload size, a Partition `mit_threshold` or the event-log capacity that differs from the integer that the App_Declaration states for that item;

   THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail the run with a non-zero exit status and report each such diagnostic, compile error or difference as a Backend defect. The report states the IronOxide output unchanged (both values, for a difference), the App_Declaration item concerned, and the instance path and source position of the AADL element from which the item was generated (R3.4, R4.7), or, where no element is found, the statement of R3.8. Diagnostics of the Profile_Lint, the Link_Checker, the Config_Checker and the Analyzer, and compile errors located in developer-owned files and regions (R18.5), are reported under R3.4 and are not Backend defects under this criterion. *Evidence: `pbt-backend-checks-match-generator` (over PAR-08 Model_Generator models per run, for every model that the Backend does not refuse, the IronOxide_Generator reports no diagnostic, the code it emits compiles, and the Task_Model shows none of the differences above), `check-generated-<model>` (the same for every corpus model), `mut-backend-checks` (the `backend-source` mutants of R30.8: for each check of this requirement and each Table M-1 refusal whose Rule cell names an IronOxide_Generator rule, a Backend mutant without that check, run on a model that the check refuses, makes the Build_System fail and report a Backend defect with the IronOxide diagnostic and the AADL element).*
5. IF any of the following holds for the Endpoints that the Backend would declare (PAR-06, MAP-26 (d) and (f)):
   - an Endpoint has a `capacity` outside 1 to 255 messages;
   - an Endpoint has a `payload` outside 4 to 65,532 bytes or not a multiple of 4 bytes;
   - an Endpoint has a role whose `ops` is outside 1 to 255;
   - the sum over all of them of roles² × `capacity` × `payload` (4 × `capacity` × `payload` for a plain Endpoint) exceeds 8,192 bytes;

   THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model, naming PR-14 and IronOxide `crates/rsk-gen/src/validate.rs`, with these diagnostics:
   - one for each out-of-range value, naming the connection, the field, the value and its range, except a value for which R11.12, R12.3 or R14.5 reports a diagnostic;
   - one for the sum, whether or not R12.3 also refuses the model for the sum, naming `MAX_ENDPOINT_STORAGE`, stating the total and 8,192 bytes, and listing every connection mapped to an Endpoint with that Endpoint's `capacity`, `payload` and storage in bytes.

   *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `test-map-26`, `pbt-limits`.*
6. IF the number of distinct peripherals that R15.1 assigns to one Partition exceeds PAR-07 (5; IronOxide `crates/rsk-gen/src/layout.rs` `MAX_WINDOWS`), counting only peripherals that the IronOxide_Target's peripheral table in IronOxide `crates/rsk-gen/src/target.rs` lists (at PAR-01 every peripheral of a Partition takes its own 4 KiB MPU window and no two share one), THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the process with a diagnostic that names IronOxide R20.4 (the rule under which `layout.rs` reports the case), IronOxide DD-07, `MAX_WINDOWS`, the number of peripherals and the limit 5. The diagnostic lists as contributing elements the process, where its `IronOxide::Peripherals` names one of them, and each device whose `IronOxide::Peripheral` names one, with the peripheral that each assigns. R15.5 states the same refusal, and the Backend reports one diagnostic that meets both criteria. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `test-map-26`, `pbt-limits`.*
7. IF the code region or the RAM region of a Partition, placed as IronOxide `crates/rsk-gen/src/layout.rs` (`plan`) places it at PAR-01, would end beyond the end of the IronOxide_Target's flash or RAM (its base address plus its size exceeds the end address of the memory), THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the process with a diagnostic that names:
   - IronOxide R20.4 and `layout.rs`;
   - the region (code or RAM);
   - the `Memory_Properties` property and the size in bytes from which the Backend sets the region (R2.2);
   - the region size and the base address at which the region would be placed;
   - the end address of the memory.

   The diagnostic lists as contributing elements the IronOxide_Processor and every process whose region of the same kind is placed before it. At PAR-01 the placement works as follows:
   - Each code region is the smallest power of two of at least the code size and 32 bytes. Each RAM region is the smallest power of two of at least the RAM size and 256 bytes.
   - The Partitions are taken in decreasing order of code size, then decreasing RAM size, then increasing Partition name compared byte by byte.
   - Each region is placed at the lowest multiple of its own size that is not below the end of the previous region of its kind. The first region of each kind is placed relative to the end of the Kernel's reservations, from `crates/rsk-gen/src/target.rs`:
     - `nrf52840`: first code address 0x0003_0000, end of flash 0x0010_0000, first RAM address 0x2000_8000, end of RAM 0x2004_0000.
     - `qemu_mps2_an386`: first code address 0x0002_0000, end of flash 0x0040_0000, first RAM address 0x2000_8000, end of RAM 0x2040_0000.

   *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `test-map-26`, `pbt-limits`.*

### Requirement 18: The generated Application (Phase P1)

**User Story:** As an application developer, I want a complete IronOxide Application generated from the model that IronOxide's own tools accept as is, so that I write only the thread bodies.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN the Backend accepts a model, THE Backend SHALL write a Cargo workspace with the crates of IronOxide's Application template `apps/p1-demo` at PAR-01 (IronOxide DD-11). The workspace holds:
   - a declaration crate holding the `rsk::app!` App_Declaration for the model's `IronOxide::Target` as its default and, each selected by one cargo feature as `apps/p1-demo/decl` selects its variants, the App_Declaration for every other IronOxide_Target of R18.2;
   - exactly one Partition crate per Partition, holding that Partition's Task skeletons and Port_Glue and no item of another Partition;
   - one payload-type crate holding the Rust types and Wire_Types of Requirement 14 that the declaration crate and the Partition crates share;
   - one system crate and one binary crate as in `apps/p1-demo/system` and `apps/p1-demo/bin`, the binary crate having no executable symbol of its own;
   - the build files:
     - a workspace manifest that lists exactly these crates, with IronOxide's `[workspace.metadata.rsk]` crate policy for each and IronOxide's release profile (`panic = "abort"`, `codegen-units = 1`, `lto = false`; IronOxide R14.1, DD-11);
     - edition 2021 for every crate (IronOxide R55.5);
     - a toolchain file that pins rustc 1.95.0 with the `thumbv7em-none-eabihf` target, as IronOxide's `rust-toolchain.toml` does (IronOxide R55.2);
     - a `Cargo.lock` whose third-party entries have the versions of IronOxide's `Cargo.lock` at PAR-01;
     - a Cargo configuration that resolves third-party crates only from the `vendor/` directory of the IronOxide extraction of criterion 9 (IronOxide's `vendor/` at PAR-01), with Cargo's network access off (IronOxide R58.1, R58.4).

   *Evidence: `test-generated-layout`.*
2. WHEN the Backend accepts a model, THE Backend SHALL generate an Application that IronOxide's tools at PAR-01 accept unchanged. This holds for each IronOxide_Target whose peripheral table (IronOxide `crates/rsk-gen/src/target.rs`) holds every peripheral that the model's `IronOxide::Peripherals` and `IronOxide::Peripheral` values name. Each tool runs on the Application as generated, with no file edited, built in the release profile for that IronOxide_Target. Each tool gets the arguments and the Profile files (`profile/profile.toml`, `profile/core_subset.toml`, `clippy.toml`) that IronOxide's verification job gives it (`xtask/src/verify.rs`, steps 8a to 8d). "Accept" means:
   - IronOxide_Generator: `rsk-gen --decl <App_Declaration> --system-crate <generated system crate> --out <Generator output directory>` exits with status 0 for each App_Declaration, and the `rsk::app!` expansion compiles without error.
   - Profile_Lint: `rsk-lint` over every generated crate and every Generator output directory exits with status 0, and Clippy with `clippy::disallowed_methods`, `clippy::disallowed_types` and `clippy::disallowed_macros` denied reports no error.
   - Link_Checker: `rsk-link` exits with status 0.
   - Config_Checker: `rsk-confcheck`, given the Task_Model, the binary and the Link_Checker's stack bounds, exits with status 0.
   - Analyzer: `rsk-analyzer` exits with status 0 (PASS) or 1 (FAIL), not 2 (rejected input) or 3 (internal error), as R31.2 states (UD-27). Its timing verdict is judged by R25.7 and is the subject of Requirements 25 and 27.

   *Evidence: `check-generated-<model>` for every corpus model and each such IronOxide_Target (one record per tool with its command line and exit status), `pbt-accepted-models-pass-ironoxide`.*
3. THE Backend SHALL write only Rust code that meets every rule below. This applies to all code the Backend writes, including the initial content of developer-owned files and developer regions:
   - every crate root and build script applies `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` (PR-20);
   - every crate root compiled for the IronOxide_Target applies `#![no_std]` (PR-35);
   - no `static mut` item and no `static` item with interior mutability (PR-05);
   - no `async` item and no `.await` (PR-09);
   - no loop whose bound is neither derivable from constants nor stated by `#[rsk::bounds]` (PR-16);
   - no direct or indirect recursion (PR-17);
   - no `dyn` type and no function pointer (PR-18);
   - no `#[panic_handler]` (PR-19, IronOxide R14.1);
   - no `core` item outside the Core_Subset of IronOxide's `profile/core_subset.toml` at PAR-01, so no `core::fmt` item and no `derive(Debug)` (PR-21);
   - no inline or global assembly (PR-22);
   - no `#![feature]` attribute (PR-36);
   - no `#[no_mangle]`, `#[export_name]`, `#[link_section]` or other link-level attribute, no `extern` block, and no function with an `extern` ABI (IronOxide DD-11, R24.2).

   THE Backend SHALL also write build files that set no `RUSTC_BOOTSTRAP` (PR-36) and that declare no dependency on a crate of the Profile's disallowed-crate list (PR-35) or on `vstd`, `verus_builtin` or `verus_builtin_macros` (IronOxide R55, R21.4). *Evidence: `check-profile-lint-<model>`, `check-profile-clippy-<model>`, `check-link-<model>`, `check-static-<model>` (IronOxide's `cargo xtask check --root <generated workspace> --workspace-only`, ORQ-37).*
4. WHEN the Backend runs twice on the same AIR_Model file, with the same Backend version and the same Front_End name and version (R1.5), each time into an empty output directory, THE Backend SHALL write the same set of relative file paths with byte-identical contents, including the generation report and the diagnostics documents of R3.3. This SHALL hold also when the two output directories have different absolute paths, the runs start at different times, and the environment variables `HOME`, `USER`, `TZ` and `LANG` differ between the runs. *Evidence: `test-determinism`.*
5. WHEN the Backend generates an Application into an output directory that already holds files, THE Backend SHALL apply these terms and rules:
   - A developer-owned file is a file that the Backend writes only when no file exists at its path.
   - A developer region is the lines strictly between a begin-marker comment line and the next end-marker comment line that name the same region identifier. This is the form of HAMR's marker merge with `invertMarkers` set (`hamr/codegen/jvm/.../codegen/CodeGen.scala`, `common/StringUtil.scala` at PAR-02), not the convention of HAMR's Microkit back end, which keeps developer code outside its markers (UD-14).
   - THE Backend SHALL leave each developer-owned file that is present byte-identical.
   - THE Backend SHALL write each generated file so that it equals the file that a generation into an empty output directory writes, except that each developer region present in the earlier file keeps its content byte for byte.
   - THE Backend SHALL delete no developer-owned file and no file that holds a developer region.
   - THE Backend SHALL list in the generation report each developer-owned file and each developer region (its file and region identifier), with the instance path of the thread it belongs to, or of the process where it belongs to no single thread (R3.4).

   *Evidence: `test-user-code-preserved`, `pbt-user-code-preserved` (PAR-08 generated models per run, each regenerated after random content is written into every developer-owned file and developer region).*
6. WHEN the Backend accepts a model whose every peripheral (Table 2-1 `Peripherals` and `Peripheral`) is in the `qemu_mps2_an386` peripheral table of IronOxide's `crates/rsk-gen/src/target.rs`, including a model that names no peripheral, THE Build_System SHALL build the Application's `qemu_mps2_an386` App_Declaration in the release profile and run the binary on `qemu-system-arm` with the options of IronOxide's `xtask/src/qemu.rs` at PAR-01 (`-M mps2-an386 -cpu cortex-m4 -icount shift=auto,sleep=off`, semihosting enabled). THE Build_System SHALL report the run as passing only when all of these hold:
   - QEMU exits by itself within a wall-clock limit of 120 s or 10 s per second of the model's `IronOxide::Operating_Duration`, whichever is longer (proposal);
   - the Build_System has obtained the Kernel's event log as it stands at the end of the run, with its overflow count (ORQ-38);
   - the log holds the operating-duration end (`EV_OPERATING_END`, IronOxide `crates/rsk-logic/src/logic/log.rs`);
   - the overflow count is 0;
   - the log holds no entry of a kind other than the operating-duration end and the safe-state entry (`EV_SAFE_STATE`), so no fault, Overrun, deadline miss, release overlap, MIT_Violation, full Endpoint or rejected message is recorded.

   This pass rule fails any MIT_Violation, full Endpoint or rejected message, whereas R32.9 tolerates the MIT_Violation and full-Endpoint entries that Table 32-1 accounts for; the two rules differ by design (UD-26). The version of `qemu-system-arm` is not yet pinned (ORQ-70, Table 42-1 row 14).

   *Evidence: `test-qemu-<model>` (recorded as not run, never as passed, for a model that names a peripheral outside that table).*
7. IF the Backend generates into an output directory that already holds files and any of these conditions holds for a file at the path of a generated file:
   - the file has a begin-marker line without a matching end-marker line, or an end-marker line without a matching begin-marker line;
   - the file holds two developer regions with the same region identifier;
   - the file holds a developer region whose identifier the new generation of that file lacks;
   - the file has lines outside its developer regions other than those that the Backend last wrote at that path, including the case of a file that the Backend never wrote;

   THEN THE Backend SHALL end the run with a non-zero exit status, write no file other than the diagnostics of R3.3, and leave every file already present in the output directory byte-identical. THE Backend SHALL report one diagnostic of severity error per such file and condition, naming the file, the line number of the marker or of the first differing line, the region identifier where one applies, and the condition. With criterion 5, this keeps developer code in developer regions and developer-owned files only, and refuses a regeneration over code edited outside them (UD-14). *Evidence: `test-regeneration-conflicts` (one case per condition, each checked for the exit status, the diagnostic and the unchanged directory).*
8. THE Backend SHALL write each thread's compute-code skeleton (R19.1) and the `const fn` initial-value functions of R20.1 and R20.2 only inside developer regions or developer-owned files of the thread's Partition crate. THE Backend SHALL give each Partition crate, and the crate that design.md designates for the `IronOxide::Safe_State` function (Table 2-1), at least one developer-owned file that the crate's root module includes, where developer code can define the functions that `IronOxide::Validation_Function` and `IronOxide::Safe_State` name. THE Backend SHALL write no Port_Glue, no Port_API, no App_Declaration item, no Creusot contract of Requirement 22 and no other generated item inside a developer region or a developer-owned file. *Evidence: `test-generated-layout`, `test-user-code-preserved`.*
9. THE Build_System SHALL build each generated Application against a workspace-local extraction of `IronOxide/` at PAR-01, and never against `../IronOxide` in place (UD-02):
   - The extraction is the tree of `IronOxide/` in commit PAR-01, written by `git archive` (R42.3) into a directory inside the workspace.
   - In the extraction, THE Build_System SHALL change only the workspace membership (the `members` of the extraction's root `Cargo.toml`) and `rsk-entry`'s binding, so that `rsk-entry` binds the generated system crate and no other Application's system crate. At PAR-01, `rsk-entry` binds the P1 demo's `apps/p1-demo/system` by default (`crates/rsk-entry/Cargo.toml`, `src/lib.rs`). The form of both changes is the one that design.md records for ORQ-37.
   - THE Build_System SHALL fail the build, naming each path, when a path of the tree of PAR-01, other than the files of these two changes, is missing from the extraction or differs from that tree in content or mode.
   - THE Trust_Base_Register SHALL list each file that the Build_System changes in the extraction, with the change. IOP-07 proposes that `rsk-entry` bind an Application outside IronOxide's repository without such a change.
   - THE Build_System SHALL leave every file of the IronOxide working tree unchanged (NG-01, R42.2).

   *Evidence: `check-pins` after every build, `check-ironoxide-extraction` (after every build, the extraction compared path by path with the tree of PAR-01: only the changes that the Trust_Base_Register lists differ), `check-registers`, `check-generated-<model>` (the Config_Checker compares the generated Task_Model with the linked binary and reports a discrepancy if another Application's tables are linked).*

### Requirement 19: Port API and run-time services (Phase P2)

**User Story:** As an application developer, I want the AADL run-time services (Receive Input, Get Value, Put Value, Send Output) generated around my compute code, so that I write the same input-compute-output code HAMR's other back ends expect.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Backend SHALL generate for each Task a Port_API that gives the developer's compute code Get Value on every input port, Put Value on every output port, and read access to the freshness and the counts that the Port_Glue keeps for the Task's ports, and that gives the compute code no value or operation that reaches a Port_Cell, a Release_Signal or an Endpoint role that the Port_Glue uses, or that writes a frozen input, a freshness or a count.
