Get Value presents the port's frozen input for the Job (criterion 2): for a data port, the frozen value and whether it is fresh (R10.4, R12.1, R12.4, R13.8, R14.7); for an event data port, the frozen messages oldest first, or absence when there are none (R10.3, R11.2, R13.5, R14.7); for an event port, the frozen event count (R10.2, R11.3), which is 1 for a Dispatch trigger whose Release_Source is a Release_Signal (R10.1) or an interrupt (R15.2) (DD candidate; DEV-04, DEV-09). For an in event or in event data port that is the destination of no port connection, Get Value presents absence, with an event count of 0, in every Job (UD-31, MAP-24); a thread with an in data port that is the destination of no port connection is refused under R16.10 (MAP-24 (k)), because nothing defines that port's value. The counts are: per output port, the outputs that Send Output could not deliver (R10.9, R11.5, R11.10, R12.5, criterion 9); per port, the `PeerChanged` results (R11.11, R12.7); and per input port, the rejections of R13.9 and the decoder rejections of R14.6. Each count is 0 at instant 0 and after each restart of the Task's Partition (UD-12, R20.6), and saturates instead of wrapping. Each count is a `u32` for R13.9 and a `u64` otherwise (DD candidate). The compute code reads each count as it stands when the compute code starts. How the compute code of an interrupt-released Task reads and clears its peripheral's events, and whether the Port_API wraps that access, is ORQ-32. *Evidence: `test-port-api` (compile-fail cases: Get Value on an output port, Put Value on an input port, a write to a frozen input, freshness or count, and a use of a Port_Cell, Release_Signal or Endpoint role from compute code; an unconnected in event port and an unconnected in event data port, each presented as absent with count 0 in every Job), `check-generated-<model>`, `proof-app-glue`.*
2. WHEN a Job starts, THE Port_Glue SHALL perform Receive Input for every input port of the Task, by the operation that R10.2, R10.3, R10.4, R11.2, R11.3 or R12.1 states for the port's connection, and complete it before the developer's compute code runs, so that every Get Value in that Job presents only what that Receive Input took and nothing that reaches the port after it (DEV-01; ORQ-07). *Evidence: `proof-app-glue`, `test-frozen-inputs` (a value that a higher-priority Task writes or sends to the port after the Job's Receive Input is presented by the next Job, not by this one).*
3. WHEN the developer's compute code returns, THE Port_Glue SHALL perform Send Output before the Job returns to its Release_Point, for exactly the output ports on which the compute code called Put Value in that Job. For each connection that leaves such a port, it SHALL use the operation that R10.1, R10.2, R10.3 (enqueue, then raise), R10.4, R11.1, R11.3 or R11.8 states, or for a cross-Partition data port a send on the Endpoint of R12.6. For a data or event data port, it SHALL send the value of the latest Put Value call on the port in that Job (DD candidate; ORQ-39). It SHALL handle each refused operation as R10.9, R11.5, R11.10, R11.11, R12.5, R12.7 and criterion 9 state. *Evidence: `proof-app-glue`, `test-map-11`, `test-map-14`, `test-map-15`.*
4. THE Port_Glue (its generated Validation_Functions included), the Wire_Type encoders and decoders of R14.2 and the executable twins that the Contract_Translator generates (R22.2, R22.9) SHALL contain no loop other than:
   - the receive drains of R11.2 and R11.3 (at most n_recv receives);
   - the receive drain of R12.1 (at most C_sample receives);
   - the FIFO dequeue of R10.3 for `Dequeue_Protocol => MultipleItems` and AllItems (at most `Queue_Size` items);
   - in Wire_Type encoders and decoders, Validation_Functions and executable twins, a loop over the elements of a fixed-size array whose iteration count is a compile-time constant (UD-28; PR-16 accepts such loops, and without them no array-typed port can cross Partitions);
   - in executable twins, the range loops of Table 22-2 row EX-12, bounded by that row's constant K (R22.9).

   Each drain also ends at a `PeerChanged` result, as R11.11 and R12.7 state. Each loop SHALL carry a Loop_Bound_Annotation (`#[rsk::bounds]`, IronOxide R35) whose bound is the connection's n_recv, C_sample or `Queue_Size`, the array's element count, or the constant K, as an integer constant, a form that IronOxide R35.5 accepts. *Evidence: `check-profile-lint-<model>`, `proof-app-glue` (the App_Prover proves each bound, IronOxide R35.2), `proof-app-creusot-<model>` (the bounds of the codecs and twins, R21.5).*
5. THE App_Prover SHALL prove two properties of the Port_Glue of every Task, whatever its Partition's Criticality_Level, under the trusted contracts of IronOxide's `rsk` API at PAR-01 (`Shared::lock`, `PerTask`, `Signal::raise`, `Endpoint::send`, `Endpoint::recv`; listed in the Trust_Base_Register, R23.6). First, the frozen input of each input port is the one that R10.2 to R10.4, R11.2, R11.3, R12.1, R12.4, R13.5, R13.8 and R14.7 define from the results of that Job's Receive Input operations. Second, Send Output passes to each Port_Cell write, enqueue and Endpoint send of an output port exactly the value of the latest Put Value on that port in that Job, through the encoder of R14.2 for an Endpoint, and raises, writes or sends nothing for an output port that had no Put Value in that Job. *Evidence: `proof-app-glue`, `mut-proof-glue` (the planted defects that PAR-17 sets for the proven postcondition, at least one per conjunct).*
6. THE Backend SHALL generate each Task body that the App_Declaration names (`fn task(cx: rsk_ctx::Task)`, `docs/hamr_study.md` section 12) as the wrapper that performs Receive Input, then calls the developer's compute function exactly once, then performs Send Output. Developer code SHALL then be reachable from a Task entry only through that call, or through a developer function of R13.2 that a Validation_Function calls during Receive Input. The wrapper calls the compute function in every Job, including a Job whose Dispatch-trigger message a Validation_Function rejected; the compute function then runs with that input absent (R13.5), and the handler clauses of the trigger, guarded by `HasEvent` (Table 22-1 CK-14), do not apply to that Job (UD-21). *Evidence: `check-link-<model>` (call graph from the Task entries: each compute function has one call site, inside its Task's wrapper, and no other path leads from a Task entry to developer code), `check-generated-<model>`.*
7. THE Backend SHALL give each Get Value and Put Value operation of the Port_API a Creusot contract over a logical model that holds one value per port, as the ghost port fields of HAMR's Microkit Rust API do (`docs/hamr_study.md` section 6; `hamr/codegen/shared/.../microkit/plugins/rust/apis/CRustApiUtil.scala`). R22.1 states the compute contracts over this model. The contracts are:
   - Get Value returns the model value of its input port, which Receive Input sets, and changes no model value.
   - Put Value sets the model value of its output port to the value put (to "event present" for an event port) and changes no other model value. The model value of every output port is absent when the compute code starts (DD candidate).
   - Put Value requires that the value satisfies the conjunction of the output port's GUMBO integration guarantee clauses.
   - Get Value ensures that its result, where present, satisfies the conjunction of the input port's GUMBO integration assume clauses (R24.4). Where the App_Prover does not prove this postcondition from the Port_Glue's code (R24.5), the Proof_Assumption_Register records it as a `[[step]]` table (R40.3) with the criterion that discharges it: R24.1, or for an Upward connection R13.1 or R13.2 (R24.3). The step is not taken by marking Get Value or another generated function trusted, which R21.5 forbids. How the App_Prover takes the step without a proof is a DD candidate, and any assumption construct used for it is also listed under R40.3 (ORQ-69).

   *Evidence: `proof-app-creusot-<model>`, `test-contracts-translation`, `check-registers`.*
8. THE Backend SHALL give the Port_API operations of each port the names and the parameter and result types that HAMR's Microkit Rust API at PAR-02 gives the same port (`CRustApiUtil.scala`), with `<port>` the port's AADL identifier:
   - `put_<port>` takes the port's application type (Table 14-1) for a data or event data port, and no argument for an event port.
   - `get_<port>` returns the application type for a data port, and `Option` of it for an event data port with `Dequeue_Protocol => OneItem`. For an event port it returns `bool`, true exactly when the frozen event count is at least 1.
   - `get_<port>_num_invalid` returns `u64` (R14.6) for a data or event data input port.

   *Evidence: `test-port-api-hamr-shape` (for every corpus model that HAMR's Microkit back end at PAR-02 accepts, these names and types match those of HAMR's generated `<thread>_api.rs` port by port), `check-generated-<model>`.*
9. IF an Endpoint send of the Port_Glue returns `Err(SendError::Refused)`, or a raise of a Release_Signal returns false, and the Partition's fault response lets the Job continue, THEN THE Port_Glue SHALL count the operation as an output that Send Output could not deliver (criterion 1), SHALL NOT repeat the operation in that Job, and SHALL perform the remaining Send Output operations of the Job. Both refusals are API misuse in IronOxide's `rsk` API at PAR-01, and the Kernel records each as a fault of the Task's Partition (`crates/rsk/src/lib.rs`). *Evidence: `proof-app-glue` (every result that the trusted `rsk` contracts allow is handled without a panic), `check-generated-<model>`.*

### Requirement 20: Initialization (Phase P2)

**User Story:** As an application developer, I want the model's initial state and initial output values set before the first Release, so that every input has a defined value from instant 0.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Backend SHALL generate, for each GUMBO state variable of each thread that R5.1 maps to a Task, one per-Task state item of that Task (`state TASK.NAME: T = INIT`, PR-25) whose type T is the application type that the Backend generates for the variable's type (MAP-19) and whose INIT is a call of a developer `const fn` that takes no parameters, returns T, and is declared by the generated skeleton of the thread's Partition crate under a name derived from the thread's instance path and the variable's name by the sanitization of R8.6 (MAP-29, DEV-03). *Evidence: `test-map-29`, `check-generated-<model>`.*
2. THE Backend SHALL generate, for each output data port (an `out` data port, not an event data port) of a thread that is the source of at least one connection that R10.4, R12.1 or R26.2 maps, one developer `const fn` that takes no parameters, returns the port's application type (MAP-19), and is declared by the source thread's skeleton under a name derived by the sanitization of R8.6 from the thread's instance path and the port's name, and SHALL use its result, for every such connection, as the initial latest value of the connection's Port_Cell (R10.4) or as the initial frozen value held in the destination Task's per-Task state (R12.1, R12.4), marked not fresh in both cases. R22.7 and R24.4 give this `const fn` a Creusot postcondition that states the port's GUMBO integration guarantee clauses. *Evidence: `test-map-29`, `proof-app-glue`, `check-generated-<model>`.*
3. IF a GUMBO initialize guarantee clause of a thread of a process bound to the IronOxide_Processor contains the GUMBO predicate `MustSend`, with one argument or with two (`uif__MustSend` and `uif__MustSendWithExpectedValue` in HAMR's `GclResolver.scala`), applied to an event or event data output port of that thread anywhere other than as the whole operand of a `not` operator, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the thread naming PR-34 and MAP-29 (a), because the Application puts no event or event data output before the first Release (DEV-03). *Evidence: `mut-refusals` (mutants `MustSend(p)`, `MustSend(p, v)` and `c implies MustSend(p)`), `test-map-29` (clauses `not MustSend(p)` and `NoSend(p)` are accepted).*
4. THE Contract_Translator SHALL translate each GUMBO initialize guarantee clause of a thread, under the semantics of R23.3 and with every event and event data output port of the thread holding no event (DEV-03), into a Creusot `ensures` clause of the `const fn` of criterion 1 or 2 where the clause refers to exactly one GUMBO state variable or output data port of the thread, and otherwise into a `proof_assert!` over the results of the `const fn` functions that the clause refers to, inside a function compiled only for the App_Prover (`cfg(creusot)`) in the way the IronOxide_Generator checks Resource initial values (`rsk_spec::<resource>_init_ok`, `crates/rsk-gen/src/emit.rs`), so that the App_Prover's run of R21.2 has one goal per clause (ORQ-41). *Evidence: `proof-app-creusot-<model>`, `mut-proof-contracts`, `test-contracts-traced`.*
5. WHEN the first Job of a Task after instant 0 (IronOxide R9.1) starts, THE Port_API SHALL present to the developer's compute code each GUMBO state variable of the thread with the result of its `const fn` (criterion 1), and each input data port whose connection R10.4, R12.1 or R26.2 maps and to which no value put by the source Task has arrived before that Job's Receive Input (R19.2) with the result of the source port's `const fn` (criterion 2), reported as not fresh, for results of any bit pattern; where this needs run-time work, the Port_Glue of that Job performs it, unprivileged and inside the Job's Budget (R6.6, DEV-08), since IronOxide runs no Application code before instant 0 (PR-34) (DD candidate; ORQ-40; IOP-08 proposes a flash load image of each Partition's `.data`). *Evidence: `test-init-first-job` (QEMU runs of corpus models whose `const fn` functions return values with non-zero bit patterns, each Task's first Job recording what the Port_API presents), `proof-app-glue`.*
6. WHEN the first Job of a Task starts after the Health_Monitor's RESTART_PARTITION response restarted the Task's Partition (IronOxide R14.5, R14.10), THE Port_API SHALL present to the developer's compute code the values of criterion 5, counted from the restart instead of from instant 0, and no event on any input event or event data port to which no event was sent after the restart (MAP-12, MAP-13, MAP-15). The restart thus re-initializes the Partition's per-Task state and Port_Cells as at instant 0, and "since instant 0" in R12.4 and R13.8 reads "since instant 0 or the last restart of the Task's Partition" (UD-12; whether IronOxide loads non-zero initial values on the restart is ORQ-40). *Evidence: `test-init-restart` (QEMU runs in which a Task of a Partition with `IronOxide::Fault_Response => RESTART_PARTITION` panics after every GUMBO state variable and every Port_Cell of that Partition has left its initial value), `proof-app-glue`.*
7. IF a thread of a process bound to the IronOxide_Processor has `Programming_Properties::Initialize_Entrypoint_Source_Text`, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the thread naming PR-34 and MAP-29 (b), because the Application runs no developer code before instant 0 and the `const fn` functions of criteria 1 and 2 replace the Initialize entry point (DEV-03). *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `test-refusal-diagnostics`.*

### Part C — Contracts

### Requirement 21: Application prover (Phase P2)

**User Story:** As a verification engineer, I want GUMBO contracts to become obligations for the prover IronOxide already uses for Application code, so that one prover, one toolchain and one set of trusted API contracts cover the generated system.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Contract_Translator SHALL emit every GUMBO-derived proof obligation (Requirements 13, 22 and 24) as a Creusot contract in Pearlite on the generated item it concerns (the compute function skeleton of R22.1, a logic function of R22.2, a Validation_Function of Requirement 13, or the function that carries a Connection_Obligation of R24.1), each contract taking effect only when the crate is compiled under `cfg(creusot)`, as the IronOxide_Generator's own contracts do, so that the Flight_Build of the Partition crate, compiled with rustc 1.95.0, has no `creusot-std` crate in its dependency graph (DD candidate). The choice rests on these facts. IronOxide designates Creusot as its single App_Prover (IronOxide R46.2, DD-13; IronOxide Verification_Plan section 7). The `rsk` API's contracts are Creusot attributes enabled by its `creusot` feature (`crates/rsk/src/lib.rs`), and the IronOxide_Generator emits each declared Resource invariant as a Pearlite logic function with an initial-value check under `cfg(creusot)` (`crates/rsk-gen/src/emit.rs`; IronOxide R46.4). IronOxide's Application crates build with rustc 1.95.0, edition 2021, without `#![feature]` and without `RUSTC_BOOTSTRAP` (PR-36; `toolchain/decision.toml` `[pin]`). HAMR's Verus output at PAR-02 declares `#![feature(proc_macro_hygiene)]` in every build and `feature(stmt_expr_attributes)` in every build without `cfg(verus_keep_ghost)`, sets `RUSTC_BOOTSTRAP=1` for every cargo call, pins rustc 1.99.1, and depends on `vstd` `=0.0.0-2026-10-25-0158` of Verus release 0.2026.10.26.d17fbd0, whereas IronOxide's `rsk-kernel` depends on `vstd` `=0.0.0-2026-10-04-0306` of Verus 0.2026.10.04.426d8b0 with rustc 1.98.1 (`docs/hamr_study.md` sections 5 and 6 and the `consumer_consumer` crate of its run 4; IronOxide `Cargo.toml` and `toolchain/decision.toml` `[verus_toolchain]`). *Evidence: `review-prover-decision`, `check-generated-<model>`, `check-deps`.*
2. WHEN `cargo xtask verify` runs, THE Build_System SHALL run the App_Prover on every Partition crate of every corpus model, compiling the same source files that the crate's Flight_Build compiles, once for each IronOxide_Target and set of Cargo features with which the Build_System builds that crate, against the `rsk` API built with its `creusot` feature, at the versions that the `[creusot]` table of IronOxide's `toolchain/decision.toml` pins at PAR-01 (Creusot 0.15.0-dev at commit `18f2d371ecb0019d37ecbd25e67f474bed2dda00`, whose check is ORQ-42, driving rustc nightly-2026-08-03, Why3 1.8.2+git, why3find 1.3.0+dev, Alt-Ergo 2.6.4, Z3 5.1.0, CVC4 1.8, CVC5 1.4.1) and with the why3find settings of IronOxide's `proofs/creusot/why3find.json` at PAR-01 (provers Alt-Ergo, Z3, CVC5 and CVC4; `time` 1.0; `depth` 6), and SHALL fail the verification job naming the function and source position of each goal that is not discharged. *Evidence: `proof-app-creusot-<model>`, `check-proof-sources-<model>` (every proved file is a file of the Flight_Build's Partition crate, not a copy), `mut-proof-contracts`.*
3. WHERE a Partition's Criticality_Level is A or B, THE App_Prover SHALL prove that every function of the Partition crate that the Backend generates or the developer writes (the Port_Glue, the compute functions and the functions they call, the `const fn` initial-value functions of R9.6, R20.1 and R20.2, which may run at run time under R20.5, and the functions named by `IronOxide::Validation_Function` included) is free of panics, arithmetic overflow (lossy `as` conversions included) and out-of-bounds access, no such function being marked trusted for the App_Prover (`creusot_std::macros::trusted`) or accepted without proof through another construct that R40.3 lists (IronOxide R46.3; ORQ-69). This admits no trusted item at levels A and B, which is stricter than IronOxide R46.5, under which the Verification_Plan records substitute evidence for a construct that the App_Prover cannot express (UD-19). What the App_Prover can prove of floating-point code is ORQ-43. *Evidence: `proof-app-creusot-<model>`, `check-proof-assumptions`.*
4. THE Build_System SHALL build the TV_Checker and every other crate of this project that contains Verus code (the Mapping_Relation of R29.2, the sampling-capacity lemma of R12.2, and the correspondence proof of R23.4 where it is written in Verus) as host-only crates outside the dependency graph of every Flight_Build, so that the only Verus crates in that graph are `vstd` `=0.0.0-2026-10-04-0306`, `verus_builtin` `=0.0.0-2026-09-16-0054` and `verus_builtin_macros` `=0.0.0-2026-09-20-0158`, reached through IronOxide's `rsk-kernel` and declared by no crate that the Backend generates, and so that the linked binary contains no `vstd` symbol. *Evidence: `check-deps` (the `cargo metadata` graph of every Flight_Build), `check-generated-<model>`, `check-link-<model>` (no `vstd` symbol; IronOxide's Link_Checker at PAR-01 reports only executable `vstd` symbols, under IronOxide R55.4 in `crates/rsk-link/src/scan.rs`, so the check of the non-executable `vstd` symbols is a DD candidate).*
5. THE App_Prover SHALL prove, in the Partition crate of every Partition whatever its Criticality_Level, that every function the Backend generates (the Port_Glue and Port_API of Requirement 19, the Validation_Functions of Requirement 13, the Wire_Type encoders and decoders of R14.2, and the executable twins of R22.2 and R22.9) is free of panics, arithmetic overflow (lossy `as` conversions included) and out-of-bounds access, that every Critical_Section it contains leaves its Resource's invariant satisfied (IronOxide R46.4), and that each of its loops executes at most the iterations of the bound annotated on it (R19.4, IronOxide R35.2), no such function being marked trusted for the App_Prover or accepted without proof through another construct that R40.3 lists (ORQ-69). At Criticality_Levels C, D and E this is stricter than IronOxide R46.3 and R46.4, which cover levels A and B only (UD-19). *Evidence: `proof-app-creusot-<model>`, `proof-app-glue`, `check-proof-assumptions`, `mut-proof-glue`.*
6. IF the App_Prover is not installed, or `cargo creusot version` reports a Creusot version, rustc toolchain, Why3 version, why3find version or solver version other than the pin that criterion 2 states, THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail the verification job naming each differing tool with its reported and pinned versions, instead of skipping the App_Prover as IronOxide's `cargo xtask verify` does when Creusot is not installed (IronOxide `xtask/src/verify.rs` step 10b'; IronOxide's `xtask/src/tools.rs` compares only the Creusot version and its rustc with the pin) (ORQ-42). *Evidence: `check-prover-pins` (one fixture per tool reporting a differing version, and one with no installation).*
7. WHERE a Partition's Criticality_Level is C, D or E, THE App_Prover SHALL prove that every function of the Partition crate that the Backend generates or the developer writes is free of panics, arithmetic overflow (lossy `as` conversions included) and out-of-bounds access, and that each Critical_Section it contains leaves its Resource's invariant satisfied (IronOxide R46.4). The only exceptions are developer functions that are marked trusted for the App_Prover (or accepted without proof through another construct that R40.3 lists, ORQ-69), that no Validation_Function calls (R13.4), and that the Proof_Assumption_Register lists (R40.3) with the Tasks that call them and the Resource invariants and GUMBO contract clauses whose proof they omit. Generated functions have no such exception at any level (criterion 5; UD-19). IronOxide R46.3 and R46.4 require no proof at these levels. *Evidence: `proof-app-creusot-<model>`, `check-proof-assumptions`, `mut-proof-contracts`.*

### Requirement 22: GUMBO contract translation (Phase P2)

**User Story:** As an application developer, I want the model's GUMBO contracts to appear as checkable contracts on my compute code, so that proving the code proves what the model states.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Contract_Translator SHALL translate the GUMBO compute clauses of each thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor into Creusot contracts on the developer's compute function in the forms of Table 22-1: each compute assume clause into a `requires` (CK-10); each compute guarantee clause and each compute case into an `ensures` (CK-11, CK-12); and, for a Sporadic thread, each assume clause of the handler of its Dispatch trigger into a `requires` and each guarantee clause and case of that handler into an `ensures`, each guarded by `HasEvent` of the Dispatch trigger (CK-14); each contract stated over the frozen inputs that Receive Input gives the compute function (R19.2), the values that the compute function puts on outputs before Send Output (R19.3), and the per-Task state at the compute function's entry (pre-state) and at its return (post-state). The integration clauses of the thread's ports are translated as criterion 7 and R24.4 state: a `requires` of the compute function on the frozen inputs, the Put Value preconditions and Get Value postconditions of the Port_API, the postconditions of the initial-value `const fn` functions, and the Port_Cell invariants. *Evidence: `test-contracts-translation` (at least one model per row CK-10 to CK-14), `proof-app-creusot-<model>`, `mut-proof-contracts`.*
2. THE Contract_Translator SHALL translate GUMBO state variables, GUMBO functions, compute and handler `modifies` clauses and data type invariants into the forms of Table 22-1 rows CK-01 to CK-05 and CK-13: per-Task state fields; for each GUMBO function, a Creusot logic function, a definedness predicate D_f (Table 22-2) and an executable twin whose Creusot contract states that, wherever D_f holds, the twin returns the logic function's value without panicking, which the App_Prover proves; frame conditions; and, for each data type with GUMBO invariant clauses, one invariant predicate that is at once the Resource `invariant` of R9.7, a conjunct of the validity check of R13.1, a `requires` on each value of the type in the compute function's frozen inputs and pre-state, and an `ensures` on each value of the type that the compute function puts on an output or leaves in its post-state. *Evidence: `test-contracts-translation`, `proof-app-creusot-<model>`, `mut-proof-contracts`.*
3. THE Contract_Translator SHALL give every GUMBO expression the value and the definedness condition D(e) that Table 22-2 states, which is this project's meaning (ASM-09) and which R23.3 records in the Verification_Plan, and SHALL translate each clause e that Table 22-1 translates as D(e) ∧ e, so that an operation undefined on its operands makes a `requires` clause or a Validation_Function false and adds to an `ensures` clause, an invariant predicate or the conclusion of a Connection_Obligation the obligation that the operation is defined. For arithmetic on a bounded integer type (Table 22-2 row EX-07) this meaning is stricter than Slang's: Slang's runtime and Logika compute the result modulo 2^n (`SlangParser.scala` `isWrapped`; Logika's bit-vector operations in `Smt2.scala`), whereas Table 22-2 makes an out-of-range result undefined, and DEV-11 records the difference (UD-04). No translated clause SHALL take the meaning of Slang's wrapping arithmetic, of HAMR's Verus translation (a result cast back to its machine type with truncation) or of HAMR's GUMBOX executable (Rust machine arithmetic, which panics with overflow checks and wraps without them) where it differs from Table 22-2 (finding F-1, ORQ-05). *Evidence: `test-contract-definedness` (for each Table 22-2 row with a definedness condition, one clause and one input on which D is false, in a `requires`, an `ensures` and a Validation_Function), `proof-app-creusot-<model>`, `diff-contract-gumbox`, `mut-proof-contracts`.*
4. IF a GUMBO clause or GUMBO function body of a kind that Table 22-1 translates (compute, handler, initialize, integration and data type invariant clauses, and GUMBO function bodies) contains an expression form that Table 22-2 marks refused or does not list, or is a case that Table 22-1 marks refused, THEN THE Contract_Translator SHALL refuse the clause, and with it the model (R3.6), with one diagnostic of R3.1 per clause that names the clause or function identifier, its Table 22-1 row, its source position, the source position and Table 22-2 row of each refused expression form in it, the rule "Backend restriction" followed by R22.4 (R3.1), and MAP-30 (a), which in Table M-1 version 2 covers data type invariant clauses and GUMBO function bodies (Tables 22-1 and 22-2), rather than drop the clause. *Evidence: `mut-refusals` (at least one mutant per refused case of Tables 22-1 and 22-2), `test-refusal-diagnostics`.*
5. IF a thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor has a GUMBO compute or initialize `flows` clause or a `monitor` clause, or a system that contains such a thread has a GUMBO `compositions` clause (Table 22-1 rows CK-15 to CK-17), THEN THE Contract_Translator SHALL report each such clause (each guarantee of a `monitor` clause counting as one clause) as not carried into the IronOxide Application, with one diagnostic of severity warning that names the clause identifier, its Table 22-1 row, its source position (R3.1, R3.7) and MAP-30, without refusing the model, and THE Trust_Base_Register SHALL list each such clause of each corpus model, by model and clause identifier, as unchecked on IronOxide. *Evidence: `test-contracts-not-carried`, `check-registers`.*
6. THE Contract_Translator SHALL write, on the line before each `requires` or `ensures` attribute, logic function, invariant predicate, executable twin and Validation_Function condition that it generates from a GUMBO clause or GUMBO function, a comment that names the clause or function identifier, its Table 22-1 row and its source position (the file URI, begin line and begin column of R3.1, or the statement of R3.7 where the AIR_Model records none), so that every App_Prover goal traces through the contract it comes from to one clause. *Evidence: `test-contracts-traced` (every generated contract item of every corpus model has such a comment naming a clause of the AIR_Model, and every translated clause is named by at least one comment).*
7. THE Contract_Translator SHALL translate every GUMBO integration guarantee clause of an output data or event data port into a `requires` on the value argument of that port's Put Value in the Port_API and, for an output data port, into an `ensures` of the `const fn` that gives the port's initial value (R20.2), and every GUMBO integration assume clause of an input data or event data port into a `requires` of the compute function over each value in the port's frozen input (Table 22-1 rows CK-06 and CK-07), using the conjunction of all integration clauses of a port where it has several (whether HAMR intends one integration clause per port is ORQ-76), and listing in the Proof_Assumption_Register, as a `[[step]]` table (R40.3) with R24.1 or R13.1 as its justification, every step by which the Port_Glue establishes such a `requires` without proving it. *Evidence: `test-contracts-translation`, `proof-app-creusot-<model>`, `mut-proof-contracts`, `check-registers`.*
8. WHEN the Backend generates an Application, THE Contract_Translator SHALL list in the generation report, exactly once each, every GUMBO clause and GUMBO function of every thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor, of every data component that is the type of a port, GUMBO state variable or Resource of those threads or of a field or element of such a type, of every GUMBO library annex that those clauses call, and of every system that contains those threads, with its identifier, its source position, its Table 22-1 row and its outcome: translated, with the names of the Creusot items generated from it, or not carried (R22.5). *Evidence: `test-contracts-accounted` (one corpus model per Table 22-1 row; the listed clauses equal the clauses of the `GclSubclause` and `GclLib` annexes in scope in the AIR_Model).*
9. THE Contract_Translator SHALL generate, for each clause e that Table 22-1 translates, the executable twin of R23.8: a Rust function over the clause's free variables (frozen inputs, outputs, pre-state and post-state values, and function parameters) that returns, without panicking, an indication that e is undefined on its input exactly where D(e) is false, and otherwise the truth value of e. D(e) ∧ e is true exactly when the twin returns the truth value true, and the Validation_Functions of R13.1 compute D(e) ∧ e in this way. The twin's only loops SHALL be the bounded loops of Table 22-2 row EX-12 and the loops over fixed-size arrays that R19.4 allows (UD-28). The twin's Creusot contract SHALL state the result above, and the App_Prover SHALL prove that contract together with the absence of panics, arithmetic overflow and out-of-bounds access in the twin, whatever the Criticality_Level of the Partition. These are the twins that R13.1 and R23.5 use. *Evidence: `proof-app-creusot-<model>`, `diff-contract-gumbox`, `mut-proof-contracts`.*

**Table 22-1 — GUMBO clause kinds and their translation.** The kinds are those of `GclSubclause` and `GclLib` in `hamr/air/shared/src/main/scala/org/sireum/hamr/ir/GumboAST.scala` at PAR-02. Contracts are attributes under `#[cfg_attr(creusot, …)]` with the macros of `rsk::creusot_std::macros`, as in IronOxide's Application crates (`apps/p1-demo/part0/src/lib.rs`), so that Flight_Builds compile with rustc 1.95.0 and without Creusot (PR-36); how logic items are gated is a DD candidate. Each translated clause e stands for D(e) ∧ e (R22.3). The "Refused" column lists the cases that R22.4 refuses under MAP-30 (a). The last column gives, for comparison only, HAMR's translation at PAR-02: the Microkit Rust (Verus) back end (`microkit/plugins/gumbo/GumboRustPlugin.scala`) unless the cell names another file.

| Row | Clause kind (AST class) | Translation | Refused (R22.4, MAP-30 (a)) | HAMR at PAR-02 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CK-01 | `state` variable of a thread (`GclStateVar`) | a per-Task state field (PR-25) of the application type that Table 14-1 gives for its classifier, initialized as R20.1 states; in a `requires`, `v` is its value at the compute function's entry; in an `ensures`, `In(v)` is its value at entry and `v` its value at return | a state variable of a component other than a thread | a field of the component struct in `<thread>_app.rs` |
| CK-02 | function with a GUMBO body (`GclBodyMethod`) in a thread's subclause | a Creusot logic function whose body is the GUMBO body translated by Table 22-2; a logic predicate D_f, the body's definedness; an executable twin with `requires` D_f and `ensures` that its result equals the logic function | a body other than one returned expression; a method contract on the function; a function that calls itself directly or through other GUMBO functions | `open spec fn` with results cast back, e.g. `square` as `(a * a) as i64` (F-1); a GUMBOX `fn` |
| CK-03 | spec function without a GUMBO body (`GclSpecMethod`), whose meaning the developer supplies | a Creusot logic function whose body the developer writes in a developer-owned region of the Partition crate (R18.5), and an executable twin that the developer writes under the generated `ensures` that its result equals the logic function, which the App_Prover proves; neither body has a generated default (DD candidate); the Trust_Base_Register lists each such logic function as defining the GUMBO function's meaning unchecked against the model | as CK-02 for the twin's contract | a wrapper `open spec fn` that calls `…__developer_verus`, and `…__developer_gumbox` with `ensures res == …__developer_verus(…)`; both default to `true` |
| CK-04 | GUMBO library function (`GclLib`, the package's `GUMBO__Library`) | as CK-02 or CK-03, generated once into each Partition crate whose clauses call it (DD candidate: one shared crate instead) | as CK-02 | `<package>::<name>_spec` functions |
| CK-05 | data type invariant (`GclInvariant`) of a data component | one logic predicate inv_T per data type T: the conjunction of T's invariant clauses over the value's fields, of inv of each field's type, and, for an array, of inv of the element type at every index; used as R22.2 states, the Port_Glue's unproven steps being listed as R22.7 states | an invariant clause that calls a function other than a GUMBO library function; an `invariants` section of a component other than a data component | not translated: warning `TODO: Need to handle datatype invariants` (line 174) |
| CK-06 | integration assume (`GclAssume` in `GclIntegration`) on an in data or event data port | a `requires` of the compute function on the port's frozen value (data port) or on each value in its frozen queue (event data port), the conjunction where the port has several (R22.7); discharged by a Connection_Obligation (R24.1) or by the Validation_Function (R13.1, R24.3) | an integration clause on an event port; an integration clause that uses a form of row EX-14 | `ensures` of the port's trusted (`external_body`) getter, for an event data port in the form "`res.is_none()` or the clause"; `GclResolver.scala` keeps one integration clause per port in `integrationMap` |
| CK-07 | integration guarantee (`GclGuarantee` in `GclIntegration`) on an out data or event data port | a `requires` on the value argument of the port's Put Value and, for a data port, an `ensures` of the `const fn` of its initial value (R20.2) (R22.7) | as CK-06 | `requires` of the port's putter |
| CK-08 | initialize guarantee (`GclGuarantee` in `GclInitialize`) | the obligation that the values that the `const fn` functions of R20.1 and R20.2 return satisfy the clause (R20.4): an `ensures` of that `const fn` where the clause names one state variable or port; the form for a clause that names several is a DD candidate | a clause that names an input port or uses `In` or `HasEvent` (Initialize reads no inputs: `docs/hamr_study.md` section 8 item 5); a clause with `MustSend` is refused by R20.3 | `ensures` of `initialize` |
| CK-09 | initialize `modifies` (`GclInitialize.modifies`) | no Creusot item: every state variable and output data port takes its value from its own `const fn` (R20.1, R20.2), so no earlier value exists to frame | — | not translated |
| CK-10 | compute assume (`GclAssume` in `GclCompute`) | a `requires` of the compute function over the frozen inputs and the pre-state | — | `requires` of `timeTriggered` over `old(api)` |
| CK-11 | compute guarantee (`GclGuarantee` in `GclCompute`) | an `ensures` of the compute function over the frozen inputs, the outputs, the pre-state and the post-state | — | `ensures` over `old(api)` and `final(api)` |
| CK-12 | compute case (`GclCaseStatement`: an optional assume A and a guarantee G) | an `ensures` of "A conditional-implies G" (row EX-04), or of G where the case has no assume | — | `ensures` of the implication |
| CK-13 | compute or handler `modifies` (`GclCompute.modifies`, `GclHandle.modifies`) | an `ensures` that each GUMBO state variable named neither in the compute `modifies` nor in the `modifies` of the handler that applies (CK-14) keeps its pre-state value; no frame condition where both are empty (ORQ-44) | — | not translated; the JVM back end (`arsit/gcl/GumboGen.scala`) combines the general and the handler `modifies` |
| CK-14 | handler (`GclHandle`: port, assumes, guarantees, cases, `modifies`) | where the port is the Dispatch trigger t of the Sporadic thread: its assumes as `requires` and its guarantees and cases as `ensures` (as CK-10 to CK-12), each in the form "`HasEvent(t)` conditional-implies the clause", and its `modifies` as CK-13; a Job whose trigger message a Validation_Function rejected still runs the compute function with t absent (R13.5, R19.6), and these clauses then hold trivially (UD-21) | a handler of a port that is not the thread's Dispatch trigger (IronOxide releases the Task only from its one Release_Source, PR-11, so the handler would never apply); a handler of a Periodic thread or of a data port (`GclResolver.scala` also rejects both) | not translated (`GumboXRustPlugin.scala` halts with `Need to handle compute handlers`); translated by the JVM back end (`arsit/gcl/GumboGen.scala` `fetchHandler`) |
| CK-15 | compute and initialize `flows` (`InfoFlowClause`) | not carried; reported by R22.5 | — | not translated |
| CK-16 | `monitor` (`GclMonitor`: guarantees and alerts) | not carried; reported by R22.5 (HAMR's runtime monitors are NG-09) | — | R2U2 runtime monitors, and an `ensures` that alert ports are left unchanged |
| CK-17 | `compositions` of a system (`GclComposition`) | not carried; reported by R22.5 (ORQ-14) | — | system-level proof crates and monitors (`GumboSysAssert*Plugin.scala`) |

**Table 22-2 — GUMBO expression forms: meaning, definedness and Creusot form.** Forms are Slang expressions (`org.sireum.lang.ast.Exp`) as GUMBO clauses use them after HAMR's resolution (`common/resolvers/GclResolver.scala`). "Value" is the meaning on defined operands. D(e) is the definedness condition: the conjunction of the operands' D, plus what the row adds. A bounded integer type is one of `S8` to `S64` and `U8` to `U64` (`Base_Types::Integer_8` to `Unsigned_64`, Table 14-1); `Z` is Slang's unbounded integer type; Int is the mathematical integers, which Creusot contracts reach through the `Int` view `x@` that IronOxide's crates use. "Conditional-implies" is the meaning of `CondImply` in row EX-04. The last column gives HAMR's Microkit translation at PAR-02 (`microkit/plugins/gumbo/SlangExpUtil.scala`) unless it names another file.

| Row | Form | Value; D(e) | Creusot form | Refused (R22.4, MAP-30 (a)) | HAMR (Verus; GUMBOX) at PAR-02 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| EX-01 | literals: `T`, `F`; `Z` literals; typed integer literals with the prefixes `s8`, `s16`, `s32`, `s64`, `u8`, `u16`, `u32`, `u64` | the literal; D adds nothing | Pearlite literal (`Int` for `Z`) | every other string interpolation | literal with a Rust suffix; other interpolations reported as errors |
| EX-02 | names: function parameters, quantifier variables, enumeration elements, field selection `r.f`, GUMBO state variables (CK-01), and ports: an in data port (its frozen value), an in event data port (the value of its frozen queue), an out data port (in a `requires`, the value it holds at entry; in an `ensures`, at return), an out event data port (the value put in this Job) | as stated; D adds `HasEvent(p)` for an in event data port and `MustSend(p)` for an out event data port | the corresponding term over the compute function's parameters and the per-Task state | a value reference to an event port; outside integration clauses (CK-06), a value reference to an in event data port whose `Dequeue_Protocol` is MultipleItems or AllItems | `old(api).p`, `final(api).p`, `self.v`; event data ports as `Option` with `unwrap()` |
| EX-03 | `In(v)` (`Exp.Input`) | the value of state variable v at the compute function's entry; D adds nothing | the per-Task state field at entry | `In(e)` where e is not a GUMBO state variable | `old(self).v`; halts on a non-identifier |
| EX-04 | Boolean connectives: negation; non-short-circuit `Exp.BinaryOp.And`, `Or`, `Imply`, bi-implication (`BinaryOpCust.BiImplication`); short-circuit `CondAnd`, `CondOr`, `CondImply` | Boolean value; for the non-short-circuit forms D = D(a) ∧ D(b); D(a CondAnd b) = D(a) ∧ (a ⇒ D(b)); D(a CondOr b) = D(a) ∧ (¬a ⇒ D(b)); D(a CondImply b) = D(a) ∧ (a ⇒ D(b)) | Pearlite negation, conjunction, disjunction, `==>` and equality on `bool` | every other Boolean operator (exclusive or among them) | Verus `&&`, `==>`; GUMBOX `implies!` for `CondImply`, `impliesL!` for `Imply` |
| EX-05 | comparisons `==`, `!=`, `<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=` on integers; `==`, `!=` on Booleans, enumerations, records and fixed-size arrays | integers by mathematical value; other types by equality of every field or element; D adds nothing | Pearlite comparison of `Int` views; logical equality otherwise | `<`, `<=`, `>`, `>=` on non-integers | the same operators |
| EX-06 | arithmetic on `Z`: `+`, `-`, `*`, unary `-`, `/`, `%` | Int result; `/` truncates toward zero and `%` takes the sign of the dividend, as Rust's integer `/` and `%`; D adds b ≠ 0 for `/` and `%` | Pearlite `Int` arithmetic; `/` and `%` through logic functions that the Backend defines by the truncation rule, not through the rounding of Pearlite's `Int` division (DD candidate) | — | Verus `int`; GUMBOX machine integers, e.g. `len() - 2` computed in `usize` in `consumer_consumer_GUMBOX.rs` line 212 |
| EX-07 | arithmetic on a bounded integer type T: `+`, `-`, `*`, unary `-`, `/`, `%` | as EX-06 on the mathematical values; D adds that the result lies in T's range (and b ≠ 0 for `/` and `%`); stricter than Slang, which wraps the result modulo 2^n (`SlangParser.scala` `isWrapped`), as DEV-11 records (UD-04) | as EX-06 on the operands' `Int` views | — | Verus: the mathematical result cast back with `as T` at function results and arguments (truncating, F-1); GUMBOX: Rust machine arithmetic, which panicked on overflow in `docs/hamr_study.md` section 4 row 5 (F-1) |
| EX-08 | bitwise and shift operations on integer operands (`<<`, `>>`, `>>>`, and, or, exclusive or, complement) | — | — | always (ORQ-05 does not fix their meaning) | Rust `<<`, `>>`; halts on complement |
| EX-09 | conversions `conversions.X.toY(v)` between bounded integer types and `Z` | the same mathematical value; D adds that it lies in Y's range | `Int` view of the result | conversions from or to any other type (floating-point, character, raw bit forms) | `(v) as Y` (truncating) in Verus and GUMBOX (F-1); conversions to `Z` reported as errors |
| EX-10 | conditional expression `if (c) a else b` (`Exp.If`) | a where c holds, else b; D = D(c) ∧ (c ⇒ D(a)) ∧ (¬c ⇒ D(b)) | Pearlite `if c { a } else { b }` | — | `if (c) { a } else { b }` |
| EX-11 | fixed-size arrays (Table 14-1, size N): indexing `a(i)`, `a.size`, construction `IS[…](e1, …, eN)` | element i; N; the array; D(a(i)) adds 0 ≤ i < N | indexing of the array's sequence view; N; array literal | a construction with a number of elements other than N; every other `IS` operation | `[i]` in both; `len()`; `[e1, …, eN]` |
| EX-12 | range quantifiers `All(lo until hi)(i => P)`, `All(lo to hi)(i => P)` and `Exists` of the same forms (`Exp.QuantRange`) | ∀ or ∃ i in Int with lo ≤ i < hi (`until`) or lo ≤ i ≤ hi (`to`); D = D(lo) ∧ D(hi) ∧ D(P) for every i in the range | Pearlite `forall<i: Int>` with the range as premise of `==>`, or `exists<i: Int>` with the range as a conjunct; in the executable twin, a loop over the range with a Loop_Bound_Annotation (PR-16, IronOxide R35.2) | a range for which the Contract_Translator derives no constant K ≥ hi − lo + 1 from integer literals and the sizes of fixed-size arrays (DD candidate: the derivation rule) | Verus `forall` or `exists` over `int` with the range guard; GUMBOX `(lo..hi).all(…)` and `.any(…)` |
| EX-13 | type and collection quantifiers (`Exp.QuantType`, `Exp.QuantEach`) | — | — | always (no bounded executable twin) | halts (the `Exp.Quant` case) |
| EX-14 | port predicates `HasEvent(p)` (in event or event data port), `MustSend(p)` (out event or event data port), `MustSend(p, v)` (out event data port), `NoSend(p)` (out event or event data port) | the frozen input of p holds at least one event or value; an event or value was put on p in this Job; a value was put and equals v; nothing was put on p in this Job; D adds nothing beyond D(v) | predicates on the Port_API's model of the frozen inputs and of the outputs put, with a `requires` that no output has been put when the compute function is entered | a port argument that is not a port's simple name; use in an integration clause (`GclResolver.scala` also rejects it) | rewritten by `GclResolver.scala` to `api.p.nonEmpty`, `api.p.nonEmpty && api.p.get == v` and `api.p.isEmpty`; a `requires` that every out event port is empty at entry (`getAadlRequires` in `GumboRustPlugin.scala`) |
| EX-15 | `MaySend(p)` | — | — | always | declared but not rewritten: `GclResolver.scala` halts with `Infeasible uif` |
| EX-16 | calls of GUMBO functions (CK-02 to CK-04) | f(args); D adds D_f(args) | call of the logic function | calls of Slang methods other than those of rows EX-11 and EX-14 | spec-function call with arguments cast by `as T` (F-1) |
| EX-17 | floating-point literals, operations, comparisons and conversions (`Base_Types::Float_32`, `Float_64`) | — | — | always (UD-08; the Robot_Model states speeds and duties as integers instead, R36.3; what the pinned App_Prover could prove over floating point is ORQ-43) | an error for contracts in models that use floating-point types (`GumboRustPlugin.scala` lines 99–101); halts on floating-point literals |
| EX-18 | every other form: `Old`, `Result`, `At`, `InvokeNamed`, `Tuple`, `ForYield`, `Fun` outside a quantifier, `This`, `Super`, string, real and character literals, temporal operators (`UnaryTemporal`, `BinaryTemporal`), `Labeled`, `StateSeq`, `Sym`, `Eta`, `StrictPureBlock`, `RS`, `TypeCond`, `AssumeAgree`, `AssertAgree`, `InfoFlowInvariant`, `LoopIndex` | — | — | always | halts on each in Verus and Rust (lines 755–793), except character literals, which it copies; temporal operators only for R2U2 monitors |

### Requirement 23: Contract semantics and their correspondence (Phase P2 statement; P4 proof)

**User Story:** As a certification engineer, I want the meaning of a GUMBO contract and the meaning of the generated obligation stated, and their correspondence proven or argued, so that a proof of the code counts as evidence for the model's contract.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Verification_Plan SHALL state, for each thread with a GUMBO subclause and as a formula over named variables, the meaning of the thread's GUMBO component contract under HAMR's semantics (ASM-04, `docs/hamr_study.md` section 8 item 5): for every dispatch, if the input values that Receive Input froze at the dispatch instant, every value of a frozen queue included, and the values of the GUMBO state variables at the dispatch (the pre-state) satisfy the compute assume clauses and the in ports' integration assume clauses, then the values that Send Output releases at completion and the state variables' values at completion (the post-state) satisfy the compute guarantee clauses, the guarantee of every compute case whose assumption holds, the Dispatch trigger's handler clauses, each out port's integration guarantee clauses for every value put on that port, and the `modifies` frame (a state variable that no `modifies` clause names keeps its pre-state value); and the initial state and the initial value of each output data port satisfy the initialize guarantee clauses and that port's integration guarantee clauses. The formula SHALL also give the value that a clause denotes for an in port whose frozen queue is empty or whose event count is 0, for an in event data port whose frozen queue holds more than one message (R11.2), and for an out port on which the dispatch puts no value or, for an event data port, more than one value (ORQ-45), and SHALL state where the data type invariants of R22.2 enter it. *Evidence: `doc-contract-semantics`, `review-contract-semantics`.*
2. THE Verification_Plan SHALL state the meaning of the generated obligation over the generated Rust, as the App_Prover proves it at the versions of R21.2. The meaning has three parts: every execution of the developer's compute function (R22.1) that starts with Port_API frozen inputs (R19.1) and per-Task state (PR-25, R20.1) satisfying the translated `requires` does not panic and, if it returns, returns with the values put through the Port_API and the per-Task state satisfying the translated `ensures`; the Port_Glue gives the compute function exactly the Receive Input values and sends exactly the put values (R19.5); and the initial per-Task state and initial output values satisfy the postconditions of R20.4. The statement SHALL name, for each kind of GUMBO clause of criterion 1 (integration assume and integration guarantee clauses included), the generated item that carries it: a `requires` or `ensures` of the compute function, a Port_API contract, a `const fn` postcondition, a Port_Cell invariant, a Connection_Obligation of R24.1 or a Validation_Function of Requirement 13; for integration clauses these are the items that R22.7 and R24.4 state. It SHALL also state whether termination of the compute function is part of the meaning. *Evidence: `doc-contract-semantics`, `review-contract-semantics`.*
3. THE Verification_Plan SHALL fix the semantics of every GUMBO operation that the Contract_Translator accepts, in a table with one row per operator and operand types that gives each form of Table 22-2 the value and definedness that Table 22-2 states (R22.3). The table covers the Slang types that HAMR gives AADL data types (S8 to S64 and U8 to U64 for `Base_Types::Integer_n` and `Unsigned_n`; Z, F32, F64, R, B, C, enumerations, arrays and records; `GclResolverUtil.scala`), the conversions (`conversions.X.toY`, `toRawY`), the shifts, and the Boolean operators, both conditional (`&&`, `||`, `___>:`) and strict (`&`, `|`, `__>:`). Each row SHALL give:
   - the result as a function of the operand values;
   - the operand values on which the operation is undefined, at least a zero divisor of `/` and `%`, an index outside an array, and an argument outside the range that a `toY` conversion requires;
   - for an arithmetic operation of S8 to S64 or U8 to U64, that a mathematical result outside the type's range is undefined (Table 22-2 row EX-07; ASM-09), together with Slang's result for it, which wraps modulo 2^n (DEV-11) (UD-04);
   - for a conditional Boolean operator, whether the result is defined when the left operand decides it and the right operand is undefined;
   - for F32 and F64, the rounding mode and the results of `==` and `~~` on NaN and on signed zeros;
   - for Z and R, the Rust type the Backend gives the value (MAP-19);
   - the Slang documentation or Slang source file at PAR-02 and the GUMBO documentation page [RN-02] that the row follows, or the statement that the GUMBO documentation does not cover the operation;
   - the row's Pearlite form and its executable twin's Rust form (criterion 8);
   - whether the App_Prover discharges obligations over the row (IronOxide RN-10, ORQ-43).

   An operation without a row is one that R22.4 refuses. *Evidence: `doc-contract-semantics`, `review-contract-semantics`, `test-contracts-translation`.*
4. THE Verification_Plan SHALL contain an argument (Phase P2) and, from Phase P4, a mechanized proof in Verus or Creusot, that four premises together imply, for every Job, the meaning of criterion 1 for the dispatch that HAMR's semantics gives the Job's Release, with the frozen inputs as Table M-2 redefines them. The premises are: the generated obligation of criterion 2; the Port_Glue's proven behaviour (R19.5); the Connection_Obligations and Validation_Functions of Requirement 24 for each integration assume clause that criterion 2 places outside the compute function's `requires`; and the deviations of Table M-2. The mechanized proof SHALL be over a model of the Port_Glue whose Receive Input and Send Output are the postconditions that R19.5 proves of the generated Port_Glue. For each row of Table M-2, and for each of the cases below, the Verification_Plan SHALL state whether that meaning holds for the Job unchanged, holds with a stated substitution, or does not hold, and under what condition it does not hold:
   - a Job that the Kernel ends before its Send Output completes (an Overrun, DEV-08, or a fault response of its Partition), which can leave some of its outputs sent (ORQ-07);
   - a stop or restart of the Task's Partition between two of its Jobs;
   - a send that returns `Full` or `PeerChanged` (R11.5, R11.11, R12.5, R12.7);
   - a message that a Validation_Function rejects (R13.5, R13.8);
   - an input on which criterion 3 gives an operation a meaning different from Slang's (criterion 7; DEV-11).

   *Evidence: `review-contract-correspondence` (P2), `proof-contract-correspondence` (P4), `mut-proof-correspondence`.*
5. WHEN the Build_System runs the contract differential test on a corpus model, THE Build_System SHALL compare each translated clause's executable twin (criterion 8) with each GUMBOX executable of the same clause that HAMR at PAR-02 generates and compiles for that model. These are the Slang executable of HAMR's JVM back end and the Rust executable of HAMR's Microkit back end, built with overflow checks enabled, as HAMR's generated tests build it.
   - Inputs SHALL be drawn from a recorded seed and SHALL include, for every scalar the clause reads, its type's minimum, maximum and 0 and, for F32 and F64, NaN, −0.0 and both infinities.
   - Drawing SHALL stop when PAR-18 inputs on which the twin and that executable are both defined have been evaluated, or when 6 × PAR-18 inputs have been drawn (HAMR's generated property tests use a reject ratio of 5).
   - The test SHALL fail, naming the clause, the input and both results, on any such input on which their Boolean results differ, and SHALL fail for any clause with fewer than PAR-18 such inputs.
   - The test SHALL report every input on which exactly one of the two is defined, and SHALL report as not compared each clause for which HAMR generates no GUMBOX executable that compiles (finding F-2).

   *Evidence: `diff-contract-gumbox`.*
6. THE Trust_Base_Register SHALL contain one entry for each row of Table 23-1 and one entry for every other step from GUMBO text to the App_Prover's verdict that no proof of this project covers, including steps that only tests cover. Each entry SHALL state, in the format of IronOxide's Trust_Base_Register (IronOxide R48.2, R48.3), what is trusted, why, the evidence that reduces the trust, the residual risk, the requirements that depend on the entry, its ASM and ORQ links, and the IronOxide Trust_Base_Register entries that its Table 23-1 row cites. *Evidence: `check-registers` (fails when a row of Table 23-1 has no entry or an entry lacks a field).*
7. THE Verification_Plan SHALL list each operation of the table of criterion 3 whose meaning there differs from the meaning that Slang at PAR-02 gives it (in its runtime library or in Logika's encoding), from HAMR's Verus translation, or from either GUMBOX executable of criterion 5, giving for each the operand values on which the meanings differ and the effect on criterion 4. The list SHALL cover at least the arithmetic of S8 to S64 and U8 to U64: Slang's runtime and Logika compute it modulo 2^n (`SlangParser.scala`: `isWrapped`; `Z.scala`; Logika's bit-vector operations in `Smt2.scala`), while HAMR's Rust GUMBOX executable panics on overflow in a test build (finding F-1). Criterion 3 makes an out-of-range result of this arithmetic undefined (UD-04, DEV-11), so the list SHALL give as differing operand values every operand pair whose mathematical result lies outside the type's range. *Evidence: `doc-contract-semantics`, `review-contract-semantics`, `diff-contract-gumbox`.*
8. THE Contract_Translator SHALL generate, for each GUMBO clause it translates, an executable twin: a Rust function of the values the clause reads that returns, without panicking, either an indication that criterion 3 leaves the clause undefined on its input or the clause's truth value. THE App_Prover SHALL prove that the twin returns the undefined indication exactly on the inputs on which criterion 3 leaves the clause undefined, and otherwise returns the truth value of the clause's Pearlite translation. This function is the executable twin of R22.9, the only form of twin for a clause; D(e) ∧ e of R22.3 is true exactly when the twin returns the truth value true. *Evidence: `proof-app-creusot-<model>`, `mut-proof-contracts`, `test-contracts-translation`.*
9. WHERE the build includes Phase P4 evidence, THE Build_System SHALL check the mechanized proof of criterion 4 with pinned prover versions (Verus and Z3 as R29.3 pins them, or the App_Prover at the versions of R21.2). It SHALL fail the proof job when the proof does not verify. It SHALL also fail the job, naming the construct, when the proof contains an assumption construct (Verus `assume`, `admit`, `external_body` or `assume_specification`; Creusot `#[trusted]` and every other Creusot construct that R40.3 lists, ORQ-69) that the Proof_Assumption_Register does not list. *Evidence: `proof-contract-correspondence`, `mut-proof-correspondence` (at least the planted defects of PAR-17, listed in the Proof_Mutation_Register).*

**Table 23-1 — Trusted steps from GUMBO text to the App_Prover's verdict and its meaning on the IronOxide_Target** (each row has one Trust_Base_Register entry under R23.6; "Rests on" names the assumptions, open questions and IronOxide Trust_Base_Register entries the row depends on)

| Row | Trusted step | What is trusted | Evidence that reduces the trust | Rests on |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CT-01 | The Front_End: parsing GUMBO annex text and instantiating the model into the AIR_Model (OSATE or FMIDE, or HAMR's SysML v2 front end) | Each `GclSubclause` holds exactly the clauses of the source text, with their source positions | Every App_Prover goal traced to its clause and position (R22.6, `test-contracts-traced`); the AADL and SysML v2 forms of one model compared (R2.6, `test-property-set-equivalence`) | ASM-01; ORQ-10 |
| CT-02 | Slang name resolution and type checking of GUMBO expressions (HAMR's GUMBO resolver in `common/resolvers` and Slang's type checker) | Each operator and operand type in the AIR_Model is the one that criterion 3's table gives a meaning for (for example `S64` for `Base_Types::Integer_64`, `GclResolverUtil.scala`) | `test-contracts-translation`; criterion 5 against GUMBOX executables generated from the same typed AIR | ASM-01; ORQ-05 |
| CT-03 | AIR JSON serialization and HAMR's AIR reader, which the Contract_Translator and the Semantic_Model_Extractor share (R28.2) | The GUMBO AST that is read is the one that the Front_End wrote | HAMR's use of the same reader in every back end; no evidence of this project | ASM-01 |
| CT-04 | The reference semantics: criterion 1's meaning and criterion 3's table, which formalize HAMR's informal semantics [RN-01, RN-02] and Slang at PAR-02 | They state what HAMR and Slang mean | `review-contract-semantics`; criterion 5 against HAMR's GUMBOX executables; the differences listed under criterion 7 | ASM-04; ASM-09; ORQ-05; ORQ-45 |
| CT-05 | HAMR's GUMBOX executables as the oracle of criterion 5 | They compute Slang's meaning of each clause | The Slang executable runs Slang's runtime library; the Rust executable is HAMR's translation and is known to differ (finding F-1, criterion 7) | F-1; F-2 |
| CT-06 | The Contract_Translator: GUMBO clause to Pearlite contract | The contract means what criterion 3 gives the clause | The twin proven equal to the contract (criterion 8); the twin tested against GUMBOX (criterion 5); `mut-proof-contracts` | R22 |
| CT-07 | The App_Prover: Creusot 0.15.0-dev at commit 18f2d371e with Why3 1.8.2+git and the solvers alt-ergo, z3, cvc4 and cvc5, as IronOxide's `toolchain/decision.toml` `[creusot]` pins them at PAR-01 | A discharged goal holds | Every goal discharged by two solvers from disjoint sets (IronOxide Verification_Plan 9.1); pinned versions recorded in each Evidence_Item | IronOxide TBR-08; IronOxide ORQ-10 |
| CT-08 | The source the App_Prover analyses compared with the source the Flight_Build compiles: Creusot drives rustc nightly-2026-08-03 under `cfg(creusot)`, the Flight_Build uses rustc 1.95.0 without it, and IronOxide's Generator emits its glue under `cfg(not(creusot))` | The proven code is the code compiled | design.md lists every generated item that is compiled only outside `cfg(creusot)`; IronOxide's property-preservation argument (IronOxide R52.3) | IronOxide TBR-01; IronOxide ORQ-08 |
| CT-09 | The trusted contracts of IronOxide's `rsk` API that the Port_Glue and the Validation_Functions call (`Shared::lock`, `PerTask`, `Signal::raise`, `now`, and the Endpoint send and receive) | Each call behaves as its Creusot contract states | Contracts reviewed against the Kernel's Verus postconditions, not derived from them; IronOxide's QEMU and HIL tests of the API (IronOxide R46.5 record) | IronOxide TBR-08; ASM-05 |
| CT-10 | Code that the App_Prover cannot express or does not prove: developer functions of Partitions at Criticality_Levels C to E that R21.7 exempts (marked `#[trusted]` or accepted through another construct of R40.3, listed in the Proof_Assumption_Register), including floating-point arithmetic (IronOxide RN-10); R21.3 and R21.5 admit no such item in generated code or at levels A and B (UD-19) | Each such item behaves as its contract states | Substitute evidence for each item, as IronOxide R46.5 requires | IronOxide TBR-08; ORQ-43; ORQ-69 |

### Requirement 24: Connection obligations (Phase P2)

**User Story:** As a verification engineer, I want each receiver's integration assumptions discharged by its sender's guarantees or by a run-time check, so that no assumption is taken on trust.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN the Backend maps a port connection whose source and destination threads are bound to the IronOxide_Processor (a thread of the MCU_Side Serial_Bridge of R34.1 included), whose destination port is a data or event data port with at least one GUMBO integration assume clause, and which is not an Upward connection (both threads in one Partition, or the source Partition's Criticality_Level equal to or higher than the destination Partition's), THE Contract_Translator SHALL emit exactly one Connection_Obligation for that connection (MAP-30), a Creusot goal stating that for every value v of the connection's data type the conjunction of the source port's integration guarantee clauses on v (true where the source port has none) implies the conjunction of every integration assume clause that the destination thread's GUMBO subclause states on the destination port (whether HAMR intends one integration clause per port is ORQ-76), each GUMBO operation having the semantics of R23.3 and an operation undefined on its operands making the clause that contains it false (R22.3), and THE App_Prover SHALL prove it; each connection of an out port with several connections (R10.7, R11.8) is classified and given its Connection_Obligation separately, and connections that R26.2 maps to Endpoints are included. *Evidence: `proof-connection-obligations-<model>`, `test-connection-obligations` (for every corpus model, the emitted Connection_Obligations correspond one to one to the connections this criterion selects; the corpus includes an out port with three connections of which one is Upward, giving two obligations, and a destination port with two assume clauses, both in the conclusion).*
2. IF the App_Prover does not discharge a Connection_Obligation of criterion 1, including that of a connection whose source port has no integration guarantee clause, THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail the proof job, whatever run-time checks the generated code contains, with a report that names the connection's instance path, the instance paths of the source and destination ports, the identifier and source position of every guarantee and assume clause in the obligation (R22.6), and the App_Prover's result for the goal. *Evidence: `mut-proof-connections` (each mutant that R33.1 registers for the Connection_Obligations fails the job with this report), `test-connection-obligation-report` (a source port without a guarantee clause and a destination assume clause that does not hold for every value).*
3. WHEN the Backend maps an Upward connection whose destination port has at least one GUMBO integration assume clause, THE Backend SHALL discharge those clauses without a Connection_Obligation and without relying on the source port's integration guarantee clauses: for each received message, by the Validation_Function of R13.1, or of R13.2 together with the obligation of R13.7, on the receiving role (MAP-18); and, for a data connection, for the initial value that the destination's Port_API presents until it accepts a message (R12.4, R13.8), which no Validation_Function checks, by a Creusot obligation that the App_Prover proves, stating that the initial value of R20.2 satisfies the conjunction of those clauses under the semantics of R23.3. *Evidence: `proof-app-validators-<model>`, `proof-connection-obligations-<model>` (initial-value obligations), `mut-proof-connections`, `test-connection-obligations` (an Upward connection gives a Validation_Function and no Connection_Obligation).*
4. THE Contract_Translator SHALL state, under the semantics of R23.3, the conjunction of the GUMBO integration guarantee clauses of each out data or event data port of a thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor as a Creusot precondition of that port's Put Value in the Port_API (the putter precondition of HAMR's Microkit Rust API, `docs/hamr_study.md` section 6), as a postcondition of the developer `const fn` that gives an out data port's initial value (R20.2), and as part of the Resource invariant (IronOxide R46.4) of each Port_Cell of R10.3 or R10.4 that the port feeds, over every value the Port_Cell holds, which R21.5 has the App_Prover prove at every Criticality_Level, because the App_Prover's model of a Resource is its invariant alone (`Shared` in IronOxide `crates/rsk/src/lib.rs`) (DD candidate); and the conjunction of the integration assume clauses of each in data or event data port as a Creusot postcondition of that port's Get Value (the getter postcondition of HAMR's Microkit Rust API). *Evidence: `test-contracts-translation`, `proof-app-creusot-<model>`, `mut-proof-connections`.*
5. THE Proof_Assumption_Register SHALL hold one `[[step]]` table (R40.3) for each in port whose Get Value postcondition of criterion 4 the App_Prover does not prove from the Port_Glue's code, among them every in port whose values arrive through an Endpoint (R11, R12, R26.2), because IronOxide's `Endpoint::recv` carries no Creusot postcondition at PAR-01 (`crates/rsk/src/lib.rs`); the entry names the connection, the postcondition and the items that discharge it: for a connection that is not Upward, its Connection_Obligation (criterion 1), the source's Put Value precondition and initial-value postcondition (criterion 4), IronOxide's FIFO refinement and copying transfer of Endpoint messages (IronOxide R39.4, R42.2) and the Wire_Type round trip (R14.3); for an Upward connection, the Validation_Function and its proofs (R13.1, R13.2, R13.4, R13.7), the initial-value obligation of criterion 3, and IronOxide's delivery of accepted messages only (IronOxide R42.4), which IOP-09 would turn into a proof. *Evidence: `check-registers` (fails for an in port of this kind without an entry, or for an entry citing an item that does not resolve, R40.5).*
6. WHEN the Backend generates the MCU_Side Serial_Bridge (Phase P5, Requirement 35), THE Backend SHALL generate, for each Serial_Bridge out port that stands for an out port of the Peer_Processor (R34.1) and carries integration guarantee clauses attached by R34.3, a check of their conjunction under the semantics of R23.3 on each value that the Frame_Codec and the Wire_Type decoder accept (R35.4). The check runs before Put Value, discards a failing value without Put Value, and adds 1 to a count for that port kept in the Serial_Bridge's per-Task state, initially 0 and saturating at 2^32 − 1, so that the App_Prover proves the Put Value precondition of criterion 4 from the check and no proof relies on the Pi_Side's code (NG-04). *Evidence: `test-serial-guarantee-check`, `proof-app-creusot-robot`, `pbt-frame-garbage`, `hil-serial-faults`.*

**Table 24-1 — Where a destination port's integration assume clauses are discharged** (a summary of criteria 1 to 6 and the requirements they cite; it adds no obligation).

| Connection (destination port with integration assume clauses) | Discharged by | Not proven: recorded or residual | When discharge fails |
|---|---|---|---|
| Both threads in one Partition, through a Port_Cell (R10.3, R10.4) | Connection_Obligation (R24.1); the source's Put Value precondition, initial-value postcondition and Port_Cell Resource invariant (R24.4); the App_Prover proves the Get Value postcondition from these | none | the proof job fails (R24.2, R21.2) |
| Both threads in one Partition through an Endpoint (R26.2), or two Partitions with the source's Criticality_Level equal to or higher than the destination's (R11, R12) | Connection_Obligation (R24.1); the source's Put Value precondition and initial-value postcondition (R24.4); Wire_Type round trip (R14.3) | Get Value postcondition: Proof_Assumption_Register `[[step]]` entry citing IronOxide R39.4 and R42.2 (R24.5) | the proof job fails (R24.2); `check-registers` fails on a missing entry |
| Upward (R13) | each message: Validation_Function (R13.1, or R13.2 with R13.7; proven by R13.4); data port initial value: Creusot obligation (R24.3) | Get Value postcondition: Proof_Assumption_Register `[[step]]` entry citing IronOxide R42.4 (R24.5; IOP-09) | a rejected message is consumed and recorded as a fault of the sending Partition (R13.5, IronOxide R42.4); an undischarged goal fails the proof job (R21.2) |
| Source is an MCU_Side Serial_Bridge out port standing for a Peer_Processor port (R34.1, R34.3) | run-time check of the attached guarantee clauses (R24.6), then the Upward row above (R35.5 makes every Serial_Bridge delivery Upward) | the Pi_Side's guarantee is not relied upon (NG-04) | a failing value is discarded and counted (R24.6) |
| Destination on the Peer_Processor (R34.1) | Connection_Obligation from the MCU_Side source to the Serial_Bridge in port that carries the Pi_Side port's assume clauses (R34.3, R24.1) | after the serial link: ASM-06 and the Pi_Side's code; not claimed (NG-04) | the proof job fails (R24.2) |
| Event port (carries no value) | none: criteria 1 and 4 translate no integration clause on an event port, so R22.4 refuses it (MAP-30 (a); ORQ-46) | none | the model is refused |

### Part D — Timing

### Requirement 25: Timing in IronOxide's analysis (Phase P3)

**User Story:** As a timing analyst, I want the model's timing analyzed by IronOxide's Analyzer with measured execution times, so that the verdict applies to the generated binary.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN the Build_System has built a generated Application for an IronOxide_Target (R18.2, R18.6), THE Build_System SHALL run the Analyzer on the Task_Model that the IronOxide_Generator wrote for that build, with the build's Build_Manifest, one Kernel_Timing_Parameters set holding every parameter that the Analyzer requires (IronOxide R13.3), and one WCET_Record set holding a record for every Task entry and every Critical_Section site of the Task_Model (IronOxide R28.2), where each record and parameter is either measured on the build's binary or on a HIL build of the same source that the Build_Manifest lists (the build with the HIL_Rig's instrumentation, IronOxide R34.7; whether IronOxide's WCET_Harness and HIL_Rig harness can measure an Application outside IronOxide's repository without change is ORQ-47, IOP-10), or has the method `placeholder` (for a Task entry, a WCET equal to the Task's Budget in CPU cycles), so that the Analyzer rejects no input for a missing record (IronOxide R28.2, R13.3) or for a binary hash, clock or cache setting that differs from the Build_Manifest (IronOxide R28.3, R33.4). *Evidence: `analysis-rta-<model>` (for every corpus model and each IronOxide_Target it is built for, the Analyzer's report lists no rejection under IronOxide R28.2, R28.3 or R13.3).*
2. WHEN the WCET_Record set of an Analyzer run of criterion 1 holds a measured record (method `measured`) for the entry of a Task, THE Verdict_Report SHALL give for the thread of that Task the record's WCET W in CPU cycles (the maximum observed value multiplied by 1 plus the record's margin and rounded up to a whole cycle by the WCET_Harness, IronOxide R34.6; the Verdict_Reporter adds no margin of its own), the record's maximum observed value in CPU cycles, the upper bound U of the thread's `Timing_Properties::Compute_Execution_Time` in its AADL unit, the Task's Budget in CPU cycles as the Task_Model records it (R6.6), and W as a percentage of U rounded up to one decimal place, and SHALL report the thread as a model defect, naming its instance path, MAP-05, DEV-08 and IronOxide R28.4, exactly when W × 10^12 > U × f, with U in picoseconds and f the CPU clock in Hz that the Task_Model records, for every such thread and not only for the first Task that the Analyzer names under IronOxide R28.4. *Evidence: `analysis-rta-<model>-measured`, `test-verdict-report` (two threads above their upper bounds, both reported; a W equal to the Budget in cycles but above U, reported; a W equal to U, not reported).*
3. THE Backend SHALL generate the Port_Glue so that it enters Critical_Sections only on Port_Cells, each giving one Critical_Section site of the Task_Model per accessing Task (R10.6, R10.7; IronOxide R25.2, R25.3) that no developer code enters (R19.1), and performs Endpoint operations only on the roles that the Task_Model lists for its Task, at most the role's `ops` operations per Job (R11.9), which the Analyzer charges at the Kernel_Timing_Parameters Δ_send and Δ_recv (IronOxide R40.2), so that every Critical_Section and every Endpoint operation of the Port_Glue is covered by a WCET_Record that the Analyzer uses, and the record of each Port_Cell site measures Port_Glue code only. *Evidence: `check-generated-<model>` (every lock call of the generated Port_Glue names a Port_Cell; every Endpoint operation names a role of its Task), `hil-wcet-<model>`, `analysis-rta-<model>`.*
4. WHEN an Analyzer run of criterion 1 ends with the result FAIL or with a rejected input (IronOxide R30.4), THE Verdict_Report SHALL state: for each Task that the Analyzer reports not schedulable, the thread's instance path, its deadline in its AADL unit and in the CPU cycles that the Analyzer uses (IronOxide R29.8), the Analyzer's lower bound on the WCRT (IronOxide R29.7) and the excess of that bound over the deadline in CPU cycles, and the AADL element behind each term that the Analyzer reports for the Task (the thread's `Compute_Execution_Time` for C_i; for B_i, the data subcomponent, connection or Endpoint of the Critical_Section site or operation that the Analyzer names, or the Kernel_Timing_Parameter it names; for each interference term, the interfering thread); for a FAIL without fixed-point iteration (IronOxide R29.9), the utilization that the Analyzer reports; for each rejected input, the diagnostic of R3.4; and, as property changes, exactly those that the Analyzer's report states, adding none of its own. *Evidence: `test-verdict-report` (a FAIL by one Task's WCRT, a FAIL by utilization, a rejection under IronOxide R28.4).*
5.
