THE Build_System SHALL label each Analyzer run of criterion 1, and THE Verdict_Report SHALL state the same label, as timing evidence only when the IronOxide_Target is `nrf52840`, the Analyzer's report lists no input of method `placeholder`, every measured WCET_Record and Kernel_Timing_Parameter of the run states at least IronOxide PAR-02 (10,000) executions and a margin equal to PAR-11, and every measured Task-entry and Critical_Section-site record states a branch coverage at or above the threshold that IronOxide's Verification_Plan records for IronOxide R34.4; a timing-evidence label SHALL name the binary hash that the records carry and whether the Build_Manifest lists it as the Flight_Build or as a HIL build; and THE Build_System SHALL label every other run development evidence, listing each placeholder input and each record with the condition it fails. *Evidence: `analysis-rta-<model>`, `test-verdict-report` (one case per condition: a placeholder record, a record of 9,999 executions, a margin other than PAR-11, a branch coverage below the threshold, a `qemu_mps2_an386` Task_Model; and one run on a HIL build labelled with its hash).*
6. THE Backend and THE Verdict_Reporter SHALL leave every model source file and the AIR_Model byte-identical whatever the result of an Analyzer run, and THE Backend SHALL read no Analyzer report, WCET_Record or Verdict_Report, so that no period, MIT, offset, deadline, Budget or Priority of a generated App_Declaration depends on an analysis result. *Evidence: `test-analysis-leaves-model-unchanged` (the hashes of the model source files, the AIR_Model and the generated App_Declaration are equal before and after a failing analysis and a regeneration).*
7. THE Build_System SHALL report `analysis-rta-<model>-measured` as passing only when the Analyzer's result is PASS (exit status 0, IronOxide R30.4), criterion 5 labels the run timing evidence, and the Verdict_Report reports no model defect under criterion 2, and SHALL otherwise report it as failed, naming each unmet condition and each thread reported as a model defect. *Evidence: `analysis-rta-<model>-measured`, `test-verdict-report`.*

### Requirement 26: End-to-end flows to chains (Phase P3)

**User Story:** As a control engineer, I want the model's end-to-end flow latencies checked by IronOxide's chain analysis, so that the sensing-to-actuation path is bounded as specified.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN the Backend generates an Application from an AIR_Model that records an end-to-end flow with a `Communication_Properties::Latency` value whose elements, in flow order, are two or more threads bound to the IronOxide_Processor, optionally preceded by a device of MAP-20 whose out event port the flow's first connection joins to the first thread's Dispatch trigger (so that R15.2 releases the first thread's Task by the device's peripheral interrupt) and optionally followed by a device of MAP-20, THE Backend SHALL declare exactly one IronOxide Chain for the flow (`chain NAME { PART.TASK -> PART.TASK … } deadline = T;`, `crates/rsk-gen/src/syntax.rs`), whose NAME is derived from the flow's instance path by the sanitization rule of R8.6, whose Tasks are the Tasks of the flow's threads in flow order and no other, the devices excluded, and whose `deadline` is the upper bound of the Latency range written as MAP-04 states for every time value the Backend writes (R6.2), which the IronOxide_Generator converts to ticks rounding down (IronOxide R21.2); each connection of the flow between two consecutive threads is a hop of the flow (MAP-21). This mapping is kept although it fires only on an AIR_Model that records end-to-end flows, which no AIR_Model at PAR-02 does (criterion 7, ORQ-48) (UD-03). *Evidence: `test-map-21` (a flow that starts at a thread, one that starts at a device, one that ends at a device, each with same-Partition and cross-Partition hops), `proof-tv-core`, `pbt-accepted-models-pass-ironoxide`.*
2. WHEN a connection between two threads of one Partition is a hop of a flow for which criterion 1 declares a Chain, THE Backend SHALL map the connection to one plain Endpoint, a single Endpoint however many such flows the connection is a hop of, declared and used as Requirements 11 and 12 state for a connection between threads of two Partitions, instead of the Release_Signal or Port_Cell of Requirement 10, so that IronOxide R41.4 applies to the hop (`validate.rs`: every hop of a chain is an Endpoint; IOP-03), each such Endpoint counting toward the Endpoint limits of PAR-05 and PAR-06 that R17.1 and R17.3 check (MAP-21, MAP-26). *Evidence: `test-map-21`, `proof-tv-core`, `test-map-26`.*
3. IF a flow for which criterion 1 would declare a Chain has (a) a hop whose destination thread is Sporadic and whose Dispatch trigger is not the hop's destination port, so that the hop's Endpoint does not release the destination Task (R11.4), (b) a hop whose Sporadic destination thread the hop's Endpoint releases under `IronOxide::MIT_Policy => discard`, (c) a hop that joins the same two threads, in either direction, as another hop of the same or another such flow over a different connection, (d) a Latency upper bound of 0 ps, or (e) a hop whose source and destination are the same thread, whose plain Endpoint would bind both roles to one Task, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the flow with one diagnostic of R3.1 per case and hop, naming the flow's instance path, for (a) to (c) and (e) the hop's connection and destination thread, and the MAP row with the label and rule of the case: (a) MAP-21 (a) with IronOxide R41.4; (b) MAP-21 (b) with IronOxide R41.4; (c) MAP-21 (c) with Backend restriction (IOP-11); (d) MAP-21 (d) with IronOxide R41.4; (e) MAP-21 (e) with IronOxide R38.1 (`crates/rsk-gen/src/validate.rs`: two roles of one Endpoint bound to one Task). Whether a hop whose receiving role takes fewer messages per Job than the hop Endpoint's capacity must also be refused is ORQ-50. *Evidence: `mut-refusals` (at least one mutant per case), `pbt-backend-checks-match-generator`.*
4. IF the AIR_Model records an end-to-end flow with a `Communication_Properties::Latency` value that has at least one thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor and for which criterion 1 declares no Chain, THEN THE Verdict_Report SHALL list the flow as not analyzed, naming its instance path and each of these reasons that applies: (i) an element bound to a processor other than the IronOxide_Processor, naming each such processor (ORQ-19); (ii) an element other than a thread that is not a device of MAP-20 in the first or the last position, for example a device between two threads, naming the element; (iii) a first device whose out event port the flow's first connection does not join to the first thread's Dispatch trigger, naming the device and the port; (iv) fewer than two threads, because an IronOxide Chain names at least two Tasks (IronOxide R41.4, `validate.rs`). *Evidence: `test-verdict-report` (at least one model per reason).*
5. WHEN the Verdict_Report lists an end-to-end flow whose `Communication_Properties::Latency` lower bound is greater than 0 ps, THE Verdict_Report SHALL state in that flow's entry the lower bound, in the unit of its AADL value, and that no analysis, test or check of this project verifies that the flow's latency is at least that bound. *Evidence: `test-verdict-report`.*
6. WHEN the Backend declares a Chain, THE Backend SHALL declare the Endpoint of each hop of the Chain before every other Endpoint of the Application whose two roles are bound to that hop's two Tasks, because the IronOxide_Generator takes as a hop's Endpoint the first Endpoint in declaration order whose roles are bound to both Tasks, in either direction (`crates/rsk-gen/src/derive.rs`, `validate.rs`), so that the Task_Model records for each hop (`chains[].hops[].endpoint`) the Endpoint of the hop's connection. *Evidence: `test-map-21` (the hop's Tasks also joined by a second connection in the same direction and by a connection in the reverse direction), `proof-tv-core`, `check-generated-<model>`.*
7. WHEN the Verdict_Reporter writes a Verdict_Report, THE Verdict_Report SHALL state the number of end-to-end flows with a `Communication_Properties::Latency` value that the AIR_Model records and that have at least one thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor, 0 included, together with the statement that an end-to-end flow that the AIR_Model does not record is not analyzed (HAMR's AIR at PAR-02 records flow specifications of kind Source, Sink and Path and no end-to-end flow, `hamr/air/shared/src/main/scala/org/sireum/hamr/ir/AadlAST.scala`; ORQ-48; flow specifications are not carried, MAP-31). Until ORQ-48 is resolved, the number is 0 for every AIR_Model at PAR-02, and the Robot_Model's stop bound PAR-15 is shown by the bound of R36.9 and the measurement of R37.6, not by a Chain (UD-03). *Evidence: `test-verdict-report` (including a committed AIR_Model that has flow specifications and no end-to-end flow, for which the number is 0).*
8. WHEN the Verdict_Report gives the Chain bound and verdict of a flow (R27.1), THE Verdict_Report SHALL state in the flow's entry the interval that the bound covers, from the Release of the first Task's Job, or from the interrupt event where a peripheral interrupt releases the first Task (IronOxide's Δ_stamp, IronOxide R41.4), to the completion of the last Task's Job, and the terms of the bound as the Analyzer reports them (R27.2): Δ_stamp where it applies, each Task's WCRT, and for each Task after the first its period (Periodic_Task) or MIT (Sporadic_Task under `defer`) (`crates/rsk-analyzer/src/lib.rs`; ORQ-49). *Evidence: `test-verdict-report`, `analysis-rta-<model>`.*

### Requirement 27: Verdicts reported against the model (Phase P3)

**User Story:** As a system modeler, I want the Analyzer's verdict expressed in terms of my threads and flows, so that I can act on it in the model.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN an Analyzer run of R25.1 on a generated Application ends with any exit status, or a QEMU or HIL_Rig run of that Application ends (R11.10, R12.8), THE Verdict_Reporter SHALL write one Verdict_Report for the Application, as one JSON document that states the schema identifier `hamr-rsk-verdict/1` and conforms to the schema that this repository publishes under that identifier, and as text, the two forms giving the same entries in the same order, containing: the overall result of the Application's latest Analyzer run as its report states it (PASS, FAIL or REJECTED, or "no report" in the cases of criterion 6), that run's exit status (IronOxide R30.4: 0 PASS, 1 FAIL, 2 rejected input, 3 internal error) and the utilization Σ E_j/T_j in percent that its report gives; the label "development evidence" where R25.1 labels the run so; one thread entry for each thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor (criteria 4 and 6); one flow entry for each end-to-end flow that has `Communication_Properties::Latency` and passes through at least one such thread, and for each flow that R26.4 lists (criteria 5 and 6, R26.4, R26.5); the entries of R6.9, R25.2 and R25.4; and the refused-send counts of R11.10 and R12.8, each with the kind of run (QEMU or HIL_Rig), the run's Evidence_Item and the hash of the run's binary, marked as outside the analysis where the analysed Build_Manifest does not record that hash; the thread entries and the flow entries each being ordered by instance path, compared segment by segment by Unicode code point. Whether the Verdict_Reporter also gives the Verdict_Report as Sireum `Message`s, for markers in OSATE or FMIDE, is ORQ-77. *Evidence: `test-verdict-schema` (for every model of the corpus, and for Analyzer runs ending with each exit status 0 to 3, the JSON form conforms to `hamr-rsk-verdict/1`, the text form gives the same entries in the same order, and the entries correspond one to one to the threads and flows that the criterion names), `test-verdict-report`, `analysis-rta-<model>`.*
2. THE Verdict_Reporter SHALL give every result, verdict, response time, lower bound, term, slack, Chain bound, utilization and rejection in a Verdict_Report exactly as the report that the Analyzer wrote in that run (`rsk-analysis-report/1`, IronOxide R30) gives it, with the meaning that schema gives each field, using no report left by an earlier run and computing no timing value or verdict of its own except the picosecond equivalents of criteria 4 and 5 and the values that R6.9 and R25.2 define, each of which the Verdict_Report marks as computed by the Verdict_Reporter; and SHALL state the Analyzer version, the Profile version and Profile_Variant, the identities that the Analyzer's report records for the Task_Model, WCET_Records, Kernel_Timing_Parameters and Build_Manifest (IronOxide R30.2), and the binary hashes that the Build_Manifest records. *Evidence: `pbt-verdict-copy` (over PAR-08 Model_Generator models per run, every value and verdict of every entry equals the corresponding field of the Analyzer's `rsk-analysis-report/1` document of the same run, and every picosecond value equals the cycle value × 10^12 / f_cpu of criterion 4), `test-verdict-report`.*
3. THE Verdict_Reporter SHALL state in every Verdict_Report the limits of IronOxide's verdict at PAR-01 (`docs/hamr_study.md` section 11): the WCET_Records and Kernel_Timing_Parameters are measured maxima increased by the margin of IronOxide R34.6 (PAR-11 for WCETs), not static bounds; L_kernel is a measured masked span, not a static bound; and the Analyzer's fixed point and term derivation are proven in Verus while its per-Task composition and verdict are plain Rust checked by tests against a reference implementation, so that the analysis is not proven sound against the Kernel's schedules (IronOxide Verification_Plan 9.5); and SHALL reproduce unchanged every statement, every omitted or approximated term, and the UPPAAL cross-check status and detail that the Analyzer's report gives (IronOxide R18.9, R27.5, R30.1, R31.2, R31.4). *Evidence: `test-verdict-report` (every statement and omitted or approximated term of the Analyzer's report appears unchanged, including the placeholder statement of a run with placeholder WCET_Records).*
4. WHEN the Analyzer's report gives a response time for the Task that the generation report of R4.7 lists for a thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor (R_i for a Task it reports schedulable, or the lower bound of IronOxide R29.7 for a Task it reports not schedulable), THE Verdict_Reporter SHALL give in that thread's entry: the thread's instance path and source position as R3.1 defines them (R3.7 where the AIR_Model records none); the Task's Task_Model identifier, Partition, Criticality_Level and Priority as the Analyzer's report gives them; the model value that the verdict is compared against, namely `Timing_Properties::Deadline` or, where that property is absent, `Timing_Properties::Period` (ASM-03), naming the property and giving its AADL value in picoseconds and as written in the App_Declaration (R6.2); the Analyzer's D_i (that value after the conversions of IronOxide R21.2 and R29.8), C_i, B_i with the item that the report names as determining it (a Critical_Section site, an Endpoint operation, L_kernel, or the Partition stop and restart work of IronOxide R14.10) and, where the generation report lists that site's Resource or Endpoint, the instance path of its AADL element, O_i, J_i, and each interference term with the instance path of the interfering Task's thread; R_i, labelled as the WCRT for a schedulable Task and as a lower bound on the WCRT for a not-schedulable Task; the slack D_i − R_i for a schedulable Task and no slack for a not-schedulable Task (the report's 0 meaning none); and the verdict "schedulable" or "not schedulable"; each Analyzer value being given in CPU cycles as the report gives it and in picoseconds as the cycle count × 10^12 / f_cpu, where f_cpu is the CPU clock rate that the Task_Model records, which is exact at both IronOxide_Targets (15,625 ps per cycle at 64 MHz on `nrf52840`; 40,000 ps per cycle at 25 MHz on `qemu_mps2_an386`). *Evidence: `test-verdict-report` (models with a schedulable Task, a not-schedulable Task, a thread without `Timing_Properties::Deadline`, and a Deadline that IronOxide R21.2 rounds down), `pbt-verdict-copy`.*
5. WHEN the Analyzer's report gives a result for the Chain that the generation report of R4.7 lists for an end-to-end flow (MAP-21, R26.1), THE Verdict_Reporter SHALL give in that flow's entry: the flow's instance path and source position as R3.1 defines them (R3.7 where the AIR_Model records none); the Chain's identifier and its Tasks in flow order, each with the instance path of its thread; the model value that the verdict is compared against, namely the upper bound of the flow's `Communication_Properties::Latency` in picoseconds and as written in the App_Declaration (R6.2), and the Chain deadline as the Analyzer's report gives it; each term of the Chain bound as the report names it; the Chain bound, labelled as a lower bound where the report gives a Task of the Chain as not schedulable, or, for the result UNBOUNDED, no bound (the report's 2^64 − 1 cycles meaning none) together with the reason term that the report gives; and the Analyzer's result PASS, FAIL or UNBOUNDED for the Chain; each Analyzer value being given in CPU cycles and in picoseconds as criterion 4 states. *Evidence: `test-verdict-report` (Chains with the results PASS, FAIL and UNBOUNDED, a Chain that contains a not-schedulable Task, and a Chain that starts at a device's out event port), `pbt-verdict-copy`, `test-map-21`.*
6. IF an Analyzer run gives no response time for the Task of a thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor, because the Analyzer wrote no report in that run (exit status 3; exit status 2 after a schema rejection under IronOxide R28.1, whose messages it writes to standard error only; or an exit status outside 0 to 3), its report's result is REJECTED, its report states that Σ E_j/T_j exceeds 1 (IronOxide R29.9) or an arithmetic overflow for that Task (IronOxide R29.6), or its report gives no result for that Task (ORQ-25; IOP-12 proposes a structured per-Task status), THEN THE Verdict_Reporter SHALL give that thread's entry, and the entry of each flow whose Chain contains the Task, the verdict "no verdict" with the cause, the exit status, and every rejection message of the report or of standard error unchanged, and SHALL give in those entries no response time, lower bound, slack or Chain bound, in particular not the 0 or 2^64 − 1 cycles that the report records in these cases. *Evidence: `test-verdict-no-result` (one Analyzer run per cause: a schema rejection, a missing WCET_Record (IronOxide R28.2), a WCET above its Budget (IronOxide R28.4), utilization above 1, an arithmetic overflow, and an internal error, each with the report of an earlier run left in the Analyzer's output directory).*

### Part E — Translation Validation

### Requirement 28: The Semantic_Model (Phase P1 format; P4 independence)

**User Story:** As a verification engineer, I want a semantic model of the AADL input extracted independently of the Backend, so that the TV_Checker compares the output with the input rather than with the Backend's own view of it.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN the Semantic_Model_Extractor is run on an AIR_Model file for which none of the conditions of R28.7 holds, THE Semantic_Model_Extractor SHALL write one Semantic_Model. The Semantic_Model is a UTF-8 JSON document that states the schema identifier `hamr-rsk-semantic-model/1` and validates against the JSON schema that this repository publishes under that identifier. It states the SHA-256 digest of the AIR_Model file's bytes as 64 lowercase hexadecimal digits (as R1.5 writes it) and the 40-digit commit hashes of both revisions of PAR-02. It records each element that Table 28-1 selects exactly once, with the items that the element's Table 28-1 row lists, its instance path (the segments of its name in the AIR_Model, R3.1) and its source position (R3.1, or the statement of R3.7 where the AIR_Model records none). Each property that the row names is recorded with its decoded value or as absent, and no default value of AADL (ASM-03), of Table 2-1 or of HAMR is applied. *Evidence: `test-semantic-model` (at least one AIR_Model per row of Table 28-1, each compared with a hand-written expected Semantic_Model, plus the committed AADL and SysML v2 AIR JSON files of `isolette`), `pbt-semantic-model-complete` (over PAR-08 Model_Generator AIR_Models per run: every element that Table 28-1 selects appears in exactly one row, exactly once, and no other element appears), `check-semantic-model-schema` (every Semantic_Model that the Build_System writes validates against the published schema).*
2. THE Semantic_Model_Extractor SHALL be independent of the Backend. Independence means three things. (a) No source file of the Semantic_Model_Extractor belongs to a module of the Backend, the Contract_Translator and the Verdict_Reporter included. (b) The Semantic_Model_Extractor depends, directly or transitively, on no Backend module and on no part of HAMR other than the AIR data types and AIR JSON reader of `hamr/air` at PAR-02 (`org.sireum.hamr.ir`, `JSON.toAadl`) and the Sireum libraries those need (the Slang runtime and the Slang AST of GUMBO expressions). In particular it does not depend on `hamr/codegen`'s symbol resolution (`common/symbols`), type resolution (`common/types`), GUMBO resolver (`common/resolvers`) or property utilities (`common/properties`, `PropertyUtil.toPicoseconds` included). (c) No Backend module depends on a module of the Semantic_Model_Extractor or reads a Semantic_Model. As a result, the Semantic_Model_Extractor and the Backend share only HAMR's AIR reader and the Semantic_Model schema, and no function or data type that selects the elements of R1.2, applies a default, decides a refusal, derives a name (R8.6) or assigns a Priority is common to both. *Evidence: `check-extractor-independence` (the Build_System computes each module's dependency closure from the build files and from the imports of every source file, and fails, naming the module and the dependency, on any dependency outside (b) or that (c) excludes), `review-extractor-independence` (at each Phase checkpoint from P4, recording the revision, the reviewer, the `check-extractor-independence` report and, for each row of Table 28-1, that the Semantic_Model_Extractor reads the row's items from the AIR data types only, applies no default and contains no code copied from a Backend module; ORQ-18), `diff-semantic-model-hamr` (for every corpus model that HAMR's symbol resolution at PAR-02 accepts, wherever the AIR_Model gives the property: the threads with their `Thread_Properties::Dispatch_Protocol`, their Period and `Compute_Execution_Time` in picoseconds, their ports with category and direction, the connection instances with their ends, and the processor bindings of the processes are equal in the Semantic_Model and in HAMR's symbol table, `AadlSymbols.scala` `AadlThread.dispatchProtocol`, `periodPs` and `computeExecutionTimePs`).*
3. THE Semantic_Model_Extractor SHALL record each time value as an integer number of picoseconds, with three items kept beside it: the value string as the AIR_Model records it, the unit as the AIR_Model records it, and an exactness mark. A time value is a value string with a unit (a `UnitProp`, alone or as a bound of a `RangeProp`) whose unit is `ps`, `ns`, `us`, `ms`, `sec`, `min` or `hr`. The integer is computed as follows:
   - read the value string as an exact decimal number: an optional sign, digits with an optional fraction, and an optional exponent `E` or `e` with an optional sign (for example `1.0E12`, `1.0000E+12`, `100`);
   - multiply it by 1, 10^3, 10^6, 10^9, 10^12, 6 × 10^13 or 3.6 × 10^15 for the respective unit;
   - round it to the nearest integer, rounding a value exactly halfway between two integers away from zero.

   The integer is not limited to 64 bits, because an `IronOxide::Operating_Duration` allowed by Table 2-1 can exceed 2^64 ps. Its only limit is the range that the schema states for time values (text before Table 28-1), and a value outside that range is a condition of R28.7. The exactness mark is one of three values:
   - `exact` where the exact product is an integer;
   - `noise` where the product differs from the recorded integer by at most its own magnitude divided by 2^50 (the bound below which HAMR's `PropertyUtil.toPicoseconds` at PAR-02 treats the difference as OSATE's floating-point scaling);
   - `fractional` otherwise.

   *Evidence: `test-semantic-model-times` (at least: `1.0E12` ps and `1.0000E+12` ps each as 1,000,000,000,000 marked `exact`; `3.3299999999999996E10` ps as 33,300,000,000 marked `noise`; `1.4` ps as 1 and `2.5` ps as 3, both marked `fractional`; `100` us as 100,000,000 marked `exact`; and a value of 2^70 ps), `diff-semantic-model-hamr`.*
4. THE TV_Checker SHALL parse, through its Semantic_Model reader (R29.6), the Semantic_Model_Extractor's printing of every Semantic_Model m that validates against the schema of R28.1 into a value equal to m, field by field and with every list in the same order (round trip). *Evidence: `pbt-semantic-model-round-trip` (PAR-08 Semantic_Models per run, generated from a recorded seed so that every row of Table 28-1 and every exactness mark of R28.3 occurs, including the integers 0, 2^53 + 1, 2^64 and 2^80, negative integers, strings with non-ASCII characters and with characters that JSON escapes, and empty lists).*
5. IF the JSON schema that this repository publishes under an identifier `hamr-rsk-semantic-model/n`, or Table 28-1 as this document states it for version n, differs from the text whose SHA-256 digest the Verification_Plan's change log (R41.2) records for identifier n, THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail the build naming the identifier and the file that differs. Every change to the schema or to Table 28-1 is therefore published under identifier n + 1, with its own change-log entry stating the change and its rationale, and no identifier is reused. *Evidence: `check-semantic-model-version` (an edited schema file under an unchanged identifier fails the build, as does an edited Table 28-1 under an unchanged identifier; the same edits under identifier n + 1 with a change-log entry pass).*
6. THE Semantic_Model_Extractor SHALL write each Semantic_Model as a function of the bytes of the AIR_Model file alone. The output is byte-identical for byte-identical input whatever the working directory, the input and output file paths, the environment variables, the locale, the date and time, and the number of earlier runs. The output is ordered as follows:
   - the elements of each row of Table 28-1 by instance path, compared as R7.5 compares instance paths;
   - the GUMBO clauses of row SM-10 by owner, Table 22-1 row, handler port and identifier, each compared by Unicode code points;
   - the properties of an element by qualified name;
   - the values of a property and the fields of a data component classifier in the order that the AIR_Model records them.

   Permuting the order in which the AIR_Model lists components (except the fields of a data component classifier), features, connection instances and properties therefore leaves the Semantic_Model byte-identical. *Evidence: `test-semantic-model-deterministic` (two runs on one AIR_Model from different working directories, input paths, environment variables and locales, compared byte for byte), `pbt-semantic-model-order-invariance` (PAR-08 Model_Generator AIR_Models per run, each with a random permutation drawn from a recorded seed).*
7. IF any of the following conditions holds, THEN THE Semantic_Model_Extractor SHALL write no Semantic_Model, end with a non-zero exit status, and report one diagnostic per occurrence. The diagnostic names the AIR_Model file and the condition and, where the condition concerns an element, the element's instance path and source position (R3.1, R3.7). The conditions are:
   - HAMR's AIR reader at PAR-02 does not read the AIR_Model file without error;
   - the AIR_Model has no processor, or more than one, whose `IronOxide::Target` value is an IronOxide_Target;
   - the AIR_Model gives a time value (R28.3), a size value or a property value that Table 28-1 decodes in a form that does not decode into the stated type, for example a value string that is not a decimal number, a time property named in Table 28-1 with no unit or with a unit outside those of R28.3, an integer property whose value is not an integer, or a reference that names no element of the AIR_Model;
   - a decoded integer, a time value in picoseconds or a size value in bits lies outside the range that the schema states for its field (text before Table 28-1);
   - two GUMBO clauses have the same identity (Table 28-1 row SM-10);
   - an integration clause's expression names no port, or more than one port, of its component (HAMR's rule at PAR-02, `GclResolver.scala`).

   *Evidence: `test-semantic-model-errors` (one AIR_Model per condition, each checked for the absence of an output file, the exit status and the diagnostic).*

**Table 28-1 — Contents of a Semantic_Model** (schema `hamr-rsk-semantic-model/1`; under R28.5, a change to this table requires a new schema identifier).

Terms used in the table:

- The IronOxide_Processor is the one processor whose `IronOxide::Target` value is an IronOxide_Target (R28.7).
- An in-scope process is a process whose `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Processor_Binding` names the IronOxide_Processor (R1.2).

For each element that a row selects, the Semantic_Model records:

- its instance path and source position (R28.1);
- its AIR category;
- every property that the AIR_Model lists for the element, with:
  - its qualified name, which is the last segment of the AIR property name (the AADL Front_End prefixes the instance path, and HAMR's SysML v2 front end does not);
  - its `appliesTo` references;
  - its values as the AIR_Model records them, with every time value also recorded as R28.3 states and every size value also as an integer number of bits.

A size value is converted by R28.3's decimal reading, rounding and exactness mark, with these factors: 1 for `bits`; 8 for `Bytes`; 8 × 10^3n for `KByte`, `MByte`, `GByte` and `TByte`; and 8 × 2^10n for `KiByte`, `MiByte`, `GiByte` and `TiByte`, where n = 1 to 4. These are the size units of HAMR's `PropertyUtil.scala` at PAR-02.

The schema states a minimum and a maximum for every integer field, and the TV_Checker's reader rejects a value outside them (R29.9). The range of every time field (in picoseconds) and every size field (in bits) contains at least −2^80 to 2^80. A picosecond value can exceed 2^64: the largest `IronOxide::Operating_Duration` that Table 2-1 allows (fewer than 2^62 ticks) lies between 2^77 and 2^78 ps on either IronOxide_Target, and R28.4's round trip uses 2^80. design.md states each range (DD candidate).

For each property that a "Recorded" cell names, the Semantic_Model also records the value decoded into the type in parentheses, or "absent" where the AIR_Model lists no value. No row applies a default. The "Needed for" column names the R29.2 conjuncts and Table M-1 rows that the Mapping_Relation checks with the row.

| Row | Selected elements | Recorded | Needed for |
|---|---|---|---|
| SM-01 | Every processor and virtual processor of the AIR_Model, and every bus that a `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Connection_Binding` of an SM-05 connection instance names | For a processor, every processor property of Table 2-1 (the header properties that the Header conjunct of R29.2 reads): `IronOxide::Target` and `IronOxide::Safe_State` (strings), `IronOxide::Operating_Duration` (time), `IronOxide::Log_Capacity` (integer), `IronOxide::FP_Flush_To_Zero` (Boolean). For a virtual processor: the instance path of the processor that contains it, and `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Processor_Binding` (instance paths). For a bus: `IronOxide::Serial_Link` (record of an integer, an integer and a string) | The Application header (the Feeds cells of Table 2-1); MAP-08, MAP-27; R1.3 |
| SM-02 | Every process of the AIR_Model | `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Processor_Binding` (instance paths). For an in-scope process also: `IronOxide::Criticality_Level`, `IronOxide::Fault_Response`, `IronOxide::Overrun_Response` and `IronOxide::Deadline_Miss_Response` (enumeration literals); `IronOxide::MIT_Violation_Threshold` (integer); `IronOxide::Peripherals` (list of strings); `Memory_Properties::Code_Size`, `Memory_Properties::Data_Size` and `Memory_Properties::Stack_Size` (sizes); and the instance paths of its thread groups at every depth | The bijection between processes and Partitions, and each Partition's level, responses, threshold, sizes and peripherals (R29.2); MAP-07, MAP-09, MAP-20, MAP-27; R1.7, R1.8 |
| SM-03 | Every thread inside an in-scope process, directly or in thread groups at any depth | The process and the thread groups that contain it; `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Processor_Binding` (instance paths); `Thread_Properties::Dispatch_Protocol` (enumeration literal); `Thread_Properties::Dispatch_Trigger` (names of the thread's ports); `Timing_Properties::Period`, `Timing_Properties::Deadline` and `Timing_Properties::Dispatch_Offset` (times); `Timing_Properties::Compute_Execution_Time` (range of times); `Thread_Properties::Priority` (integer); `IronOxide::MIT_Policy` (enumeration literal); `IronOxide::Uses_FPU` (Boolean); `IronOxide::Serial_Bridge_Role` (enumeration literal: `Receive`, `Send` or `Receive_And_Send`) | The bijection between threads and Tasks, and each Task's release kind, Release_Source (its Dispatch trigger), period or MIT, offset, deadline, Budget, Priority (MAP-06) and FPU flag (R29.2); the declared Serial_Bridge threads of the Serial_Bridge conjunct (R29.2, R34.8); MAP-01 to MAP-06, MAP-24 (h), (i), MAP-25, MAP-27, MAP-28; R1.8 |
| SM-04 | Every feature of an SM-03 thread or an SM-07 device whose AIR category is `DataPort`, `EventPort` or `EventDataPort`, in every direction, `InOut` included | The owning thread or device; category; direction; the data classifier name, or none; `Communication_Properties::Queue_Size` and `Thread_Properties::Dequeued_Items` (integers); `Communication_Properties::Overflow_Handling_Protocol`, `Thread_Properties::Dequeue_Protocol` and `Communication_Properties::Queue_Processing_Protocol` (enumeration literals); `IronOxide::Validation_Function` (string); the identities (SM-10) of the GUMBO integration clauses that name the port | Each connection's Release_Signal, Port_Cell or Endpoint with its capacity, `ops` and Validation_Function (R29.2); MAP-11 to MAP-19, MAP-24 (a), (g) |
| SM-05 | Every connection instance (AIR `ConnectionInstance`) one of whose ends is a feature of an in-scope process or of a component inside one, including each cross-processor port connection instance (R34.1) that has one end at a thread of an in-scope process, which R34.5 relates to Task_Model items | The AIR kind (`Port`, `Access`, `Feature`, `FeatureGroup`, `Parameter` or `ModeTransition`); the source and destination ends (component instance path, feature name, direction). For an end at a component that is not inside an in-scope process: that component's category and instance path, and the `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Processor_Binding` of that component or of the process that contains it. Also `Communication_Properties::Timing` (enumeration literal), `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Connection_Binding` (instance paths) and `Communication_Properties::Latency` (range of times) | Each connection's Release_Signal, Port_Cell or Endpoint with its roles (R29.2); the Serial_Bridge substitutions (R34.5); MAP-10 to MAP-18, MAP-20, MAP-24 (d), (e), (f), MAP-27; R1.4 |
| SM-06 | Every data subcomponent of an in-scope process or of its thread groups, and every data access feature of an SM-03 thread | The classifier name; `Thread_Properties::Concurrency_Control_Protocol` (enumeration literal); `Data_Model::Initial_Value` (as the AIR_Model records it). For a data access feature: the access type (`Provides` or `Requires`) and `Memory_Properties::Access_Right` (enumeration literal). The SM-05 connection instances of kind `Access` that join them; whether the AIR_Model carries these is ORQ-03 | Each shared data component's Resource and accessors (R29.2); MAP-10 |
| SM-07 | Every device at an end of an SM-05 connection instance | `IronOxide::Peripheral` (string); `Thread_Properties::Dispatch_Protocol` (enumeration literal); `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Processor_Binding` (instance paths) | Each Partition's peripherals and each interrupt Release_Source (R29.2); MAP-20 |
| SM-08 | Every data component classifier that an SM-04 port, an SM-06 element or a GUMBO state variable (SM-10) names and, transitively, the classifiers of its fields and array elements, as the AIR_Model's data components record them | The classifier name; its fields (name and classifier name, in AIR order); its properties as the text before this table states; whether it is a floating-point type as the `Uses_FPU` row of Table 2-1 defines one (Boolean; how the AIR_Model records that a data type extends a floating-point base type is ORQ-78) | Wire_Types, payload sizes and FPU use (R29.2); MAP-19, MAP-28 |
| SM-09 | Every flow specification (AIR `Flow`, of kind `Source`, `Sink` or `Path`) of an SM-03 thread, an SM-07 device or an in-scope process | Kind; source and sink feature names. No end-to-end flow is recorded, because AIR at PAR-02 has no data type for one (`hamr/air` `AadlAST.scala`; R26.7, ORQ-48). Under this schema version no Chain is therefore the image of a model element. Recording end-to-end flows with their `Communication_Properties::Latency` requires a new schema identifier (R28.5) | Each end-to-end flow's Chain and deadline (R29.2); MAP-21. The flow specifications themselves are not carried (MAP-31, R3.5), and no conjunct reads them |
| SM-10 | Every GUMBO subclause (`GclSubclause`) annexed to an SM-03 thread or to an SM-08 classifier | Its state variables (name and classifier name). For each clause: its identity, made of the owner's instance path or classifier name, the clause's Table 22-1 row, the handler's port for a handler clause, and the clause identifier (HAMR's resolver checks identifiers for uniqueness within a section only, `GclResolver.scala`); its source position; and, for an integration clause, the SM-04 port that its expression names | MAP-18 (a) (whether the destination port has an integration assume clause), MAP-29, MAP-30; R22.8 |
| SM-11 | Every other element: each subcomponent, feature, mode and annex subclause of an in-scope process or of a component inside one, each feature of an SM-07 device, and each mode of a component that contains an in-scope process, that rows SM-01 to SM-10 do not record | Its AIR category: the component category; the feature category and direction; `Mode`; or the annex name of an annex subclause other than GUMBO | The refusals of MAP-08, MAP-22, MAP-23 and MAP-24 (b), (c), (j); no Task_Model item is its image (R29.2) |

### Requirement 29: The translation-validation checker (Phase P4)

**User Story:** As a certification engineer, I want every generated configuration checked against the model by a proven checker, so that HAMR's and the Backend's code need not be trusted for the configuration.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE TV_Checker's core, written in Verus inside `verus!`, SHALL take the values that the readers of criterion 6 build from a Semantic_Model and from the Task_Model that the IronOxide_Generator wrote for the generated App_Declaration, SHALL return either Accept or Reject, and SHALL return Accept only if the Mapping_Relation holds between the views of those values (soundness). This implication is a postcondition of the core's entry function, and Verus proves it under criterion 3. *Evidence: `proof-tv-core`, `mut-proof-tv`, `test-tv-accepts-<model>`.*
2. THE Mapping_Relation SHALL be stated as Verus specification functions over the views of both models, with every time and frequency value a mathematical integer (`int`), SHALL read no source position and no file URI that the Semantic_Model records, and SHALL require at least the following. Each item below is stated as one or more conjuncts. A conjunct is a named Verus specification function that the Mapping_Relation's top-level specification function conjoins directly; criterion 10 gives each conjunct its identifier, criterion 4 reports mismatches by it, R30.1 plants `task-model` mutants against each, and row PM-01 of Table 33-1 (R33.5) derives its proof-mutation conjuncts from the conjuncts' bodies.
   - **Correspondence.** A bijection between the processes of R1.2 and the Task_Model's `partitions`, and between their threads and its `tasks`. Each pair is related by the naming rule of R8.6, stated as a Verus specification function and not taken from the generation report of R4.7 (MAP-07, R8.2). Whether that function can be stated over `String` in pinned Verus, or needs code-point sequences that the readers of criterion 6 build, is ORQ-72.
   - **Header.**
     - `target` names the IronOxide_Target that the IronOxide_Processor's `IronOxide::Target` gives.
     - `clock.cpu_hz` and `clock.tick_hz` equal the rates that R6.7 states for that IronOxide_Target: 64,000,000 Hz and 16,000,000 Hz for `nrf52840`; 25,000,000 Hz and 25,000,000 Hz for `qemu_mps2_an386`. The relation holds these rates as constants and does not read them from the Task_Model.
     - `operating_duration_ticks` = ⌊O × f_tick / 10^12⌋, with O the `IronOxide::Operating_Duration`.
     - `safe_state`, `fp_flush_to_zero` and `capacities.event_log` equal `IronOxide::Safe_State`, `IronOxide::FP_Flush_To_Zero` and `IronOxide::Log_Capacity`, with the defaults of Table 2-1 (MAP-27).
   - **Task timing.** f_tick and f_cpu are `clock.tick_hz` and `clock.cpu_hz` in Hz. Every model value is in picoseconds as the Semantic_Model records it (R28.3). Each conversion follows IronOxide R21.2 as `crates/rsk-gen/src/convert.rs` and `derive.rs` implement it at PAR-01:
     - `kind` is periodic or sporadic, as MAP-01 or MAP-02 gives.
     - `period_ticks` (Periodic_Task) or `mit_ticks` (Sporadic_Task) = ⌊T × f_tick / 10^12⌋, with T the Period.
     - `offset_ticks` = ⌊(2 × S × f_tick + 10^12) / (2 × 10^12)⌋, with S the `Dispatch_Offset`: rounded to the nearest tick, a value exactly halfway rounded up. It is 0 when the property is absent.
     - `deadline_ticks` = ⌊D × f_tick / 10^12⌋, with D the Deadline, or the Period where Deadline is absent.
     - `budget_cycles` = ⌈C × f_cpu / 10^12⌉, with C the upper bound of `Compute_Execution_Time`.

     (MAP-01, MAP-02, MAP-04, MAP-05, R6.1.)
   - **Task attributes.**
     - `partition` is the image of the thread's process.
     - `priority` is the value that R7.1 or R7.2 assigns (MAP-06).
     - `mit_policy` is `IronOxide::MIT_Policy`, or defer when absent (MAP-02).
     - `source` is the Release_Source that MAP-02 gives: the Release_Signal of MAP-11 or MAP-13, the receiving role of MAP-15, or the interrupt of the MAP-20 device's peripheral.
     - `fpu` is `IronOxide::Uses_FPU`, with the default of Table 2-1 (MAP-28).
   - **Partitions.**
     - `level`, `fault`, `overrun`, `deadline_miss` and `mit_threshold` are as R8.1 states (MAP-09).
     - `regions.code`, `regions.ram` and `regions.stack` are the byte sizes of R2.2 (MAP-07).
     - `peripherals` is exactly the process's `IronOxide::Peripherals` together with the peripheral of each MAP-20 device connected to its threads (R15.1, MAP-20).
   - **Shared data.** For each data subcomponent of MAP-10, exactly one Resource in its process's Partition, whose `accessors` are exactly the Tasks of the threads that access it.
   - **Connections.** For each port connection, exactly the items of the one row among MAP-11 to MAP-16 and MAP-21 that maps it:
     - a Release_Signal in `signals`, with `bound_task` the destination Task and `raised_by` exactly the source Task; or
     - a Port_Cell Resource whose `accessors` are exactly the source and destination Tasks; or
     - a plain Endpoint with exactly two roles, bound to the source Task and the destination Task, where:
       - `capacity` and the receiving role's `ops` are as MAP-15 or R12.6 gives, and the sending role's `ops` is 1;
       - `release` is true on the receiving role exactly when the destination port is the destination thread's Dispatch trigger, and false on the sending role;
       - a role has a `validate` entry exactly when R13.1, R13.2 or R13.6 declares one (MAP-18). The relation follows Requirement 13; R2.9 refuses `IronOxide::Validation_Function` on every port that is not the destination of an Upward connection (UD-29), and R13.2 gives every port that keeps the property a `validate` entry;
       - for a MAP-16 Endpoint, `capacity` and the receiving role's `ops` equal C_sample. C_sample is computed by the Verus specification function over which `proof-tv-sampling-capacity` is stated (R12.2), from the two Tasks' `period_ticks` or `mit_ticks`, `deadline_ticks` and `offset_ticks`.
   - **Chains.** For each end-to-end flow for which R26.1 declares a Chain, exactly one element of `chains`, where:
     - the `hops` join the flow's Tasks in flow order;
     - each hop's `endpoint` is the Endpoint of that hop's connection (R26.6);
     - `deadline_ticks` = ⌊L × f_tick / 10^12⌋, with L the upper bound of `Communication_Properties::Latency` (MAP-21).

     Under schema `hamr-rsk-semantic-model/1` the Semantic_Model records no end-to-end flow (Table 28-1 row SM-09), so until ORQ-48 is resolved this conjunct holds vacuously and the No-extra-items conjunct requires `chains` to be empty.
   - **Serial_Bridge.** For a model with a Peer_Processor (R34.1), the Serial_Bridge substitution that R34.5 states, over the Serial_Bridge threads that the model declares (R34.8): the threads of the IronOxide_Processor's side that the Semantic_Model records with `IronOxide::Serial_Bridge_Role` (Table 28-1 row SM-03). A cross-processor port connection instance whose source is a thread on the IronOxide_Processor is related to the items of the connection from that thread to the thread whose role is `Send` or `Receive_And_Send`. One whose destination is a thread on the IronOxide_Processor is related to the items of the connection to that thread from the thread whose role is `Receive` or `Receive_And_Send`.
   - **No extra items.** Every element of `partitions`, `tasks`, `resources`, `signals`, `endpoints` (each of its `roles` included) and `chains` is the image, under the conjuncts above, of exactly one model element. A Port_Cell, and the Endpoint of a MAP-21 hop, is the image of its connection.
   - **No refused construct.** The Semantic_Model records no construct of a kind that Table M-1 refuses (R28.1; MAP-03, MAP-08, MAP-22 to MAP-24).

   The Mapping_Relation does not cover the values that the Task_Model marks as derived and that IronOxide's Config_Checker recomputes (IronOxide R26.2): Ceilings, vectors, slots, nesting depths, the priority map, the MPU layout, Endpoint `k` and storage offsets, and checksums. It reads no source position or file URI because HAMR's SysML v2 front end writes absolute file URIs, which depend on where the model is checked out (ORQ-24); these appear only in the mismatch reports of criterion 4. *Evidence: `review-mapping-relation`, `proof-tv-core`, `mut-translation`, `test-tv-accepts-<model>` (one corpus model's Semantic_Model with every source position and file URI changed is still accepted).*
3. THE Build_System SHALL check the proof of criterion 1 by running `cargo verus verify` on the TV_Checker's crate, built as a host-only crate as R21.4 states. The run SHALL use the Verus release, its rustc and its Z3 that the `[verus_toolchain]` table of IronOxide's `toolchain/decision.toml` pins at PAR-01 (Verus 0.2026.10.04.426d8b0, rustc 1.98.1, Z3 4.16.0), with `vstd` `=0.0.0-2026-10-04-0306`. THE Build_System SHALL fail the verification job in each of these cases:
   - `verus --version` or `z3 --version` reports a version other than the pinned one. The failure names the tool and both versions.
   - Verus reports an error, or reports no results line for a library or binary target of the crate. The failure names the failing items.
   - The core contains an assumption construct that the Proof_Assumption_Register does not list (R40.3). The failure names the construct.

   *Evidence: `proof-tv-core` (records the three versions), `check-verus-pins` (one fixture per tool reporting a differing version), `check-proof-assumptions`, `mut-proof-tv` (at least the planted defects of PAR-17, at least one per conjunct of the Mapping_Relation, each removing or inverting the core's check of that conjunct and each rejected by Verus with its registered text; R33.1, R33.2).*
4. IF the Mapping_Relation does not hold between the views of a pair that the readers accepted, THEN THE TV_Checker SHALL return Reject and report one mismatch for each violated instance of a conjunct, not only the first. The report SHALL be written as text and as one JSON document in the published versioned schema `hamr-rsk-tv-report/1`, both in the order of R3.2. Each mismatch SHALL name:
   - the conjunct's identifier and the MAP row it cites (criterion 10);
   - the AADL element's instance path and source position as the Semantic_Model records them, or the statement of R3.7 where it records no position;
   - the Task_Model item by its `id` (the document root for a header value) and the JSON path of the compared field;
   - the value that the Mapping_Relation requires and the value found.

   Where a model element has no counterpart item, or an item has no model element, the mismatch names the side that lacks one and, as its conjunct, the conjunct that requires the counterpart (the No-extra-items conjunct for an item without a model element), and no other conjunct is reported for that element or item. A pair that violates exactly one conjunct therefore gets a report that names that conjunct and no other, which R30.10 relies on. *Evidence: `test-tv-rejects` (for each conjunct, a pair that violates only that conjunct; one pair that violates two conjuncts on different items, with both reported; one model element without an item; one item without a model element), `mut-translation` (R30.1).*
5. WHEN the Build_System has run the IronOxide_Generator on a generated Application (each corpus model, each Model_Generator model of R31.2, and the IronOxide sub-model of the Robot_Model, R34.5), THE Build_System SHALL run the TV_Checker on these inputs:
   - the Task_Model that this run of the IronOxide_Generator wrote;
   - the Semantic_Model that the Semantic_Model_Extractor wrote from the Application's source AIR_Model file (criterion 11).

   THE Build_System SHALL run the TV_Checker before it runs the Profile_Lint, the Link_Checker, the Config_Checker, the Analyzer, the WCET_Harness, or a QEMU or HIL_Rig run on that Application. IF the TV_Checker exits with a status other than 0, THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail the build and run none of these on the Application. *Evidence: `check-tv-<model>` (stores the report with both input digests, the table version of criterion 10 and the Verus version of criterion 3), `test-tv-gate` (a planted mismatch stops the build before the Config_Checker runs).*
6. THE TV_Checker SHALL consist, outside its Verus core, only of the following parts, each listed in the Trust_Base_Register with what is trusted:
   - one JSON reader for each of the two schemas. Each reader validates the document against its schema before it builds the executable values whose views criterion 1 speaks about. For the Task_Model the schema is IronOxide's `crates/rsk-model/schemas/task_model.json` at PAR-01. For each reader, the Trust_Base_Register entry states that the view of the value it builds is trusted to equal the document's content, and states the conversion of identifiers to code-point sequences where the answer to ORQ-72 requires the reader to make one.
   - a report printer.
   - a command-line entry point that exits with status 0 only when the core returns Accept, 1 when the core returns Reject, 2 under criterion 9, and 3 on an internal error.

   *Evidence: `pbt-semantic-model-round-trip` (R28.4), `pbt-task-model-reader-round-trip` (over PAR-08 generated Task_Models per run), `diff-task-model-reader` (for every corpus Task_Model, each field that criterion 2 reads equals the value that IronOxide's `rsk_model::read_task_model` reads), `test-tv-exit-status`, `check-registers`.*
7. THE Verification_Plan SHALL state that the evidence from the AADL model to the binary consists of two steps:
   - the TV_Checker, from Semantic_Model to Task_Model, whose soundness is proven (criterion 1) and whose completeness is tested only (criterion 8). The reason that no proof applies to completeness (R39.3) is that an incomplete TV_Checker rejects a correct pair and so stops the build under criterion 5; it cannot let a mismatched configuration pass.
   - IronOxide's Config_Checker, from Task_Model to binary (IronOxide R26). This is an independent recomputation tested by IronOxide's mutation suite (`docs/config_mutations.toml`, IronOxide R26.7), not a proof. This project relies on it under ASM-05 and cites it by IronOxide Evidence_Item name and revision (R39.5).

   THE Verification_Plan SHALL also list what neither step covers, at least:
   - the Front_End (ASM-01) and the Semantic_Model_Extractor (R28);
   - the Port_Glue's behaviour (covered by the App_Prover, R19.5);
   - developer code;
   - the App_Declaration items that the Task_Model does not record: per-Task state, Resource initial values and invariants, and Endpoint payload types and Wire_Types (R14);
   - the readers, report printer and entry point of criterion 6.

   IOP-13 would let the Task_Model record per-Task state declarations and Resource initial values and invariants, so that the TV_Checker could cover them. *Evidence: `doc-verification-plan`, `check-registers`.*
8. WHEN the TV_Checker's readers have read a pair between whose views the Mapping_Relation holds, THE TV_Checker SHALL return Accept (completeness). *Evidence: `pbt-tv-complete` (over PAR-08 Model_Generator models per run, the pair is accepted for every model that the Backend accepts and whose App_Declaration the IronOxide_Generator accepts; each Reject fails the run with its mismatches and is classified as a Backend defect or a TV_Checker completeness defect), `test-tv-accepts-<model>` (every corpus model), `test-tv-boundaries` (pairs whose times convert exactly, lie 1 ns above or below a tick or cycle boundary, or convert an offset exactly halfway between two ticks; a Budget of exactly 2^32 − 1 cycles; an Endpoint `capacity` and `ops` of 255).*
9. IF a Semantic_Model or Task_Model given to the TV_Checker is not valid JSON, states a schema identifier other than `hamr-rsk-semantic-model/1` or `rsk-task-model/1` respectively (a later identifier of R28.5 included), violates that schema, or holds an integer outside the range that its schema states, THEN THE TV_Checker SHALL return Input_Rejected with exit status 2 without running the core and without returning Accept. The diagnostic SHALL name the document, which of these conditions holds, and the JSON path of the first violation. *Evidence: `test-tv-input-rejected` (one fixture per condition and document).*
10. THE Mapping_Relation SHALL cite, as one constant in its Verus source, the version of Tables M-1 and M-2 that it encodes (R4.6). It SHALL give each conjunct an identifier and cite the MAP row, or the criterion of this document, that the conjunct encodes. The citations SHALL cover at least the mapping rows MAP-01, MAP-02, MAP-04 to MAP-07, MAP-09 to MAP-16, MAP-18, MAP-20, MAP-21, MAP-27 and MAP-28, and the refusal rows MAP-03, MAP-08 and MAP-22 to MAP-24. Every cited row SHALL exist in the cited version. *Evidence: `check-mapping-relation-rows` (fails naming each row in this list that no conjunct cites and each cited row that does not exist in the cited version), `check-mapping-version`.*
11. IF the SHA-256 digest of the AIR_Model file that a Semantic_Model states (R28.1) differs from the digest of the Application's source AIR_Model file, THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail the build naming both digests, the Semantic_Model file and the Application, and SHALL run neither the TV_Checker nor any tool that criterion 5 lists on the Application. The source AIR_Model file is the file whose digest the Application's generated files record (R1.5) or, for an IronOxide sub-model, the two-processor AIR_Model file on which the Build_System ran the Deployment_Splitter (R34.5). The digest links a Semantic_Model to the generation run whose Task_Model the TV_Checker checks, so that no Semantic_Model of another or an earlier AIR_Model is compared with it. *Evidence: `test-tv-gate` (a Semantic_Model written from another AIR_Model and one written from an earlier version of the same AIR_Model each stop the build before the TV_Checker runs; the Robot_Model's IronOxide sub-model is checked against the Semantic_Model of its two-processor AIR_Model).*

### Requirement 30: Translation mutations (Phase P1 refusal and Backend-check mutants; P4 translation mutants)

**User Story:** As a verification engineer, I want planted defects in generated configurations and in models, so that the TV_Checker and the Backend's refusals are shown to catch what they claim to catch.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHERE the build includes the TV_Checker (Phase P4 onward), THE Translation_Mutation_Register SHALL hold, for each conjunct of the Mapping_Relation (one named Verus specification function that the Mapping_Relation's top-level specification function of R29.2 conjoins directly), at least as many `task-model` mutants (Table 30-1) as PAR-17 requires planted defects per conjunct (1), each changing the Task_Model of its base model so that the pair violates only that conjunct, each naming that conjunct as its target, and with base models that together include at least one model generated for `nrf52840` and one generated for `qemu_mps2_an386`, because IronOxide's conversions to ticks and cycles depend on the IronOxide_Target (IronOxide R21.2). These mutants plant defects in the TV_Checker's input; planted defects in its Verus source are the `mut-proof-tv` mutations of Requirement 33. *Evidence: `check-registers` (count per conjunct, base models per IronOxide_Target), `review-translation-mutations` (each mutant violates only its target conjunct; R29.4 reports every violated conjunct, so the Build_System checks this under criterion 10), `mut-translation`.*
2. THE Translation_Mutation_Register SHALL hold at least one `air` mutant (Table 30-1) for each labelled refusal of each row of the current version of Table M-1 (R4.4, R4.5), and at least one for each acceptance criterion of this document whose Evidence names `mut-refusals`, among them every mutant that the criterion's Evidence describes (for example, for R16.4, Immediate and Delayed, each set once on a connection instance and once on a declared connection only). Each `air` mutant changes a base AIR_Model that the Backend accepts so that the mutated AIR_Model contains only the refused construct of the labelled refusal or criterion that the mutant names. It lists as expected diagnostics every error diagnostic that this document requires for that construct, one of which names the mutated element, the MAP row with the label, and the rule that the row's "Rule" cell gives under that label (R3.1, R4.5). Where two criteria each require a diagnostic for one construct (R7.4 with R17.2; R12.3 with R17.5), the mutant lists both. A labelled refusal whose "Refused" cell assigns the case to another row's label (MAP-15 (a), refused under MAP-24 (d)) is covered by the mutants of that label. MAP-07 (e) needs no `air` mutant, because the sanitization rule of R8.6 gives no two items one name, so no AIR_Model reaches the refusal; `test-name-collision` shows it instead (R8.7). The Phase refusals of R16.8 are shown by `test-phase-refusals`, not by `air` mutants. *Evidence: `check-registers` (one mutant per labelled refusal and per citing criterion), `review-translation-mutations` (each citing criterion's described mutants are present; each mutant contains only its construct), `mut-refusals`.*
3. IF, for the Phase for which the Backend is built (R16.8), any of the following lacks the registered mutants that its criterion requires, THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail the verification job naming each such item and the criterion:
   - a conjunct of the Mapping_Relation (criterion 1);
   - a labelled refusal of Table M-1, or an acceptance criterion whose Evidence names `mut-refusals`, whose `phase` (criterion 4) is not later than that Phase (criterion 2);
   - a Semantic_Model field or item list (criterion 5), a Table M-1 row of criterion 6, or a Task_Model field or item list (criterion 7) that also has no exclusion entry;
   - a check of criterion 8 whose `phase` is not later than that Phase.

   The items of criteria 1, 5, 6 and 7 count from Phase P4. An exclusion entry counts only where criterion 5, 6 or 7 allows one and every Evidence_Item or Gap_Register entry in its `covered_by` resolves (R40.5); no exclusion entry is allowed for a conjunct, a labelled refusal, a citing criterion or a check of criterion 8. *Evidence: `check-registers` (one fixture register per item kind above, each with the entries of one item removed, failing and naming that item).*
4. THE Translation_Mutation_Register SHALL be the file `docs/translation_mutations.toml`, in the form of IronOxide's `docs/config_mutations.toml` at PAR-01: a line `schema = 1`, then one `[[mutation]]` table per mutant and one `[[exclusion]]` table per exclusion entry, with these fields and no others. A mutant is a registered list of edits applied to a copy of one base artifact (Table 30-1).
   - `id`: lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, unique in the register.
   - `kind`: `air`, `task-model`, `semantic-model`, `app-declaration` or `backend-source`.
   - `base`: for `air`, the path of a committed AIR_Model JSON file; for `task-model`, `semantic-model` and `app-declaration`, the name of a model of the corpus of `check-tv-<model>`; for `backend-source`, the path of a Backend source file; paths relative to the repository root.
   - `edit`: one or more tables, applied in order, each with `find` (text that occurs exactly once in the artifact as the earlier edits left it) and `replace` (text different from `find`). IronOxide's registers allow one pair per mutation; several pairs let one mutant remove an item together with every reference to it.
   - `phase`: for `air`, the earliest Phase in which the criterion that the mutant names must hold, as its requirement heading states (Table 16-1 for a labelled refusal that no criterion states); for `backend-source`, the `phase` of the `air` mutant that it names; P4 for the other kinds.
   - `conjunct`: for `task-model`, `semantic-model` and `app-declaration`, the name of the target conjunct (criterion 1).
   - `fields`: for `task-model` and `semantic-model`, each field path or item list that the edits change, written as a JSON pointer with `*` for every array index (for example `/tasks/*/budget_cycles`).
   - `row`: for `air`, the Table M-1 row with the label (for example `MAP-24 (d)`); for `app-declaration`, the Table M-1 row whose output the edits change.
   - `criterion`: for `air`, the criterion that states the refusal; for `backend-source`, the criterion whose check the edits remove.
   - `expect`: for `task-model`, `semantic-model` and `app-declaration`, the substrings that the TV_Checker's rejection must contain: the target conjunct's name, the `id` of each Task_Model item and the instance path of each Semantic_Model element that the edits concern; for `backend-source`, substrings of the Backend-defect report of R17.4: the IronOxide rule and the instance path of the AADL element.
   - `diagnostics`: for `air`, the expected diagnostics of severity error, each given as the instance paths of the elements it names, its MAP row with label, and its rule, as R3.1 writes them.
   - `refusal`: for `backend-source`, the `id` of the `air` mutant on which the mutant Backend runs.
   - In an `[[exclusion]]` table: `scope` (`task-model-field`, `semantic-model-field` or `map-row`), `item` (a field path, item list or MAP row), `reason`, and `covered_by` (the Evidence_Items or Gap_Register entries that cover the item).

   *Evidence: `check-registers`.*
5. WHERE the build includes the TV_Checker, THE Translation_Mutation_Register SHALL hold, for each field path of the Semantic_Model schema `hamr-rsk-semantic-model/1` (R28.1) whose value is a scalar or an array of scalars, at least one `semantic-model` mutant that changes that field's value, and for each array of objects of that schema (an item list, for example the threads or the connections), one `semantic-model` mutant that removes an item and one that adds an item, or else an exclusion entry stating that no conjunct of the Mapping_Relation reads the field or item list (for example the AADL value and unit kept beside each picosecond value, R28.3). These mutants show that the TV_Checker compares the Task_Model with the Semantic_Model rather than checking the Task_Model alone. *Evidence: `check-registers`, `mut-translation`.*
6. WHERE the build includes the TV_Checker, THE Translation_Mutation_Register SHALL hold, for each row of Table M-1 except MAP-03, MAP-08, MAP-22, MAP-23 and MAP-24 (mapping "none"), MAP-25 (ignored properties), MAP-26 (limit checks) and MAP-31 (flow specifications, not carried), at least one `app-declaration` mutant that changes an App_Declaration item or value that the row's "IronOxide mapping" cell produces, or else, for a row whose output the Task_Model does not record (for example the `const fn` initial values of MAP-29), an exclusion entry naming the Evidence_Items that cover that output. Examples of such mutants, where the IronOxide_Generator accepts the result: two Tasks' `priority` values exchanged; a Task's `deadline` shortened but kept at or above its `budget`; a role bound to another Task of the same Partition; `mit_policy = defer` changed to `discard` on a Task released by a Release_Signal. The App_Declaration is the only Backend output that reaches the Task_Model; planted defects in the other generated code are R32.4's `mut-diff` and the proof mutations of Requirement 33. *Evidence: `check-registers`, `mut-translation-decl`.*
7. WHERE the build includes the TV_Checker, THE Translation_Mutation_Register SHALL hold, for each field path of IronOxide's Task_Model schema at PAR-01 (`crates/rsk-model/schemas/task_model.json`, `rsk-task-model/1`) whose value is a scalar or an array of scalars, at least one `task-model` mutant that changes that field's value, and for each array of objects of that schema (for example `/partitions`, `/tasks`, `/resources`, `/signals`, `/endpoints`, `/endpoints/*/roles`, `/chains` and `/chains/*/hops`), one `task-model` mutant that removes an item and one that adds an item. The register may instead hold an exclusion entry for a field or item list that the Mapping_Relation does not constrain because the IronOxide_Generator derives it from declared values (for example `ceiling`, `vector`, `nesting_depth`, `slots`, `priority_map`, `capacities`, `layout` and the two checksums). That entry names in `covered_by` either the IronOxide Evidence_Item, cited as R39.5 requires, that checks the field (the Config_Checker recomputation of IronOxide R26.2 with its mutation cases of IronOxide R26.7, relied on through ASM-05 and R29.7) or a Gap_Register entry that records the field as unchecked. A mutant counts toward every minimum of criteria 1, 5 and 7 that it meets. *Evidence: `check-registers`, `mut-translation`.*
8. THE Translation_Mutation_Register SHALL hold, as `backend-source` mutants, the Backend mutants of R17.4's `mut-backend-checks`: at least one for each check of R17.2, R17.3, R17.5, R17.6 and R17.7 and one for each labelled refusal of Table M-1 whose Rule cell names an IronOxide_Generator rule, except MAP-07 (e), which R30.2 exempts from `air` mutants and `test-name-collision` shows instead (R8.7). Each such mutant removes only that check from a copy of the Backend's source. It names as `refusal` an `air` mutant of criterion 2 for the same check or refusal, so that the mutant Backend of R17.4 runs on a model that criterion 11 shows the unmutated Backend refusing. *Evidence: `check-registers`, `review-translation-mutations` (the labelled refusals whose rule the IronOxide_Generator reports at PAR-01), `mut-backend-checks`.*
9. WHEN `cargo xtask verify` runs (R39.2), THE Build_System SHALL apply every registered mutant whose `phase` is not later than the Phase for which the Backend is built (R16.8) to a copy of its base artifact in a work directory inside the workspace (R42.3), leaving the base artifact and the Backend's source byte-identical, and SHALL judge each mutant with the tool and kill condition that Table 30-1 gives for its kind. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `mut-translation`, `mut-translation-decl`, `mut-backend-checks`, `doc-checkpoint-p<n>`.*
10. WHEN the Build_System judges a `task-model`, `semantic-model` or `app-declaration` mutant, THE Build_System SHALL count it as killed only when two conditions hold. The TV_Checker must reject the pair formed by the mutated input and the base model's other, unmutated input; for an `app-declaration` mutant, that pair is the base model's Semantic_Model and the Task_Model that the IronOxide_Generator at PAR-01 writes for the mutated App_Declaration. Its rejection (R29.4) must also name the mutant's `conjunct` and no other conjunct and contain every `expect` string of the mutant. *Evidence: `mut-translation`, `mut-translation-decl`, `test-mutation-verdicts` (fixture outputs: an acceptance, a rejection naming another conjunct, a rejection lacking one `expect` string, and a rejection with every expected item).*
11. WHEN the Build_System judges an `air` mutant, THE Build_System SHALL count it as killed only when all of the following hold. The Backend is run on the mutated AIR_Model with the options with which the Build_System runs it on the corpus, or the Deployment_Splitter is run instead for a mutant that names R34.4. That tool ends with a non-zero exit status and writes no generated artefact other than the diagnostics of R3.3 (R3.6). Its diagnostics of severity error match the mutant's `diagnostics` one for one: the same number, and for each the same instance paths, MAP row with label, and rule. Diagnostics of severity warning are not compared. *Evidence: `mut-refusals`, `test-mutation-verdicts`.*
12. IF the register departs from the format of criterion 4, or any of the following holds for a registered mutant, THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail the verification job, giving the mutant the verdict invalid and naming the mutant and the condition:
    - the `find` text of an edit does not occur exactly once in the text to which the edit applies;
    - the `base` names no committed AIR_Model file, no model of the corpus of `check-tv-<model>`, or no Backend source file, as the mutant's kind requires;
    - the unmutated base is not accepted: the Backend reports a diagnostic of severity error on the base AIR_Model of an `air` mutant, or the TV_Checker rejects the base model's unmutated Semantic_Model and Task_Model;
    - a mutated Task_Model does not conform to IronOxide's Task_Model schema, or a mutated Semantic_Model does not conform to `hamr-rsk-semantic-model/1`, so that the TV_Checker's JSON reader (R29.6) and not its Verus core would decide the case;
    - for an `app-declaration` mutant, the IronOxide_Generator reports a diagnostic on the mutated App_Declaration or writes no Task_Model, or the Task_Model it writes equals the base model's Task_Model in every field and item list that criterion 7 does not exclude.

    *Evidence: `check-registers`, `test-mutation-verdicts` (one fixture per condition).*
13. IF a mutant that criterion 9 applies is neither killed (criteria 10 and 11, R17.4) nor invalid (criterion 12), because the judging tool accepted it (survived) or rejected it without every expected item (unexpected: another conjunct, a missing `expect` string, or a missing or extra error diagnostic), THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail the verification job, whatever option, register field or environment setting is given. The report SHALL give the mutant's `id`, kind, base, target (`conjunct`, or `row` with label), verdict, the judging tool's exit status and output, and each missing or extra item. For a surviving mutant, the report SHALL state the meaning of the survival: the Mapping_Relation does not exclude a defect of the target conjunct (`task-model`, `semantic-model`, `app-declaration`), the Backend does not make the refusal (`air`), or the Build_System does not report the Backend defect (`backend-source`). These verdicts are those of IronOxide's proof-mutation runner (`xtask/src/mutations.rs`: killed, survived, unexpected), plus the invalid verdict of criterion 12. *Evidence: `test-mutation-verdicts`.*
14. IF, compared with the register that the last Phase checkpoint recorded (R41.1), a mutant `id` is missing or a mutant's `kind`, `base`, `edit`, `conjunct`, `row`, `criterion` or `diagnostics` differs, and the Verification_Plan change log (R41.2) has no entry for that change stating its rationale and the user's approval, THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail the verification job naming the mutant and the change. The rationale for removing a surviving mutant is a `review-translation-mutations` record showing that the mutated input still satisfies Table M-1 for its base model. *Evidence: `check-registers`, `check-criteria-approved`.*
15. WHEN the Build_System has judged the mutants of one kind, THE Build_System SHALL store in that kind's Evidence_Item of Table 30-1 (R39.2) one record per registered mutant and the number of mutants with each verdict. Each record SHALL give:
    - the mutant's `id`;
    - its verdict: killed, survived, unexpected, invalid, or not run, with the Phase of criterion 9 that excludes it;
    - the SHA-256 digests of the register file, the base artifact and the mutated artifact;
    - the judging tool's exit status and complete output.

    *Evidence: `check-evidence`.*

**Table 30-1 — Mutation kinds** (criteria 1 to 15). The base artifact is the artifact whose copy the mutant's edits change (criterion 9).

| Kind | Base artifact | Judged by | Killed when | Minimum count | From Phase | Evidence_Item |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `air` | a committed AIR_Model JSON file that the Backend accepts; for R34.4, a committed two-processor AIR_Model that the Deployment_Splitter accepts | the Backend; the Deployment_Splitter for R34.4 | criterion 11 | 1 per labelled refusal of each Table M-1 row except MAP-07 (e), and 1 per criterion whose Evidence names `mut-refusals`, plus the mutants its Evidence describes (criterion 2) | the mutant's `phase` (criterion 4): P1 to P5 | `mut-refusals` |
| `task-model` | the Task_Model that the IronOxide_Generator wrote for a corpus model's App_Declaration (R29.1) | the TV_Checker, with the base model's unmutated Semantic_Model | criterion 10 | PAR-17 (1) per conjunct (criterion 1); 1 per Task_Model schema field and 2 per item list (one removal, one addition), or an exclusion (criterion 7) | P4 | `mut-translation` |
| `semantic-model` | the Semantic_Model that the Semantic_Model_Extractor wrote for a corpus model (R28.1) | the TV_Checker, with the base model's unmutated Task_Model | criterion 10 | 1 per Semantic_Model schema field and 2 per item list, or an exclusion (criterion 5) | P4 | `mut-translation` |
| `app-declaration` | the App_Declaration that the Backend wrote for a corpus model | the IronOxide_Generator, which must accept it (criterion 12), then the TV_Checker with the base model's unmutated Semantic_Model | criterion 10 | 1 per Table M-1 row of criterion 6, or an exclusion | P4 | `mut-translation-decl` |
| `backend-source` | a Backend source file | the Build_System's Backend-defect check of R17.4, run on the `air` mutant that the entry names | R17.4: the Build_System fails and reports a Backend defect, its report containing every `expect` string | 1 per check of criterion 8 | the `phase` of the named `air` mutant | `mut-backend-checks` |

### Part F — Testing

### Requirement 31: Property-based tests over generated models (Phase P1; P3 timing)

**User Story:** As a verification engineer, I want randomly generated models pushed through the Backend and IronOxide's tools, so that the claim "everything the Backend accepts passes IronOxide's checks" is tested beyond hand-written examples.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Model_Generator SHALL generate, from Phase P1 (UD-25), in each run, for each IronOxide_Target as the models' `IronOxide::Target` value, models (Model_Generator models), each with a record of the Table M-1 rows, mapping alternatives and refused cases that it exercises and the instance paths of the elements concerned. The Model_Generator starts in Phase P1 because criteria of Phase P1 cite `pbt-` Evidence_Items (Table 31-1). Together these models SHALL cover each item below, starting from the Phase of the criterion that implements the item (the parenthesized Rn.m of a mapping cell, or the criterion that states a refusal):
   - every alternative that the "IronOxide mapping" cell of every row of Table M-1 states, counted as R4.1 counts alternatives for the `test-map-` Evidence_Items, in at least one model that the Backend accepts;
   - every labelled refused case of every row of Table M-1, and every refusal that a criterion of this document states outside Table M-1's labels, in at least one model;
   - every property that MAP-25 ignores, in at least one model that the Backend accepts;
   - for each label (a) to (g) of MAP-26, at least one accepted model at the limit and at least one model one step past it. One step past is the smallest excess over the limit that adding one element to an at-limit model, or changing one of its property values, can produce: for example 8 Tasks for (b), or 8,208 bytes of Endpoint storage for (d) where every Endpoint is plain;
   - every item above that HAMR's SysML v2 front end can express (ORQ-51), in at least one model of the SysML path of criterion 8.

   *Evidence: `pbt-generator-coverage` (for each IronOxide_Target, the run's coverage report lists each item above with the number of models on each path that cover it. A model counts only where the Backend's outcome agrees with the model's record (criterion 9). The run fails on any count of 0).*
2. WHEN the Backend accepts a Model_Generator model, THE Backend SHALL generate an Application that passes every check below for each IronOxide_Target of R18.2, built as R18.2 and R18.9 state:
   - the IronOxide_Generator, the Profile_Lint (with Clippy), the Link_Checker and the Config_Checker, each with the acceptance that R18.2 defines;
   - from Phase P3, the Analyzer, which SHALL exit with status 0 (PASS) or 1 (FAIL), not 2 (rejected input) or 3 (internal error) (IronOxide R30.4). Either PASS or FAIL counts as acceptance here; R25.7 judges the timing verdict (UD-27). It runs as R25.1 states, with every WCET_Record and Kernel_Timing_Parameter of method `placeholder`: `rsk-analyzer --placeholders` with the build's Task_Model and Build_Manifest sets the WCET of each Task entry and Critical_Section site to the Task's Budget in CPU cycles and each Kernel_Timing_Parameter to IronOxide's nominal placeholder value (`crates/rsk-analyzer/src/lib.rs`, `placeholders::make`). The run is labelled development evidence (R25.5);
