   - from Phase P4, the TV_Checker, which accepts the pair of the model's Semantic_Model and the Task_Model (R29.1) before any IronOxide tool runs (R29.5).

   *Evidence: `pbt-accepted-models-pass-ironoxide` (PAR-08 accepted models per run, with one record per model, IronOxide_Target and check giving its command line and exit status).*
3. IF the Backend refuses a Model_Generator model (R3.6), THEN THE Backend SHALL give every diagnostic of severity error that it reports for the model:
   - an instance path that the AIR_Model contains, for the element and for every contributing element (R3.1);
   - for each such path, the source position that the AIR_Model records for that element, or the statement of R3.7 where the AIR_Model records none;
   - a MAP row of Table M-1;
   - the rule that the Rule cell of that row gives under the label of the refused case, including a Backend restriction with the item it rests on (R4.5), or, for a refusal stated outside Table M-1's labels, the rule that the stating criterion names.

   *Evidence: `pbt-refusals-named`.*
4. WHEN the Backend accepts a Model_Generator model, THE Backend SHALL also accept each permuted copy of it and SHALL write for the copy the same set of relative file paths as for the model. After every source position and every AIR_Model digest of R1.5 is replaced in both outputs by one fixed text, each generated file and the generation report SHALL be byte-identical, and the diagnostics documents of R3.3 SHALL list the same diagnostics, compared as multisets. A permuted copy is the model in which the declaration order of the subcomponents, of the features and of the connections of every component classifier and instance that has at least two of them is permuted at random from the run seed, with at least one list in a different order. The permutation applies to the SysML v2 text for a model of the SysML path and to the order of the AIR_Model's component, feature and connection lists for a model of the AIR path (criterion 8). *Evidence: `pbt-order-invariance` (one permuted copy per accepted model).*
5. WHEN all of the following hold, THE Backend SHALL generate from M′ an Application whose Analyzer run of criterion 2, for the same IronOxide_Target, does not exit with status 0 (PASS) and reports as schedulable no Task that the Analyzer run of criterion 2 on M does not report as schedulable, Tasks of M and M′ corresponding by name (metamorphic property over per-Task verdicts) (UD-23):
   - the Analyzer run of criterion 2 on an accepted Model_Generator model M for an IronOxide_Target exits with status 1 (FAIL);
   - the Application generated from M declares no Chain (R26.1), because a Chain bound adds the period or MIT of each Task after the first, so a shorter period can lower it (R26.8);
   - M has no connection that MAP-16 maps, because C_sample, and with it the receiving role's `ops` that the Analyzer charges, depends on the two Tasks' periods (R12.2);
   - every thread of M bound to the IronOxide_Processor has `Thread_Properties::Priority`, so that R7.1 gives M and M′ the same Priorities, whereas the deadline-monotonic ranks of R7.2 can change with a Period;
   - the Backend accepts a copy M′ of M that differs from M in exactly one way: for one thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor, either the upper bound of its `Timing_Properties::Compute_Execution_Time` is larger, or its `Timing_Properties::Period` (the MIT for a Sporadic thread, ASM-03) is smaller, by a whole number of nanoseconds of at least 1 ns.

   The placeholder WCET_Records of the M′ run follow the larger Budget. *Evidence: `pbt-timing-monotone` (Phase P3; PAR-08 pairs per run, at least PAR-08 / 2 with each kind of change).*
6. WHEN a property-based test finds an input (a model, a pair of models of criterion 4 or 5, or a value) that violates its pass condition, THE Build_System SHALL:
   - shrink the input. It repeatedly replaces the input by a candidate that the Model_Generator derives from it by removing one component, feature, connection, property association or GUMBO clause, or by moving one numeric property value toward the lower end of the Model_Generator's range for that property; for a pair, the same derivation is applied to both models. A candidate replaces the input only if it violates the same part of the pass condition: for criterion 2, the same tool with the same exit status; for criterion 3, the same missing item; for criterion 9, the same unreported recorded case, or a refusal of a model whose record lists none. Shrinking stops when no candidate violates it, or when 200 candidates or 2 h of wall-clock time have been spent on that input (proposal);
   - add the following to that test's regression corpus, which is under version control in this workspace: the shrunk input as the Backend received it (the SysML v2 text and its AIR_Model, or the AIR_Model); its per-model seed and the run seed; the violated part of the pass condition with the tool output; and whether shrinking ended because no candidate violated the condition or because the budget ran out;
   - end the run with a non-zero exit status, naming the test, the run seed and the regression case.

   *Evidence: `pbt-regressions`, `mut-pbt-regressions` (for each test of Table 31-1, a Backend mutant with one planted defect makes a run of that test fail and store a shrunk case, which the next run replays and reports as failing).*
7. WHEN the Build_System runs a property-based test (a `pbt-` Evidence_Item), THE Build_System SHALL:
   - take the run seed from the invocation or, where the invocation names none, from the operating system's random source. A run seed is 64 bits, written as 16 lowercase hexadecimal digits. Before it evaluates any input, the Build_System writes the run seed, the Model_Generator's version, PAR-08 and the pins of R42.1 into the test's Evidence_Item;
   - draw the models from the Model_Generator, which derives each model from the run seed and the model's index alone (the per-model seed). A run with the same run seed, Model_Generator version and pins therefore draws byte-identical models in the same order, and any one model can be regenerated alone from its per-model seed, which is also how the Verification_Plan lists a Model_Generator model as a candidate of the differential corpus (R32.7);
   - evaluate the test's pass condition first on every case of its regression corpus (criterion 6), and then on PAR-08 drawn inputs that meet the test's precondition: the "Inputs" column of Table 31-1, or, for a test outside that table, the inputs that the requirement defining the test states. Several tests may share one sequence of drawn models;
   - fail the run, stating both counts, when more than 5 × PAR-08 drawn inputs fail the precondition before PAR-08 inputs meet it (proposal). This is the ratio of HAMR's generated property tests: `numValidComputeTestCases = 100` and `computeRejectRatio = 5`, with `max_global_rejects` set to their product, in `hamr/codegen/shared/src/main/scala/org/sireum/hamr/codegen/microkit/plugins/gumbo/GumboXRustPlugin.scala` at PAR-02;
   - record for each drawn input its per-model seed, its path (criterion 8), whether it met the precondition, and the outcome of each check.

   *Evidence: `test-pbt-protocol` (a rerun with a recorded run seed draws byte-identical models; a model regenerated from its per-model seed is byte-identical; a precondition that no input meets fails the run after 5 × PAR-08 rejected draws; a run stopped before its first input completes has its seed recorded).*
8. WHEN the Model_Generator generates a model, THE Build_System SHALL deliver the model to the Backend through exactly one of the two paths below and record which. A model that its path fails to deliver SHALL be reported as a Model_Generator defect that fails the run.
   - SysML path: the Model_Generator writes SysML v2 text that uses HAMR's SysML v2 AADL libraries (RN-06) and the IronOxide_Property_Set's SysML v2 library package. HAMR's SysML v2 front end at PAR-02, run as `docs/hamr_study.md` run 9 runs it (`sireum hamr sysml codegen`; whether it can write the AIR_Model without a platform's code generation is part of ORQ-01), writes the AIR_Model JSON file. The Backend receives that file with the front end's name and version as the Front_End of R1.5. An error that the front end reports is a failure to deliver. This path trusts the front end as ASM-01 states, and it reaches only the constructs that the front end and the libraries can express (ORQ-51).
   - AIR path: the Model_Generator writes the AIR_Model JSON file directly, and the Backend receives it with the Model_Generator's name and version as the Front_End of R1.5. A file that HAMR's AIR JSON reader at PAR-02 (`JSON.toAadl`, `hamr/air/shared/src/main/scala/org/sireum/hamr/ir/JSON.scala`) does not read without error is a failure to deliver. This path trusts, without a check, that each such AIR_Model is one that a Front_End produces from some model text (ORQ-52). A failure found only on AIR-path models may therefore be unreachable from model text. A pass says nothing about AIR shapes that the Model_Generator does not write, for example how OSATE records data-access connections (ORQ-03).
   - On either path, a model that the Backend refuses as an input under R1.6 is a failure to deliver.

   *Evidence: `test-pbt-input-paths` (one model per path reaches the Backend, and its outputs state the Front_End name and version of R1.5; a SysML v2 text that the front end rejects, an AIR file that the reader rejects, and an input that R1.6 refuses each fail the run as a Model_Generator defect), `pbt-generator-coverage` (the counts per path).*
9. WHEN the Backend runs on a Model_Generator model, THE Backend SHALL refuse the model (R3.6) exactly when the model's record of criterion 1 lists at least one refused case. For each listed refused case, the Backend SHALL then report a diagnostic that names the case's element (as the element or as a contributing element), the case's MAP row, and the rule that criterion 3 requires for that case. *Evidence: `pbt-refusal-oracle` (every drawn model; each disagreement is shrunk as criterion 6 states and triaged as a Backend defect or a Model_Generator defect).*

**Table 31-1 — Property-based tests over Model_Generator models.** Each test runs as criterion 7 states. It runs from Phase P1 (UD-25), or from the later Phase of the criteria it shows. "Inputs" is the precondition that a drawn input meets to count toward PAR-08. A test passes when its pass condition holds for every such input and for every case of its regression corpus.

| Evidence_Item | Inputs | Pass condition | Shows |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pbt-generator-coverage` | every drawn model | the coverage report of criterion 1 has no count of 0 | R31.1, R31.8 |
| `pbt-accepted-models-pass-ironoxide` | models that the Backend accepts | criterion 2, for each IronOxide_Target of R18.2 | R31.2, R18.2, R26.1 |
| `pbt-refusals-named` | models that the Backend refuses (R3.6) | criterion 3 | R31.3, R3.1, R4.2, R10.8, R14.8 |
| `pbt-refusal-oracle` | every drawn model | criterion 9 | R31.9, R4.5 |
| `pbt-order-invariance` | accepted models, each with one permuted copy | criterion 4 | R31.4, R7.5 |
| `pbt-timing-monotone` | pairs (M, M′) of criterion 5 | criterion 5 | R31.5 (Phase P3) |
| `pbt-processor-scope` | accepted models, each with one copy that the Backend also accepts, in which one element that R1.2 does not list is added, removed or changed (for example a thread bound to a Peer_Processor, or a property of such an element) | every generated file of the copy is identical to the model's, apart from source positions and the provenance of R1.5 | R1.2 |
| `pbt-thread-task-bijection` | accepted models | through the generation report of R4.7, the App_Declaration's Tasks correspond one to one to the threads that R5.1 maps, each Periodic thread to a Periodic_Task and each Sporadic thread to a Sporadic_Task | R5.1 |
| `pbt-time-exact` | accepted models | every time value of the App_Declaration is written as R6.2 states, and n × u equals the AADL value in picoseconds | R6.2 |
| `pbt-time-limits-agree` | every drawn model, with time values drawn at and around the conversion limits of R6.7 for its IronOxide_Target | as R6.7 states, with the IronOxide_Generator run on the App_Declaration that the Backend writes with the refusals of R6.7 suspended (the test-only option of R17.1) | R6.7 |
| `pbt-priority-rank` | accepted models | each Task's Priority in the Task_Model equals the rank that R7.1 or R7.2 gives from the AIR_Model's values | R7.1, R7.2 |
| `pbt-names-injective` | accepted models, with identifiers drawn to include pairs that differ only in letter case, or only in characters that IronOxide's App_Declaration parser (`crates/rsk-gen/src/syntax.rs`) does not accept in identifiers | as R8.6 states | R8.6 |
| `pbt-sampling-no-full` | accepted models with at least one cross-Partition data port connection whose destination thread is Periodic; for each such connection, PAR-08 executions of at least 1,000 sends of the source Task each (proposal), drawn from the executions that the hypothesis of R12.2 permits under the IronOxide rules it cites | no send of the source Task finds C_sample messages in the channel | R12.2 |
| `pbt-wire-round-trip` | for each Wire_Type of an accepted model: PAR-08 application values, including each integer type's minimum and maximum, every enumerator, strings of length 0 and n, a quiet NaN, a signalling NaN, +0 and −0; and PAR-08 byte patterns of the Wire_Type's size, at least PAR-08 / 2 of them a valid encoding with one byte changed | the decoder rejects exactly the patterns that Table 14-1 lists as rejected (R14.2), and both round trips of R14.3 hold | R14.2, R14.3 |
| `pbt-backend-checks-match-generator` | accepted models, for each IronOxide_Target of R18.2 | as R17.4 states | R5.6, R15.1 to R15.3, R15.5, R17.4, R26.3 |
| `pbt-limits` | every drawn model, drawn at and around each limit of R17.2, R17.3 and R17.5 to R17.7 | as R17.1 states | R17.1 to R17.3, R17.5 to R17.7 |
| `pbt-user-code-preserved` | accepted models | as R18.5 states | R18.5 |
| `pbt-verdict-copy` | accepted models whose Analyzer run of criterion 2 wrote a report | as R27.2 states | R27.2, R27.4, R27.5 (Phase P3) |
| `pbt-regressions` | the regression corpora of every test of this table, replayed together | every case meets its test's pass condition | R31.6 |

### Requirement 32: Differential tests against HAMR's JVM simulation (Phase P3 QEMU; P4 HIL_Rig)

**User Story:** As a verification engineer, I want the same model run on HAMR's JVM simulation and on IronOxide and the observable traces compared, so that differences in port semantics and dispatching show up as test failures.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Backend SHALL generate, for each thread of each model of the differential corpus (criterion 7), two Trace_Bodies. One is in Slang, in the component file that HAMR's JVM back end at PAR-02 generates for the thread's developer code (`src/main/component/…`, which HAMR marks as not overwritten). The other is in Rust, as the compute function in the developer regions and developer-owned files (R18.5, R18.8) of a trace variant of the generated Application that differs from the Application of R18.1 in no other file (DD candidate). The two Trace_Bodies of a thread SHALL compute one function that design.md documents, with these properties:
   - Whether the Trace_Body puts on an output port in Job n, and the value it puts on an event data port, depend only on n and the port. Here n is the Job's dispatch index, 1 for the thread's first Job.
   - The value put on a data port depends only on n, the frozen input values that Get Value presents in that Job (R19.1, without freshness and Port_API counts) and the Trace_Body's own state. No reading of a time base, random value or platform property enters a put decision or a value.
   - Each value put on a data or event data port holds n in the provenance component of the port's type (criterion 7); prov(v) denotes this index of a value v.
   - Each value put satisfies the conjunction of the port's GUMBO integration guarantee clauses and of its data type's GUMBO invariants under the semantics of R23.3.
   - Each output data port gets the same initial value on both sides, with provenance 0: on IronOxide the result of the `const fn` of R20.2, on the JVM a Put Value in the Initialize entry point. No event or event data port gets a value at initialization (DEV-03).
   - The Trace_Body handles an input that Get Value reports absent (Slang `None`) without halting (finding F-3).
   - The Trace_Body writes the Job record of criterion 2 once per Job, and the initialization record of criterion 2 once (in the Initialize entry point on the JVM, in the first Job on IronOxide). On the JVM it writes them through the thread's `logInfo` API, which ART at PAR-02 writes as one JSON line per call (`ArtNative_Ext.scala` of HAMR's JVM output). Under QEMU it writes them through `rsk::debug`, IronOxide's semihosting console (`crates/rsk/src/lib.rs`), whose writes stall QEMU's instruction-counted guest time together with the guest's execution (`xtask/src/qemu.rs`) (DD candidate; ORQ-13). On the HIL_Rig it writes them as design.md records for ORQ-53 (IOP-14 proposes a Kernel Job-trace ring for this).

   *Evidence: `test-trace-bodies` (for each thread of each corpus model, the Slang and the Rust Trace_Body, given the same 1,000 recorded-seed sequences of frozen inputs (proposal), make the same put decisions and put equal values, and each value put satisfies the executable twin (R23.8) of the port's integration guarantee clauses), `check-generated-<model>` (the trace variant differs from the Application only inside developer regions and developer-owned files).*
2. THE Build_System SHALL run each model of the differential corpus on HAMR's JVM simulation and on the trace variant of its IronOxide Application, under QEMU from Phase P3 (with the build, command line and wall-clock limit of R18.6) and on the HIL_Rig's target board (`hil-rig.toml` `[boards.target]`) from Phase P4, and SHALL record the observable trace of each run. The terms are:
   - H is the least common multiple of the periods and MITs, in Kernel time-base ticks, that the Task_Model records for the model's Tasks. Each run covers N_run = max(100, ⌈10 s / H⌉) hyperperiods (PAR-09).
   - The JVM run uses ART's static scheduler (`art.scheduling.static.StaticScheduler`). ART's round-robin scheduler is not used because it dispatches by `Art.time()`, which reads `System.nanoTime()`, and ART's legacy scheduler is not used because it runs each thread on its own Java thread (ASM-08). The run starts from a generated system test in the form of HAMR's generated `SystemTests.scala` at PAR-02: `Art.initializePhase`, then `Art.computePhase` with an `ISZCommandProvider` holding `Hstep(N_run)` and `Stop()`, the scheduler being built by `Schedulers.getStaticSchedulerH` with the JVM_Schedule. HAMR's default static schedule is not used, because it has one slot per thread whatever the thread's period, and HAMR's default command provider is not used, because it never stops.
   - The JVM_Schedule is the slot sequence of one hyperperiod. It holds one slot for each Release instant O_T + i × P_T in [0, H) of each Periodic thread T and, for each Sporadic thread, one slot after each slot of the thread that is the source of its Dispatch trigger. The slots are in the order in which IronOxide would start the Jobs if every Job took no time: by Release instant, then by Priority with the higher first, then by instance path (DEV-07). A Sporadic thread's slot takes the instant of its source's slot and is placed by the same rule among the slots that follow its source's slot (DD candidate; ORQ-12).
   - The initialization record of a thread holds the initial value of each of its output data ports and GUMBO state variables.
   - The Job record of the n-th Job of thread T holds T's instance path and n. For each input port, it holds the frozen input that Get Value presented: for a data port, the value and, on IronOxide, its freshness; for an event data port, the messages, oldest first; for an event port, the count. For each output port, it holds the value put, an event put, or no put. It also holds the values of T's GUMBO state variables at the start and at the return of the compute code. On IronOxide the record also holds the Port_API counts of R19.1 as the compute code read them, and the Kernel time base (`rsk::now()`, ticks, PR-26) that the Trace_Body read as its first and as its last operation, written s(T, n) and e(T, n). On the JVM the record also holds its position in the run's single sequence of Jobs.
   - The observable trace of a run consists of the initialization records and, for each thread, its Job records in dispatch-index order. For an IronOxide run it also includes the Kernel event log at the end of the run with its overflow count (ORQ-38 under QEMU; the HIL_Rig's log reader on the rig). The trace orders each thread's Jobs by dispatch index, not by wall-clock time.

   *Evidence: `diff-jvm-qemu-<model>`, `diff-jvm-rig-<model>`, `test-jvm-schedule` (for each corpus model, the JVM_Schedule equals the start order of a zero-execution-time simulation of the Task_Model over one hyperperiod).*
3. THE Trace_Comparator SHALL compare the observable trace of the JVM run with that of each IronOxide run under the comparison relation that design.md states. That relation SHALL contain every row of Table 32-1, and design.md may narrow the tolerance of a row but not widen it (DD candidate; ORQ-12). The relation thus requires at least the following:
   - for every event data connection with no lost message in either trace, the same received value sequence in both traces, with no loss, duplication or reordering (CR-04, CR-05);
   - for every data connection, that each frozen value is the initial value or a value the source put, within the window that CR-03 derives from the two Tasks' Release instants and deadlines and from DEV-01;
   - for every Periodic thread, Job counts that differ by at most 1 at every hyperperiod boundary (CR-01).

   *Evidence: `diff-jvm-qemu-<model>`, `diff-jvm-rig-<model>`, `mut-diff`.*
4. IF the two traces differ under the comparison relation, or one trace breaks a rule that Table 32-1 states for that trace alone, THEN THE Trace_Comparator SHALL report the first difference, and THE Build_System SHALL fail the run. The report SHALL give the difference's Table 32-1 row, its connection (source and destination port instance paths) or thread, the dispatch indices and the recorded values or counts in both traces, and the IronOxide time stamps, together with the number of differences found under each row. The first difference is the one whose IronOxide Job has the smallest s(T, n) or, for a difference that involves no IronOxide Job, the one earliest in the JVM's sequence of Jobs. *Evidence: `diff-jvm-qemu-<model>`, `diff-jvm-rig-<model>`, `mut-diff` (planted defects in the generated Port_Glue, App_Declaration or Rust Trace_Body of a test build, or in the JVM_Schedule, at least one for each of rows CR-01 to CR-07 and CR-09, each reported under the row it targets).*
5. THE Trace_Comparator SHALL evaluate, on both traces, the GUMBOX executable that HAMR's JVM back end at PAR-02 generates for each GUMBO initialize, compute, handler and integration clause of each thread: each initialize clause on the thread's initialization record, and each other clause on each of the thread's Job records. It SHALL report:
   - each violated clause, with the trace (JVM, QEMU or HIL_Rig), the thread's instance path, the dispatch index and the clause identifier;
   - for each clause, the number of Jobs on which it was evaluated and the number on which it was violated;
   - each Job on which the executable halted or threw;
   - each GUMBO clause of another kind, as not evaluated.

   A violated integration clause is also a difference under criterion 4. Criterion 1 makes every value put satisfy the source port's guarantee clauses, and the Connection_Obligations of R24.1 make these imply the destination port's assume clauses. *Evidence: `diff-jvm-qemu-<model>`, `diff-jvm-rig-<model>`.*
6. THE Verification_Plan SHALL list each candidate model of criterion 7 that is not in the differential corpus, with the condition of criterion 7 that it fails. For a model recorded as not compared under criterion 8, the entry SHALL also give the failing step at PAR-02 and the finding (F-2, F-3) or open question (ORQ-15) that the failure falls under. *Evidence: `doc-verification-plan`, `check-diff-corpus`.*
7. THE Build_System SHALL compare at least PAR-09 models of the differential corpus in each run of the verification job, under QEMU from Phase P3 and on the HIL_Rig from Phase P4. It SHALL fail the job when fewer are compared, naming each candidate model not compared and the condition it fails. A candidate model is one that the Verification_Plan lists for the corpus: a model written for this project, or a Model_Generator model listed by its Model_Generator version and per-model seed (R31.7). The two study models of `docs/hamr_study.md` are not in the corpus, because `structs_arrays` fails HAMR's JVM steps (finding F-2: its JVM project does not compile) and `isolette` has devices and its JVM system test halts on `None.get`, an unset input or device value (finding F-3); the corpus is therefore written or generated. A candidate model belongs to the differential corpus when all of these hold:
   - the Backend accepts it, its only processor is the IronOxide_Processor (MAP-27), and it names no peripheral (Table 2-1), so that R18.6 runs it under QEMU;
   - it has no Resource (MAP-10), since HAMR's back ends do not support shared data (`docs/hamr_study.md` section 9), and no device (MAP-20; ORQ-55);
   - every in port of every thread is the destination of a connection from a thread, since nothing sets an unconnected in port in the JVM run (finding F-3 is an unset input);
   - every Sporadic thread has exactly one in event or event data port, which is its Dispatch trigger, because ART at PAR-02 dispatches a Sporadic thread whenever any of its in event ports holds a message (`ArtNative_Ext.shouldDispatch`);
   - no thread has `IronOxide::MIT_Policy => discard` (DEV-06; ORQ-54);
   - the Task_Model's `conversions` list no period, MIT, offset or deadline (IronOxide R21.2), and every offset is less than its Task's period;
   - the type of every data and event data port has a provenance component: an integer component of at least 32 bits that no GUMBO integration clause and no data type invariant reads;
   - `IronOxide::Operating_Duration` is at least N_run × H plus the largest relative deadline of the model's Tasks;
   - the JVM_Schedule of one hyperperiod has at most 10,000 slots (proposal);
   - HAMR's JVM steps of criterion 8 succeed.

   Every row of Table 32-1 other than CR-08 SHALL apply to at least one model of the corpus. *Evidence: `check-diff-corpus`.*
8. IF, for a candidate model, HAMR's JVM back end at PAR-02 exits with a non-zero status, or the Slang project with the Trace_Bodies does not compile, or the JVM run ends other than by the `Stop()` command (an exception or a `halt`, as in finding F-3), THEN THE Build_System SHALL record the model's `diff-jvm-qemu-<model>` and `diff-jvm-rig-<model>` as not compared, with the failing step, its exit status and its first error message. It SHALL NOT record either as passed, and SHALL NOT count the model toward PAR-09. *Evidence: `check-diff-corpus`, `test-diff-not-compared` (a candidate with a GUMBO spec function that has a developer body, finding F-2, is recorded as not compared).*
9. IF an IronOxide run of a corpus model meets any of these conditions, THEN THE Build_System SHALL report the run as failed, naming the condition, and not as passed or not compared:
   - the trace variant was not accepted by every IronOxide tool of R18.2;
   - under QEMU, QEMU did not exit by itself within the wall-clock limit of R18.6;
   - on the HIL_Rig, the binary read back from the target board differs from the Build_Manifest's hash (IronOxide R48.4);
   - the Kernel event log at the end of the run was not obtained;
   - the log has a non-zero overflow count;
   - the log lacks the operating-duration end (`EV_OPERATING_END`), or records it earlier than N_run × H plus the largest relative deadline;
   - the log holds an entry of a kind other than `EV_OPERATING_END`, `EV_SAFE_STATE`, the `EV_MIT_VIOLATION` and `EV_ENDPOINT_FULL` entries that rows CR-02 and CR-05 tolerate (unlike R18.6's QEMU pass rule, which fails on any such entry; UD-26), and, on the HIL_Rig only, `EV_DEBUGGER` where design.md's answer to ORQ-53 attaches a debugger. Every fault, Overrun (DEV-08), deadline miss, release overlap and rejected message therefore fails the run (IronOxide `crates/rsk-logic/src/logic/log.rs`);
   - the Job record of a Job in the compared window W (Table 32-1) is missing, truncated or out of dispatch-index order, or the record channel of ORQ-53 reports a lost record.

   *Evidence: `diff-jvm-qemu-<model>`, `diff-jvm-rig-<model>`, `test-diff-run-validity` (one injected case per condition).*

**Table 32-1 — The comparison relation.** Notation:
- A row applies to a connection from port q of thread S to port p of thread D, or to the thread it names. T(n) is the n-th Job of thread T in a trace (n ≥ 1).
- In the JVM trace, T(n) ≺ U(m) means that T(n) comes before U(m) in the run's sequence of Jobs.
- In an IronOxide trace, s(T, n) and e(T, n) are as criterion 2 defines them. r(T, n) = O_T + (n − 1) × P_T is the n-th Release instant of a Periodic thread (PR-10). P_T (period or MIT), O_T (offset) and Dl_T (relative deadline) are in Kernel time-base ticks as the Task_Model records them.
- A put-Job of q is a Job of S that put on q. prov(v) is as criterion 1 defines it; the initial value of a data port has prov 0.
- The sent sequence of q is the values put on q in Job order, or for an event port the events put on q. The received sequence of p is the messages frozen on p, concatenated in Job order.
- The compared window W holds all Jobs of the N_run hyperperiods in the JVM trace, and the Jobs with s(T, n) < N_run × H in an IronOxide trace.
- A message is lost in three cases. It is a JVM loss when ART overwrote it: ART at PAR-02 keeps at most one message in each input port whatever the port's `Queue_Size` (`ArtNative_Ext.sendOutput` replaces the message and `receiveInput` takes it). It is also lost when an overflow policy dropped it (R10.9), or when its send returned `Full`.
- A message is in transit when a Job in W put it and it was neither lost nor frozen by a Job in W.
- T(n) is Job-equal across the two traces when T(1) to T(n) froze equal input values in both. Values are compared as the AADL values they denote, without freshness and Port_API counts.

DEV-06 and DEV-09 are exercised only once ORQ-54 and ORQ-55 are resolved (criterion 7). DEV-08 has no row, because an Overrun fails the run (criterion 9).

| Row | Applies to | JVM trace (ART at PAR-02) | IronOxide trace (Table M-2 deviations tolerated) | Between the two traces |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| CR-01 | each Periodic thread T (MAP-01) | for 1 ≤ k ≤ N_run, the first k hyperperiods hold exactly k × H / P_T Jobs of T | each T(n) in W has r(T, n) ≤ s(T, n) and e(T, n) ≤ r(T, n) + Dl_T; for 1 ≤ k ≤ N_run, the Jobs with s(T, n) < k × H number k × H / P_T or one fewer (DEV-01) | at each boundary k × H, the Job counts of T differ by at most 1 |
| CR-02 | each Sporadic thread D whose Dispatch trigger p has the source S.q (MAP-02 with MAP-11, MAP-13 or MAP-15) | D has one Job after each put-Job S(k) of q whose message is not a JVM loss, in the order of those put-Jobs | the Jobs of D in W number the put-Jobs of q in W, minus the `EV_MIT_VIOLATION` entries in W that the Kernel event log attributes to the connection (DEV-04, DEV-10; ORQ-54), minus at most 1 event in transit; for each n, at least n put-Jobs S(k) of q have e(S, k) ≤ s(D, n); under MAP-11, D(n) presents the count 1 on p (R19.1) | — |
| CR-03 | each data connection (MAP-14; MAP-16 and R26.2 hops, with drained sampling, DEV-02) | the frozen value of D(n) has as prov the largest k such that S(k) is a put-Job of q and S(k) ≺ D(n), and 0 when there is none (DEV-03) | prov(D(n)) ≥ prov(D(n − 1)), with prov(D(0)) = 0; prov(D(n)) ≤ the largest k with S(k) a put-Job of q and e(S, k) ≤ s(D, n) (DEV-01); prov(D(n)) ≥ the largest k with S(k) a put-Job of q and s(S, k) + Dl_S + Dl_D < s(D, n), and, where S and D are both Periodic, ≥ the largest such k with r(S, k) + Dl_S < r(D, n); the value is fresh exactly when prov(D(n)) > prov(D(n − 1)); prov 0 presents the initial value, not fresh (R20.5); no `EV_ENDPOINT_FULL` entry concerns a MAP-16 Endpoint (R12.2) | — (CR-09 compares the values) |
| CR-04 | each event data connection within a Partition (MAP-13) | D(n) froze the value of the latest put-Job S(k) of q with D(n − 1) ≺ S(k) ≺ D(n), or nothing when there is none; the other values put in that interval are JVM losses | the received sequence is the sent sequence of W without the messages in transit and without those that the destination port's overflow policy dropped (R10.9); for DropNewest and Error, the number of missing messages is the increase of the source's discard count; for DropOldest, a message v may be missing only if at least `Queue_Size` later put-Jobs S(k) of q have e(S, k) ≤ s(D, n′), D(n′) being the first Job of D that froze a message with prov > prov(v); each message v frozen by D(n) has e(S, prov(v)) ≤ s(D, n) (DEV-01); D(n) froze at most 1, `Dequeued_Items` or `Queue_Size` messages for OneItem, MultipleItems or AllItems (R10.3) | where neither trace lost a message of the connection, the received value sequences are equal over their common length, and their lengths differ by at most the number of messages in transit in the two traces |
| CR-05 | each event or event data connection across Partitions (MAP-15, MAP-17; R26.2 hops) | as CR-04 | as CR-04, except that: the missing messages are exactly the sends that returned `Full` (DEV-05), each matched by one increase of the source's refused-send count (R11.5) and one `EV_ENDPOINT_FULL` entry against S's Partition; no send returns `Full` where the destination port has DropOldest (MAP-17, R11.10); a message whose arrival was an MIT_Violation is frozen by a later Job of D (DEV-10); D(n) froze at most n_recv messages (R11.2); for an event connection, token counts replace value sequences (R11.3) | as CR-04 |
| CR-06 | each event connection to a Periodic thread within a Partition (MAP-12) | the count of D(n) is 1 when some put-Job S(k) of q has D(n − 1) ≺ S(k) ≺ D(n), and 0 otherwise | the sum of the counts of D(1) to D(n) lies between the number of put-Jobs S(k) of q with s(S, k) + Dl_S + Dl_D < s(D, n) and the number with e(S, k) ≤ s(D, n) | — |
| CR-07 | each pair of Periodic threads T and U of equal Priority, T first in instance-path order (DEV-07) | the JVM_Schedule places T's slot before U's at each Release instant they share | s(T, n) ≤ s(U, m) whenever r(T, n) = r(U, m) | — |
| CR-08 | each Task released by an interrupt (MAP-20, DEV-09), on the HIL_Rig only, once ORQ-55 is resolved | one Job per stimulus edge | for the m ≥ 1 stimulus edges e_1 < … < e_m in W that the logic analyzer captures, the Jobs number at most m and at least 1 plus the number of j > 1 with e_j − e_(j−1) > 2 × MIT + Δ_stamp, where Δ_stamp is `delta_stamp_cycles` of `hil-rig.toml` and all times are in seconds (DD candidate) | — |

### Requirement 33: Proof mutations (Phase P2 Creusot and the Verus proof of R12.2; P4 TV_Checker and contract correspondence; P5 frame parser)

**User Story:** As a verification engineer, I want every proof of this project to reject planted defects, so that no proof is vacuous.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Proof_Mutation_Register SHALL hold the planted defects that PAR-17 requires for each row of Table 33-1, from the Phase that the row gives: for each conjunct of the row's conjunct list (criterion 5), at least one mutation that names that conjunct in the form of criterion 6. The mutations of row PM-08 are the Connection_Obligation mutants that R24.2 cites. This applies to this project's proofs the check that IronOxide R52.2 applies to IronOxide's own specifications (IronOxide Verification_Plan 9.2). *Evidence: `check-registers` (fails naming each conjunct, of a row whose Phase has been reached, that no mutation names, and each mutation whose `artifact` names no row of Table 33-1 or whose `conjunct` names no conjunct of its row).*
2. WHEN `cargo xtask verify` runs, THE Build_System SHALL apply each mutation of the Proof_Mutation_Register to its own copy of this repository's tree, with no other mutation applied to that copy. It SHALL run the prover that the mutation's `prover` names on the crate that its `package` names, and SHALL count the mutation as killed only when all of the conditions below hold. IronOxide runs its `cargo xtask mutations` outside `cargo xtask verify` (IronOxide Verification_Plan 9.2); here, by contrast, every mutation runs in every verification job, with a neutralized run per mutation where it has `neutralize` (UD-17).
   - Baseline: the unmutated proof of the mutation's row passed in the same job. The App_Prover ran at the versions and why3find settings of R21.2, or Verus and Z3 ran at the pins of R29.3.
   - Mutant: for a mutation with `model`, the copy first holds the files that the unmutated Backend generates for that corpus model, together with the model's committed developer code. The mutant differs from the copy only in that the one occurrence of `find` in `file` is replaced by `replace`.
   - Rejection: the prover type-checks the mutant, exits with failure, and reports as not discharged at least one goal of the function or lemma that carries the targeted conjunct (criterion 5), and its output contains every `expect` string. This holds in each configuration (IronOxide_Target and set of Cargo features) in which R21.2 or R29.3 runs the prover on that crate. A goal counts as not discharged when no prover of the pinned set discharges it within the pinned limits. A compile, parse or type error makes the result unexpected, not a kill.
   - Attribution, for a mutation with `neutralize`: in each such configuration, the same prover runs on the mutant with the one occurrence of `neutralize.find` also replaced by `true`, and it discharges every goal of that function or lemma. This shows that the mutant violates the targeted conjunct and no other conjunct of that function or lemma.
   - Attribution, for an implicit-goal conjunct (criterion 5): every goal that the prover reports as not discharged belongs to the function that contains the planted change. Where the prover's output names the kind of check, each such goal is of the targeted kind (ORQ-56).

   *Evidence: `mut-proof-tv`, `mut-proof-sampling-capacity`, `mut-proof-correspondence`, `mut-proof-glue`, `mut-proof-validators`, `mut-proof-codecs`, `mut-proof-connections` and `mut-proof-contracts`. Each item records, besides what R39.2 records, for every mutation: the verdict, each configuration run, the goals not discharged in the mutant and in the neutralized run, the hashes of the mutated file before and after the change, and the prover's log. The register holds no verdict, as in IronOxide, whose runner writes one log per mutation to `target/xtask-mutations/<id>.log`. Also `test-proof-mutation-runner`.*
3. IF a mutation of the Proof_Mutation_Register meets any of the conditions below, THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail `check-registers`, naming the mutation and each condition that it meets:
   - it lacks a field that criterion 6 requires;
   - its `id` is the `id` of another mutation;
   - its `find` equals its `replace`;
   - its `find` or its `neutralize.find` does not occur exactly once in its file (for a mutation with `model`, in the files that the unmutated Backend generates for that corpus model);
   - its `find` occurrence overlaps the `neutralize.find` occurrence, or lies in text that the "Planted change" cell of its row of Table 33-1 excludes;
   - none of its `expect` strings identifies a goal as criterion 6 states.

   *Evidence: `check-registers` (one fixture register per condition).*
4. WHEN the conjunct list that criterion 5 derives for a row of Table 33-1 differs from the list recorded in that row's mutation Evidence_Item of the last passing verification job (the row's artifact is added, or a conjunct is added, removed or has changed text), THE Build_System SHALL fail the verification job, naming each added, removed and changed conjunct. The job SHALL keep failing until three things hold: every added or changed conjunct is named by a mutation whose `neutralize.find` is the conjunct's current text (or, for an implicit-goal conjunct, by a mutation without `neutralize`); no mutation names a removed conjunct; and criterion 1 holds. *Evidence: `check-registers` (fixtures: a conjunct added to an `ensures`, a conjunct whose text changed, a conjunct removed, and an artifact without mutations).*
5. THE Build_System SHALL derive each row's conjunct list by the rules below, SHALL record the list in the row's mutation Evidence_Item, and SHALL give each conjunct an identifier:
   - Hand-written rows (PM-01 to PM-03): the top-level statements are the `ensures` clauses of the functions and lemmas that the row's "Top-level statement" cell describes. A statement splits into conjuncts:
     - at separate `ensures` clauses;
     - at a top-level `&&` or `&&&` (Verus) or `&&` (Pearlite);
     - inside a top-level `forall`, or in the conclusion of a top-level `==>`, at the conjunctions of the body, each conjunct keeping the binders and the premise;
     - at an equivalence (`<==>`, or `==` between Booleans), into its two implications;
     - at a call of a non-recursive specification or logic function defined in this project, which is replaced by its body and split again by the same rules.

     A disjunction, an `exists`, an `if` or `match` expression, and a call of a recursive function or of a function defined outside this project (`vstd`, `creusot_std`, IronOxide's crates) are not split. A conjunct's identifier is the path of the function or lemma whose `ensures` clause it comes from, which carries it, and the conjunct's 1-based position in the textual order of that function's or lemma's conjuncts.
   - Generated rows (PM-04 to PM-10): the conjuncts are the items of the row's "Conjuncts" cell. Each item counts once, however many instances the Backend generates for any model. Its identifier is the row and the item. In the mutation's corpus model, the conjunct is carried by the generated or developer function whose contract states it.
   - Implicit-goal conjuncts: where a row's "Conjuncts" cell lists them, the row has one conjunct for each of the following: the absence of panics; the absence of arithmetic overflow, lossy `as` conversions included; the absence of out-of-bounds access; the Resource invariant after each Critical_Section, for rows whose functions contain one; and each loop's annotated bound, for rows whose functions contain a loop.

   *Evidence: `test-conjunct-split` (fixtures, each with its expected list: separate clauses, a `&&&` block, a conjunction under `forall` and under `==>`, an equivalence, a specification function of this project, a recursive one, a disjunction, an `exists`, and a call into `vstd`), `check-registers`.*
6. THE Proof_Mutation_Register SHALL be a TOML file in `docs/` in the format of IronOxide's `docs/proof_mutations.toml` at PAR-01 (`schema = 1`, one `[[mutation]]` table per mutation). Each mutation SHALL have:
   - IronOxide's fields, with the meaning that IronOxide's `xtask/src/mutations.rs` gives them:
     - `id`;
     - `kills`, the statement that the mutation must trip, in words;
     - `file`;
     - `find`, exact text;
     - `replace`;
     - `expect`, substrings that the prover's output must all contain;
     - `package`, the crate that the prover runs on;
     - `prover`, `verus` or `creusot`.

     `package` and `prover` are stated in every mutation, since IronOxide's defaults (`rsk-kernel` and `verus`) name no crate of this project.
   - `artifact`, the mutation's row of Table 33-1, and `conjunct`, the conjunct's identifier under criterion 5.
   - `neutralize`, for every conjunct that is not an implicit-goal conjunct: a table with `file` and `find`, where `find` is the exact text of the targeted conjunct. Whether replacing a conjunct's text by `true` always leaves the contract well-formed is ORQ-56; a neutralized run that does not type-check makes the mutation's result unexpected (criterion 2).
   - `model`, for a generated row (PM-04 to PM-10): a corpus model whose AIR_Model and developer code are committed in this repository. For such a mutation, `file` and `neutralize.file` are relative to the root of the Application that the Backend generates for that model. Otherwise they are relative to this repository's root.
   - Among the `expect` strings, at least one that identifies the goal. For Verus, this is the text of the targeted conjunct, or of the clause that contains it, as Verus prints it with the error (the header of IronOxide's register). For the App_Prover, it is the name of the goal of the function that carries the conjunct, as the App_Prover prints it (`vc_send_req` in the test of IronOxide's `xtask/src/mutations.rs`). A file name alone does not identify a goal.

   IronOxide's register reader reads only its own fields, so the added fields leave the file readable by it. *Evidence: `check-registers`.*
7. IF a mutation is not killed under criterion 2, THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail the verification job and SHALL fail every later verification job until either of the following holds: that mutation is killed; or the Verification_Plan's change log (R41.2) holds an entry for it that states its verdict, its cause and the `id` of the mutation that replaces it for the same conjunct. The cause is either a defect of the proof or of its specification, with the correction, or the argument that the mutant does not violate the conjunct. The failure report SHALL name:
   - the mutation's `id`, row, conjunct and, where it has one, its corpus model;
   - its verdict: survived (the prover discharged every goal of the mutant), or unexpected, naming the first condition of criterion 2 that failed;
   - the goals not discharged;
   - the prover's first error line.

   *Evidence: `test-proof-mutation-runner` (fixtures: a surviving mutant, a mutant rejected only on a goal of another function, a mutant that does not compile, a mutant whose neutralized run fails, a mutant whose baseline proof fails, and a surviving mutation removed from the register without a change-log entry), `check-registers`.*

**Table 33-1 — Proof artifacts and the basis of their planted defects.** There is one row per proof artifact of this project. "Conjuncts" is the list against which criterion 5 counts PAR-17. "Planted change" states where a mutation's `find` may and may not lie (criterion 3). "Phase" is the Phase of the requirement that names the artifact; criterion 1 applies from that Phase.

| Row | Proof artifact (requirements) | Prover | Top-level statement | Conjuncts | Planted change | Mutation Evidence_Item | Phase |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PM-01 | `proof-tv-core` (R29.1, R29.3) | Verus and Z3 at the pins of R29.3 | The TV_Checker core returns Accept only if the Mapping_Relation holds | The conjuncts that criterion 5 derives from the postcondition of the core's entry function. Each lies inside one conjunct of the Mapping_Relation (R29.2), the unit that R29.4 reports and R30.1 mutates, and each conjunct of R29.2 contains at least one of them | In the core's executable code; not in a specification function | `mut-proof-tv` | P4 |

### Part G — Robot Case Study

### Requirement 34: Two-processor models (Phase P5)

**User Story:** As a system modeler, I want to model the robot's two processors in one model and generate each side with its own back end, so that the interface between them has one definition.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN the Deployment_Splitter splits a model with one IronOxide_Processor and one Peer_Processor that criterion 4 does not refuse into an IronOxide sub-model (the sub-model of the IronOxide_Processor) and a peer sub-model (the sub-model of the Peer_Processor) as criterion 6 states, THE Deployment_Splitter SHALL replace each cross-processor port connection instance c, that is each port connection instance of the AIR_Model from a port s of a thread on one processor A to a port d of a thread on the other processor B (a thread being on the processor to which its process is bound, directly or through a virtual processor), by exactly two connection instances and no other: in the sub-model of A, one from s to an in port that the Deployment_Splitter inserts on A's sending Serial_Bridge thread (criterion 8), which stands for d; and in the sub-model of B, one from an out port that it inserts on B's receiving Serial_Bridge thread, which stands for s, to d. An out port with n cross-processor connection instances therefore gets n bridge paths, one per connection instance (R35.1). Each inserted port has the feature category (data, event or event data port) and the data classifier of the port it stands for, and exactly those of the properties `Communication_Properties::Queue_Size`, `Communication_Properties::Overflow_Handling_Protocol`, `Thread_Properties::Dequeue_Protocol` and `Thread_Properties::Dequeued_Items` that the port it stands for has, with the same values. Each inserted connection instance has every property of c except `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Connection_Binding`, and comes with the declared connections that its connection references name, inserted in the way HAMR's MonitorInjector inserts the declared connections and connection instances of its monitor at PAR-02 (`hamr/codegen/shared/.../microkit/plugins/monitors/MonitorInjector.scala`). Each inserted port and connection carries the source position of the port it stands for (a port) or of c (a connection), and a marker property, which design.md names (DD candidate), whose value is the instance path of c and the instance path of the bus that c's `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Connection_Binding` names, so that a reader of either sub-model finds the bus of each bridge path (R35.1, R35.8 (a)). *Evidence: `test-splitter`, `pbt-splitter`, `check-splitter-recompose-<model>`.*
2. THE Deployment_Splitter SHALL write each peer sub-model so that HAMR's `sireum hamr codegen` at PAR-02, unmodified and run on the sub-model's AIR JSON file with no edit to that file, ends with exit status 0 and reports no message of severity error for the platform P, where P is the Pi_Side platform that the Verification_Plan records as the resolution of ORQ-09, or, until the Verification_Plan records one, each of the JVM, Linux and ros2 platforms. At PAR-02 none of these back ends selects threads by processor binding: Arsit (JVM, Linux) reads bindings only to collect processor timing properties (`hamr/codegen/jvm/.../arsit/util/SchedulerUtil.scala`), and ros2 does not read them (finding F-4). The peer sub-model therefore contains no thread on the IronOxide_Processor (criterion 6). *Evidence: `test-splitter-peer-codegen` (the Robot_Model and every two-processor model of the corpus, on each platform P).*
3. THE Deployment_Splitter SHALL keep every GUMBO subclause and every property of a component, unchanged as AIR values, in the sub-model that holds the component, apart from the clauses and functions that this criterion attaches; and SHALL attach to the GUMBO subclause of the Serial_Bridge thread that carries each port inserted by criterion 1 the GUMBO integration clauses of the port that the inserted port stands for (the assume clauses of d on an inserted in port, the guarantee clauses of s on an inserted out port), each with its expression and source position unchanged, every reference to the original port replaced by a reference to the inserted port, under an identifier that design.md derives from the clause's identifier and the instance path of c (DD candidate), together with every function of the original thread's GUMBO subclause that the clause calls directly or through other functions, renamed by the same rule. *Evidence: `test-splitter`, `test-splitter-gumbo` (a Peer_Processor port with two assume clauses, a clause that calls a GUMBO function, and two attached clauses whose original identifiers are equal), `proof-tv-core`.*
4. IF the model that the Deployment_Splitter is run on has any of the following:
   - (a) a number of processor component instances other than two, or no IronOxide_Processor among them (R1.3);
   - (b) a process with no `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Processor_Binding`, a process or thread whose binding names both processors, or a thread whose binding names a processor other than the one its process is bound to;
   - (c) a data access or subprogram access connection between a component on one processor and a component on the other;
   - (d) a cross-processor port connection instance (criterion 1) for which the AIR_Model records no `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Connection_Binding`, or records one that names no bus with `IronOxide::Serial_Link` (ORQ-57);
   - (e) a device with port connection instances to threads on both processors;
   - (f) on either processor, a number other than one of threads marked as the receiving Serial_Bridge thread (`IronOxide::Serial_Bridge_Role` `Receive` or `Receive_And_Send`), or a number other than one of threads marked as the sending Serial_Bridge thread (`Send` or `Receive_And_Send`), or the two marked threads in different processes (criterion 8, UD-15);

   THEN THE Deployment_Splitter SHALL refuse the model and write no sub-model, evaluating every case over the whole model in one run and reporting one diagnostic per case and element in the form, order and documents that R3.1, R3.2, R3.3 and R3.7 state for the Backend. Each diagnostic names MAP-27 with the label of its case and the rule that Table M-1 gives for that label: case (a) as MAP-27 (a) (IronOxide NG-01; NG-05); case (b) as MAP-27 (g); case (c) as MAP-27 (c) with PR-30 for a data access, or as MAP-27 (d) with PR-32 for a subprogram access; case (d) as MAP-27 (b); case (e) as MAP-27 (h); case (f) as MAP-27 (i). For example, a case (e) diagnostic names MAP-27 (h) with "Backend restriction (R34.4 (e))". *Evidence: `mut-refusals` (at least one AIR mutant per case), `test-splitter-refusals`.*
5. WHEN the Build_System runs the TV_Checker (Requirement 29) on the Application that the Backend generated from an IronOxide sub-model, THE TV_Checker SHALL check its Task_Model against the Semantic_Model that the Semantic_Model_Extractor writes from the original two-processor AIR_Model, not from the sub-model, and SHALL accept the pair only if the Mapping_Relation holds with the Serial_Bridge substitution of criterion 1 as one of its conjuncts: each cross-processor port connection instance c that has an end at a thread on the IronOxide_Processor is related to exactly the Task_Model items that Table M-1 prescribes for the connection instance that criterion 1 puts in place of c in the IronOxide sub-model (between that thread and the Serial_Bridge thread that criterion 1 names for that side), and to no other item. *Evidence: `check-tv-robot`, `mut-translation` (in the IronOxide sub-model, a bridge path dropped, duplicated, or attached to the wrong Serial_Bridge thread or port, each rejected naming the substitution conjunct).*
6. WHEN the Deployment_Splitter writes the sub-model of a processor P (the IronOxide_Processor or the Peer_Processor), THE Deployment_Splitter SHALL write it as one AIR_Model JSON file, without reading or writing AADL or SysML v2 text. HAMR's AIR JSON printer at PAR-02 (`JSON.fromAadl` in `hamr/air/shared/.../ir/JSON.scala`) prints the file from the AIR value that HAMR's AIR JSON reader at PAR-02 (`JSON.toAadl`) read from the original AIR_Model file. The file contains exactly these elements of the original:
   - P, and every virtual processor bound to P;
   - every process bound to P, directly or through such a virtual processor, with every component, feature, declared connection, connection instance, flow specification, property and annex subclause inside it;
   - every device whose port connection instances all end at threads of those processes;
   - every component instance that is not a processor, virtual processor, process or device and lies inside none of them, such as the root system instance, the systems that contain kept components, buses and memories;
   - every declared connection and connection instance outside those processes whose two ends lie in kept components;
   - all data component classifiers and annex libraries of the original AIR_Model.

   The file also contains the elements that criteria 1 and 3 insert, and no other element. Each kept element has the instance path and the AIR value, source position included, that it has in the original AIR_Model, except that a kept component instance holds only the kept and inserted subcomponents, declared connections and connection instances. *Evidence: `test-splitter`, `pbt-splitter`, `check-splitter-recompose-<model>`.*
7. WHEN the Deployment_Splitter has written the two sub-models of a model, THE Build_System SHALL check them with a recomposition tool that shares no code with the Deployment_Splitter other than HAMR's AIR JSON reader and that the Trust_Base_Register lists, and SHALL fail the run unless both of the following hold:
   - (i) the union of the two sub-models, which merges by instance path the component instances that both hold and leaves out every element that criterion 1 or 3 inserted, equals the original AIR_Model without its cross-processor port connection instances and without the declared connections that only those connection instances reference, with every list of both values ordered by instance path before the comparison;
   - (ii) for each cross-processor port connection instance c of the original, from s to d, the sub-model of s's processor holds exactly one inserted connection instance whose marker names c, from s to an inserted in port, and the sub-model of d's processor holds exactly one, from an inserted out port to d; and no inserted connection instance's marker names any other connection instance.

   *Evidence: `check-splitter-recompose-<model>` (every two-processor model of the corpus, the Robot_Model included), `pbt-splitter` (over PAR-08 Model_Generator two-processor models per run), `mut-splitter` (each planted Deployment_Splitter defect that the Translation_Mutation_Register lists makes the check fail; the list includes at least a dropped, duplicated or misdirected bridge path, and a changed or dropped property, GUMBO clause or source position).*
8. THE Deployment_Splitter SHALL insert the ports of criterion 1 only on the Serial_Bridge threads that the model declares (UD-15). On each of the two processors these are the one thread that the model marks as the receiving Serial_Bridge thread and the one thread that it marks as the sending Serial_Bridge thread, both in one process bound to that processor. The mark is the thread property `IronOxide::Serial_Bridge_Role` of the IronOxide_Property_Set (Table 2-1 of Requirement 2), an enumeration with no default: `Receive` marks the receiving Serial_Bridge thread, `Send` the sending one, and `Receive_And_Send` one thread that carries both marks. A thread without the property is not a Serial_Bridge thread. On the IronOxide_Processor, the Backend refuses `Receive_And_Send` (R35.8 (c), UD-33), because the MCU_Side Serial_Bridge consists of two Tasks (R35.7); on the Peer_Processor it stays allowed. Criterion 6 lets the Deployment_Splitter add no thread, process or property value other than its markers of criterion 1, so the model alone states each Serial_Bridge thread's `IronOxide::Serial_Bridge_Role`, Dispatch_Protocol, Period, Compute_Execution_Time and `Thread_Properties::Priority`, and the IronOxide_Property_Set and `Memory_Properties` values of its process. As a result:
   - Requirements 2, 5, 6, 7 and 17 apply to the Serial_Bridge threads on the IronOxide_Processor as to every other thread bound to it, so R7.3 refuses a model in which the other threads have `Thread_Properties::Priority` and a Serial_Bridge thread does not;
   - R35.5 and R36.2 constrain the Criticality_Level that the model gives the Serial_Bridge's process.

   *Evidence: `test-splitter`, `test-map-06` (a two-processor model in which only the Serial_Bridge threads lack `Thread_Properties::Priority` is refused under R7.3), `check-splitter-recompose-<model>`.*

### Requirement 35: The serial link bridge (Phase P5)

**User Story:** As a safety engineer, I want the serial link framed, checked and decoded by generated code with a proven parser on the MCU, so that corrupted or hostile bytes from the Pi cannot reach the motor controller unchecked.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Serial_Bridge SHALL send each message that it takes from a cross-processor port connection (R34.1; on the MCU_Side, for a data port, each frozen value that the Port_API reports as fresh, R12.1) as exactly one frame, at the line settings of PAR-12 (1,000,000 baud, 8 data bits, no parity, 1 stop bit; R2.4 requires the baud rate of the bus's `IronOxide::Serial_Link` to equal it). A frame consists of five fields: a frame type that identifies the connection; a w-bit sequence number (criterion 9); the payload length in bytes; a payload; and a CRC over the four preceding fields. The payload is the message's Wire_Type value (R14.1), byte for byte as the IronOxide_Target stores it in memory, at most the frame payload bound of the bus's `IronOxide::Serial_Link` (Table 2-1; at most PAR-13, 64 bytes), and empty for an event port connection. The whole frame is encoded by a byte stuffing that excludes the frame delimiter byte and is followed by one frame delimiter. design.md's frame grammar states the field widths, the stuffing, the delimiter value, and the CRC's polynomial, width and initial value (DD candidate; ORQ-59). The Verification_Plan states the CRC's Hamming distance at the maximum encoded frame length and its residual probability of an undetected frame error (ASM-06). *Evidence: `test-serial-frames`, `doc-frame-grammar`, `doc-verification-plan`.*
2. THE Backend SHALL generate the Frame_Codec of each side from the frame grammar of criterion 1 and the data types of the cross-processor connections: for the MCU_Side in Rust, in the Serial_Bridge's Partition crate; for the Pi_Side as code of the Pi_Side Serial_Bridge that the existing HAMR back end selected under ORQ-09 compiles unchanged (R34.2, R36.7). Each side SHALL have a printer and a parser for every frame type, both sides SHALL give each connection the same frame type, and the Pi_Side Serial_Bridge SHALL open the device path that the bus's `IronOxide::Serial_Link` names. *Evidence: `test-serial-frames`, `pbt-frame-cross-codec` (for random valid frames of every frame type, the other side's parser returns an equal frame from the bytes of each side's printer), `test-splitter-peer-codegen`.*
3. THE Frame_Codec of each side SHALL satisfy two round-trip properties for every frame type. First, for every valid frame f, the parser applied to the bytes that the printer gives for f returns f and consumes exactly those bytes. Second, for every byte string b that the parser accepts as one frame, the printer gives b for the frame that the parser returns. A valid frame is a frame type, a sequence number from 0 to 2^w − 1 (criterion 9), and a payload that the Wire_Type decoder of R14.2 for the frame type's connection accepts (empty for an event port connection). Two frames are equal when these three parts are equal. *Evidence: `pbt-frame-round-trip-mcu`, `pbt-frame-round-trip-peer`, `proof-frame-parser` (Creusot, MCU_Side: both properties, and the printer and parser free of panics, overflow and out-of-bounds access, R21.5), `mut-proof-codecs`.*
4. IF the MCU_Side parser obtains a frame candidate of one or more bytes before its delimiter that is not a valid frame (criterion 3), THEN THE Serial_Bridge SHALL discard the whole candidate without a Put Value for any part of it, add 1 to the count of the first reason in this list that applies, and parse the bytes after the candidate's delimiter as a new candidate. A frame candidate is the bytes received after a frame delimiter, or after the start of reception, up to and including the next frame delimiter. The reasons, in order: (i) more bytes before the delimiter than the maximum encoded frame length L_max that the frame grammar states; (ii) invalid byte stuffing; (iii) a CRC mismatch; (iv) a frame type that identifies no connection from the Pi_Side to the MCU_Side; (v) a payload length beyond the frame payload bound of the bus's `IronOxide::Serial_Link`, or different from the Wire_Type size of the frame type's connection (0 for an event port connection); (vi) a payload that the Wire_Type decoder of R14.2 rejects. The parser SHALL at no time hold more than L_max bytes of a candidate. A candidate of zero bytes (two consecutive delimiters) is ignored and not counted. Each count is held in the receiving Task's per-Task state (PR-25), is 0 at instant 0 and after each restart of the Partition, and saturates at 2^32 − 1. *Evidence: `pbt-frame-garbage` (for random byte strings g and valid frames f, the parser given g, a frame delimiter and the printing of f delivers f exactly once and no part of g, and the counts grow by the number of non-empty candidates in g that are not valid frames), `proof-frame-parser` (no Put Value for a discarded candidate; at most L_max bytes held), `hil-serial-faults` (each single-bit error, and each error burst no longer than the CRC width, that the Pi_Stand_In injects into a frame causes that frame to be discarded and counted, and no part of it is delivered).*
5. THE Robot_Model SHALL place the MCU_Side Serial_Bridge's threads in a process of their own that contains no other thread. That process's Criticality_Level SHALL be the lowest among the processes bound to the IronOxide_Processor, and strictly lower than that of every process to which the Serial_Bridge delivers peer data. Every such delivery is then an Upward connection: the receiving role's Validation_Function checks its messages (MAP-18, R13.1, R13.2), and its destination port therefore carries a GUMBO integration assume clause or `IronOxide::Validation_Function` (R13.3). The Robot_Model declares that process, its threads with their `IronOxide::Serial_Bridge_Role`, and their properties (R34.8, Table 36-1, UD-15). *Evidence: `check-tv-robot`, `review-robot-model`, `mut-refusals` (criterion 8 (b)).*
6. WHEN PAR-14 (100 ms) has elapsed since the MCU_Side Serial_Bridge last accepted a frame under criterion 9 (or, where it has accepted none since then, since instant 0 or since the last restart of its Partition), THE Serial_Bridge SHALL report link loss (UD-10) to every Task that consumes peer data, that is, every Task with an in port connected to an out port of the MCU_Side Serial_Bridge. The report SHALL be sent by the first Job of the transmitting Task (criterion 7) whose Release is due at or after that instant, so that it is sent no later than one period plus one relative deadline of that Task after the instant. Every later Job of that Task SHALL repeat the report until the Serial_Bridge accepts a frame under criterion 9. The transmitting Task makes the reports because the receiving Task is released only by the UARTE0 interrupt and therefore does not run while the Pi_Side is silent; a bound of one period of the receiving Task cannot hold (UD-10). How the report reaches each consumer's Port_API is ORQ-60. *Evidence: `hil-serial-faults` (the Pi_Stand_In falls silent, and in a separate run sends only frames that criterion 4 discards; each report is sent within the bound; no report is sent while the Pi_Stand_In sends a valid frame at least once every PAR-14 / 2, 50 ms), `test-serial-link-loss`.*
7. THE MCU_Side Serial_Bridge SHALL consist of two Tasks of the process of criterion 5, whose Partition owns UARTE0 (R15.1). At PAR-01, the `nrf52840` table of IronOxide `crates/rsk-gen/src/target.rs` gives UARTE0 interrupt line 2 and the EasyDMA channels RXD and TXD, in that order. The two Tasks are: (i) a receiving Sporadic_Task, the Task of the thread with `IronOxide::Serial_Bridge_Role => Receive` (R34.8, UD-15), released by the UARTE0 interrupt as R15.2 states for a device whose `IronOxide::Peripheral` is UARTE0 (the device `uarte0_events` that the Robot_Model declares, Table 36-1), with `mit_policy = defer` and an MIT that design.md derives from PAR-12 and PAR-13 (criterion 10); and (ii) a transmitting Periodic_Task, the Task of the thread with `IronOxide::Serial_Bridge_Role => Send` (R34.8, UD-15), which sends the frames of criterion 1 and makes the reports of criterion 6. Both Tasks SHALL access UARTE0 only in these ways (this answers ORQ-32 for UARTE0): bytes move only by `rsk::dma_request` on the RXD or TXD channel, over buffers held in the Tasks' per-Task state (IronOxide R17.1); every other UARTE0 register is written only by `rsk::dma_write`, including event clearing; registers are read only by `rsk::mmio::read`, because the window is read-only to the Partition (IronOxide DD-08); and a buffer is read or written only while no transfer over it is in progress (IronOxide R17.5). The Robot_Model gives both threads their Priority and timing properties (R34.8, Table 36-1, UD-15). How frames are delimited in the receive buffers is ORQ-58; IOP-15 proposes a Kernel service or Kernel-configured PPI/TIMER that reports the byte count of a receive in progress. *Evidence: `check-generated-robot`, `proof-app-creusot-robot`, `hil-serial-throughput`.*
8. IF any of the following cases holds, THEN THE Backend SHALL refuse the model with one diagnostic of R3.1 per connection, process or thread and case. Case (a): a cross-processor port connection that the Deployment_Splitter replaces (R34.1) has a data type whose Wire_Type (R14.1, Table 14-1) is larger than the frame payload bound of its bus's `IronOxide::Serial_Link`, the bus being the one that the marker of R34.1 names. Case (b): the process of the MCU_Side Serial_Bridge contains another thread, has a Criticality_Level higher than that of another process bound to the IronOxide_Processor, or has a Criticality_Level not strictly lower than that of a process to which the Serial_Bridge delivers peer data. Case (c): a thread bound to the IronOxide_Processor has `IronOxide::Serial_Bridge_Role => Receive_And_Send`, because criterion 7 requires the MCU_Side Serial_Bridge to consist of two Tasks (UD-33); on the Peer_Processor, `Receive_And_Send` stays allowed (R34.8). For (a), the diagnostic SHALL name the connection, its Wire_Type size and the bound; for (b), the processes, their Criticality_Levels and the connections concerned; for (c), the thread and the property value. Each diagnostic SHALL name MAP-27 (j) for case (a) or MAP-27 (k) for case (b), with the rule "Backend restriction (Requirement 35)", or MAP-27 (l) for case (c), with the rule "Backend restriction (R35.7)" (R3.1). This ensures that no message is split across frames, that no peer data reaches a Partition except through a Validation_Function, and that the MCU_Side Serial_Bridge has the two Tasks of criterion 7. *Evidence: `mut-refusals` (for (c), an AIR mutant that gives the receiving Serial_Bridge thread on the IronOxide_Processor `Receive_And_Send` and removes the mark of the sending one), `test-refusal-diagnostics` (for (a), a Wire_Type of exactly the bound is accepted and a larger one refused; for (c), `Receive_And_Send` on a Peer_Processor thread gets no MAP-27 (l) diagnostic).*
9. WHEN the MCU_Side parser obtains a frame of frame type t and sequence number s that criterion 4 does not discard, THE Serial_Bridge SHALL compute d = (s − e) mod 2^w, where e is the sequence number it expects next for t, and act on d as follows. Where d = 0, it accepts the frame. Where 0 < d < 2^(w−1), it accepts the frame and adds d to t's lost-frame count. Where d ≥ 2^(w−1), it discards the frame without a Put Value and adds 1 to t's duplicate-or-reordered count. After accepting a frame, it sets e to (s + 1) mod 2^w and, where the check of R24.6 passes, puts the frame's message by Put Value on the Serial_Bridge out port that stands for t's connection (R34.1). The first frame of t obtained since instant 0, since the last restart of the Partition, or since the last link-loss report of criterion 6 is accepted and counted in no count. For an event or event data port connection, each accepted frame whose message a later accepted frame of t replaces before the same Job's Send Output (R19.3) adds 1 to t's superseded count. w is the sequence-number width that the frame grammar states, at least 16 (DD candidate). The counts are kept as criterion 4 keeps its counts. *Evidence: `test-serial-sequence` (d = 0, 1, 2^(w−1) − 1, 2^(w−1) and 2^w − 1; the first frame after instant 0, after a restart and after a link-loss report; two frames of one event data connection in one Job), `pbt-frame-garbage`, `proof-app-creusot-robot`, `hil-serial-faults` (frames that the Pi_Stand_In drops, duplicates or reorders are counted exactly).*
10. WHILE the Pi_Side, or the Pi_Stand_In in its place, sends bytes at the full line rate of PAR-12 (100,000 bytes per second: 10 bits per byte at 1,000,000 baud with 8 data bits, no parity and 1 stop bit), THE MCU_Side Serial_Bridge SHALL obtain every received byte in the order received, so that every valid frame among them reaches criterion 9. The receive buffer sizes and the MIT of criterion 7 SHALL be those that design.md derives from PAR-12, PAR-13 and the receiving Task's WCRT as the Analyzer computes it, with the frame delimitation that design.md records for ORQ-58. The Serial_Bridge SHALL not rely on hardware flow control, because the Pi_Side is an untrusted peer (NG-04). *Evidence: `hil-serial-throughput` (over the UART path of ASM-07, the Pi_Stand_In sends valid frames back to back, separated only by frame delimiters, for PAR-10; every frame sent is accepted under criterion 9; the counts of criterion 4 and the lost-frame and duplicate-or-reordered counts of criterion 9 stay 0), `analysis-rta-robot-measured`.*

### Requirement 36: The robot case-study model (Phase P5)

**User Story:** As the project lead, I want a two-processor ground robot modeled and generated end to end, so that the back end is exercised by a system with real control, safety and communication needs.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Robot_Model SHALL be one model of a ground robot, written in AADL and instantiated into its AIR_Model by FMIDE installed inside the workspace (Table 42-1 row 10; the installation awaits the user's approval, ORQ-10) (UD-05). The SysML v2 path is not used for this model: HAMR's SysML v2 instantiator at PAR-02 turns allocation usages into processor bindings only (`processActualProcessorBindings` in `hamr/sysml/frontend/.../instantiation/Instantiate.scala`), and no Scala source under `hamr/sysml` names `Actual_Connection_Binding`, so R34.4 (d) would refuse every cross-processor connection (ORQ-57); whether that path also lists `Dispatch_Protocol` on devices is ORQ-33. The robot hardware that the model's devices and pins stand for is chosen under ORQ-66 (R38.1). Its AIR_Model SHALL contain:
   - a Peer_Processor for the Raspberry Pi 5 running Linux. The processes bound to it have at least one thread each for perception, planning, operator commands and telemetry, plus the Pi_Side Serial_Bridge threads that R34.8 marks. The planning thread that sends the velocity commands of Table 36-2 CN-01 is Periodic, with a period of at least 2 × T_sup (proposal: 50 ms), so that in a nominal run two velocity commands do not arrive within one supervisor period, which with CN-01's `Queue_Size => 1` and DropNewest would give a full Endpoint (UD-34). Every thread on the Peer_Processor has the properties that design.md lists as required by HAMR's common linter (`common/symbols/Linter.scala`) and by the back end of the platform of ORQ-09, each listed with the HAMR source file at PAR-02 that requires it. The serial-port driver of the Pi_Side Serial_Bridge has the provenance that the resolution of ORQ-09 states;
   - the IronOxide_Processor with `IronOxide::Target => nrf52840`. Bound to it are exactly the processes, threads and devices of Table 36-1, which carry wheel motor control at PAR-16, wheel encoders, safety supervision and the MCU_Side Serial_Bridge;
   - one bus with `IronOxide::Serial_Link` (Table 2-1). `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Connection_Binding` binds every cross-processor port connection to it (R34.4 (d), ORQ-57);
   - the port connections of Table 36-2. No other port connection between threads of different processes has an end on the IronOxide_Processor.

   *Evidence: `review-robot-model`, `test-robot-model-parses`, `test-splitter-peer-codegen`.*
2. THE Robot_Model SHALL bind exactly the three processes of Table 36-1 to the IronOxide_Processor (5 Tasks in 3 Partitions, UD-20), with `IronOxide::Criticality_Level` A for the safety process (the safety supervisor and the encoder thread), B for the motor control process and C for the Serial_Bridge process. The three levels are then distinct and the safety supervisor's level is the highest. The Serial_Bridge's level is the lowest and strictly lower than that of the safety process, which is the only process to which the Serial_Bridge delivers peer data (R35.5, R35.8 (b)). *Evidence: `check-tv-robot`, `review-robot-model`, `test-robot-generation` (the Backend accepts the IronOxide sub-model with no R35.8 (b) diagnostic).*
3. THE Robot_Model SHALL state GUMBO contracts on the supervisor thread (TH-01) and the motor control thread (TH-03) that the Contract_Translator translates without refusal (R22.4) and the App_Prover proves (R21.2). In these contracts, every port, GUMBO state variable and GUMBO function uses only the integer, Boolean and record types of Table 14-1, each integer type with an explicit width (Table 36-2 uses `Base_Types::Integer_16`, `Base_Types::Integer_32` and `Base_Types::Unsigned_32`), because how other integer widths are obtained is ORQ-31. Wheel speeds are signed whole mm/s and duties are signed steps of 0.1 % of full duty, as Table 36-2 states, so that no clause uses a floating-point form (Table 22-2, EX-17; ORQ-43; UD-08). The contracts SHALL state at least:
   - (i) as integration guarantees (Table 22-1, CK-07): each setpoint that the supervisor puts in a Job that freezes a velocity command (CN-04) equals the requested speed limited to −PAR-19 to PAR-19 (−1,000 to 1,000 mm/s), and each duty that motor control puts (CN-06) lies in −1,000 to 1,000;
   - (ii) as compute guarantees (CK-11): the supervisor holds the duty it applies to the motor outputs in a Job in a GUMBO state variable. That duty is 0 for both wheels in every Job in which the stop latch is set, or in which N consecutive Jobs, this one included, have frozen no velocity command (criterion 4) or no duty command (criterion 10 (a)). Otherwise it equals the most recent duty frozen from motor control;
   - (iii) as compute guarantees: the e-stop sample is a GUMBO state variable that the supervisor's code writes from the GPIOTE latch and the P0 level that it reads in the Job. The stop latch is set at the end of every Job whose e-stop sample shows an assertion, and is cleared only in a Job that freezes an operator reset command while the sample reads released;
