   - (iv) as initialize guarantees (CK-08, R20.4): the stop latch is set, both consecutive-Job counts of (ii) equal N, and both applied duties are 0. The outputs are then at zero duty from instant 0, and after each restart of the safety Partition, until the reset of criterion 5 (UD-20);
   - (v) as integration assumes (CK-06): on the velocity-command port, clauses that every value of the port's data type satisfies, so that no Validation_Function rejects a command for its speeds (R13.5) and criterion 6 applies to every command beyond PAR-19 (UD-20); on the duty port, that each duty lies in −1,000 to 1,000.

   Two steps between these GUMBO state variables and the hardware are not proven: that the e-stop sample equals the GPIOTE latch and P0 level that the supervisor's code reads, and that the applied duty is what its PWM0 accesses produce on the pins. *Evidence: `proof-app-creusot-robot`, `mut-proof-contracts`, `test-contracts-accounted`, `check-registers` (the Proof_Assumption_Register lists both steps), `review-robot-peripheral-code`.*
4. WHEN N consecutive Jobs of the supervisor Task (Table 36-1, TH-01) have each frozen no velocity command, THE MCU_Side SHALL drive every motor PWM output to zero duty no later than PAR-15 (20 ms) after the Release of the N-th of these Jobs, and SHALL keep every motor PWM output at zero duty until a later Job of the supervisor Task freezes a velocity command. In this criterion:
   - N = PAR-14 / T_sup, which is 10 with the T_sup of Table 36-1. The Release of the N-th Job therefore lies PAR-14 after the Release of the last Job that froze a velocity command;
   - a Job freezes a velocity command when `HasEvent` holds for its velocity-command port (Table 36-2, CN-01), which requires that the Serial_Bridge accepted the command's frame (R35.9) and that the receiving role's Validation_Function accepted the message (R13.1, R13.5);
   - a motor PWM output is one of the PWM0 pins that design.md lists for the wheel motors. It is at zero duty from an instant t when it stays, from t to the end of the interval that the criterion states, at the level that design.md records as its motor-off level (ASM-10).

   *Evidence: `hil-robot-timeout` (the Pi_Stand_In stops sending velocity commands at least 100 times per run (proposal; R37.6 requires at least 1,000 command gaps per run), at instants spread over the supervisor's period. Each time, the outputs are at zero duty within the interval of criterion 12, judged by the rule of R37.6, and stay at zero duty until the next frozen command. Where R37.2 (b) records each Job's Release (UD-24), the interval from the Release of the N-th Job to zero duty on every output is also at most PAR-15. Where the supervisor's freeze flags of R37.2 (g) are recorded for the Jobs concerned, the first Job that reports the command timeout is the N-th Job after the last Job that froze a velocity command. In a run with a command every PAR-14 / 2 (50 ms), the telemetry reports no command timeout), `proof-app-creusot-robot` (criterion 3 (ii)), `analysis-robot-stop-bound` (criterion 9).*
5. WHEN the emergency-stop input becomes asserted, THE MCU_Side SHALL drive every motor PWM output to zero duty (criterion 4) no later than PAR-15 (20 ms) after the input became asserted, and SHALL keep every motor PWM output at zero duty until the first Job of the supervisor Task that both freezes an operator reset command (`HasEvent` on its reset port, CN-02) and reads the input as released. The input is asserted when its pin reaches the level that design.md records as asserted. The criterion holds for an assertion of any duration down to the shortest pulse that the GPIOTE IN channel of TH-01 latches, as design.md shows from the nRF52840 Product Specification. A reset frozen in a Job that reads the input as asserted has no effect. *Evidence: `hil-robot-estop` (the stimulus board asserts the input at least 100 times per run (proposal), at instants spread over the supervisor's period; some assertions are held until a reset and some are released after 1 ms (proposal). Each time, every output is at zero duty within PAR-15 of the assertion edge and stays there. A reset sent while the input is asserted leaves every output at zero duty, and so does a release followed by no reset for 1 s (proposal)), `proof-app-creusot-robot` (criterion 3 (ii), (iii)), `analysis-robot-stop-bound`.*
6. IF a Job of the supervisor Task freezes a velocity command in which the speed of at least one wheel has a magnitude greater than PAR-19 (1,000 mm/s in the unit of Table 36-2, CN-01), THEN THE MCU_Side SHALL, in that Job, set the setpoint that the supervisor puts for each such wheel (CN-04) to PAR-19 with the sign of the requested speed, set the setpoint of every other wheel to its requested speed, and add 1 to the limit count. The limit count is a GUMBO state variable of the supervisor of type `Base_Types::Unsigned_32`. It is 0 at instant 0 and after each restart of the safety Partition, saturates at 2^32 − 1, and is carried in every telemetry message (CN-07). *Evidence: `proof-app-creusot-robot` (criterion 3 (i)), `hil-robot-limits` (the Pi_Stand_In sends one command per 20 ms with wheel speeds of 0, ±999, ±1,000, ±1,001, −32,768 and 32,767 mm/s; the setpoints and the limit count in the telemetry match this criterion).*
7. WHEN the Build_System generates the case study, THE Build_System SHALL run three steps, with no edit to any input or generated file between them: the Deployment_Splitter on the Robot_Model's AIR_Model (R34.6); the Backend on the IronOxide sub-model; and HAMR's `sireum hamr codegen` at PAR-02 on the peer sub-model, for the platform that the Verification_Plan records as the resolution of ORQ-09 (or, until it records one, for each of the JVM, Linux and ros2 platforms, R34.2). THE Build_System SHALL report the generation as passing only when all of these hold:
   - the Backend accepts the IronOxide sub-model, and IronOxide's tools accept the generated MCU_Side as R18.2 states for `nrf52840`;
   - the TV_Checker accepts the MCU_Side's Task_Model against the Semantic_Model of the Robot_Model (R34.5);
   - HAMR's codegen ends as R34.2 states, and the generated Pi_Side, including the Pi_Side Serial_Bridge and its Frame_Codec (R35.2), compiles with the build that HAMR's generated build files define for that platform;
   - on both sides, every generated file equals the file that a generation into an empty output directory writes, outside developer regions and developer-owned files (R18.5 on the MCU_Side; the marker regions of HAMR's back end on the Pi_Side).

   *Evidence: `test-robot-generation`, `check-generated-robot`, `check-tv-robot`, `test-splitter-peer-codegen`.*
8. THE Robot_Model SHALL fit the limits of MAP-26 with the structure of Tables 36-1 and 36-2:
   - 5 threads on the IronOxide_Processor, including the 2 Serial_Bridge threads of R35.7, within PAR-04 (7 Tasks), and therefore at most 5 distinct Priorities, within PAR-03 (7);
   - at most 6 Endpoints (CN-01 to CN-04, CN-06 and CN-07), within PAR-05 (8), whose capacities, `ops` values and total storage stay within PAR-06 as R11.2, R12.2 and R12.6 compute them;
   - at most 3 peripheral windows in each Partition, within PAR-07 (5);
   - exactly one owning Partition for each peripheral: the safety Partition owns P0, GPIOTE and PWM0, the Serial_Bridge's Partition owns UARTE0, and the motor control Partition owns none (UD-20).

   design.md SHALL record this assignment as the resolution of ORQ-11, with its reasons: P0 has no interrupt line (R15.3 (v)); GPIOTE's one interrupt releases exactly one Task (R15.7), so the encoder Task and the e-stop latch share GPIOTE's owner; and the safety Partition owns every motor output and the e-stop input, so that no Task of a lower Criticality_Level lies on the stop paths of criteria 4, 5 and 10. The path of criterion 12 also contains the receiving Serial_Bridge Task (TH-04, level C), which carries the last valid command to the supervisor; criterion 9 bounds its part by the terms t_rx and WCRT_rx. *Evidence: `check-generated-robot` (R17.1, and R18.2 for `nrf52840`), `review-robot-model`.*
9. WHEN the Analyzer has analyzed the MCU_Side with WCET_Records measured on the HIL build that the HIL_Rig runs (R25.1, R37.1, R37.3; UD-11), THE Build_System SHALL compute B_estop = T_sup + WCRT_sup + t_pwm, B_timeout = WCRT_sup + t_pwm and B_gap = t_rx + WCRT_rx + T_sup + WCRT_sup + t_pwm, SHALL record the three bounds, PAR-15 and the limits of the claim stated below in `analysis-robot-stop-bound`, and SHALL fail the run unless the Analyzer's verdict is PASS, B_estop ≤ PAR-15, B_timeout ≤ PAR-15 and B_gap ≤ PAR-15. The terms are:
   - T_sup, the supervisor Task's period;
   - WCRT_sup, its WCRT as the Analyzer reports it;
   - t_pwm, the bound that design.md states, with its source, on the time from the supervisor's last PWM0 access in a Job to every motor PWM output being at zero duty (ORQ-62);
   - WCRT_rx, the WCRT of the receiving Serial_Bridge Task (TH-04) as the Analyzer reports it;
   - t_rx, the bound that design.md states, with its source, on the time from the end of a valid frame on the UARTE0 receive line to the Release of the TH-04 Job that obtains the frame, deferral under `mit_policy = defer` included (ORQ-58).

   In every Job, the supervisor reads the e-stop latch and level, evaluates both timeouts and then accesses PWM0. B_estop therefore bounds criterion 5, and B_timeout bounds criteria 4 and 10 (a). B_gap bounds criterion 12 after PAR-14: the last valid velocity command reaches the supervisor's Endpoint within t_rx + WCRT_rx of its frame's end; the supervisor Job that freezes it is released no later than T_sup after that; and criterion 4 then applies from that Job. The bounds hold only while every Task of the safety Partition stays within its Budget and every Release_Source of that Partition respects its MIT (IronOxide R18.7, ASM-05); B_gap also rests on WCRT_rx and t_rx. They exclude the e-stop circuit before the pin, the motor drivers and motors (ASM-10, Requirement 38), the Pi_Side (NG-04), and cases (b) and (c) of criterion 10. No IronOxide Chain checks PAR-15, for these reasons: each stop path of criteria 4, 5 and 10 lies inside the supervisor Task, and a Chain names at least two Tasks (R26.4 (iv)); the path of criterion 12 ends in the absence of a message, which a Chain's latency does not cover; and HAMR's AIR at PAR-02 records no end-to-end flow (R26.7, ORQ-48). PAR-15 is shown by these bounds and by the HIL_Rig measurements of criteria 4, 5, 10 and 12 (R37.6) (UD-03). *Evidence: `analysis-robot-stop-bound`, `analysis-rta-robot-measured`.*
10. IF any of the following cases occurs, THEN THE MCU_Side SHALL drive every motor PWM output to zero duty (criterion 4) no later than PAR-15 after the instant that the case names, and SHALL keep every motor PWM output at zero duty for as long as the case states:
    - (a) N consecutive Jobs of the supervisor Task (criterion 4) freeze no duty command from motor control (CN-06), whether because the Health_Monitor stopped or restarted the motor control Partition or ended its Jobs, or because the supervisor's Validation_Function rejected its duties (R13.5). The instant is the Release of the N-th Job. The outputs stay at zero duty until a later Job of the supervisor Task freezes a duty command;
    - (b) the Health_Monitor applies STOP_PARTITION or RESTART_PARTITION to the safety Partition; the Kernel first stops that Partition's EasyDMA transfers, those of PWM0 included (IronOxide R14.5, R17.6). The instant is the one that the event log records for the response (IronOxide R14.6). The outputs stay at zero duty until the reset of criterion 5 after a restart (criterion 3 (iv)), and until the Target is reset after a stop;
    - (c) the Kernel enters the system safe state (IronOxide R9.9, R12.2, R14.4) and runs the function that `IronOxide::Safe_State` names (Table 2-1). The instant is the one that the event log records. The outputs stay at zero duty until the Target is reset.

    How PWM0's pins behave after its transfers stop, and whether the safe-state function can access PWM0 and P0, is ORQ-62. *Evidence: `hil-robot-faults` (R37.4: at least one run per case and, for (a), per cause; R37.4 cases (e) and (f) give the restart and the rejection causes of (a), the causes that the responses of Table 36-1 produce; R37.4 case (g) gives case (b) through the safety Partition's RESTART_PARTITION of Table 36-1, and R37.4 case (h) gives case (c); how the rig times the Kernel's response against the capture is ORQ-79), `proof-app-creusot-robot` (case (a), criterion 3 (ii)), `analysis-robot-stop-bound` (case (a)).*
11. THE Robot_Model SHALL give the encoder thread (TH-02) an MIT no longer than the shortest interval between two consecutive edges of one encoder signal while its wheel turns at PAR-19 (1.0 m/s). design.md computes this interval from the encoder resolution and wheel circumference that it states for the HIL_Rig's stimulus and for the robot (R38.1). With this MIT, the Kernel's masking of the GPIOTE interrupt line (DEV-09) merges no two edges of one encoder signal at wheel speeds up to PAR-19 (ORQ-61; the alternatives are QDEC under IOP-04 and PPI counting under IOP-16). *Evidence: `review-robot-model`, `hil-robot-encoders` (R37.9: the stimulus board generates both wheels' encoder signals at the edge rates of wheel speeds from 0 to PAR-19 in both directions; the encoder counts in the telemetry equal the edges generated), `analysis-rta-robot-measured`.*
12. WHEN PAR-14 has elapsed since the end of the last valid velocity-command frame, THE MCU_Side SHALL drive every motor PWM output to zero duty (criterion 4) no later than PAR-15 after that instant, that is, PAR-14 + PAR-15 (120 ms) after the end of the frame, and SHALL keep every motor PWM output at zero duty until a later Job of the supervisor Task freezes a velocity command. In this criterion:
    - a valid velocity-command frame is a frame of the velocity-command connection (Table 36-2, CN-01) that the MCU_Side Serial_Bridge accepts under R35.9 and whose message the receiving role's Validation_Function accepts (R13.1, R13.5); it is the last one when no later such frame follows it;
    - the end of a frame is the instant at which its last byte has been received on the UARTE0 receive line.

    This is the interval from the last valid command to zero duty that the safety case of R38.2 for R36.4 states. Criterion 4 covers its part from the Release of the N-th Job, and the bound B_gap of criterion 9 covers the whole of it. *Evidence: `hil-robot-timeout` (the command-gap events of R37.6, whose rule judges this interval), `analysis-robot-stop-bound` (B_gap ≤ PAR-15), `proof-app-creusot-robot` (criterion 3 (ii)).*

**Table 36-1 — MCU-side threads** (all values are proposals; changing one follows Requirement 41). T_sup and D_sup are TH-01's period and relative deadline; T_sup divides PAR-14 (criterion 4).

| Row | Thread | Process (Criticality_Level) | Dispatch and Release_Source | Period or MIT | Relative deadline | Peripherals accessed | Function |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TH-01 | `supervisor` | `safety` (A) | Periodic | 10 ms (T_sup) | 5 ms (D_sup) | P0: reads the e-stop level, and writes the motor direction pins where design.md assigns any. GPIOTE: one IN channel latches e-stop assertions, and its event does not drive the GPIOTE interrupt. PWM0: every motor PWM output | e-stop latch and reset (criterion 5); command and duty timeouts (criteria 4, 10 (a)); speed limit (criterion 6); applies the duty to PWM0; telemetry (CN-07) |
| TH-02 | `encoders` | `safety` (A) | Sporadic, released by the GPIOTE interrupt through the device `encoder_events` (R15.2), `mit_policy = defer` | MIT of criterion 11 | equal to the MIT | GPIOTE (the encoder channels); P0 (reads encoder levels) | counts each wheel's encoder edges and puts the counts on CN-05 |
| TH-03 | `motor_control` | `motor` (B) | Periodic | PAR-16 (10 ms) | 10 ms | none | wheel speed control from the setpoints and counts of CN-04; puts duties and measured speeds on CN-06 |
| TH-04 | `bridge_rx` | `serial_bridge` (C) | Sporadic, released by the UARTE0 interrupt through the device `uarte0_events` (R15.2, R35.7), `mit_policy = defer`; `IronOxide::Serial_Bridge_Role => Receive` (R34.8, UD-15) | MIT of R35.7 and R35.10 | equal to the MIT | UARTE0 | receives and checks frames (R35.3, R35.4, R35.9) |
| TH-05 | `bridge_tx` | `serial_bridge` (C) | Periodic; `IronOxide::Serial_Bridge_Role => Send` (R34.8, UD-15) | 10 ms | 10 ms | UARTE0 | sends frames (R35.1) and link-loss reports (R35.6) |

No MCU thread has `Thread_Properties::Priority`, so R7.2 assigns deadline-monotonic Priorities (MAP-06). The `safety` process has `IronOxide::Peripherals => ("P0", "PWM0")`. R15.1 adds GPIOTE to it through `encoder_events`, and assigns UARTE0 to `serial_bridge` through `uarte0_events`. Each process has `Fault_Response`, `Overrun_Response` and `Deadline_Miss_Response` set to RESTART_PARTITION. The `MIT_Violation_Threshold` values and the `Memory_Properties` sizes are DD candidates.

**Table 36-2 — Connections between processes, and within the safety process** (proposals). All types are those of Table 14-1. "mm/s" values are signed whole millimetres per second. "Duty" values are signed steps of 0.1 % of full duty, with the sign selecting the direction.

| Row | Source → destination | Category and destination port properties | Data type | Path | Mapping and checks |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CN-01 | a planning thread on the Peer_Processor, Periodic with a period of at least 2 × T_sup (proposal: 50 ms; criterion 1, UD-34) → TH-01 `velocity_cmd` | event data; `Queue_Size => 1`, `Dequeue_Protocol => OneItem`, `Overflow_Handling_Protocol => DropNewest` (R11.6 and MAP-17 refuse DropOldest, because no bound limits the Pi_Side's send rate) | record: `left`, `right` (`Base_Types::Integer_16`, mm/s) | Peer_Processor → serial bus → TH-04 → TH-01 | bridge path (R34.1); plain Endpoint C → A (MAP-15); Upward, so a Validation_Function built from the assume clauses of criterion 3 (v) |
| CN-02 | an operator-command thread on the Peer_Processor → TH-01 `reset` | event; port properties as CN-01 | none (a 4-byte token, R11.3) | as CN-01 | bridge path; plain Endpoint C → A (MAP-15); Upward, so `IronOxide::Validation_Function` on `reset`, because an event port carries no integration clause (Table 22-1, CK-06; R13.3) |
| CN-03 | TH-05 → TH-01 link-loss report (R35.6) | as ORQ-60 decides | as ORQ-60 decides | within the MCU_Side, C → A | at most one plain Endpoint |
| CN-04 | TH-01 `setpoint` → TH-03 `setpoint` | data | record: `left`, `right` setpoints (`Base_Types::Integer_16`, mm/s, each in −1,000 to 1,000); `left_count`, `right_count` (`Base_Types::Integer_32`, encoder edges counted by TH-02) | A → B | plain Endpoint (MAP-16, C_sample of R12.2); Connection_Obligation (R24.1) |
| CN-05 | TH-02 `counts` → TH-01 `counts` | data | record: `left_count`, `right_count` as in CN-04 | within A | Port_Cell (MAP-14) |
| CN-06 | TH-03 `duty` → TH-01 `duty` | event data; `Dequeue_Protocol => OneItem`; `Queue_Size` and `Overflow_Handling_Protocol` as design.md sets them under R11.6 and MAP-17 | record: `left`, `right` duties (`Base_Types::Integer_16`, duty, each in −1,000 to 1,000); `left_speed`, `right_speed` measured (`Base_Types::Integer_16`, mm/s) | B → A | plain Endpoint (MAP-15); Upward, so a Validation_Function built from the assume clause of criterion 3 (v) |
| CN-07 | TH-01 `telemetry` → a telemetry thread on the Peer_Processor | data | record: the setpoints of CN-04 and the measured speeds of CN-06 (`Base_Types::Integer_16`, mm/s); the counts of CN-05 (`Base_Types::Integer_32`); stop latched, command timeout and duty timeout (`Base_Types::Boolean`); the limit count (`Base_Types::Unsigned_32`, criterion 6); the index of the supervisor Job that put the message (`Base_Types::Unsigned_32`: 0 for the first Job after instant 0 or after a restart of the safety Partition, saturating at 2^32 − 1) and whether that Job froze a velocity command, a duty command and an operator reset command (`Base_Types::Boolean` each; R37.2 (g)) | TH-01 → TH-05 → serial bus → Peer_Processor | plain Endpoint A → C (MAP-16, with the reject-all role of R13.6), then the bridge path |

### Requirement 37: The MCU side on the HIL_Rig (Phase P5)

**User Story:** As a timing analyst, I want the robot's MCU side run on IronOxide's rig with its observed response times compared with the Analyzer's bounds, so that the analysis is confirmed on hardware as in IronOxide's task 19.4.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN the Build_System runs `hil-robot-nominal`, THE Build_System SHALL run the MCU_Side's HIL build on the HIL_Rig's target board from instant 0 to the Kernel's operating-duration end, for at least PAR-10, that is max(3,600 s, 1,000 × H) with H the hyperperiod of R32.2 over the MCU_Side's Task_Model. The Pi_Stand_In and the stimulus board drive the MCU_Side's inputs as follows:
   - **The HIL build.** It is the MCU_Side's `nrf52840` App_Declaration, built in the release profile with IronOxide's HIL features at PAR-01 (`nrf52840,debug-port,quiet,trace-ram,trace-pins`, `xtask/src/hil.rs` `FEATURES`) from the generated source of the MCU_Side's Flight_Build.
     - Its Generated_Config checksum equals the Flight_Build's.
     - The Build_Manifest lists its binary hash among the HIL binaries (`hil_binaries`).
     - Criteria 2 and 3 take their records from this binary and compare its responses (R25.1, R25.5).
     - The Flight_Build is not run on the rig (UD-11). At PAR-01 the Kernel keeps the response-time, execution and event records of criterion 2 only in a build with `trace-ram`, and IronOxide measures its own Applications only on HIL builds (`xtask/src/hil.rs`; IronOxide Verification_Plan 11.12, 11.14). Criterion 8 records the resulting gap.
     - How a generated Application is built and measured this way is ORQ-37 and ORQ-47 (IOP-07, IOP-10).
   - **The rig description.** Each board is selected by its J-Link serial in the rig description that design.md records for ORQ-63. Its `[boards]` and `[analyzer]` sections equal those of IronOxide's `hil-rig.toml`.
   - **The Pi_Stand_In.** It sends over the UART path that the Verification_Plan records for ASM-07 and ORQ-20, at the line settings of PAR-12.
     - For each connection from the Peer_Processor to the IronOxide_Processor in the peer sub-model (R34.1), it sends one frame per Job of the connection's source thread, at that thread's period (Periodic) or MIT (Sporadic), in sequence-number order. For the velocity commands of CN-01, this is the period of the planning thread that R36.1 states, at least 2 × T_sup (proposal: 50 ms), so that two velocity commands do not arrive within one supervisor period (UD-34).
     - Payload values come from a seed that the run's report records and satisfy the source port's GUMBO integration guarantee clauses. Velocity commands request speeds within PAR-19 (1.0 m/s).
     - It prints each frame with the Pi_Side Frame_Codec of R35.2, or with a printer that `pbt-frame-cross-codec` checks against it.
     - It sends each frame as one burst of back-to-back bytes, followed by at least 100 µs with the line idle (proposal).
     - It logs each burst's index, frame type and sequence number, and whether the burst is a valid frame sent as this bullet states.
     - It also makes the command gaps and sends the operator reset commands of criterion 6.
   - **The stimulus board.** It drives each wheel-encoder input of the Robot_Model at the edge rate given by a wheel speed of PAR-19 and by the encoder resolution and wheel circumference that design.md records. It drives the emergency-stop input as criterion 6 states. Its program is ORQ-65.

   *Evidence: `hil-robot-nominal`.*
2. WHEN a run of criterion 1 or 4 has reached the Kernel's operating-duration end, THE Build_System SHALL record the following with the run's report:
   - (a) The Kernel's records, read by the rig over the target board's probe (`RELEASE_STATS`, `crates/rsk-hil/src/stats.rs`). For each Task: the Jobs completed; the largest response time, from the Release's due instant to the Job's end, in Kernel time-base ticks (IronOxide R18.8); the largest Job execution in CPU cycles from the Budget accounting; and the largest execution of each of its Critical_Section sites (IronOxide task 19.1). For the run: the Kernel_Timing_Parameter observations J_rel, Δ_irq, Δ_stamp, the longest masked span (L_kernel) and the Kernel entries by kind (IronOxide Verification_Plan 11.5, 11.11, 11.13).
   - (b) Where the Task_Model declares at least one Chain (UD-24, ORQ-64): each Job's Release instant and completion instant, through the record channel that design.md records for ORQ-53 (IOP-14). At PAR-01 the Kernel's record holds only per-Task counts and maxima and a ring of the last 256 Releases. Where the Task_Model declares no Chain, the report states that (b) is not recorded under UD-24.
   - (c) The number of Chains of the Task_Model, 0 included. Where it is at least 1 (UD-24): for each Chain, the latency of each Chain instance over the interval of R26.8, computed from the records of (b).
   - (d) The logic analyzer's capture of every channel of Table 37-1 over the whole run, the motor PWM outputs among them, with its sample rate and its count of lost samples.
   - (e) The Kernel's event mirror (`EVENT_MIRROR`: the last events up to its capacity, 512 in `crates/rsk-hil/src/events.rs`; the number of events written; and the sequence number at which the trace freeze stopped the Kernel's trace ring) and its fault record (`FAULT_RING`).
   - (f) The Pi_Stand_In's burst log, and the stimulus board's seed and list of driven events.
   - (g) The supervisor's freeze flags: for each Job of the supervisor Task (Table 36-1, TH-01), its index and whether it froze a velocity command (CN-01), a duty command (CN-06) and an operator reset command (CN-02), as the telemetry message that the Job put carries them (Table 36-2, CN-07) and the Pi_Stand_In logs them. Each index between the first and the last logged index for which no message was logged is listed as missing (R36.4, R36.10 (a)).

   *Evidence: `hil-robot-nominal`, `hil-robot-faults`.*
3. WHEN a run of criterion 1 has been recorded under criterion 2 and criterion 7 does not fail it, THE Verdict_Report SHALL compare the following, and SHALL give for each Task the thread's instance path, its Criticality_Level, the WCRT and the relative deadline in CPU cycles and ms, the observed maximum in CPU cycles and ms, and the Jobs completed, as IronOxide Verification_Plan 11.14 tabulates them:
   - for each Task, the observed maximum (the largest response time of criterion 2 (a) multiplied by the CPU cycles per Kernel tick, 4 at PAR-01: 64 MHz over 16 MHz, `xtask/src/hil.rs`) against the WCRT that the Analyzer computes under R25.1 from the WCET_Records and Kernel_Timing_Parameters measured on the HIL build of criterion 1, the runs of criterion 1 on that build being folded as IronOxide's rig folds the runs of one binary;
   - for each Chain, the largest observed latency of criterion 2 (c) against the Analyzer's Chain bound.

   THE Build_System SHALL report `hil-robot-nominal` as passing only when all of these hold:
   - `analysis-rta-robot-measured` passes under R25.7 for that Analyzer run, labelled timing evidence under R25.5 with the HIL build's hash;
   - every Task completed at least one Job in the run;
   - every Task's observed maximum is at most its WCRT;
   - every Chain's largest observed latency is at most its Chain bound;
   - criterion 7 reports no failure condition for the run.

   *Evidence: `hil-robot-nominal`, `analysis-rta-robot-measured`, `test-verdict-report` (an observed maximum equal to the WCRT passes; one cycle above fails; a Task with no completed Job fails).*
4. WHEN the Build_System runs `hil-robot-faults`, THE Build_System SHALL make one run as criterion 1 states for each case below, and SHALL report a pass only when every run passes criterion 7 and, in every run, each of the following that applies to its case holds:
   - every Task of the safety supervisor's Partition (the Partition of the highest Criticality_Level, R36.2) completed at least one Job, and its largest response time, converted to CPU cycles as in criterion 3, is at most the WCRT of criterion 3's Analyzer run (IronOxide R18.7, R18.8);
   - the event mirror holds no entry for a Task of that Partition, and the trace freeze did not stop at one. In cases (e) and (f) only, this bullet tolerates `EV_ENDPOINT_FULL` entries, and entries for sends or receives that returned `PeerChanged`, that the Kernel records against the safety Partition on an Endpoint whose other role is in the faulted Partition, during a fault stretch of the case and the restart of the faulted Partition that follows it. A restart of the motor control Partition re-initializes the shared Endpoint (IronOxide R42.3) and can fill it, which is an expected effect of the injected fault and not a fault of the safety Partition (UD-32). Every other entry fails the run;
   - each Kernel_Timing_Parameter observation of criterion 2 (a) is at most the value of that parameter in criterion 3's Analyzer run, so that the WCRTs bound the faulted run (IronOxide R13.4);
   - every stop event of the run meets the rule of criterion 6 (R36.4, R36.5, R36.12);
   - in cases (g) and (h), the stop rule that follows the cases holds.

   The first four bullets apply to the runs of cases (a) to (f) and (h). They do not apply to case (g), in which the safety Partition itself is faulted; criterion 7 and the stop rule judge that run.

   The cases:
   - (a) Overruns. The run uses a fault-injection variant of the HIL build that differs from it only in the Serial_Bridge's Partition crate, as design.md states (DD candidate), and that has the same Task_Model and Generated_Config checksum. Each Task of the Serial_Bridge's Partition exceeds its Budget in at least 1 of every 10 of its Jobs (proposal), outside a Critical_Section and, for a Task that enters one, also inside it, so that the Partition's Overrun_Response applies.
   - (b) Malformed frames. Among the valid frames of criterion 1, the Pi_Stand_In sends at least 100 frames (proposal) for each discard reason (i) to (vi) of R35.4, and at least 100 frames that R35.4 and R35.9 accept but whose message the receiving role's Validation_Function rejects (R13.5).
   - (c) Late frames. Each valid frame is sent later than in criterion 1, by a delay drawn uniformly from 0 to 2 × PAR-14 (0 to 200 ms).
   - (d) Missing frames. Valid frames are left out, singly and in runs longer than PAR-14 (100 ms), so that lost-frame counts (R35.9) and link-loss reports (R35.6) occur.
   - (e) Overruns in the motor control Partition. The run uses a fault-injection variant of the HIL build that differs from it only in the motor control Partition's crate, as design.md states (DD candidate), and that has the same Task_Model and Generated_Config checksum. The motor control Task (TH-03) exceeds its Budget in at least 1 of every 10 of its Jobs (proposal), and in every Job of stretches that the seed of criterion 2 (f) places, each at least PAR-14 + PAR-15 + 50 ms long (proposal), so that the motor control Partition's Overrun_Response (RESTART_PARTITION, Table 36-1) applies and the supervisor freezes no duty command for N consecutive Jobs (R36.10 (a)).
   - (f) Rejected duties. The run uses a fault-injection variant as in case (e), in which TH-03 puts, in at least 1 of every 10 of its Jobs (proposal) and in every Job of stretches placed as in case (e), duties outside −1,000 to 1,000, which the supervisor's Validation_Function rejects (R13.5, R36.3 (v)), so that the motor control Partition's Fault_Response applies.
   - (g) A fault of the safety Partition. The run uses a fault-injection variant of the HIL build that differs from it only in the safety Partition's crate and that has the same Task_Model and Generated_Config checksum. A Task of that crate panics on a stimulus edge that the stimulus board drives, in the same register write, together with the mirror line on D6, at an instant drawn from the seed of criterion 2 (f), so that the safety Partition's Fault_Response (RESTART_PARTITION, Table 36-1) applies (R36.10 (b)). The injection method, including the input that carries the edge, is a DD candidate (ORQ-79). From that edge to the end of the run, the stimulus board makes no further stop event of criterion 6, and the Pi_Stand_In sends no operator reset command and keeps sending velocity commands of non-zero speed. d_k is the first sample at which the mirror channel shows the edge.
   - (h) The safe state at the operating-duration end. The run uses the HIL build of criterion 1 and judges the Kernel's entry into the system safe state at the operating-duration end (R36.10 (c)). d_k is the end of the run's last Job on the capture, as design.md records for ORQ-79. The capture continues for at least PAR-15 + 50 ms (proposal) after the operating-duration end.

   The stop rule of cases (g) and (h): every PWM channel shows the inactive level at every sample from d_k + PAR-15 − T_s to the last sample of the capture, so that every motor PWM output is at its inactive level within PAR-15 of d_k and stays there to the end of the capture. This is the rule of criterion 6, with r_k the last sample of the capture. How the HIL_Rig times the Kernel's response instant (the Partition restart, the safe state) against the capture is ORQ-79.

   Cases (b) to (d) and (h) use the HIL build of criterion 1. In every case, the operator reset commands of criterion 6 are sent unmodified. In cases (e) and (f), the stop events of criterion 6 check that R36.4, R36.5 and R36.12 hold while motor control is faulted, and the run also fails where a supervisor Job reports no duty timeout in its telemetry although criterion 2 (g) holds the freeze flags of that Job and of the N − 1 Jobs before it and they show that none of these Jobs froze a duty command (R36.3 (ii), R36.10 (a)). The time from the N-th such Job to zero duty is bounded by B_timeout of R36.9. *Evidence: `hil-robot-faults` (one run per case).*
5. WHEN the Build_System has flashed the HIL_Rig's boards for a run of criterion 1 or 4, THE Build_System SHALL read back the binary of the target board, of the stimulus board and, where the Verification_Plan's answer to ORQ-20 makes the Pi_Stand_In a board, of that board, each selected by its J-Link serial. It SHALL start the run only when each read-back hash equals the hash that the Build_Manifest lists for that board's build (IronOxide R48.4). *Evidence: `hil-robot-nominal`, `hil-robot-faults`, `test-hil-robot-run-validity`.*
6. WHEN a run of criterion 1 or 4 has been recorded under criterion 2, THE Build_System SHALL judge every stop event of the run on the capture of criterion 2 (d), whose sample period is T_s, by the rule below. For a run of criterion 1, it SHALL report `hil-robot-estop` (R36.5) and `hil-robot-timeout` (R36.4, R36.12) as passing only when the run holds at least 1,000 stop events of the kind (proposal), every one of them meets the rule, and design.md states for every motor PWM output an inactive level (ASM-10) and a shortest active-level time at a non-zero duty of at least 2 × T_s. The terms:
   - **Emergency-stop event k.** The stimulus board asserts the emergency-stop input and, in the same register write, the mirror line (as `hil-rig.toml` `[wiring] stimulus_mirror` states for the stimulus line). d_k is the first sample at which the mirror channel shows the asserted level. The sequence is:
     1. The stimulus board holds the input asserted for at least PAR-15 + 50 ms (proposal). Throughout, the Pi_Stand_In keeps sending velocity commands of non-zero speed.
     2. At least PAR-15 + 50 ms after d_k, the Pi_Stand_In sends one operator reset command, with the input still asserted.
     3. At least 50 ms after that command, the stimulus board releases the input.
     4. At least 50 ms after the release, the Pi_Stand_In sends an operator reset command. r_k is the last level change of that command's burst.

     In half of the emergency-stop events of each run (proposal), steps 1 to 3 are replaced by a pulse, as R36.5's evidence requires for short assertions: the stimulus board releases the input 1 ms (proposal) after asserting it, the Pi_Stand_In keeps sending velocity commands of non-zero speed, and step 4's reset is sent at least 1 s (proposal) after the release. A pulse can end before the supervisor's next Job reads the input level, so it checks the GPIOTE latch, and it checks that a release followed by no reset for 1 s leaves every output at zero duty.
   - **Command-gap event k.** A command gap is an interval longer than PAR-14 + PAR-15 + 50 ms between the bursts of two consecutive valid velocity commands, as the burst log of criterion 2 (f) identifies them.
     - d_k is the last level change of the earlier burst plus PAR-14. That level change precedes the frame's end by at most one byte time (10 µs at PAR-12), which makes the measurement conservative.
     - r_k is the last level change of the later burst.
     - In each run of criterion 1, the Pi_Stand_In makes at least 1,000 command gaps (proposal): half with the line idle, and half with the frames of the other connections continuing.
   - **Spacing.** Each event starts at an offset from r_(k−1), drawn from the seed of criterion 2 (f) uniformly from [0, H), plus 50 ms (proposal). No two events overlap. The stimulus board and the Pi_Stand_In keep the order and the spacing of the steps on the common time base that design.md records for ORQ-65.
   - **Rule.** Every PWM channel shows the inactive level at every sample from d_k + PAR-15 − T_s to r_k. This bounds an emergency stop by PAR-15 (20 ms) from detection to zero duty (R36.5), and a command gap by PAR-14 + PAR-15 (120 ms) from the last valid velocity command (R36.12). No Chain bound of the Analyzer covers either path (criterion 8); R36.9 bounds both from the Analyzer's WCRTs (B_estop, B_gap; UD-03).
   - **Count check.** For a command-gap event, where criterion 2 (g) holds the freeze flags of every supervisor Job from the last Job that froze the earlier command to the first Job whose telemetry reports the command timeout, that first Job is the N-th Job after the last one (R36.4); an event for which it is not fails the rule. The report gives the number of command-gap events for which the flags were complete.
   - **Report.** For each event: d_k, r_k, and the reaction, that is, the instant one sample period after the last active-level sample on any PWM channel in [d_k, r_k], minus d_k (0 when no such sample exists). For each kind: the number of events and the largest reaction.

   *Evidence: `hil-robot-estop`, `hil-robot-timeout`, `hil-robot-faults`, `test-stop-reaction-measure` (synthetic captures: a last active-level sample at d_k + PAR-15 − 2 × T_s passes; one at d_k + PAR-15 − T_s fails; a PWM output that resumes after the reset command sent with the input asserted fails; a shortest active-level time below 2 × T_s fails both items).*
7. IF any of the following holds for a run of criterion 1 or 4, THEN THE Build_System SHALL report as failed, naming the condition, the run and every Evidence_Item of this requirement taken from it, and SHALL NOT report them as passed:
   - a board that the rig description names is absent, or a read-back hash of criterion 5 differs from the Build_Manifest's;
   - the model's `IronOxide::Operating_Duration` is shorter than PAR-10 plus the largest relative deadline of the Task_Model, or the event mirror lacks the operating-duration end (`operating_end`) or records it earlier than PAR-10 after instant 0;
   - a record of criterion 2 (a), (d), (e), (f) or (g), or, where the Task_Model declares at least one Chain, of criterion 2 (b) (UD-24), was not obtained, a Kernel record is bound to a binary other than the run's, or the capture has lost samples or ends before the operating-duration end;
   - in a run of criterion 1, criterion 2 (g) lists a supervisor Job as missing;
   - in a run of criterion 1, the number of events written exceeds the entries the event mirror holds, or the mirror holds an entry of a kind other than `operating_end`, `safe_state` and, where design.md's answer to ORQ-53 attaches a debugger, `debugger`; every fault, Overrun, deadline miss, release overlap, MIT_Violation, full Endpoint and rejected message therefore fails a nominal run, as in R18.6 and R32.9;
   - in a run of criterion 4, the event mirror holds, or the trace freeze stopped at, an entry for a Task or Partition outside the Partition into which the run's case injects faults (the Serial_Bridge's Partition in cases (a) to (d), the motor control Partition in cases (e) and (f), the safety Partition in case (g), and no Partition in case (h)), other than `operating_end`, `safe_state`, the `debugger` entries above and the entries that criterion 4 tolerates in cases (e) and (f) together with, in case (g), the entries of the same kinds recorded against the motor control or Serial_Bridge Partition on an Endpoint whose other role is in the safety Partition, during the fault stretch and the restart that follows it (UD-32);
   - the log of the Pi_Stand_In or of the stimulus board shows that it stopped before the operating-duration end, or that a stop event departed from the sequence of criterion 6.

   *Evidence: `hil-robot-nominal`, `hil-robot-faults`, `test-hil-robot-run-validity` (one injected case per condition).*
8. THE Verification_Plan SHALL do all of the following:
   - allocate each measurement of Table 37-1 to the claim that its row names;
   - label every observation of this requirement as testing evidence on the HIL build of criterion 1;
   - state that no Analyzer bound covers the stop reactions of criterion 6 while the MCU_Side's Task_Model declares no Chain over the stop paths (R26.7, ORQ-48), and that R36.9 bounds them from the Analyzer's WCRTs (UD-03);
   - cite a Gap_Register entry stating that no observation of this requirement is of the Flight_Build (UD-11), with the instrumentation shares of IronOxide Verification_Plan 11.12 (the Task-entry WCETs of IronOxide's instrumented HIL build differ from those of its lean HIL build by −8.6 % to +15.3 %).

   *Evidence: `doc-verification-plan`, `check-registers`.*
9. WHEN the Build_System runs `hil-robot-encoders`, THE Build_System SHALL make one run as criterion 1 states, except that the stimulus board drives the wheel-encoder inputs by the profile below and the run makes no stop event of criterion 6, and SHALL report a pass only when criterion 7 reports no failure condition for the run and every check of the profile holds (R36.11). Criteria 2, 5 and 7 apply to the run as to a run of criterion 1. The profile:
   - **Steps.** For each wheel alone and for both wheels together, in each direction, the stimulus board generates the encoder signals at the edge rates of the wheel speeds 0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75 and 1.0 × PAR-19 (proposal), computed from the encoder resolution and wheel circumference that design.md records (R36.11). Each step lasts at least 10 s (proposal), and the steps repeat in an order drawn from the seed of criterion 2 (f) until the operating-duration end.
   - **Pauses.** After each step, the stimulus board generates no edge for at least 100 ms (proposal), and logs the number of edges of each wheel that it has generated since instant 0, signed by direction as design.md defines the counts of CN-05.
   - **Check.** For each pause, the encoder counts (CN-05) in the last telemetry message (CN-07) that the Pi_Stand_In receives in the pause equal the signed edge numbers that the stimulus board logged for that pause.
   - **Report.** For each step: the wheels, the direction, the speed, the edges generated and the counts received; and the number of pauses in which the check failed.

   *Evidence: `hil-robot-encoders`, `test-hil-robot-run-validity`.*

**Table 37-1 — Rig measurements, their sources and analyzer channels, and the claims they support.**
- The analyzer is IronOxide's fx2lafw device, with channels D0 to D7 (`hil-rig.toml` `[analyzer]`).
- In that file the trace pins take D1 to D4 and the stimulus mirror takes D6. That leaves D0, D5 and D7 for the motor PWM outputs and the Pi_Stand_In's transmit line (ORQ-63).
- "New" marks a measurement that IronOxide's rig does not make at PAR-01. The claim in its row justifies each new measurement.

| Row | Measurement | Source and channels | New | Claim supported |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| RM-01 | per Task: Jobs completed and largest response time (R37.2 (a)) | Kernel record `RELEASE_STATS` of the HIL build, read over the target board's probe | no: the method of IronOxide task 19.4 (`measured_wcrt_step`); new runs for this Application | the Analyzer's WCRTs hold on the board (R37.3; IronOxide R29.5) |
| RM-02 | WCET_Records and Kernel_Timing_Parameter observations (R37.2 (a)) | Budget accounting, Critical_Section, Kernel-entry and masked-span records of the HIL build (IronOxide tasks 19.1, 19.3) | no new method; new runs, because the parameters are per Application (R25.1; ORQ-47) | `analysis-rta-robot-measured` as timing evidence (R25.5, R25.7); the inputs of RM-01's WCRTs |
| RM-03 | executing Task and Kernel-active flag | trace pins P1.11 to P1.14 on D1 to D4 (`task_id_channels`, `kernel_channel`) | no: IronOxide's per-run check of the Budget accounting against the trace (task 19.1) | the execution figures of RM-02 |
| RM-04 | the safety supervisor's Jobs and the Kernel's events under injected faults (R37.4) | RM-01's record, the event mirror and its trace freeze | no: the method of IronOxide's stress runs (Verification_Plan 11.4) | IronOxide R18.7 and R18.8 for the robot's Partitions |
| RM-05 | emergency-stop reaction (R37.6) | stimulus mirror on D6 (`stimulus_channel`); one channel per motor PWM output | yes | R36.5 within PAR-15; no Chain bound exists for the path (R26.7) |
| RM-06 | command-timeout reaction (R37.6) | the Pi_Stand_In's transmit line into the MCU on one channel; the PWM channels of RM-05 | yes | R36.12 (and R36.4 after PAR-14) within PAR-14 + PAR-15 from the last valid velocity command |
| RM-07 | each Job's Release and completion; Chain latencies (R37.2 (b), (c)) | the record channel of ORQ-53 (IOP-14) | yes | the Chain bounds of R26.8 only; recorded only where the Task_Model declares a Chain (UD-24) |
| RM-08 | the supervisor's freeze flags per Job (R37.2 (g)) | the telemetry messages of CN-07, logged by the Pi_Stand_In | yes | the count of N Jobs in R36.4 and R36.10 (a) (R37.4, R37.6) |
| RM-09 | encoder counts against generated edges (R37.9) | the telemetry messages of CN-07; the stimulus board's edge log | yes | R36.11: no edge merged up to PAR-19 |
| RM-10 | zero duty after a fault of the safety Partition and in the safe state (R37.4 (g), (h)) | stimulus mirror on D6 for case (g); the end of the last Job for case (h), timed as design.md records for ORQ-79; the PWM channels of RM-05 | yes | R36.10 (b) and (c) within PAR-15 |

### Requirement 38: Robot integration (Phase P6, later phase)

**User Story:** As the project lead, I want the integration on the physical robot specified now and run later, so that the rig results lead to a planned, hazard-controlled robot test.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Robot_Integration_Plan SHALL specify, before Phase P6 starts, the configuration on which every Phase P6 test runs. It SHALL name the manufacturer and part number of each hardware item and the version or image hash of each software item, covering:
   - (a) the chassis: the number of driven wheels, the wheel diameter in mm, the mass in kg with the nRF52840-DK, the Raspberry Pi 5 and the battery mounted, and the wheel speed in m/s at 100 % PWM duty. This speed comes from the motor and wheel data until the stage B measurement of criterion 3 replaces it;
   - (b) the motor drivers: each input (PWM, direction, enable, brake) with the nRF52840-DK pin that drives it. For each input, the motor's behaviour (braking, coasting or driven) at zero PWM duty, with the enable input deasserted, with the brake input asserted, with the input undriven, and on loss of the driver's logic supply. This is the enable and brake behaviour that ASM-10 requires the plan to document;
   - (c) the encoders: the type, the counts per wheel revolution, the output voltage in V, and the nRF52840 peripheral that reads them (GPIOTE, as R36.8 assigns it to the safety Partition; or QDEC under IOP-04, or PPI counting under IOP-16), as resolved under ORQ-11 and ORQ-61;
   - (d) the emergency stops: the switch or switches of the hardware emergency stop of criterion 5, the circuit by which it removes motor power, the source of the emergency-stop input of R36.5, and whether one switch operates both;
   - (e) the power: the battery's chemistry, nominal voltage in V and capacity in Ah; the motor supply (the supply of the motor drivers' power stages) and the rating in A of its fuse or circuit breaker; and the supplies of the nRF52840-DK and the Raspberry Pi 5, stating which of them the hardware emergency stop leaves on;
   - (f) the Raspberry Pi 5 software image: the operating system release, the Linux kernel version, the Pi_Side build generated under R36.7, and the hash of the whole image;
   - (g) the wiring: one row per signal between the nRF52840-DK and the robot or the Raspberry Pi 5, giving the nRF52840-DK pin, the nRF52840 peripheral, the Robot_Model device or port that uses the signal (R36.1), the far end, and the signal voltage in V. The rows include the UARTE0 transmit, receive and ground lines of the serial link to the Raspberry Pi 5 UART at PAR-12 (ORQ-09).

   Each item of (a) to (e) that has not been chosen when the plan is written is listed as open under ORQ-66, which criterion 4 requires to be resolved. *Evidence: `doc-robot-integration-plan`, `review-robot-wiring` (each row of (g) checked against the pin that the MCU_Side's code uses for that signal, and against the robot as built).*
2. THE Robot_Integration_Plan SHALL contain, before the first powered test, a hazard analysis and safety cases. A powered test is any interval during which the motor supply of criterion 1 (e) is connected to a motor driver.

   The hazard analysis states, for each hazard of the stages of criterion 3: its cause; its consequence; the stages in which it can occur; each hardware, software or procedural measure that limits it; the criterion or Evidence_Item that verifies each measure; and the residual risk after the measures. It covers at least these causes:
   - wheel motion while no Job of the MCU_Side controls the motor outputs, in each of these states: power-up before instant 0; reset; the core halted by the debug probe; flash programming; the Kernel's system safe state (IronOxide R9.9, R12.2, R14.4); a stop or restart of the Partition of the motor control process (R36.2, R36.10 (a), IronOxide R14.5); and a stop or restart of the safety Partition, which owns PWM0, P0 and GPIOTE and so drives every motor output (R36.8, R36.10 (b), IronOxide R14.5, R17.6). Each state is listed with the motor driver input levels that ORQ-67 determines and, for PWM0's pins, ORQ-62;
   - a Pi_Side that is silent, restarts, or sends malformed, late or out-of-range commands (NG-04; R35.4, R35.6, R36.4, R36.6), and a disconnected serial-link wire;
   - a motor driver output stuck on, and an encoder that reports no motion while its wheel turns;
   - a wheel speed above PAR-19, up to the speed at 100 % duty of criterion 1 (a);
   - wheel motion when motor power returns, on release of the hardware emergency stop or on connection of the battery;
   - a failure of the path of the emergency-stop input of R36.5;
   - contact of a person with a turning wheel in stage B, and collision with a person or object in stage F;
   - a short circuit, overcurrent or overheating of the battery or the motor supply.

   There is one safety case for each of R36.4, R36.5, R36.6 and the hardware emergency stop. Each safety case states:
   - the claim with its bound: for R36.4, PAR-14 + PAR-15 (120 ms) from the end of the last valid velocity-command frame to zero duty (R36.12), of which PAR-15 runs from the Release of the N-th supervisor Job without a command (R36.4); PAR-15 for R36.5; PAR-19 for R36.6;
   - for R36.4, R36.5 and the hardware emergency stop: the time in ms from the trigger to the standstill of every wheel, and the distance in m that the robot travels in that time. For R36.4 and R36.5 these are computed from PAR-19. For the hardware emergency stop they are computed from the speed at 100 % duty of criterion 1 (a) and include the operator reaction time that the plan assumes;
   - the argument from the criteria and Evidence_Items it rests on, among them R35.6, R36.9 (B_estop, B_timeout and B_gap), R36.10, R36.12, R37.3, R37.4 and the P5 Evidence_Items of criterion 3;
   - the assumptions it rests on, at least ASM-06, ASM-10 and NG-04;
   - the test of criterion 3 that confirms it.

   *Evidence: `doc-robot-integration-plan`, `review-robot-hazards` (the recorded review, and the user's approval in the change log of R41.2).*
3. WHEN Phase P6 runs after the check of criterion 4 has passed, THE Build_System SHALL run the Pi_Side generated under R36.7 on the Raspberry Pi 5 with the image of criterion 1 (f), and the MCU_Side Flight_Build that criterion 4 (ii) names on the robot's nRF52840-DK, in stage B and then in stage F as listed below. Before the runs, it checks that the binary read back from the board matches the Build_Manifest's hash, as R37.5 does on the HIL_Rig. AND THE Verification_Plan SHALL name, for each of R36.4, R36.5 and R36.6 and each stage, the test that shows the criterion on the physical robot. The following holds for every test:
   - Its pass criteria are those of the P5 Evidence_Item for the same criterion (`hil-robot-timeout`, `hil-robot-estop`, `hil-robot-limits`), plus the standstill times and stopping distances that the safety cases of criterion 2 state.
   - The PWM outputs, the emergency-stop input and the serial lines are recorded at the nRF52840-DK pins by a logic analyzer that the Trust_Base_Register lists.
   - Each test case runs at least 10 times in each stage and speed step.
   - Every run other than the tests of the first cause of criterion 2 passes only if the Kernel's event log, read as ORQ-68 resolves, shows no deadline miss, no Overrun and no fault.

   The stages:
   - Stage B (bench): the robot stands on a stand on which no wheel touches any surface.
     - First, with the motor supply disconnected: the tests of R36.4, R36.5 and R36.6 at the pins.
     - Then, powered: ASM-10 on each motor, with zero duty applied from a commanded PAR-19, the standstill time measured, and the braking or coasting of criterion 1 (b) observed.
     - Each state of the first cause of criterion 2, passed when no wheel turns and the motor driver inputs are at the levels the plan states under ORQ-67.
     - R36.4 with the Pi_Side process stopped, with the Raspberry Pi 5 powered off, and with the MCU's serial receive wire disconnected.
     - R36.5, and the hardware emergency stop, each followed by its release, passed when no wheel turns until the operator reset of R36.5.
     - R36.6 with commanded speeds of 1.5 × PAR-19 (1.5 m/s) and of the largest value that the command's data type holds, with the wheel speed measured by an instrument independent of the robot's encoders.
     - The wheel speed at 100 % duty (criterion 1 (a)), with the motor drivers' PWM inputs driven by a signal source other than the MCU_Side, which the plan names.
   - Stage F (fenced area): begins only after every stage B test has passed and those tests have resolved ORQ-67.
     - The tests run on the surface that the plan states. The fence is at least the stopping distance of the hardware emergency stop's safety case (criterion 2) away from every point of the marked test path.
     - Commanded speeds rise in steps. The first step is at most 0.25 × PAR-19 (0.25 m/s), each later step is at most 0.25 × PAR-19 above the one before, and no step exceeds PAR-19. Each step begins only after every test of the step before has passed.
     - At each step: the tests of R36.4, R36.5, R36.6 and the hardware emergency stop, with the stopping distance measured from the trigger to the standstill of every wheel.

   *Evidence: `test-robot-bench-<n>`, `test-robot-field-<n>` (Phase P6).*
4. WHEN Phase P6 is to start, or an item of criterion 1 or the MCU_Side Flight_Build changes during Phase P6, THE Build_System SHALL run the Phase P6 entry check. It passes the check only when all of the following hold, and otherwise fails it naming each condition that does not hold:
   - (i) the Verification_Plan records the Phase P5 checkpoint (R41.1) as closed by the user, with every Evidence_Item of Phases P1 to P5 passing;
   - (ii) `hil-robot-nominal`, `hil-robot-faults`, `hil-robot-timeout`, `hil-robot-estop`, `hil-robot-limits`, `hil-robot-encoders`, `hil-serial-faults` and `hil-serial-throughput` have passed on the HIL build of R37.1 that the Build_Manifest of the MCU_Side Flight_Build to be run on the robot lists among its HIL binaries, built from that Flight_Build's generated source and with its Generated_Config checksum (UD-11); the binary to be run on the robot is that Flight_Build, whose Build_Manifest hash criterion 3 checks; the Gap_Register entry of R37.8 records that the rig observed no Flight_Build; and `check-tv-robot`, `proof-app-creusot-robot` and `test-robot-generation` have passed on the Robot_Model from which that binary and the Pi_Side of criterion 1 (f) were generated;
   - (iii) design.md or the Verification_Plan records ORQ-09, ORQ-11, ORQ-66 and ORQ-68 as resolved. Every other open research question whose "Affects" cell cites Requirements 34 to 38, or a criterion of them, is resolved, or is deferred with a rationale that `review-robot-hazards` records as affecting no measure of criterion 2 and no pass criterion of criterion 3;
   - (iv) `review-robot-hazards` records the Robot_Integration_Plan, with the content of criteria 1, 2, 3 and 5, as reviewed, and the change log of R41.2 records the user's approval of it.

   *Evidence: `check-p6-entry` (one case per condition, each failing case named), `doc-checkpoint-p5`.*
5. THE Robot_Integration_Plan SHALL require the following for every powered test (criterion 2):
   - (a) a human operator is present for the whole test. The operator's only task is to watch the robot and operate the hardware emergency stop. The operator can reach its switch at every instant of the test from a position outside the fenced area in stage F, and outside the guard line that the plan marks around the stand in stage B;
   - (b) a hardware emergency stop removes motor power from every motor driver through a circuit that neither the nRF52840, the Raspberry Pi 5, nor any software of this project controls or can override. Where its switch acts by radio, loss of the radio link removes motor power;
   - (c) the hardware emergency stop gets a function check before the first powered test of each session. A session runs from that check to the disconnection of the motor supply, with one operator. The check is done in stage B with the wheels off the ground and a non-zero commanded speed. It passes when every wheel reaches standstill within the time that the hardware emergency stop's safety case (criterion 2) states. No powered test of the session runs after a failed check;
   - (d) a documented stop procedure states the steps that end a test (the hardware emergency stop, then disconnection of the motor supply) and the steps that make the robot safe to approach. The operator applies it at the end of every test and at any wheel motion that the current test step does not command;
   - (e) after any wheel motion that the test step does not command, the session ends. No powered test resumes until the cause is recorded in the Gap_Register and `review-robot-hazards` is repeated;
   - (f) no person is inside the fenced area during a stage F test, and no person reaches past the guard line during a stage B test.

   *Evidence: `doc-robot-integration-plan`, `doc-robot-session-<n>` (per session: the operator, the function check's result, and each application of the stop procedure).*
6. THE Verification_Plan SHALL record the evidence of Phase P6 as follows. AND THE Build_System SHALL refuse to store a P6 Evidence_Item that lacks any datum of (i):
   - (i) each `test-robot-bench-<n>` and `test-robot-field-<n>` is stored under R39.2 with: the MCU_Side Build_Manifest hash read back from the robot; the Raspberry Pi 5 image hash of criterion 1 (f); the version of the Robot_Integration_Plan; the stage and speed step; the logic analyzer captures; the measured standstill times and stopping distances; the `doc-robot-session-<n>` it belongs to; and its verdict;
   - (ii) these items are labelled as testing evidence on the configuration of criterion 1 only (R39.3), in rows of the property allocation table (R39.1) for R36.4, R36.5 and R36.6 on the physical robot;
   - (iii) the Traceability_Matrix (R40.4) links R36.4, R36.5, R36.6 and each criterion of this requirement to their P6 Evidence_Items;
   - (iv) ASM-10 is recorded, for each motor driver, as confirmed or refuted, citing the stage B Evidence_Item;
   - (v) the Gap_Register (R40.1) lists each failed P6 test and each residual risk of criterion 2, with its owner and plan; and the Trust_Base_Register (R40.2) lists the Raspberry Pi 5 image and every measurement instrument of criterion 3;
   - (vi) the Verification_Plan states the scope of the claim. Phase P6 claims only R36.4, R36.5 and R36.6 on the tested configuration, stages and speed steps. It does not claim real-time behaviour of the Pi_Side (NG-04), certification approval or readiness (NG-06), or safe operation outside the fenced area, with people inside it, or above the highest speed step that passed.

   *Evidence: `check-evidence`, `check-registers`, `check-trace`, `doc-checkpoint-p6`.*

### Part H — Verification Plan, Registers, and Build

### Requirement 39: Verification plan and evidence (Phase P1)

**User Story:** As a certification engineer, I want every claim of this project allocated to a tool and an Evidence_Item, so that no claim stands without evidence, as in IronOxide's own plan.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Verification_Plan SHALL contain a property allocation table with one row per guarantee that this project claims, as IronOxide R43.1 and R43.2 and section 3 of IronOxide's Verification_Plan do. The rows SHALL include at least: mapping correctness (R4.1, Requirement 29); refusal completeness (R4.2, R4.5, Requirement 30); profile conformance of generated code (R18.2, R18.3); wire and frame round trips (R14.3, R35.3); sampling capacity (R12.2); Validation_Function totality (Requirement 13); contract correspondence (R23.4); timing verdict mapping (Requirements 25 to 27); MCU_Side response times (R37.3); and serial-link robustness (Requirement 35, R37.4). Each row SHALL give:
   - a row identifier, unique in the table, of the form `P-` followed by two or more digits, which the `properties` field of the Trust_Base_Register cites (R40.2) and R40.5 resolves;
   - the acceptance criteria of this document that the row covers;
   - the tool that establishes the guarantee (for example Verus, the App_Prover, the TV_Checker, the Analyzer, a property-based test, the HIL_Rig or review);
   - each Evidence_Item, by a name that the table of criterion 6 defines, with the kind of evidence that it gives for the row: proof, test, argument or review;
   - the Phase in which the evidence is first produced;
   - the ASM identifiers, Trust_Base_Register entries and Proof_Assumption_Register entries that the evidence depends on, and the IronOxide Evidence_Items of criterion 5 that it relies on;
   - the status at the plan's revision. The status is "established" only where every Evidence_Item of the row has the outcome passed (criterion 2) in its newest record that criterion 7 does not count as missing and, for a row whose guarantee is a timing bound on `nrf52840` (at least the MCU_Side response times row), every Analyzer run that the row cites carries the label timing evidence (R25.5). Otherwise the status names each Evidence_Item that prevents "established", with its outcome or label.

   Every acceptance criterion of this document SHALL be named in at least one row, or in a list after the table that gives for each criterion it holds the criterion's Evidence_Items and why the criterion states no guarantee of the table, as IronOxide R43.5 does (for example R39.3, which fixes the content of this plan). *Evidence: `doc-verification-plan`, `check-evidence` (fails naming each acceptance criterion that is neither in a row nor in the list, and each row whose status is "established" while one of its Evidence_Items has another outcome, or one of its Analyzer runs is labelled development evidence where the row needs timing evidence).*
2. THE Build_System SHALL produce every Evidence_Item that the table of criterion 6 defines through one entry point, `cargo xtask verify` of this workspace (a run of it is a verification job), at the frequency that the table states for the item. It SHALL store each run's result in the evidence store, the directory of Evidence_Item records inside this workspace (R42.3), as one record in IronOxide's Evidence_Item format (`rsk-evidence/1`, IronOxide `xtask/src/evidence.rs` at PAR-01), extended with the items below that the format lacks: input digests, exit statuses, pins, the Phase, outcomes other than pass and fail, and labels (DD candidate). For each name whose Phase is later than the Phase for which the Backend is built (R16.8), it SHALL store a record with the outcome not run and that condition. Each record SHALL give:
   - the Evidence_Item's name, its kind (criterion 6) and the acceptance criteria that it covers;
   - the commit hash of this repository (40 hexadecimal digits) and whether its working tree had uncommitted changes, the commit hashes of PAR-01 and of both revisions of PAR-02, the IronOxide Profile version, and the Phase for which the Backend is built;
   - the name and version of each tool that the item runs, as the tool reports it (for example `verus --version`), with its solver and back-end versions (Z3 for Verus; Why3 and each solver for the App_Prover);
   - the SHA-256 digest, as 64 lowercase hexadecimal digits, of each input file (for example the AIR_Model, the Semantic_Model, the Task_Model, the App_Declaration, a register or a binary) and, for an item that concerns a binary, the Build_Manifest identity;
   - each command line run, in order, with its exit status;
   - for a `review-` or `doc-` item, the review record or document that it stands for, with its SHA-256 digest and, for a review, the reviewer, the review date and the commit hash reviewed;
   - the outcome, exactly one of passed, failed, not run and not compared, with the condition of criterion 6 for not run and not compared, and, for an Analyzer run, the label of R25.5;
   - the name and SHA-256 digest of each log and report stored with the record;
   - the date and time at which the record was written, in UTC to the second.

   A run that ends without a verdict (a crash, a timeout or a stop by the Build_System) has the outcome failed. A `review-` item has the outcome passed only where its review record names the reviewer, the date and the reviewed commit and states no open finding (DD candidate). An item made of several runs (one per corpus model, IronOxide_Target, configuration or named part) records each run's outcome. Its own outcome is failed where any run failed; otherwise not run where any run was not run; otherwise not compared where any run was not compared; otherwise passed. Runs that a criterion excludes for the Phase for which the Backend is built (for example the mutants that R30.15 records as not run) do not count. *Evidence: `check-evidence` (fails naming each record that lacks an item of the list; fixtures: a tool that exits with a non-zero status, a tool that crashes, a `review-` record with an open finding, and an item with one passed part and one part not run).*
3. THE Verification_Plan SHALL label each row of the table of criterion 1 whose Evidence_Items give no proof evidence, and each part of a row's guarantee that the row names separately and that has no proof evidence (for example the TV_Checker's completeness, R29.7), with the kinds of evidence that they give: testing, argument or review evidence. For each such row or part, THE Verification_Plan SHALL state, in a table with one entry per row or part, why no proof tool applies, naming each tool considered and the limitation that excludes it, as IronOxide R43.4 and the "Testing-only evidence" table of section 3 of IronOxide's Verification_Plan do. *Evidence: `doc-verification-plan`, `check-evidence` (fails naming each row or named part of criterion 1 without proof evidence that has no entry in that table).*
4. THE Verification_Plan SHALL record, for each Evidence_Item of kind measurement in the table of criterion 6 (a value that this project measures under QEMU or on the HIL_Rig, such as an execution time, a response time, a latency, a byte rate or an event count), the acceptance criterion of this document that it supports or the design decision of design.md, by its identifier, that it informs. THE Build_System SHALL refuse to store a measurement record that cites neither an acceptance criterion of this document nor a design decision of design.md that exists, and SHALL fail the verification job naming the record. *Evidence: `doc-verification-plan`, `check-evidence` (fixtures: a measurement record that cites neither, one that cites a criterion that does not exist, and one that cites an existing criterion; only the last is stored).*
5. THE Verification_Plan SHALL list, in a table with one row per item, each IronOxide Evidence_Item on which this project relies under ASM-05. Each row SHALL give:
   - the item's name exactly as IronOxide's Verification_Plan at PAR-01 writes it;
   - the IronOxide revision, which is PAR-01 as its 40-digit commit hash;
   - the section of IronOxide's Verification_Plan that states the item's result, and that result;
   - the rows of criterion 1, the acceptance criteria and the Trust_Base_Register entries of this project that depend on it;
   - the statement that this project relies on the item without re-deriving it.

   Each citation of IronOxide evidence in this document, in the Verification_Plan and in the registers of Requirement 40 SHALL be written "IronOxide `<name>` at PAR-01", with a name from that table. The table of criterion 6 SHALL define no Evidence_Item that re-runs an IronOxide proof, Kani harness, measurement or HIL suite on IronOxide's own crates or Applications. *Evidence: `doc-verification-plan`, `check-registers` (fails naming each cited IronOxide name that does not occur in IronOxide's `docs/verification_plan.md` at PAR-01, and each citation that gives a revision other than PAR-01).*
6. THE Verification_Plan SHALL contain an Evidence_Item table that defines, in one row per name, every Evidence_Item name that an acceptance criterion of this document cites (criterion 7). This table plays the role of section 1 of IronOxide's Verification_Plan. One row SHALL define each name that contains the placeholder `<model>` or `<n>` as a pattern, and that row SHALL list the values the placeholder takes. For `<model>` these are the corpus models concerned, with `robot` for the Robot_Model, so that, for example, `check-generated-robot` is an instance of `check-generated-<model>`. `test-map-01` to `test-map-31` SHALL be defined by one row per row of Table M-1 at its current table version (R4.4; MAP-01 to MAP-31 at version 2). The table SHALL also define each name of the lists below, by name or as an instance of a pattern. Together with the range `test-map-01` to `test-map-31`, the lists hold every Evidence_Item name that an acceptance criterion or a table of this document cites at this revision; a name that a later revision adds gets a row too, as the first sentence of this criterion requires:
   - the names that the lists after this one do not hold, by kind:
     - proofs: `proof-app-creusot-<model>`, `proof-app-glue`, `proof-app-validators-<model>`, `proof-connection-obligations-<model>`, `proof-contract-correspondence`, `proof-frame-parser`, `proof-tv-core`, `proof-tv-sampling-capacity`, `proof-wire-round-trip`;
     - property-based tests: `pbt-accepted-models-pass-ironoxide`, `pbt-backend-checks-match-generator`, `pbt-frame-garbage`, `pbt-generator-coverage`, `pbt-limits`, `pbt-names-injective`, `pbt-order-invariance`, `pbt-priority-rank`, `pbt-processor-scope`, `pbt-refusal-oracle`, `pbt-refusals-named`, `pbt-regressions`, `pbt-sampling-no-full`, `pbt-semantic-model-complete`, `pbt-semantic-model-order-invariance`, `pbt-semantic-model-round-trip`, `pbt-task-model-reader-round-trip`, `pbt-thread-task-bijection`, `pbt-time-exact`, `pbt-time-limits-agree`, `pbt-timing-monotone`, `pbt-tv-complete`, `pbt-user-code-preserved`, `pbt-verdict-copy`, `pbt-wire-round-trip`;
     - example tests: `test-analysis-leaves-model-unchanged`, `test-annex-subclauses`, `test-conjunct-split`, `test-connection-obligation-report`, `test-connection-obligations`, `test-contract-definedness`, `test-contracts-accounted`, `test-contracts-not-carried`, `test-contracts-traced`, `test-contracts-translation`, `test-conversions-reported`, `test-declaration-order`, `test-defaults-reported`, `test-determinism`, `test-deviations-reported`, `test-devices-as-thread`, `test-diagnostic-no-position`, `test-diagnostics-schema`, `test-diff-not-compared`, `test-diff-run-validity`, `test-frozen-inputs`, `test-generated-layout`, `test-generation-trace`, `test-init-first-job`, `test-init-restart`, `test-input-aadl-air`, `test-input-rejected`, `test-input-sysml-air`, `test-ironoxide-diagnostic-backmap`, `test-jvm-schedule`, `test-mutation-verdicts`, `test-name-collision`, `test-output-provenance`, `test-partition-sizes`, `test-pbt-input-paths`, `test-pbt-protocol`, `test-peer-restart`, `test-phase-refusals`, `test-port-api`, `test-port-api-hamr-shape`, `test-priority-report`, `test-processor-selection`, `test-proof-mutation-runner`, `test-property-bounds`, `test-property-set-equivalence`, `test-property-set-parses`, `test-qemu-<model>`, `test-read-only-view`, `test-refusal-diagnostics`, `test-refusal-no-output`, `test-regeneration-conflicts`, `test-semantic-model`, `test-semantic-model-deterministic`, `test-semantic-model-errors`, `test-semantic-model-times`, `test-serial-guarantee-check`, `test-time-reading`, `test-trace-bodies`, `test-tv-accepts-<model>`, `test-tv-boundaries`, `test-tv-exit-status`, `test-tv-gate`, `test-tv-input-rejected`, `test-tv-rejects`, `test-user-code-preserved`, `test-verdict-no-result`, `test-verdict-report`, `test-verdict-schema`, `test-wire-decoder-reject`;
     - static checks and IronOxide tool runs: `check-criteria-approved`, `check-deps`, `check-diff-corpus`, `check-evidence`, `check-extractor-independence`, `check-ironoxide-extraction`, `check-link-<model>`, `check-mapping-relation-rows`, `check-mapping-version`, `check-pins`, `check-profile-clippy-<model>`, `check-profile-lint-<model>`, `check-proof-assumptions`, `check-proof-sources-<model>`, `check-property-set-table`, `check-prover-pins`, `check-registers`, `check-semantic-model-schema`, `check-semantic-model-version`, `check-static-<model>`, `check-tv-<model>`, `check-verus-pins`;
     - mutation runs: `mut-backend-checks`, `mut-diff`, `mut-pbt-regressions`, `mut-proof-codecs`, `mut-proof-connections`, `mut-proof-contracts`, `mut-proof-correspondence`, `mut-proof-glue`, `mut-proof-sampling-capacity`, `mut-proof-tv`, `mut-proof-validators`, `mut-refusals`, `mut-translation`, `mut-translation-decl`;
     - differential tests: `diff-contract-gumbox`, `diff-jvm-qemu-<model>`, `diff-jvm-rig-<model>`, `diff-semantic-model-hamr`, `diff-task-model-reader`;
     - HIL_Rig runs: `hil-dropoldest-no-full`, `hil-sampling-no-full`, `hil-serial-faults`, `hil-validation-reject`, `hil-wcet-<model>`;
     - Analyzer runs: `analysis-glue-share-<model>`, `analysis-rta-<model>`, `analysis-rta-<model>-measured`;
     - reviews: `review-air-coverage`, `review-contract-correspondence`, `review-contract-semantics`, `review-extractor-independence`, `review-mapping-relation`, `review-prover-decision`, `review-translation-mutations`;
     - documents: `doc-contract-semantics`, `doc-property-set`, `doc-verification-plan`;
   - the Deployment_Splitter and the serial link: `test-splitter`, `pbt-splitter`, `mut-splitter`, `test-splitter-refusals`, `test-splitter-gumbo`, `test-splitter-peer-codegen`, `check-splitter-recompose-<model>`, `test-serial-frames`, `test-serial-sequence`, `test-serial-link-loss`, `pbt-frame-cross-codec`, `pbt-frame-round-trip-mcu`, `pbt-frame-round-trip-peer`, `doc-frame-grammar`, `hil-serial-throughput`;
   - the case study on the HIL_Rig: `review-robot-model`, `review-robot-peripheral-code`, `test-robot-model-parses`, `test-robot-generation`, `analysis-robot-stop-bound`, `hil-robot-nominal`, `hil-robot-faults`, `hil-robot-timeout`, `hil-robot-estop`, `hil-robot-limits`, `hil-robot-encoders`, `test-stop-reaction-measure`, `test-hil-robot-run-validity`, and the `robot` instances `check-generated-robot`, `check-tv-robot` and `analysis-rta-robot-measured`;
   - the robot integration: `doc-robot-integration-plan`, `review-robot-wiring`, `review-robot-hazards`, `check-p6-entry`, `test-robot-bench-<n>`, `test-robot-field-<n>`, `doc-robot-session-<n>`, and the instances `doc-checkpoint-p5` and `doc-checkpoint-p6` of `doc-checkpoint-p<n>`;
   - plan, registers, checkpoints and pins: `review-registers`, `check-trace`, `check-checkpoint`, `check-workspace-local`, `check-rebuild-<model>`, `check-commit-scope`, `check-licenses`, `review-reuse`, `test-git-read-only`, `test-unlisted-tool`, `test-air-normalized`.

   Each row SHALL give:
   - the name or pattern;
   - its kind: one of IronOxide's kinds (proof, test, analysis, measurement, check, review), or document for a `doc-` name;
   - the tool and the step of `cargo xtask verify` that produce it or, for a `review-` or `doc-` name, the review record or document;
   - the acceptance criteria that cite it;
   - the earliest Phase in which it is produced;
   - its frequency: every verification job; at most a stated number of hours between runs, with the reason (for example an hour-long HIL_Rig run of PAR-10); or only in the checkpoint run of R41.1. Criterion 7 judges each item's freshness by this frequency, so a HIL_Rig item may be hours old when a job uses it (UD-18);
   - each condition under which an acceptance criterion permits the outcome not run or not compared, with that criterion. Examples: R2.1 and R2.6 where FMIDE is not installed; R18.6 for a model that names a peripheral outside the `qemu_mps2_an386` peripheral table; R23.5 for a clause for which HAMR generates no GUMBOX executable that compiles; R32.8 for a candidate model whose JVM steps fail. Every row also lists the condition that the item's Phase is later than the Phase for which the Backend is built;
   - at each revision of the plan, the outcome, the tool versions and the date of the item's newest record in the evidence store.

   *Evidence: `doc-verification-plan`, `check-evidence` (fails naming each row that lacks a field and each pattern row that lists no value).*
