7. IF any of the following holds, THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail the verification job, recording `check-evidence` as failed and naming each criterion, name or Evidence_Item concerned and the condition:
   - an acceptance criterion of this document cites no Evidence_Item;
   - an acceptance criterion cites an Evidence_Item name that the table of criterion 6 defines neither as a name nor as an instance of a pattern whose row lists the instance's value;
   - a row of that table names, as citing it, an acceptance criterion that does not exist or that does not cite the row's name;
   - an Evidence_Item of a completed Phase has the outcome failed;
   - an Evidence_Item of a completed Phase has the outcome not run or not compared under a condition that its row of the table of criterion 6 does not list;
   - an Evidence_Item of a completed Phase is missing. It is missing when the evidence store holds no record of it from this verification job (frequency every job), none written within its stated number of hours before the job started (frequency in hours), or none from this checkpoint run (frequency checkpoint, judged only in the checkpoint run of R41.1) (UD-18). It is also missing when its newest such record concerns a binary whose Build_Manifest identity differs from that of this job's build.

   A completed Phase is a Phase whose checkpoint the Verification_Plan records as closed by the user (R41.1). In the checkpoint run that ends a Phase, that Phase also counts as completed. A criterion cites a name when its text, its Evidence note included, contains the name in backticks. The name is one of the prefixes `test-`, `pbt-`, `proof-`, `check-`, `mut-`, `diff-`, `hil-`, `analysis-`, `review-` and `doc-`, followed only by lowercase letters, digits, hyphens, underscores and the placeholders `<model>` and `<n>`. A name preceded by the word IronOxide is not cited in this sense; criterion 5 governs it. A range written "`X-01` to `X-nn`", such as "`test-map-01` to `test-map-31`", cites each name of the range. *Evidence: `check-evidence` (one fixture per condition, including a cited name with one character changed, an instance whose value its pattern's row does not list, a not-run record whose condition its row does not list, and a HIL_Rig record of a binary other than the current build's).*
8. WHEN a verification job ends, THE Build_System SHALL store in the evidence store, and print, a summary of the job with the following content:
   - each name of the table of criterion 6, and each instance of a pattern separately, listed once under exactly one of the outcomes passed, failed, not run, not compared and missing. The outcome is that of the item's newest record that criterion 7 does not count as missing, with the permitting condition for not run and not compared;
   - the Analyzer runs labelled development evidence, listed apart from those labelled timing evidence (R25.5);
   - the number of names under each outcome. Only names whose outcome is passed count as passed, and only passed Analyzer runs labelled timing evidence count as timing evidence.

   *Evidence: `check-evidence` (fixtures: a not-run item, a not-compared item, a missing item and a passed Analyzer run labelled development evidence; each is listed under its outcome or label, none is counted as passed except the Analyzer run, and the Analyzer run is not counted as timing evidence).*

### Requirement 40: Registers (Phase P1)

**User Story:** As a certification engineer, I want gaps, trusted components, proof assumptions, mutations and traceability kept in checked registers, so that I can see what the evidence does not cover.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. THE Gap_Register SHALL be the TOML file `docs/gap_register.toml` in the format of IronOxide's `docs/gap_register.toml` at PAR-01: the top-level keys `schema = 1`, `version` and `updated` (a date written YYYY-MM-DD), one `[[gap]]` table per gap, and one `[[orq]]` table per open research question of this document.
   - Each `[[gap]]` SHALL have these fields:
     - `id`: `GAP-` followed by two or more digits, unique in the register. IronOxide's gaps are cited as "IronOxide GAP-nn".
     - `title`.
     - `status`: `open`, `mitigated` or `closed`.
     - `owner`, one of four values: `prototype` for a deliverable of this project; `applicant` for an activity that only a certification applicant can perform; `shared` where this project supplies inputs to such an activity (IronOxide R54.4); `upstream` where closing the gap needs a change to IronOxide or HAMR, which NG-01 and NG-07 exclude.
     - `links`: the identifiers that the gap concerns.
     - `description`.
     - `plan`.
   - Each `[[orq]]` SHALL have these fields:
     - `id`.
     - `status`: `resolved`, `partly-resolved` or `deferred`, as design.md or the Verification_Plan records it, or `open` where neither records one.
     - `certification`: `true` where the question affects certification evidence, otherwise `false`.
     - `gaps`: the `id`s of the related gaps.
     - `note`.
   - The register SHALL hold, whatever their `status`, gaps for at least the following:
     - the unqualified Front_End, linking ASM-01 and ORQ-10;
     - HAMR's and the Backend's unverified code, which only the translation validation of the Task_Model covers, linking NG-02 and R29.7;
     - the trusted Semantic_Model_Extractor, linking ORQ-18;
     - measured WCETs, linking R25 and IronOxide GAP-04;
     - the Analyzer's unproven soundness against the Kernel's schedules (IronOxide Verification_Plan 9.5), linking IronOxide TBR-12;
     - the deviations of Table M-2: every DEV row of the current table version (R4.4) is linked by at least one gap;
     - the Pi_Side's unanalyzed timing, linking NG-04;
     - the proposals of `docs/ironoxide_proposals.md`: every IOP identifier defined there, and every IOP identifier that this document cites (IOP-01 to IOP-16), is linked by at least one gap.

   *Evidence: `check-registers` (fixtures: the register without each required gap, a DEV row linked by no gap, an IOP linked by no gap), `review-registers`.*
2. THE Trust_Base_Register SHALL be the TOML file `docs/trust_base_register.toml` in the format of IronOxide's `docs/trust_base_register.toml` at PAR-01 (IronOxide R48): the top-level keys `schema = 1`, `version` and `updated` (YYYY-MM-DD), one `[[entry]]` table per trusted component, and one `[[r40_2]]` table per item listed below.
   - Each `[[entry]]` SHALL have the following fields. IronOxide's fields keep the meaning that the header of IronOxide's register gives them (IronOxide R48.2, R48.3).
     - `id`: `TBR-` followed by two or more digits, unique in the register. IronOxide's entries are cited as "IronOxide TBR-nn".
     - `component`: the component, with its version or revision where it has one.
     - `version`: present exactly when PAR-01, PAR-02, R21.2 or R29.3 fixes the component's version, and then equal to that version or revision.
     - `trusted_for`: what the argument assumes of the component.
     - `why`: why the argument has to trust it.
     - `evidence`: what reduces the trust, or `none`.
     - `residual_risk`: what remains.
     - `requirements`: at least one criterion or requirement of this document that depends on the entry.
     - `properties`: the rows of the property allocation table of R39.1 that depend on the entry, or `all`.
     - `links`: the ASM, ORQ and GAP identifiers that the entry relates to, possibly none.
     - `ironoxide_tbr`: the IronOxide Trust_Base_Register entries at PAR-01 that the entry rests on, possibly none (R23.6).
     - `instances`: only in the entries that cover the items `not-carried-clauses` and `developer-spec-functions`. It holds one string per clause or function: the corpus model, followed by the clause identity of Table 28-1 row SM-10 or by the function's owner and identifier.
   - Each `[[r40_2]]` table SHALL have `item`, one of the keys below, and `entries`, the `id`s of at least one `[[entry]]` that covers the item. One entry may cover several items. The items are:
     - `hamr`: HAMR at PAR-02 (Front_End, AIR, symbol resolution, Slang type checking of GUMBO);
     - `sireum`: Sireum at PAR-02, whose build reports `Sireum v4.20261104.e4595836` (`docs/hamr_study.md` section 1);
     - `jvm`: the JVM that the Sireum build at PAR-02 installs, OpenJDK 25.0.4.1+1-LTS (Liberica);
     - `aadl-front-end`: OSATE or FMIDE, with the version that R1.5 records. Neither was installed on 2026-11-09 (ORQ-10). The Robot_Model is instantiated through FMIDE (R36.1, UD-05; Table 42-1 row 10);
     - `sysml-front-end`: HAMR's SysML v2 front end with the SysML v2 parser `hamr-sysml-parser` 4.20260911.6320233;
     - `ironoxide`: IronOxide at PAR-01, with its own Trust_Base_Register, whose entries this entry cites in `ironoxide_tbr` (ASM-05);
     - `ironoxide-extraction`: the workspace-local extraction of `IronOxide/` at PAR-01 against which generated Applications are built (R18.9, UD-02). The entry lists each file that the Build_System changes in the extraction (the workspace membership and `rsk-entry`'s binding), with the change, and cites IOP-07;
     - `verus`: Verus with Z3 and `vstd`, at the pins of R29.3;
     - `app-prover`: Creusot with Why3, why3find and its solvers, at the pins of R21.2;
     - `rust-toolchains`: the Rust toolchains of the Flight_Build (rustc 1.95.0), of Verus (R29.3) and of Creusot (R21.2);
     - `qemu`: QEMU;
     - `ironoxide-rig`: IronOxide's HIL_Rig, with its debug probe and flashing tool;
     - `pi-stand-in`: the Pi_Stand_In;
     - `tv-readers`: the two JSON readers of R29.6;
     - `tv-printer-and-entry`: the report printer and the command-line entry point of R29.6;
     - `CT-01` to `CT-13`: one item per row of Table 23-1 (R23.6);
     - `air-modes`, `air-subprogram-calls` and `air-annexes`: the three constructs of R16.9;
     - `air-reader-shared`: HAMR's AIR JSON reader, the one part that the Backend and the Semantic_Model_Extractor share (R28.2). The entry SHALL state that a misreading by this reader reaches both inputs of the TV_Checker alike, so the TV_Checker cannot detect it;
     - `semantic-model-extractor`: the Semantic_Model_Extractor (R28, R29.7; ORQ-18);
     - `air-path`: the AIR path of R31.8, which trusts that the AIR_Models the Model_Generator writes are ones a Front_End produces (ORQ-52);
     - `air-printer`: HAMR's AIR JSON printer `JSON.fromAadl`, which writes the sub-models (R34.6);
     - `deployment-splitter`: the Deployment_Splitter. The proof of R34.5 covers its IronOxide sub-model; its peer sub-model has test evidence only (R34.6, R34.7);
     - `recomposition-tool`: the recomposition tool of R34.7;
     - `not-carried-clauses`: the GUMBO clauses that R22.5 reports as not carried (CT-13);
     - `developer-spec-functions`: the developer-written logic functions of Table 22-1 row CK-03, each of which defines a GUMBO function's meaning without a check against the model;
     - `build-system`: the Build_System, which runs every tool, applies the register and assumption gates, and aggregates the verdicts (as IronOxide TBR-19).

   *Evidence: `check-registers` (one fixture per item without an entry), `review-registers`.*
3. THE Proof_Assumption_Register SHALL be the TOML file `docs/proof_assumptions.toml` in the format of IronOxide's `docs/proof_assumptions.toml` at PAR-01 (`schema = 1`).
   - Scope. The register SHALL list every assumption construct in this project's proofs. The proofs are those of two kinds of crate: every crate of this project that contains Verus or Creusot code (R21.4), and every Partition crate that the Backend generates for a corpus model, together with its developer code (R21.2). IronOxide's crates and vendored dependencies are excepted; IronOxide's own register and the items `ironoxide`, `verus` and `app-prover` of R40.2 cover them.
   - Constructs. The assumption constructs are:
     - Verus: `assume`, `admit`, `#[verifier::external_body]`, `#[verifier::external_fn_specification]`, `#[verifier::external_type_specification]`, `#[verifier::external]` and `assume_specification`, as IronOxide's register lists and uses them;
     - Creusot: `#[trusted]` (`creusot_std::macros::trusted`), and every other Creusot construct that the Verification_Plan records as making the App_Prover accept a contract or a body without proof (ORQ-69).

     A construct counts wherever it appears outside comments and string literals, inside `#[cfg_attr(…)]` included.
   - Construct entries. Each construct SHALL have one `[[assumption]]` table with these fields:
     - IronOxide's fields `file`, `construct` (as `check-proof-assumptions` prints it), `item` (the enclosing function, or `""` for every item of the file), `why` and `evidence`;
     - `model`, for a construct in the Application generated for a corpus model. `file` is then relative to that Application's root, as in R33.6;
     - for a developer function of R21.7: `tasks`, and `omits` (the Resource invariants and GUMBO contract clauses whose proof the function omits).
   - Step entries. The register SHALL also hold one `[[step]]` table for each proof step that R19.7, R22.7 or R24.5 records as not proven, with these fields:
     - `model`;
     - `item`: the instance path of the port or connection;
     - `statement`: the precondition or postcondition that is not proven;
     - `criterion`: the criterion that requires the entry;
     - `discharged_by`: the criteria, IronOxide criteria, Evidence_Items and IOP identifiers that discharge the step.

   IF a proof in this scope contains an assumption construct that no `[[assumption]]` table matches by `model`, `file`, `construct` and `item`, THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail `check-proof-assumptions`, and with it the verification job, naming the construct, the file, the line and the item, as IronOxide R50.2 does. *Evidence: `check-proof-assumptions` (one fixture per construct kind, each unlisted, failing and naming the construct; one fixture with the construct inside `#[cfg_attr(creusot, …)]`), `check-registers`.*
4. THE Traceability_Matrix SHALL be the Markdown file `docs/traceability_matrix.md`. `cargo xtask trace` generates it, as IronOxide generates its `docs/traceability_matrix.md` at PAR-01, from these inputs: this document, design.md, tasks.md, the Verification_Plan, the sources, and the evidence store of the last `cargo xtask verify` run (R39.2). The file's first line marks it as generated and records that run's source revision and the SHA-256 digest of its evidence store. The file SHALL contain:
   - a summary line giving the number of requirements, the number of acceptance criteria, how many criteria have a passing Evidence_Item and how many do not, and the number of Evidence_Items in the store;
   - a table of the same counts per Phase, where each requirement belongs to the Phase that its heading names;
   - for each requirement, a table with one row per acceptance criterion and these columns:
     - Criterion;
     - Allocation: the rows of the property allocation table of R39.1 that cover the criterion;
     - Design: the design.md sections that cite it;
     - Tasks: the tasks.md tasks that list it, each marked done or open;
     - Code: the source files that contain its identifier;
     - Evidence_Items: each Evidence_Item that the criterion's Evidence names, with the verdict that the store records for it, or `absent`;
   - a section "Criteria without a passing Evidence_Item", grouped by Phase, that lists each criterion for which the store holds none of its named Evidence_Items with the verdict pass, together with the criterion's tasks and design sections (IronOxide R50.6).

   A name with a placeholder (`<model>`, `<n>`) stands for every Evidence_Item in the store whose name it matches. It counts as passing only when the store holds at least one such item and every one of them passes. IF the committed file differs from the file that `cargo xtask trace` generates from the inputs that its first line records, THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail `check-trace`, naming the first line that differs. *Evidence: `check-trace` (fixtures: a hand-edited row; a criterion whose only named Evidence_Item is absent from the store; a `<model>` item that fails for one corpus model).*
5. IF an identifier that this document, design.md, tasks.md, the Verification_Plan, a register of this project or the Traceability_Matrix cites does not resolve, THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail `check-registers`, and with it the verification job. The failure SHALL name the identifier, the citing file and line, and the document or register in which the identifier was looked up. An identifier resolves only in the following cases:
   - a criterion `Rn.m`, a `Requirement n`, a row of Table M-1 of the current table version (R4.4) with its label where one is given (for example `MAP-24 (d)`), a row of Table M-2 of that version, a row of another table of this document (for example `CT-07`, `CK-03`, `SM-10`, `PM-04`), or an `ASM-nn`, `ORQ-nn`, `NG-nn`, `PAR-nn` or `RN-nn`: this document defines it;
   - an `IOP-nn`: `docs/ironoxide_proposals.md` defines it;
   - an `F-n`: section 10 of `docs/hamr_study.md` defines it;
   - a `GAP-nn` or `TBR-nn`: it is the `id` of an entry of the Gap_Register or the Trust_Base_Register;
   - an Evidence_Item name: the Evidence_Item table of the Verification_Plan (R39.6) has a row of that name. A name with a placeholder resolves to a row with the same placeholder;
   - a `P-nn`: the property allocation table of the Verification_Plan (R39.1) has a row of that identifier;
   - a `UD-nn`: the section "Decisions applied pending the user's approval" of this document defines it;
   - a `PR-nn`, or an identifier prefixed "IronOxide": the IronOxide document at PAR-01 that holds identifiers of its kind defines it. These are `docs/profile.md` for `PR-nn`; the documents of `.kiro/specs/rsk-ravenscar-profile/` for `Rn.m`, `DD-nn`, `ASM-nn`, `ORQ-nn` and `NG-nn`; `docs/trust_base_register.toml` for `TBR-nn`; `docs/gap_register.toml` for `GAP-nn`; and `docs/verification_plan.md` or `docs/traceability_matrix.md` for an Evidence_Item.

   An identifier of the form `ORQ-new-…` or `IOP-new-…`, the drafting labels that the merge replaced by numbers, never resolves; the check keeps failing on any that reappears. *Evidence: `check-registers` (one fixture per identifier kind, each citing an undefined identifier, failing and naming it).*
6. IF any of the following conditions holds for the Gap_Register, the Trust_Base_Register or the Proof_Assumption_Register, THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail `check-registers`, and with it the verification job, naming the register, the entry and the condition. The mutation registers are checked under R30.12 and R33.3. The conditions are:
   - the file is not valid TOML, or it lacks a top-level key that R40.1, R40.2 or R40.3 requires;
   - an entry lacks a field that those criteria require, or a required text field is empty or contains only white space;
   - a field holds a value outside the set or form that those criteria give for it;
   - two entries of one register have the same `id`;
   - a gap or item that R40.1 or R40.2 requires has no entry, or an `[[r40_2]]` table names an `id` that no `[[entry]]` has;
   - an ORQ of this document has no `[[orq]]` table, or an `[[orq]]` `status` differs from the status that design.md or the Verification_Plan records for that ORQ;
   - an entry's `version` differs from the version of the same component that an Evidence_Item in the evidence store of the last `cargo xtask verify` run records;
   - the `instances` of the entry that covers `not-carried-clauses` differ from the clauses that the generation reports of the corpus models list as not carried (R22.8);
   - the `instances` of the entry that covers `developer-spec-functions` differ from the functions that those reports list under Table 22-1 row CK-03, or under CK-04 where such a function is generated as CK-03;
   - an `[[assumption]]` table matches no construct in the proofs of R40.3;
   - a `[[step]]` table names a port or connection that its corpus model does not have;
   - a `GAP-nn` or `TBR-nn` that the register held at the last Phase checkpoint (R41.1) is missing, and the Verification_Plan's change log (R41.2) has no entry for its removal that states the rationale and the user's approval.

   *Evidence: `check-registers` (one fixture register per condition).*
7. IF an acceptance criterion of this document does not end with an Evidence line that names at least one Evidence_Item, or its Evidence line names an Evidence_Item, other than one prefixed "IronOxide", whose name does not begin with one of the prefixes of the Conventions (`test-`, `pbt-`, `proof-`, `check-`, `mut-`, `diff-`, `hil-`, `analysis-`, `review-`, `doc-`), THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail `check-registers`, and with it the verification job, naming the criterion and the Evidence_Item. *Evidence: `check-registers` (fixtures: a criterion without an Evidence line, a criterion with an empty Evidence line, a criterion that names `foo-x`).*

### Requirement 41: Phase checkpoints and changes to criteria (Phase P1)

**User Story:** As the user, I want each Phase to end in a checkpoint that runs everything and asks me questions, and no threshold or obligation weakened without my approval, so that progress is never bought by lowering the bar.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN the checkpoint of Phase Pn runs (P1 to P6; the last task of Pn in tasks.md, as IronOxide's tasks 9, 18, 20 and 22 end its Phases), THE Build_System SHALL write the checkpoint record `doc-checkpoint-p<n>` of criterion 4. The record states the outcome (R39.2) of every Evidence_Item that the Evidence of an acceptance criterion of Phase Pn or earlier names. This covers every test, property-based test, proof, mutation suite, differential test, analysis, check, HIL_Rig suite, review and document, with each `<model>` item once for each model of its corpus. Each item is produced by `cargo xtask verify` (R39.2) on one committed revision, the checkpoint revision, with the Backend built for Pn (R16.8; for P6, built for P5). The record also holds a copy, at the checkpoint revision, of the Gap_Register, the Trust_Base_Register, the Proof_Assumption_Register, the Proof_Mutation_Register, the Translation_Mutation_Register and the Traceability_Matrix. Two definitions apply:
   - A criterion's Phase is the Phase that its requirement heading names. Where the heading names several Phases, it is the Phase that the heading, Table 16-1, Table 30-1 or Table 33-1 assigns to the criterion, as the Traceability_Matrix records it for each criterion (R40.4).
   - An Evidence_Item counts as produced only when the evidence store (R39.2) holds it for the checkpoint revision, made by the tool and on the platform that its criterion names, with at least the runs, models and duration that the criterion and its PAR values state. A `doc-` item counts as produced when the document exists at the checkpoint revision. A `review-` item counts as produced when its record names a revision at which the files it reviews are byte-identical to those of the checkpoint revision. Any other item has the outcome not run, for example a QEMU run in place of a HIL_Rig run, or an item stored for an earlier revision.

   *Evidence: `doc-checkpoint-p<n>`, `check-checkpoint` (fixtures: a record that omits an Evidence_Item that the Traceability_Matrix links to a criterion of Phase Pn or earlier; an item stored for an earlier revision listed as produced; a QEMU item listed in place of a `hil-` item; a `review-` item whose reviewed file changed after the review).*
2. THE Verification_Plan SHALL keep a change log, in a form that the Build_System reads, that records every change to a unit of Table 41-1. This covers this document's requirements, Tables M-1 and M-2, the Parameters table, the decisions of the section "Decisions applied pending the user's approval" (UD-nn), the Semantic_Model schema digests of R28.5, and the removal or change of registered mutants and mutations (R30.14, R33.7). One entry may record several units. The user's approval of a UD-nn decision is a change-log entry: it names the UD row, states whether the user approves the decision applied or chooses its alternative, and, where the user chooses the alternative, records as changed units the row and each criterion and table row that the change touches. How the Build_System tells the user's approval from an entry that someone else wrote is ORQ-73. Entries are numbered from 1 in order, and no number is reused. Entry 1 records the revision of this document that the user first approved, with the digest (criterion 3) of each of its units. Each later entry states:
   - its number and its date (YYYY-MM-DD);
   - for each unit that it changes: the unit's kind and identifier from Table 41-1; the old text or value, or "added"; the new text or value, or "removed"; and the digest of the new text;
   - whether the change relaxes a unit. Relaxing means one of the following: removing a criterion, refusal, mutant, mutation or conjunct; lowering a minimum count, a margin, a number of runs or models, or a duration; raising a bound that a measured or analysed value must stay within; moving a criterion to a later Phase; or adding an exclusion entry (R30.4) or a proof assumption (R40.3). For a relaxing change, the entry also names the Evidence_Item whose verdict led to the change, with that verdict, and explains why the unit is changed rather than the implementation;
   - the rationale;
   - the affected requirements and Evidence_Items;
   - for a row of Table M-1 or M-2, the fields of R4.4;
   - for a schema, the identifier n and the digest of R28.5;
   - for a Proof_Mutation_Register mutation that is not killed, the verdict, the cause and the `id` of the replacing mutation (R33.7);
   - for a removed or retargeted Translation_Mutation_Register mutant, the `review-translation-mutations` record (R30.14);
   - the user's approval: the user's name, the date, and the record in which the user gave the approval (a numbered answer in a checkpoint record of criterion 4, or another record that the entry cites). An entry without this field approves no unit.

   *Evidence: `doc-verification-plan`, `check-criteria-approved` (one fixture entry for each field, each lacking that field).*
3. IF a run of `cargo xtask verify` (R39.2) finds a unit of Table 41-1 that differs from its approved state as the table's "Counts as a change" cell states, or a change-log entry that lacks a field that criterion 2 requires, or a change log without entry 1, THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail the verification job, whatever option, register field or environment setting is given. For each finding, the report SHALL name the unit's kind and identifier, its approved text or value with the change-log entry or checkpoint record it comes from, and its current text or value.
   - Digest: the digest of a unit of this document is the SHA-256 of its UTF-8 text, delimited by the parser that builds the Traceability_Matrix (R40.4), after each run of white space is replaced by one space and leading and trailing white space is removed. A change of layout alone is therefore no difference.
   - Approval: a change-log entry without the user's approval approves no unit. No option or setting skips this check.
   - Precedent: the check follows IronOxide's Profile change log, whose current entry must carry the digest of the Profile source (`content_hash` in `profile/profile.toml`, IronOxide R1.4).

   *Evidence: `check-criteria-approved` (fixtures for each row of Table 41-1: the unit changed with no entry, failing; the unit changed with an entry that lacks the approval, failing; the unit changed with an approved entry, passing. Also: a change of layout alone, passing; an Evidence_Item that records PAR-08 as 100, failing; and each option and environment variable of `cargo xtask verify` set in turn, failing alike).*
4. THE Verification_Plan SHALL hold each checkpoint record `doc-checkpoint-p<n>` that criterion 1 writes, as IronOxide's Verification_Plan sections 1.1 to 1.3 hold its checkpoints, with the following contents:
   - Phase Pn; the checkpoint revision as a 40-digit commit hash; the dates of the first and the last run; the Phase for which the Backend was built (R16.8); and the outcome of `check-pins` (R42.1), with its warnings (R42.9);
   - one line for each Evidence_Item of criterion 1. The line gives the item's name (with the model, for a `<model>` item), the criteria it covers, its outcome (passed, failed, not run, not compared or missing; R39.2, R39.7) and the SHA-256 digest of the stored item (R39.2). For an outcome other than passed, it also gives the reason (for failed, the first failed check in the item's output) and, where criterion 5 requires one, the Gap_Register entry;
   - the number of items with each outcome, and the summary of R39.8 for the checkpoint run;
   - each acceptance criterion of Phase Pn or earlier for which the Traceability_Matrix links no Evidence_Item (R40.4), with its Gap_Register entry;
   - the report of `check-criteria-approved`, and every change-log entry recorded since the previous checkpoint record;
   - the register copies of criterion 1 and, for each row of Table 33-1 whose Phase is Pn or earlier, the conjunct list of R33.5;
   - the questions put to the user, numbered from 1:
     - one for each change-log entry recorded since the previous checkpoint record, asking the user to confirm the approval;
     - one for each Evidence_Item with an outcome other than passed;
     - one for each ORQ whose "Affects" column names a requirement or criterion of Phase Pn or earlier and that neither design.md nor the Verification_Plan resolves or defers with a recorded rationale;
     - one for each UD-nn decision that no change-log entry with the user's approval names, asking the user to approve the decision applied or to choose its alternative;
     - one for each decision that design.md or the Verification_Plan marks as needing the user;
     - last, whether to close the checkpoint;
   - the user's answer to each question, with its date;
   - the checkpoint verdict: pass when every Evidence_Item of criterion 1 has the outcome passed, otherwise fail;
   - the user's closing decision (closed or not closed), with its date and the revision at which the user gave it.

   The record lists an Evidence_Item as passed only when the evidence store holds that item for the checkpoint revision with the outcome passed. *Evidence: `doc-checkpoint-p<n>`, `check-checkpoint` (fixtures: an item listed as passed whose stored outcome is failed; an outcome count that differs from the item lines; a UD-nn decision without approval and without a question; a question without an answer; a record whose last question is not the closing question).*
5. IF, at the checkpoint of Phase Pn, an Evidence_Item of criterion 1 has the outcome not run, not compared or missing (R39.2, R39.7), or the Traceability_Matrix links no Evidence_Item to an acceptance criterion of Phase Pn or earlier (R40.4), THEN THE Gap_Register SHALL hold an entry for that criterion naming the criterion, the Evidence_Item, its outcome, the reason that the evidence cannot be produced in Pn (for example, hardware that is not available), the owner, the plan, and the Phase in which the evidence will be produced.
   - The entry does not change the item's outcome or the checkpoint verdict of criterion 4.
   - The checkpoint passes only when the item passes, or when an approved change (criteria 2 and 3) moves the criterion to a later Phase.
   - This follows IronOxide, whose hardware-pending items stayed open with QEMU results as pre-hardware evidence only, and which its Phase 1 checkpoint still required (IronOxide tasks 7 and 9).

   *Evidence: `check-registers` (fixture: a checkpoint record with a not-run item and no Gap_Register entry, failing and naming the criterion), `doc-checkpoint-p<n>`.*
6. IF the Build_System builds the Backend for Phase Pn+1 (R16.8, n from 1 to 4), or produces an Evidence_Item of a criterion of Phase P6, and the Verification_Plan holds no checkpoint record of the preceding Phase (Pn, or P5 before P6) that has the checkpoint verdict pass, an answer to every question and the user's decision closed, THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail the build, naming the Phase and each of these conditions that is not met (UD-09: the Backend for Phase Pn+1 is not built until the Pn checkpoint is closed). *Evidence: `check-checkpoint` (fixtures: no record; a record with the verdict fail; a record with an unanswered question; a record that the user did not close).*

**Table 41-1 — Units under the user's approval** (criteria 2 and 3). A unit's digest is computed as criterion 3 states. "Approved state" is what criterion 3 compares the unit against. Whether design.md content that criteria defer to (for example the diagnostic table of R16.3, the C_sample formula of R12.2 and the DD candidates) becomes a unit is ORQ-74.

| Unit kind | Units and their identifiers | Counts as a change | Approved state |
|---|---|---|---|
| Acceptance criterion | each criterion `Rn.m` of this document, with its Evidence and every list under it | its digest differs, or it is added or removed | the digest that the latest approved change-log entry naming the unit records; entry 1 for a unit that no later entry names |
| Requirement heading | each requirement heading, with its Phase text and its User Story, identified `Rn` | as for an acceptance criterion | as for an acceptance criterion |
| Table row | each row of each table of the section "Requirements" (Tables M-1 and M-2 by MAP or DEV identifier; Tables 2-1, 16-1, 28-1, 30-1, 33-1, 41-1 and the others by their first cell); each row of the Parameters table, by PAR identifier; each table's caption paragraph, by table number | as for an acceptance criterion | as for an acceptance criterion |
| Defining text | each entry of the Glossary, the Non-Goals and the Assumptions; each bullet of the Conventions and of the Fixed inputs; each row of the Phases table | as for an acceptance criterion | as for an acceptance criterion |
| Pending decision | each row of the table of the section "Decisions applied pending the user's approval", by its UD identifier, with its Criteria cell | as for an acceptance criterion | as for an acceptance criterion. Entry 1 records the row's digest but does not approve the decision: a row stays pending until a change-log entry with the user's approval names it (criterion 2), and criterion 4 asks the user about each pending row |
| Semantic_Model schema | each schema identifier `hamr-rsk-semantic-model/n` with Table 28-1 at version n | as R28.5 states | the digest that the change log records for identifier n (R28.5) |
| Translation mutant | each `[[mutation]]` of the Translation_Mutation_Register, by `id` | its `id` is missing, or its `kind`, `base`, `edit`, `conjunct`, `row`, `criterion` or `diagnostics` differs (R30.14) | the copy in the last checkpoint record (criterion 1), with every approved change-log entry recorded since then applied; none before the first checkpoint record |
| Translation exclusion | each `[[exclusion]]` of the Translation_Mutation_Register, by `scope` and `item` | it is added, or one of its fields differs | as for a translation mutant |
| Proof mutation | each `[[mutation]]` of the Proof_Mutation_Register, by `id` | its `id` is missing, or a field of R33.6 differs | as for a translation mutant |
| Proof conjunct | each conjunct in the R33.5 list of a Table 33-1 row, by row, by the function or lemma that carries it, and by text | a conjunct text of the approved list is absent from the current list of the same row and the same function or lemma | the list in the last checkpoint record (criterion 4), with every approved change-log entry recorded since then applied; none before the first checkpoint record |
| Proof assumption | each entry of the Proof_Assumption_Register (R40.3), by the construct it lists | it is added, or one of its fields differs | as for a translation mutant |
| Applied parameter | each PAR value that an Evidence_Item records, in the evidence store (R39.2), as the value it applied | the value differs from the PAR's value in the Parameters table, or an Evidence_Item that an Evidence naming the PAR also names does not record the value it applied | the PAR's row of the Parameters table at its approved state |

### Requirement 42: Pins, reproducibility and the external repositories (Phase P1)

**User Story:** As a verification engineer, I want the HAMR and IronOxide revisions pinned and the build reproducible inside the workspace, so that evidence produced once applies to the next build.

#### Acceptance Criteria

1. WHEN a Build_System command starts (`cargo xtask verify` of R39.2 or any other `cargo xtask` command), THE Build_System SHALL, before any other step, do the following for each row of Table 42-1 whose item the command runs, reads or builds against (for `cargo xtask verify`, every row needed from the current or an earlier Phase):
   - read the value named in the row's "Compared by `check-pins`" cell;
   - compare it with the row's pin as an exact string, so that a commit matches only on all 40 hexadecimal digits;
   - record both values in the `check-pins` Evidence_Item;
   - fail the command if the two values differ or the value cannot be read, naming the row, the pinned value and the value found.

   IronOxide has no git repository of its own. It is the `IronOxide/` directory of the git repository that also holds this workspace, and that repository is shared with other work, which may commit under `IronOxide/` (UD-01). For IronOxide, the compared item is therefore the workspace-local extraction of R18.9, against which every generated Application is built (UD-02), and not the working tree:
   - the extraction's tree must equal the tree of `IronOxide/` in commit PAR-01, path by path, in content and mode, with no path missing and none added. The Build_System compares each file's git blob identifier and mode with those that `git ls-tree -r <PAR-01> -- IronOxide` lists, each as an exact string; how it computes the identifiers of the extracted files without writing to the repository is a DD candidate;
   - each file that R18.9 changes is compared instead with the file of PAR-01 carrying exactly the change that the Trust_Base_Register lists for it, and a difference fails the command like any other;
   - where no extraction exists when the command starts, the Build_System first writes it from commit PAR-01, as R18.9 states, and then compares it.

   A difference between the working tree and PAR-01 does not fail the command: criterion 9 lists each one as a warning, and criterion 2 fails a command only on a change made under `IronOxide/` during the command. When this was checked (2026-11-12, HEAD 4d288ef of branch `jsinfer-rewrite`), `IronOxide/` differed from PAR-01 by one added tracked file, `IronOxide/docs/tutorial.md` (commit f5f776d), and held one untracked directory, `IronOxide/docs/paper/`. *Evidence: `check-pins` (fixtures: for each row, one with a differing value and one with the item absent; a commit equal to the pin in its first 7 digits only; an extraction with one added file, with one missing file, with one file changed outside the changes of R18.9, and with one file of R18.9 whose change differs from the Trust_Base_Register's; a working tree with one added tracked file under `IronOxide/`, which gives a warning of criterion 9 and the outcome passed), `check-ironoxide-extraction`.*
2. IF a path under `IronOxide/` differs between the snapshot that the Build_System takes of the IronOxide working tree when a Build_System command starts and a snapshot that it takes later in the same command, THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail the command, naming each differing path (relative to the repository root) and its kind of difference (NG-01; UD-01). In this criterion:
   - THE Build_System SHALL take the later snapshots when the command ends and after each build that compiles against the extraction of R18.9 (UD-02).
   - A snapshot lists every path under `IronOxide/`, tracked or untracked, ignored by git or not (for example under `IronOxide/target/`), with its kind (file, directory or link), mode, size and modification time, and the status that `git status --porcelain --ignored -- IronOxide` gives it.
   - A path differs when it appeared, disappeared, or changed in kind, mode, size, modification time or status between the start snapshot and the later one.

   The Build_System builds only against the extraction and writes nothing under `IronOxide/` (R42.3), so a difference between the snapshots is a change made during the command, which this criterion attributes to the Build_System. A change that other work makes under `IronOxide/` while the command runs cannot be told apart from it and also fails the command. A difference from PAR-01 that is present at the start and does not change during the command fails nothing; criterion 9 lists it as a warning (UD-01). *Evidence: `check-pins` (fixtures: a tracked file modified, one deleted and one mode-changed during the command; an untracked file created during the command; an untracked file present at the start and changed during the command; a file written under `IronOxide/target/` during the command; a tracked difference from PAR-01 and a staged change, each present at the start and unchanged, giving a warning of criterion 9 and no failure).*
3. THE Build_System SHALL write no file outside the workspace (the `HAMR-DAHN/` directory). This has the following consequences:
   - Every tool installation, toolchain, download cache, Cargo target directory, Generator output directory, work directory (R30.9), log and Evidence_Item that the Build_System creates lies inside the workspace.
   - Every other item that Table 42-1 places outside the workspace is only read and run in place. These items are the rustup toolchains and `cargo-creusot` installed before this project, the Creusot checkout, Why3 and its solvers, `qemu-system-arm`, `probe-rs` and `sigrok-cli`.
   - IronOxide is read in three ways only: by the read-only git commands of this criterion; by the listing of paths and their kind, mode, size and modification time that the snapshots of criterion 2 take; and by `git archive` of commit PAR-01 into the workspace-local extraction of R18.9, against which every generated Application is built; no build compiles against `../IronOxide` in place (UD-02). The extraction changes only the workspace membership and `rsk-entry`'s binding, as R18.9 and the Trust_Base_Register state (ORQ-37, IOP-07).
   - Whether FMIDE (Eclipse/p2) or its JVM writes outside its installation directory is ORQ-71; `check-workspace-local` covers `~/.eclipse` and `~/.p2` until then.

   THE Build_System SHALL set these environment variables for the processes it starts:
   - `CARGO_HOME`, `SIREUM_CACHE` and `COURSIER_CACHE`, for every process, to directories inside the workspace (at the study `external/.cache/cargo`, `external/.cache/sireum` and `external/.cache/coursier`, `docs/hamr_study.md` section 3);
   - `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`, for each Cargo build of a package that lies outside the workspace or in the extraction of R18.9 (IronOxide's tools), to a directory inside the workspace;
   - `RUSTUP_HOME`, for each process that installs a toolchain or runs one installed by the Build_System, to a directory inside the workspace.

   The repository's `.git` directory lies outside the workspace. THE Build_System SHALL therefore run git only with `--no-optional-locks` and only with subcommands that write neither the index, a ref nor a working-tree file. An item that no row of Table 42-1 lists falls under criterion 5. *Evidence: `check-workspace-local` (the environment of every process in the command log; a listing by path, size and modification time of `~/.cargo`, `~/.rustup`, `~/.m2`, `~/.ivy2`, `~/.sireum`, `~/Downloads/sireum`, `~/Library/Caches/Coursier`, `~/.cache/coursier`, `~/.eclipse`, `~/.p2` and each location of Table 42-1 outside the workspace, taken at the start and at the end of the verification run, with no difference), `test-git-read-only` (every git command in the command log is one of `git rev-parse`, `git ls-tree`, `git ls-files`, `git diff`, `git status`, `git log`, `git archive`, `git check-ignore` and `git submodule status`, each with `--no-optional-locks`).*
4. WHEN a generated Application is rebuilt on a clean checkout from the same model, Backend version and pinned toolchains, THE Build_System SHALL produce, byte for byte, the same results as the original build:
   - the AIR_Model file after criterion 6 (for a SysML v2 model, the Front_End runs again);
   - every file that the Backend writes, with the same set of relative paths and the same contents, including the generation report and the diagnostics documents of R3.3;
   - the Semantic_Model;
   - the Generated_Config and the Task_Model;
   - for each IronOxide_Target that R18.2 builds, the binary hash over loadable sections, computed as IronOxide R59.1 hashes a Flight_Build (IronOxide `rsk_elf::Elf::hash_input`).

   In this criterion:
   - A clean checkout is an extraction of the committed tree of `HAMR-DAHN/` at HEAD and of the tree of `IronOxide/` in commit PAR-01 (made with `git archive`, as IronOxide's `xtask/src/rebuild.rs` makes its copy), with the IronOxide tree changed as R18.9 changes its extraction (UD-02). The two trees sit side by side as in the repository. The extraction goes into a directory inside the workspace that holds no file the original build wrote and whose absolute path differs from that of the original build.
   - The clones and installations of Table 42-1 are those that criterion 1 accepted, either used in place or recreated at the same pins.
   - Both builds compile with each source root (the generated workspace and the IronOxide extraction) mapped to a fixed path in the compiled output, as IronOxide's builds do with `--remap-path-prefix` (DD candidate).
   - Criteria 1 and 2 apply to the original checkout.

   *Evidence: `check-rebuild-<model>` (both values of every compared file and hash, and the first difference; recorded as not run, never as passed, while a file of this project is uncommitted or untracked, because the copy would then not be the revision under test).*
5. IF a Build_System step needs a tool, toolchain, library or repository that no row of Table 42-1 lists, or whose row gives no pin (row 14 until ORQ-70 is resolved, row 16 until ORQ-09 is resolved), THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail the step before it runs, install and download nothing for it, and report the step, the item, the version it needs and the location where it would be installed. The report lets the user approve the installation and the new or changed row through a change under Requirement 41. Nothing is installed outside the workspace without that approval. *Evidence: `test-unlisted-tool` (a step that needs an unlisted tool; a step that needs a row without a pin; in both cases no file is written outside the workspace), `check-pins`.*
6. WHEN the Front_End writes an AIR_Model file, THE Build_System SHALL rewrite the file before the Backend, the Semantic_Model_Extractor or the Deployment_Splitter reads it (DD candidate; ORQ-24):
   - Each source-position file reference (a `file:` URI or an absolute path) that lies under the workspace, under `IronOxide/` or under a clone of Table 42-1 is replaced by the path relative to that root, prefixed by a fixed name for the root that design.md records.
   - Every other byte stays unchanged.
   - The step fails, naming the JSON path, for each string value of the result that still contains the absolute path of the workspace, of a location of Table 42-1 or of the home directory.

   The rewritten file is the AIR_Model file whose digest R1.5 records, and the file on which R18.4 and criterion 4 compare runs. *Evidence: `test-air-normalized` (one SysML v2 model run through the Front_End from two directories with different absolute paths gives byte-identical files; a fixture with the home-directory path in a field other than a source position fails, naming its JSON path), `check-rebuild-<model>`.*
7. IF any of the following holds in the git repository that holds the workspace (kiro-experimentation, which also holds `IronOxide/`), THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail the verification job, naming each such commit and path:
   - a commit reachable from HEAD changes a path under `HAMR-DAHN/` and also a path outside `HAMR-DAHN/`;
   - a path under `HAMR-DAHN/external/` or `HAMR-DAHN/target/` is tracked;
   - `git check-ignore` does not report both of these directories as ignored.

   This project commits its files only under `HAMR-DAHN/`, in commits that change nothing else. It never commits the clones and installations of Table 42-1, which are recreated from their pins. *Evidence: `check-commit-scope` (fixture repositories: a commit that also changes a file under `IronOxide/`; a tracked file under `external/`; a `.gitignore` without `external/`).*
8. IF a tracked file of this project breaks the reuse rule that Table 42-1 gives for the repository it comes from, THEN THE Build_System SHALL fail the verification job, naming the file, the row and the rule. A tracked file breaks a rule when any of these holds:
   - the reuse record of the Verification_Plan (each adapted file with its source repository, source path and Table 42-1 row) lists the file as adapted from a BSD-licensed repository of rows 2 to 4 or row 8, and the file lacks that repository's license notice;
   - the file is byte-identical to a file of a clone of rows 2 to 4 or row 8, and the reuse record does not list it;
   - the file is byte-identical to a file of the sysml-aadl-libraries clone (row 9, ORQ-21), or of a submodule of row 4 whose license is not recorded;
   - the file is the `hamr-sysml-parser` jar or byte-identical to an entry of that jar (row 7).

   *Evidence: `check-licenses` (one fixture per condition), `review-reuse` (each adapted file that is not a byte-identical copy, reviewed against its source).*
9. WHEN `check-pins` runs at the start of a Build_System command, THE Build_System SHALL list as a warning, in the `check-pins` Evidence_Item and in the command's output, every difference between the IronOxide working tree and the tree of `IronOxide/` in commit PAR-01 (UD-01), each with its size and modification time:
   - each tracked path that differs from PAR-01 in content, mode or presence, whether the change is committed, staged or neither (`git diff --name-status <PAR-01> -- IronOxide`);
   - each untracked path that git does not ignore;
   - each ignored path, as `git status --porcelain --ignored -- IronOxide` reports it (one entry per ignored directory).

   A warning does not change the outcome of `check-pins`. Every listed path is part of the start snapshot of criterion 2, so a listed path that changes or disappears during the command is a difference under criterion 2. When this was checked (2026-11-12, HEAD 4d288ef), the list held the added tracked file `IronOxide/docs/tutorial.md` (commit f5f776d), the untracked directory `IronOxide/docs/paper/`, and ignored build outputs such as `IronOxide/target/`. *Evidence: `check-pins` (fixtures: a tracked file that differs from PAR-01, a committed added file, a staged change, an untracked file and an ignored directory, each listed as a warning with the outcome passed; the untracked file changed during the command, failing under criterion 2).*

Versions and licenses are those of `docs/hamr_study.md` sections 1 and 2 unless the cell names another source.
