## Open Research Questions

Each question must be resolved, or deferred with a recorded rationale, in design.md or the Verification_Plan.

| ID | Question | Affects |
|---|---|---|
| ORQ-01 | How the Backend plugs into HAMR: HAMR's platforms are a closed enumeration in its CLI and `CodeGen.scala` (`docs/hamr_study.md` section 8), and whether an external jar can add plugins to the stock CLI is unconfirmed. Candidates: a separate tool that calls HAMR's Front_End and symbol table as libraries, unmodified; or a HAMR change adding a platform, proposed upstream. | R1, R18, R42 |
| ORQ-02 | The Backend's implementation language: Slang or Scala on HAMR's symbol table and GUMBO AST (reuses HAMR's resolution and typing, BSD-2-Clause), or Rust over AIR JSON (one language with IronOxide, but re-implements resolution). | R1, R22, R28.2 |
| ORQ-03 | Whether the AIR produced by OSATE and by the SysML front end carries data-access connections and `Concurrency_Control_Protocol` for shared data, which HAMR's back ends do not support; and whether it carries `Memory_Properties::Access_Right` on data access features (req-09 notes). | R9, R9.2, R28 (Table 28-1) |
| ORQ-04 | The direction of AADL `Priority` values in HAMR models (ASM-02), and whether a property to invert it is needed. | R7 |
| ORQ-05 | The Slang semantics of GUMBO operations on bounded types (overflow, division, conversions), given that HAMR's Verus translation truncates and its GUMBOX Rust panics (finding F-1). Partly answered by the Slang source at PAR-02 ([RN-12], UD-04): Slang marks the full-width `@bits` types S8 to S64 and U8 to U64 as wrapping (`SlangParser.scala`, `isWrapped`), its runtime wraps their arithmetic modulo 2^n (`Z.scala`), and Logika encodes them as SMT bit-vectors without a range check (`Smt2.scala`; `Logika.scala` `checkRange` skips bit-vector types) while it checks that divisors are non-zero; Slang's `toY` conversions require an argument in range. On overflow, HAMR's truncating Verus translation therefore agrees with Slang, and only HAMR's Rust GUMBOX executable differs. This project keeps a stricter meaning (ASM-09, DEV-11). Still open: Slang's rounding of division and remainder (Table 22-2 EX-06, EX-07), bitwise and shift operations (EX-08) and the `toRawY` conversions, each checked against Slang's source. | R22.3, R23.3, R23.7, ASM-09, DEV-11 |
| ORQ-06 | The formula for C_sample and for the DropOldest acceptance check, including release jitter J_rel, the start delay of DEV-01 and Sporadic sources; and its tightness. | R11.6, R12.2 |
| ORQ-07 | Whether freezing inputs at Job start (DEV-01) is acceptable for every connection, or whether some connections need freezing at the nominal release instant (for example a glue buffer indexed by Release instants). Also whether it is acceptable that Receive Input and Send Output are not atomic across ports: each port is a separate Critical_Section or Endpoint operation, so a higher-priority Task can write a Port_Cell between two reads of one Job's Receive Input or see some of a Job's outputs and not others, and an Overrun during Send Output (DEV-08) can leave some outputs sent and others not, while HAMR releases all outputs at completion. Candidates: state these cases under R23.4, or add a Table M-2 row (req-10, req-19 notes). | R10, R19.2, R19.3, R23.4 |
| ORQ-08 | Multiplexing several connections between the same pair of Tasks onto one Protocol-governed Endpoint, to stay within 8 Endpoints, and its effect on the Validation_Functions and the Chains. | R11, R17.3 |
| ORQ-09 | Which existing HAMR back end generates the Pi_Side: Linux (Slang transpiled to C), JVM, or ROS 2; and how its serial-port driver is supplied (HAMR's `Platform_Provided` provenance). | R34.2, R36.7 |
| ORQ-10 | The case-study model language: SysML v2 runs headless today; AADL needs OSATE or FMIDE, which can be installed inside the workspace (`$SIREUM_HOME/bin/install/fmide.cmd`) with the user's approval. UD-05 applies AADL through FMIDE, pending approval; ORQ-57 and ORQ-71 bear on the choice. | R1, R36 |
| ORQ-11 | Peripheral ownership on the robot's MCU: the GPIO port P0 and GPIOTE are single peripherals with one owner (PR-29) and one interrupt, and the Application holds at most PAR-04 Tasks; whether the design fits, or needs IOP-02 or IOP-04. UD-20 applies a structure of 5 Tasks in 3 Partitions that needs no IOP for the rig run; encoder counting at PAR-19 stays open as ORQ-61. | R15, R36.8 |
| ORQ-12 | The Trace_Comparator's comparison relation, given that HAMR's JVM simulation runs a static schedule (ASM-08) and IronOxide runs preemptive SRP with time stamps. | R32.3 |
| ORQ-13 | How to capture port-level traces from HAMR's JVM simulation (the generated `src/main/inspector` points at the HAMR Inspector; unconfirmed). | R32.2 |
| ORQ-14 | Whether GUMBO `flows`, `monitor` and `compositions` clauses can be carried later, for example compositions as system-level Connection_Obligations. | R22.5 |
| ORQ-15 | Whether HAMR's JVM back end supports GUMBO spec functions with developer bodies at a later revision (finding F-2), which limits the differential corpus. | R32.6 |
| ORQ-16 | Whether constant initial values (DEV-03) are enough for the case study, or whether some Initialize entry point code needs a first-Job initialization pattern. | R20 |
| ORQ-17 | Whether `IronOxide::Criticality_Level` should also accept the ARINC 653 property set's criticality property as an alias. | R2 |
| ORQ-18 | How the Semantic_Model_Extractor itself gains evidence beyond testing: a second independent AIR reader, a Verus-verified reader, or review. | R28, R40.1 |
| ORQ-19 | Whether IronOxide's Analyzer can be given the Serial_Bridge's receive path as a Chain start (an interrupt event) with the frame time of PAR-12 and PAR-13 added, for cross-processor latency budgets. | R26.4, R35.7 |
| ORQ-20 | The Pi_Stand_In on the HIL_Rig: the J-Link virtual COM port, a USB-UART adapter, or the stimulus board over the spare board-to-board pins (ASM-07). | R37.1 |
| ORQ-21 | The license of `hamr.aadl.library` in sysml-aadl-libraries (no license file found); the models reference it from its clone until it is known. | R2.1, R36.1 |
| ORQ-22 | Whether PAR-02's AIR JSON reader reads, without error, the AIR that FMIDE's OSATE writes. At PAR-02, `bin/install/fmide.cmd` installs by default OSATE `2.14.0-vfinal` on Eclipse `2023-12` with the HAMR plug-in `1.2026.07131214.177108b3` and the GUMBO plug-in `1.2026.09042016.febee0d9` ([RN-16]); these builds are older than PAR-02 (2026-11-04). Table 42-1 row 10 pins these versions; until FMIDE is installed (ORQ-10, UD-05), R1.1 is tested on the committed AIR files of the INSPECTA models. | R1.1, R42 (Table 42-1 row 10) |
| ORQ-23 | Where each Partition's stack size and RAM size come from, and the factors of AADL's size units. In HAMR's SysML AADL library, `Memory_Properties::Stack_Size` does not apply to processes (`Memory_Properties.sysml` line 54) and `Process` declares only `Period` (`AADL.sysml` lines 107 to 112). UD-06 sets `ram` = Data_Size + Stack_Size, because IronOxide's RAM region holds the stack (IronOxide DD-07). Candidates for the stack size: an IronOxide property on the process; a stack derived from the threads' `Stack_Size`. Also unconfirmed: whether AADL's KByte and MByte are 1,024 and 1,048,576 bytes, as IronOxide's KiB are; the req-28 notes report that AIR records sizes in bits. | R2.2, R8.8, MAP-07 |
| ORQ-24 | Which AIR element kinds carry a source position and a file URI, in the AIR of OSATE or FMIDE and of HAMR's SysML v2 front end (`AadlAST.scala`: `Name.pos` is an `Option[Position]` whose `uriOpt` is optional); whether HAMR's AIR JSON reader accepts the root-relative URIs that R42.6 writes in place of the SysML front end's absolute `file:///…` URIs; whether OSATE or FMIDE also write absolute URIs; and whether a Front_End's AIR output is otherwise byte-identical between runs (no time stamps, stable order). | R3.1, R3.7, R42.6, ASM-01 |
| ORQ-25 | Whether every IronOxide tool diagnostic names an item that the Backend can trace to an AADL element: a Link_Checker violation holds only a rule and a message (`rsk-link` `Violation`); a Config_Checker discrepancy holds an item, the expected and the found value, and no rule (`rsk-confcheck` `Discrepancy`); an Analyzer rejection is a `"[rule] message"` string, as are its utilization-above-1 and overflow cases (IronOxide R29.9, R29.6) and the items behind `b_source` and the interference terms. Candidates: parse the rule tags and messages in the Backend (a trusted step); IOP-12. | R3.4, R3.8, R27.6; IOP-12 |
| ORQ-26 | Which jitter-aware acceptance check applies to connections that release a Sporadic_Task. R10.5 (MAP-11 (a)) compares the source's period or MIT with the destination's MIT only; but sends and raises happen at Job completion, so two of them can be as little as T_src − (WCRT_src − BCRT_src) apart, and a source at the destination's rate can still cause MIT_Violations. A cross-Partition Dispatch trigger (R11.4, MAP-15) has no rate check at all (DEV-10). IronOxide records each arrival inside the MIT, or while a Release is pending, as an MIT_Violation of the sending Partition (IronOxide R10.2, R10.3, R10.7) and applies that Partition's fault response once the count exceeds its `IronOxide::MIT_Violation_Threshold` (IronOxide R10.4), which a recurring violation eventually reaches, for example in a run of PAR-10. Candidates: a check against the Analyzer's response times in P3, with MAP-11 and MAP-15 refusing connections that fail it; or a recorded residual. | R10.5, R11.4, MAP-11, MAP-15, DEV-04, DEV-10 |
| ORQ-27 | How entry points other than Initialize and Compute (Finalize, Recover, Activate, Deactivate) appear in the AIR_Model from each Front_End. HAMR's source at PAR-02 names only `Programming_Properties::Initialize_Entrypoint_Source_Text` and `Compute_Entrypoint_Source_Text` (`common/properties/Properties.scala`); the other names come from the AADL standard, which the project does not hold; HAMR's SysML AADL library refers to entry points as action definitions in `Threads.sysml`, which the clone lacks. | R5.5 |
| ORQ-28 | How the Port_Glue's WCET of R6.9 is obtained. IronOxide's WCET_Harness measures Task entries, Critical_Section sites, Endpoint operations, release conditions and Kernel operations (IronOxide R34.2), never part of a Task body. Candidates: (a) a lower bound with no new measurement, the sum of the Port_Cell site records (glue-only under R25.3), Δ_lock_in and Δ_lock_out per site, and `ops` × Δ_send or Δ_recv, which leaves out Wire_Type encoding and decoding, the drain of R12.1 and Validation_Function calls outside those operations; the req-25 notes recommend (a) with R6.9 reworded as a lower bound; (b) a separate measurement of the glue, which IronOxide R34.7 counts as an instrumentation difference. | R6.9, R25.2 |
| ORQ-29 | The string format of `Data_Model::Initial_Value`, the rule that turns it into a Rust constant of the Resource's type, and whether the AIR carries it. The `Data_Model` package of `external/sysml-aadl-libraries` does not declare the property, so a SysML model can give an initial value only through the `const fn` path, and an AADL model that sets it breaks the equivalence of R2.6. Candidates: drop `Initial_Value` and keep only the `const fn`; add an `IronOxide` property for initial values (changes Table 2-1); keep it pending this question. | R9.6, R9.8, R2.6 |
| ORQ-30 | Cross-Partition data port connections to Sporadic destination threads. A Sporadic_Task has a minimum but no maximum separation between Jobs, so no finite C_sample exists for its drain (R12.2). Candidates: refuse (applied: UD-07, R12.3, MAP-16 (c)); require a maximum separation between Releases that the model declares; accept with run-time counting only (R12.5, R12.8). | R12.3, MAP-16 |
| ORQ-31 | How the Backend obtains integer widths and string bounds. HAMR at PAR-02 reads neither `HAMR::Default_Bit_Width` nor `HAMR::Max_String_Size` (only `common/properties/Properties.scala` names them); its widths and bounds come from the CLI options `--bit-width` (default 64) and `--max-string-size` (default 100). HAMR's SysML `HAMR.sysml` library declares neither property, and it spells the `Array_Size_Kinds` literal `Bound` where `TypeUtil` matches `Bounded`. Also open: whether `Default_Bit_Width` applies to `Base_Types::Float`, and how `HAMR::Bit_Codec_Raw_Connections` is treated (its types resolve to BitType, which R14.8 refuses). As written, R14.4 refuses every `Base_Types::Integer` and `Base_Types::String` of a SysML v2 model. Candidate: define these properties in the IronOxide_Property_Set. | R14.1, R14.4, R14.8, R36 |
| ORQ-32 | How the code of an interrupt-released Task reads and clears its peripheral's events. IronOxide DD-06 leaves clearing the event to the Task's code, and the windows of EasyDMA-capable peripherals (RADIO, UARTE0, SPIM0 and SPIM1, SAADC, PWM0) are read-only to the Partition, so register writes go through `rsk::dma_write` and `rsk::dma_request` (IronOxide DD-08). Open: whether the Port_API wraps these calls. R35.7 settles the case of the Serial_Bridge. | R15.9, R19.1, R35.7 |
| ORQ-33 | Whether the AIR of HAMR's SysML instantiator lists `Dispatch_Protocol` on a device that does not set it. In SysML v2, `AADL::Device` declares the attributes `Dispatch_Protocol`, `Period` and `Compute_Execution_Time`, and the instantiator's base options set `devicesAsThreads = T` (`FrontEnd.scala`, with a TODO comment; [RN-14]). If it does, R15.4 refuses every SysML device. | R15.4, R36 |
| ORQ-34 | Whether OSATE or FMIDE fill the mode lists of AIR and emit mode-transition connections (`AadlAST.scala` has a `ModeTransition` connection kind), and how they treat `in modes` clauses on properties, connections and subcomponents, for which AIR at PAR-02 has no field; more generally, whether each Front_End drops the constructs that AIR cannot hold (subprogram call sequences, `in modes`, end-to-end flows) silently or reports them. No AIR file in the workspace has a mode, and HAMR's SysML front end always writes an empty mode list. | R16.1, R16.9, ASM-01 |
| ORQ-35 | Whether AIR records subprogram calls in any form outside BLESS. AIR at PAR-02 has no call-sequence element (`AadlAST.scala`). | R16.2, R16.9 |
| ORQ-36 | How each Front_End records annexes other than GUMBO. Non-empty EMV2 appears as `Emv2Clause` (HAMR's pca-pump test model); OSATE attaches an empty `Emv2Clause` to every component (the committed AIR of isolette, structs_arrays and INSPECTA `port_queues`; UD-16); the committed attestation-gate AIR has no entry for its thread's AGREE subclause; no AIR sample was found for BLESS, SMF or `OtherAnnex`. | R16.7, R16.9 |
| ORQ-37 | How the Build_System binds IronOxide's `rsk-entry` to a generated Application outside IronOxide's tree. At PAR-01, `rsk-entry` binds `rsk_system::Sys` from `apps/p1-demo/system` (default feature `app-p1-demo`) or `hil_stimulus_system::Sys` from `apps/hil-stimulus/system` (feature `app-hil-stimulus`), both by path inside IronOxide's tree (`crates/rsk-entry/Cargo.toml`, `src/lib.rs`), and IronOxide's `cargo xtask` commands `gen`, `qemu` and `verify` work on IronOxide's own root only; only `check --root` takes another root ([RN-09]). Candidates: a workspace-local extraction of `IronOxide/` at PAR-01 in which only the workspace membership and `rsk-entry`'s binding change (applied: UD-02, R18.9); a Cargo override of the path dependency (unconfirmed whether `[patch]` or `paths` overrides apply to path dependencies); IOP-07. | R18.9, R25, R37, R42; IOP-07 |
| ORQ-38 | How the Build_System obtains the Kernel event log, with its overflow count, at the end of a QEMU run. The P1 demo's hand-written safe state prints the log over semihosting and exits with status 0 (`apps/p1-demo/system/src/lib.rs`; `xtask/src/verify.rs` passes the run on `RSK-OK`), but in a generated Application `IronOxide::Safe_State` names a developer function. Candidates: a QEMU test variant with a generated safe state that reports the log; reading Kernel RAM through QEMU's gdb stub. | R18.6, R32 |
| ORQ-39 | What a Job means that calls Put Value more than once on one output port. HAMR is inconsistent at PAR-02: its Verus model of the Microkit Port_API keeps one value (`put_` ensures `final == Some(value)`), while its C runtime enqueues once per call (`producer_producer.c`). R10.1 to R10.3, R19.3, R19.5 and R19.7 assume that the latest value wins and that an event is raised or sent once per Job (DD candidate). | R10.1 to R10.3, R19.3, R19.5, R19.7 |
| ORQ-40 | Whether IronOxide at PAR-01 loads non-zero `INIT` values of Partition `resource` and `state` items at reset and on RESTART_PARTITION. The generated linker script places each Partition crate's `.data` in the NOLOAD section `.p{i}.bss` (`crates/rsk-gen/src/emit.rs`; `apps/p1-demo/gen/link.x`), whose range `rsk-entry`'s `Reset` zeroes, and the copy table of `Reset` holds only the Kernel's `.data`; the P1 demo uses only zero `INIT`s. This was read from source, not observed in a run; a QEMU test with a non-zero `INIT` settles it. Until then R20.5 initializes in the Task's first Job; IOP-08 proposes the change. | R9.6, R20.5, R20.6; IOP-08 |
| ORQ-41 | Whether Creusot at IronOxide's pinned version accepts `ensures` on a `const fn`. If it does not, every initial-value clause of R20.4 takes the `proof_assert!` form. | R20.4 |
| ORQ-42 | How the Build_System confirms that the installed Creusot is built from the pinned commit 18f2d371e. `cargo creusot version` reports only `0.15.0-dev`, and IronOxide copies the commit from its `decision.toml` instead of reading it from the installation. | R21.2, R21.6, R42 |
| ORQ-43 | Which floating-point operations and properties Creusot at the pins of R21.2 (0.15.0-dev at 18f2d371e, Why3 1.8.2) can state and prove: bit-pattern equality of `f32` and `f64` (R14.3), the IEEE semantics of arithmetic and comparison, and the results of `==` and `~~` on NaN and signed zeros. IronOxide's Verification_Plan section 7 calls Creusot's floating-point support partial, and IronOxide trusts its one level-B `f32` function (`part2::lowpass`) under IronOxide R46.5. Logika has an option that encodes F32 and F64 as reals, and reads `==` on floats as structural equality and `~~` as IEEE equality. UD-08 refuses floating-point forms in GUMBO clauses (Table 22-2 EX-17); still open: floating-point ports and compute code under R21.3 and R21.5. | R14.3, R21.3, R21.5, R22.3, R22.4, R23.3, R36.3 |
| ORQ-44 | What a GUMBO compute or handler clause without a `modifies` clause means (no frame condition, or nothing modified), and whether `modifies` may list ports. | CK-13 |
| ORQ-45 | HAMR's meaning of a GUMBO expression in three cases: an in event data port whose frozen queue holds more than one message; an out port on which a dispatch puts no value; and an event data out port on which a dispatch puts more than one value. HAMR's Microkit Rust API keeps one ghost value per port (an `Option` for event data) and carries the integration guarantee as the putter's `requires` (`external/runs/structs_arrays_sysml/…/producer_producer_api.rs`); this was not confirmed against HAMR's documentation. | R23.1, R23.4 |
| ORQ-46 | Whether GUMBO integration clauses may be attached to event ports, which carry only a 4-byte token. HAMR's Microkit `GumboRustPlugin` halts on them ("not a data or event data port"), while Arsit's `GumboGen` accepts them with an empty type; a comment in `GumboRustPlugin.scala` says that HAMR's resolver allows integration clauses only on data and event data ports, but no such check was found in `GclResolver.scala` ([RN-15]). CK-06 refuses them. If they are not allowed, R13.3 refuses every Upward event connection without `IronOxide::Validation_Function`, and the token's valid value (R11.3) is undefined; candidate: a generated token check. | R13.3, R24, CK-06 |
| ORQ-47 | Whether IronOxide's WCET and HIL path can measure and run a generated Application from outside its repository without change. At PAR-01 the WCET_Records come from `rsk-hil`'s Budget accounting (`crates/rsk-hil/src/wcet_records.rs`); `rsk-wcet` prints "not implemented yet"; `rsk-wcet-agent` holds no code; `hil-rig.toml` describes IronOxide's demo; `rsk_hil::release_timing::run` needs the demo's `RSK-OK` console verdict and runs demo-specific steps (pulse loopback, EasyDMA counters), and its trace freeze takes one expected Task; Δ_entry and Δ_fp come from demo-only cycle configurations ([RN-10], [RN-11]). Kernel_Timing_Parameters are per Application, because IronOxide's `docs/hil/kernel_timing_nrf52840_*.json` carry the hashes of IronOxide's demo builds. If the answer is no: IOP-10. | R25.1, R25.5, R37; IOP-10 |
| ORQ-48 | How the Backend obtains end-to-end flows and their `Latency`, given that AIR at PAR-02 records none: `AadlAST.scala` has `Flow` with the kinds Source, Sink and Path only and no properties; the flows in the isolette AIR are flow specification instances; HAMR's SysML front end writes `flows = ISZ()` (`Instantiate.scala`); and the SysML AADL library declares `Latency` but no end-to-end flow. Candidates: an upstream AIR extension (in conflict with NG-07 unless HAMR adopts it); a component property whose `appliesTo` names an `ElementKind.Flow` (unconfirmed); a property of the IronOxide_Property_Set that declares flows (the alternative of UD-03). UD-03 keeps the mapping, which reports zero flows meanwhile (R26.7). | R26, R28 (SM-09), R36.9, R37 |
| ORQ-49 | Whether the interval that the Chain bound covers (R26.8) is the latency that AADL and HAMR (ASM-04) assign to a flow: in particular, whether that latency begins at the first thread's dispatch, and whether it includes actuation after the last Job completes. | R26.8 |
| ORQ-50 | Chain hops whose receiver drains fewer messages per Job than the hop Endpoint's capacity (n_recv < `capacity` under R11.2, for OneItem or MultipleItems). The Analyzer charges each hop the receiver's period or MIT plus its WCRT, which assumes that the receiver's next Job reads the message; a message can wait several Jobs, so a PASS may be optimistic. Candidates: refuse such hops (Backend restriction); require n_recv = `capacity` on Chain hops; rely on ASM-05. | R26.3, R11.2 |
| ORQ-51 | Which Table M-1 rows, mapping alternatives and refused cases HAMR's SysML v2 front end and its libraries can express at PAR-02 (known gaps: `Overflow_Handling_Protocol`, `Input_Time` and `Output_Time`, end-to-end flows, modes), and whether the front end writes AIR for models that HAMR's linters refuse. | R31.1, R31.8 |
| ORQ-52 | How to obtain evidence that the AIR-path models of the Model_Generator are ones that a Front_End can produce. Candidates: re-derive a sample through the SysML path and compare the AIR; compare with the committed AIR of the INSPECTA models. The trust in the AIR path is listed in the Trust_Base_Register (R40.2). | R31.8, R40.2 |
| ORQ-53 | How IronOxide Job records leave the target. Under QEMU, `rsk::debug` is the candidate; unconfirmed whether the Profile_Lint and the Link_Checker accept it in Task code (the `rsk` crate's documentation says that the Link_Checker reports it in Flight_Builds). On the HIL_Rig, semihosting needs a debugger, halts the core on each write and logs `EV_DEBUGGER`. Candidates on the rig: record buffers in per-Task state, read through the probe after the run as the rig reads the Kernel log (Partition RAM bounds their size); the trace pins (Task index on analyzer channels D1 to D3, Kernel flag on D4, sampled at 2 MHz) for the order of Jobs; IOP-14. On the rig, Budgets must also cover the cost of writing the records (DEV-08). | R32.1, R32.2, R37.2; IOP-14 |
| ORQ-54 | Whether the record of IronOxide's event log (`kind`, `task`, `partition`, `prs`, `instant`, `detail` in `crates/rsk-logic/src/logic/log.rs`) identifies the Release_Signal or Endpoint of each `EV_MIT_VIOLATION` and `EV_ENDPOINT_FULL` entry, which Table 32-1 rows CR-02 and CR-05 need to attribute an entry to one connection; and whether events dropped under `discard` are logged at all. Until this is answered, R32.7 excludes `discard`, so DEV-06 is not exercised. | R32.7, Table 32-1 |
| ORQ-55 | How the JVM side of a differential test receives device events that match the rig's stimulus edges. HAMR handles devices only as threads (`--devices-as-thread`), and MAP-20 (b) refuses a device `Dispatch_Protocol`. Until this is answered, the differential corpus has no devices and CR-08 (DEV-09) is not exercised. | R32.7, CR-08 |
| ORQ-56 | Whether Creusot and why3find at the pins of R21.2, and Verus at the pins of R29.3, name the failed `ensures` clause or the kind of implicit check in their output (IronOxide's runner test shows only the function's goal, `Goal Coma.vc_send_req`); until this is answered, an implicit-goal kill requires only that every failing goal lies in the mutated function. Also whether replacing a conjunct's text by `true` always gives a well-formed contract (a risk: a conjunct that holds the only `#[trigger]` of a Verus quantifier); a neutralized run that fails to type-check counts as unexpected, not killed. | R33.2, R33.6 |
| ORQ-57 | `Deployment_Properties::Actual_Connection_Binding` on the SysML path. HAMR's SysML instantiator converts only `Actual_Processor_Binding` allocations into AIR (`Instantiate.scala`, `processActualProcessorBindings`; [RN-14]), and no conversion of `Actual_Connection_Binding` was found; OSATE's export records it on connection instances (the AIR of HAMR's `uav_june_step6` test model). On the headless SysML path, R34.4 (d) therefore refuses every cross-processor connection. Candidates: the AADL path through FMIDE (applied for the case study: UD-05; ORQ-10); inferring the bus where exactly one bus has `IronOxide::Serial_Link` (changes R34.4 (d)); a HAMR change, which NG-07 rules out. | R34.4, R36.1 |
| ORQ-58 | How UARTE0 EasyDMA receive buffers are delimited into frames. EasyDMA ends a receive only when the buffer is full or on STOPRX (as the req-35 notes state; not checked against Nordic's product specification in this workspace), and PPI is Kernel-owned (IronOxide DD-08), so a Partition cannot count the bytes of a receive in progress. Candidates: the Pi_Side sends delimiter fill, so buffers fill at the line rate (frame latency then depends on the untrusted peer); the transmitting Task triggers STOPRX periodically to flush a partial buffer; IOP-15. R36.9's B_gap ≤ PAR-15 leaves about 5 ms for t_rx + WCRT_rx + t_pwm with Table 36-1's proposals (PAR-15 = 20 ms, T_sup = 10 ms, WCRT_sup up to D_sup = 5 ms), which rules out a STOPRX flush every 10 ms, the period of TH-05. | R35.7, R35.10, R36.9; IOP-15 |
| ORQ-59 | The CRC (polynomial, width, initial value) and the byte stuffing of the frame grammar, the CRC's Hamming distance at the maximum encoded frame length, and its residual probability of an undetected frame error (ASM-06). No values are stated, because none appear in the workspace sources. | R35.1, ASM-06 |
| ORQ-60 | How a link-loss report (R35.6) reaches the Port_API of each consuming Task. A status port per consuming Partition costs one of PAR-05's 8 Endpoints (related: ORQ-08); in the Robot_Model the only consumer is TH-01. A related open point: whether the Pi_Side Serial_Bridge sends a heartbeat frame, because without one a Pi_Side that has nothing to send for PAR-14 causes a link-loss report (req-35 notes). | R35.6, R36 (CN-03) |
| ORQ-61 | How encoder edges are counted at wheel speeds up to PAR-19 within an MIT that the Analyzer accepts. Counting each edge through GPIOTE needs the MIT of R36.11; as an illustration only, 1,200 edges per wheel turn on each signal with a 65 mm wheel gives about 5,900 edges per second per signal at 1.0 m/s, an MIT of about 170 µs, which the Analyzer may reject. Candidates: low-resolution wheel encoders; QDEC under IOP-04 (the number of QDEC instances of the nRF52840 is unconfirmed); IOP-16. Also open: wheel speeds above PAR-19 (coasting, or a pushed robot). | R36.11; IOP-04, IOP-16 |
| ORQ-62 | PWM0 at a stop: the pin levels after TASKS_STOP, after the Kernel stops a Partition's EasyDMA transfers on a stop or restart (IronOxide R14.5, R17.6), and in the safe state; whether the `IronOxide::Safe_State` function may access PWM0 and P0; how the supervisor changes the duty without writing a buffer while a transfer over it is in progress (IronOxide R17.5); and the value of t_pwm in the bounds of R36.9. | R36.9, R36.10 |
| ORQ-63 | The robot rig's wiring and logic-analyzer channels. At PAR-01, `hil-rig.toml` wires only P1.01 to P1.04 board to board (P1.01 is the stimulus line), the spare board is `wired = false`, and with the trace pins kept on D1 to D4 and the stimulus mirror on D6, analyzer channels D0 (the demo's response line), D5 and D7 remain: at most two PWM outputs and the transmit line ([RN-10]). Encoders and the emergency stop need more lines, and more again if ASM-07 puts the UART on the spare pins. IronOxide's `hil-rig.toml` is read-only (R18.9), so the robot needs its own rig description. Interacts with ORQ-11 and ORQ-20. | R37.1, Table 37-1, ORQ-20 |
| ORQ-64 | Whether per-Job Release and completion records are needed when the Task_Model declares no Chain: the per-Task maximum response times, each at most its WCRT, already cover every Job. UD-24 requires the records only where the Task_Model declares a Chain. | R37.2 |

IronOxide's `apps/hil-stimulus` drives only the demo's pulse, `rsk-entry` binds only Applications inside IronOxide's tree (ORQ-37), and the emergency-stop and reset sequences need one time base, because two free-running clocks drift apart over an hour. | R37.1, R37.6 |

Stage B of the Robot_Integration_Plan measures them before stage F. | R38.2, R38.3 |

| ORQ-69 | Which constructs of Creusot at the pins of R21.2, other than `#[trusted]`, make the App_Prover accept a contract or body without proof (for example external specifications or axioms). | ORQ-71 | Whether FMIDE (Eclipse and p2) or its JVM writes outside its installation directory, for example to `~/.eclipse`, `~/.p2` or the Java preferences, which R42.3 forbids. | R42.3, ORQ-10 |
| ORQ-72 | Whether identifiers and the naming rule of R8.6 can be stated and compared in Verus 0.2026.10.04.426d8b0 directly over `String`, or whether the readers must first convert identifiers to sequences of code points, which adds a trusted step. | R29.1, R29.2, R29.6 |
| ORQ-73 | How the Build_System tells the user's approval from an entry that someone else, such as an agent, wrote. Candidates: commits that the user signs; approvals accepted only as answers in checkpoint records; a file that only the user edits. At present the only protection is the user's confirmation of every entry at the next checkpoint (R41.4). | R41.2, R41.3 |
| ORQ-74 | Whether design.md content to which criteria defer becomes a unit of Table 41-1: for example the diagnostic table of R16.3, the C_sample formula of R12.2 and ORQ-06, and the DD candidates. A change there can weaken a criterion without a change to this document. | R41, Table 41-1 |
| ORQ-75 | Whether properties that no Table M-1 row reads but that change port or dispatch semantics, such as `Thread_Properties::Urgency`, `Communication_Properties::Input_Time` and `Communication_Properties::Output_Time`, are refused or ignored. R4.2 covers element kinds only (req-04 notes). | R4.2, R16 |
| ORQ-76 | Whether HAMR intends one integration clause per port: `GclResolver.scala` keeps one clause per port in `integrationMap` (the last one), while R22.7 and R24.1 use the conjunction of all integration clauses of a port (req-24 notes). | R22.7, R24.1 |
| ORQ-77 | Whether the Verdict_Report is also given as Sireum `Message`s, so that OSATE or FMIDE can show it as markers on the model (req-27 notes). | R27 |
| ORQ-78 | How AIR records that a data type extends a floating-point base type, which the `Uses_FPU` row of Table 2-1 and Table 28-1 row SM-08 need; the isolette SysML AIR holds nested `Data_Model::IEEE754_Precision` subcomponents, and no confirmed source shows how extension is recorded (edit-log-E5). | Table 2-1, R28 |
| ORQ-79 | How the HIL_Rig times the Kernel's response instant (a Partition stop or restart, the safe state) against the logic-analyzer capture for R36.10 (b) and (c); and how the fault of R37.4 case (g) is injected into the safety Partition's crate on a stimulus edge mirrored on D6. R37.4 cases (g) and (h) are judged from the mirror edge or from the end of the run's last Job, which design.md times on the capture under this question. | R36.10, R37.4 |

## Research Notes and Sources

Facts below were checked on 2026-11-09. Content was rephrased for compliance with licensing restrictions.

- **RN-01** HAMR's informal semantics: the supported component categories (system, process, thread group, thread, data as types), unsupported ones (subprograms, shared data blocks, devices as such), unidirectional typed ports, no fan-in on data ports, Periodic and Sporadic dispatch only, Initialize and Compute entry points only, and the input-compute-output model with infrastructure and application port states, inputs frozen at dispatch and outputs released at completion. [HAMR modeling elements and semantics](https://hamr.sireum.org/hamr-doc/hamr-semantics-concepts/)
- **RN-02** HAMR documentation of GUMBO contracts and their translation to Verus in Rust application code. [GUMBO component contracts](https://hamr.sireum.org/hamr-doc/gumbo-component-contracts/), [GUMBO to Verus translation](https://hamr.sireum.org/hamr-doc/gumbo-verus-translation/), [HAMR Rust implementation](https://hamr.sireum.org/hamr-doc/hamr-rust-implementation/), [HAMR documentation index](https://hamr.sireum.org/hamr-doc/)
- **RN-03** HAMR source at PAR-02: platform dispatch (`hamr/codegen/jvm/src/main/scala/org/sireum/hamr/codegen/CodeGen.scala`), plugin interfaces (`common/plugin/Plugin.scala`, `microkit/plugins/MicrokitPlugin.scala`), linters (`common/symbols/Linter.scala`, `microkit/plugins/linters/MicrokitLinterPlugin.scala`), the domain schedule (`microkit/plugins/c/components/CComponentPlugin_DomainScheduler.scala`), the GUMBO-to-Rust translation (`microkit/plugins/gumbo/GumboRustPlugin.scala`), the GUMBO AST (`hamr/air/shared/src/main/scala/org/sireum/hamr/ir/GumboAST.scala`). [sireum/kekinian](https://github.com/sireum/kekinian)
- **RN-04** IronOxide at PAR-01: `.kiro/specs/rsk-ravenscar-profile/{requirements,design,tasks}.md`, `docs/profile.md`, `docs/verification_plan.md`, the registers in `docs/`, `apps/p1-demo/decl/src/lib.rs`, and the crates `rsk-gen`, `rsk-model`, `rsk-analyzer`, `rsk-protocol`, `rsk-lint`, `rsk-link`, `rsk`, and, for the criteria and proposals of this draft, `rsk-entry` (the Application binding and `Reset`), `rsk-confcheck`, `rsk-logic` (`logic/log.rs`, the event record), `rsk-kernel` (its features) and `toolchain/decision.toml` (the Creusot pins). In the repository, `IronOxide/` has no tracked difference from PAR-01 at commit f0b7c99; re-checked on 2026-11-12 with read-only git, HEAD 4d288ef of branch `jsinfer-rewrite` contains commit f5f776d, which adds the tracked file `IronOxide/docs/tutorial.md`, and `IronOxide/docs/paper/` is untracked (UD-01, R42.1, R42.9). [mars2292-umn/kiro-experimentation IronOxide](https://github.com/mars2292-umn/kiro-experimentation)
- **RN-05** INSPECTA models used in the study: `micro-examples/microkit/gumbo-verus/structs_arrays` and `isolette`. [loonwerks/INSPECTA-models](https://github.com/loonwerks/INSPECTA-models)
- **RN-06** The SysML v2 AADL and HAMR libraries that the SysML front end needs; the AADL property defaults of ASM-03 are taken from their comments where present. [santoslab/sysml-aadl-libraries](https://github.com/santoslab/sysml-aadl-libraries)
- **RN-07** The study record of this project: versions, licenses, commands and outcomes, the HAMR architecture, findings F-1 to F-4, and the confirmation of IronOxide's claims. `docs/hamr_study.md`
- **RN-08** The IronOxide change proposals IOP-01 to IOP-16 (IOP-13 optional). `docs/ironoxide_proposals.md`
- **RN-09** IronOxide's `cargo xtask` at PAR-01, `IronOxide/xtask/src/`: `main.rs` (the commands; only `check --root <dir> [--workspace-only]` takes another root, while `gen`, `qemu` and `verify` work on IronOxide's root), `verify.rs` (the verification job; a QEMU run passes on exit status 0 and the console text `RSK-OK`), `qemu.rs` (the QEMU command line of R18.6), `hil.rs` (the HIL features `FEATURES` = `nrf52840,debug-port,quiet,trace-ram,trace-pins` and `FEATURES_LEAN`; HIL runs never use the Flight_Build binary), `rebuild.rs` (the rebuild check with `--remap-path-prefix`), `evidence.rs` (the record format `rsk-evidence/1`), `tools.rs` and `proofs.rs` (tool and solver version records), `mutations.rs` and `confcheck_mutations.rs` (the mutation runners), and `checks/toolchain.rs`. Used by R18, R25, R29.3, R30, R33, R37, R39 and R42.
- **RN-10** IronOxide's rig description at PAR-01, `IronOxide/hil-rig.toml` (schema `rsk-hil-rig/1`): the target, stimulus and spare boards by probe serial (the spare `wired = false`), the fx2lafw logic analyzer at 2 MHz with channels D0 to D7, the trace pins (Task index on D1 to D3, Kernel flag on D4), and the wiring (P1.01 the stimulus line; P1.02 to P1.04 board-to-board spares; the stimulus mirror on D6; the demo's response line on D0). Used by R32.2, R37, ASM-07, ORQ-63.
- **RN-11** IronOxide's HIL_Rig reader at PAR-01, `IronOxide/crates/rsk-hil/src/`: `events.rs` (the event mirror of 512 entries and the trace freeze), `wcet_records.rs` (WCET_Records from the Kernel's Budget accounting), `release_timing.rs` (the rig run harness, which needs the demo's `RSK-OK` verdict), `kernel_timing.rs`, `response.rs` and `stats.rs`; and `IronOxide/crates/rsk-wcet/src/main.rs`, which at PAR-01 prints that it is not implemented, with `crates/rsk-wcet-agent`, which holds no code. Used by R25, R37, ORQ-47, IOP-10.
- **RN-12** Slang and Logika at PAR-02 (UD-04, ORQ-05): `slang/parser/shared/src/main/scala/org/sireum/lang/parser/SlangParser.scala` (`isWrapped` for full-width `@bits` types), `runtime/library/shared/src/main/scala/org/sireum/Z.scala` (wrapping arithmetic), `logika/shared/src/main/scala/org/sireum/logika/Smt2.scala` (bit-vector encoding) and `logika/shared/src/main/scala/org/sireum/logika/Logika.scala` (`checkRange`, which skips bit-vector types), all in `external/kekinian`. [sireum/kekinian](https://github.com/sireum/kekinian)
- **RN-13** HAMR's ART runtime at PAR-02, `hamr/codegen/art/shared/src/main/scala/art/` (copied into each JVM output under `src/main/art`): `Art.scala`; `scheduling/static/StaticScheduler.scala`, `Explorer.scala` (`executeSlotIMP`), `Command.scala` (`Hstep`, `RunToHP`), `ISZCommandProvider.scala` and `DefaultCommandProvider.scala`; `ArtNative_Ext.scala` (`logInfo`). The per-model `Schedulers.scala` of HAMR's JVM output holds the default static schedule. Used by R32 and ASM-08.
- **RN-14** HAMR's SysML v2 front end at PAR-02, `hamr/sysml/frontend/jvm/src/main/scala/org/sireum/hamr/sysml/`: the instantiator `instantiation/Instantiate.scala` (`processActualProcessorBindings`, which converts only processor bindings; components built with `flows = ISZ()`), `FrontEnd.scala` (base options with `devicesAsThreads = T`), and `integration/IntegrationConstraints.scala` (the claims that `sireum hamr sysml logika` checks). Used by R24, R26, R31, R34, ORQ-33, ORQ-48, ORQ-57.
- **RN-15** HAMR code generator sources at PAR-02 cited by the criteria, under `hamr/codegen/shared/src/main/scala/org/sireum/hamr/codegen/`: `common/properties/PropertyUtil.scala` (`toPicoseconds`, which converts a time value and unit to the nearest whole picosecond and warns when the rounding exceeds a relative noise bound of 2^-50), `common/resolvers/GclResolver.scala` (GUMBO resolution; `integrationMap` keeps one integration clause per port) and `GclResolverUtil.scala` (the Slang types of AADL base types), `common/sysvc/VCGenerator.scala` (`generateIntegrationVCs`), `microkit/plugins/monitors/MonitorInjector.scala` (insertion of declared connections and connection instances into AIR), `microkit/plugins/rust/apis/CRustApiUtil.scala` (the shapes of the Microkit Port_API), `common/types/TypeResolver.scala` and `common/StringUtil.scala` (marker merge); and under `hamr/codegen/jvm/src/main/scala/org/sireum/hamr/codegen/`: `CodeGen.scala` (`invertMarkers`) and `arsit/gcl/GumboGen.scala`. Used by R14, R18.5, R22, R24, R28.3, R34.1, ORQ-46. [sireum/kekinian](https://github.com/sireum/kekinian)
- **RN-16** The FMIDE installer of Sireum at PAR-02, `bin/install/fmide.cmd` in `external/kekinian`: by default OSATE `2.14.0-vfinal` on Eclipse `2023-12`, the HAMR plug-in `1.2026.07131214.177108b3` and the GUMBO plug-in `1.2026.09042016.febee0d9`; it writes a `VER` file into the installation, which R42.1 reads. Used by R42 (Table 42-1 row 10), ORQ-10, ORQ-22, ORQ-71.
